## XIV A Village Wedding / XIV 乡村婚礼

===Original===
He had made but a few steps within the forest, when he found himself surrounded by his wolves. He was pleased to see them again; he slackened his pace; he called to them; and the wolves came crowding round him. Thibault caressed them as a shepherd might his sheep, as a keeper of the hounds his dogs. They were his flock, his hunting pack; a flock with flaming eyes, a pack with looks of fire. Overhead, among the bare branches, the screech-owls were hopping and fluttering, making their plaintive calls, while the other owls uttered their melancholy cries in concert. The eyes of these night-birds shone like winged coals flying about among the trees, and there was Thibault in the middle of it all, the centre of the devilish circle.

Even as the wolves came up to fawn upon him and crouch at his feet, so the owls appeared to be attracted towards him. The tips of their silent wings brushed against his hair; some of them alighted to perch upon his shoulder.

“Ah!” murmured Thibault, “I am not then the enemy of all created things; if men hate me, the animals love me.”

He forgot what place the animals, who loved him, held in the chain of created beings. He did not remember that these animals which loved him, were those which hated mankind, and which mankind cursed.

He did not pause to reflect that these animals loved him, because he had become among men, what they were among animals; a creature of the night! a man of prey! With all these animals together, he could not do an atom of good; but, on the other hand, he could do a great deal of harm. Thibault smiled at the thought of the harm he could do.

===Chinese===
他在林中才走出没几步，便发觉自己已被那群狼围在了当中。他又见着它们，心中欢喜；便放慢了脚步，唤了它们几声；群狼便争先恐后地簇拥到他身边来。蒂博（Thibault）抚弄它们，犹如牧人抚弄他的羊群，又如猎犬管事抚弄他的猎狗。它们便是他的羊群，他的猎队；一群眼中燃着火焰的羊，一队目光如火的猎犬。头顶上方，光秃秃的枝桠间，鸣角鸮正蹦跳扑棱，发出一声声哀啼，而旁的鸮鸟也跟着齐声啼唤，叫声凄切。这些夜鸟的眼睛亮闪闪的，活像一团团长着翅膀的炭火在树林间飞来飞去，而蒂博便置身于这一切的正中，是这魔圈圆心的所在。

正如群狼会上来讨好他、卧倒在他脚边，那些鸮鸟也似乎被他吸引了去。它们那悄无声息的翅膀尖儿擦过他的头发；有几只落下来，栖在了他的肩头。

“啊！”蒂博喃喃道，“那么，我并不是万物之敌了；人虽恨我，畜生却是爱我的。”

他却忘了，这些爱他的畜生，在万物生灵的链条里占据的是怎样的位置。他也不记得，这些爱他的畜生，正是那些恨人类的畜生，正是人类所诅咒的畜生。

他更不肯停下来想一想：这些畜生之所以爱他，是因为他在人当中，已经变成了它们在畜生当中所是的那种东西——一个属于黑夜的活物！一个食人的猛兽！把这一窝畜生凑在一块儿，他连一丝一毫的善也做不出来；可反过来，他却能造下天大的孽。一想到自己能造下的那些孽，蒂博便笑了。

===Original===
He was still some distance from home, and he began to feel tired. He knew there was a large hollow oak somewhere near, and he took his bearings and made for it; but he would have missed his way if the wolves, who seemed to guess his thoughts, had not guided him to it. While flocks of owls hopped along from branch to branch, as if to illuminate the way, the wolves trotted along in front to show it him. The tree stood about twenty paces back from the road; it was, as I have said, an old oak, numbering not years, but centuries. Trees which live ten, twenty, thirty times the length of a man’s life, do not count their age by days and nights, but by seasons. The autumn is their twilight, the winter, their night; the spring is their dawn, the summer their day. Man envies the tree, the butterfly envies man. Forty men could not have encircled the trunk of the old oak with their arms.

The hollow made by time, that daily dislodged one more little piece of wood with the point of its scythe, was as large as an ordinarily sized room; but the entrance to it barely allowed a man to pass through. Thibault crept inside; there he found a sort of seat cut out of the thickness of the trunk, as soft and comfortable to sit in as an armchair. Taking his place in it, and bidding good night to his wolves and his screech owls, he closed his eyes and fell asleep, or at least appeared to do so.

The wolves lay down in a circle round the tree; the owls perched in the branches. With these lights spread around its trunk, with these lights scattered about its branches, the oak had the appearance of a tree lit up for some infernal revel.

===Chinese===
他离家还有相当一段路，已开始觉得疲倦。他记得附近某处有一棵中空的大橡树，便辨了辨方向，朝它走去；可若不是那些狼——它们仿佛能猜透他的心思——一路把他领了过去，他几乎要迷了路。成群的鸮鸟在枝桠间一跳一跳地跟着，仿佛在为路途点灯照明，群狼则在他前头一路小跑，替他指引路径。那棵树离大路约莫二十步开外；正如我方才所说，这是一棵老橡树，它的年岁不是以年计，而是以世纪计。那些寿数比人长上十倍、二十倍、三十倍的树木，不是拿昼夜来计算年纪的，而是拿节气来计算的。秋天是它们的黄昏，冬天是它们的夜晚；春天是它们的黎明，夏天是它们的白昼。人嫉妒树，蝴蝶嫉妒人。要四十个汉子张开臂膀，才能把这棵老橡树的树干合抱过来。

岁月所凿出的那个树洞——岁月每天都要拿它那柄镰刀的刀尖，从木头里再剔落一小片下来——已经有一间寻常屋子那么大了；可洞口却窄得只容一个汉子勉强钻进去。蒂博手脚并用地爬了进去；他在里头寻见一处像是座位的东西，是从那粗厚的树身里凿出来的，坐上去竟如安乐椅一般柔软适意。他便在里头坐定，向他的狼群和他的鸣角鸮道了声晚安，闭上眼睛，睡着了——至少看上去是睡着了。

群狼在树四周卧成了一个圈；鸮鸟栖在枝桠上。树干周遭布满了这点点的亮光，枝桠间也撒满了这点点的亮光，那棵橡树瞧去便活像一棵为某场鬼域狂欢而张灯结彩的树。

===Original===
It was broad daylight when Thibault awoke; the wolves had long ago sought their hiding-places, the owls flown back to their ruins. The rain of the night before had ceased, and a ray of sunlight, one of those pale rays which are a harbinger of spring, came gliding through the naked branches of the trees, and having as yet none of the short-lived verdure of the year to shine upon, lit up the dark green of the mistletoe.

From afar came a faint sound of music, gradually it grew nearer, and the notes of two violins and a hautboy could be distinguished.

Thibault thought at first that he must be dreaming. But as it was broad daylight, and he appeared to be in perfect possession of his senses, he was obliged to acknowledge that he was wide awake, the more so, that having well rubbed his eyes, to make quite sure of the fact, the rustic sounds came as distinctly as ever to his ear. They were drawing rapidly nearer; a bird sang, answering the music of man with the music of God; and at the foot of the bush where it sat and made its song, a flower﻿—only a snowdrop it is true﻿—was shining like a star. The sky above was as blue as on an April day. What was the meaning of this springlike festival, now, in the heart of winter?

===Chinese===
蒂博一觉醒来，天已大亮；群狼早已回了各自的藏身之处，鸮鸟也飞回了它们栖身的废墟。昨夜的雨已经停了，一道阳光——那种苍白得犹如春天信使的阳光——从树木光秃秃的枝桠间滑了进来，因这一年里那转瞬即逝的新绿此时还无处可照，便照亮了槲寄生的墨绿。

远远地传来一阵隐隐约约的乐声，渐渐地近了，听得出是两把小提琴和一把高音双簧管（hautboy）的音符。

蒂博起初以为自己准是在做梦。可此时天已大亮，他又神志清明，便不得不承认自己是醒着的——更何况他把眼睛揉了又揉，好确认自己果真醒着，那一派乡野的乐声却照旧清清楚楚地送入他的耳中。乐声正飞快地逼近；一只鸟儿唱了起来，拿上帝的音乐来应和人的音乐；在它落脚唱歌的那丛灌木脚下，一朵花——不错，只是一朵雪花莲罢了——正像一颗小星星似的闪闪发亮。头顶上的天蓝莹莹的，一如四月里的光景。这隆冬时节里，这一场春意盎然的欢庆，究竟是什么来头？

===Original===
The notes of the bird as it sang in salutation of this bright, unexpected day, the brightness of the flower that shone as if with its radiance to thank the sun for coming to visit it, the sounds of merrymaking which told the lost and unhappy man that his fellow-creatures were joining with the rest of nature in their rejoicings under the azure canopy of heaven, all the aroma of joy, all this up-springing of happiness, brought no calmer thoughts back to Thibault, but rather increased the anger and bitterness of his feelings. He would have liked the whole world to be as dark and gloomy as was his own soul. On first detecting the sounds of the approaching rural band, he thought of running away from it; but a power, stronger than his will, as it seemed to him, held him rooted to the spot; so he hid himself in the hollow of the oak and waited. Merry voices and lively songs could be heard mingling with the notes of the violins and hautboy; now and again a gun went off, or a cracker exploded; and Thibault felt sure that all these festive sounds must be occasioned by some village wedding. He was right, for he soon caught sight of a procession of villagers, all dressed in their best, with long ribbons of many colours floating in the breeze, some from the women’s waists, some from the men’s hats or buttonholes. They emerged into view at the end of the long lane of Ham.

They were headed by the fiddlers; then followed a few peasants, and among them some figures, which by their livery, Thibault recognised as keepers in the service of the lord of Vez. Then came Engoulevent, the second huntsman, giving his arm to an old blind woman, who was decked out with ribbons like the others; then the majordomo of the Castle of Vez, as representative probably of the father of the little huntsman, giving his arm to the bride.

And the bride herself﻿—Thibault stared at her with wild fixed eyes; he endeavoured, but vainly, to persuade himself that he did not recognise her﻿—it was impossible not to do so when she came within a few paces from where he was hiding. The bride was Agnelette.

Agnelette!

===Chinese===
那鸟儿为迎接这明媚而意外的一天而唱出的歌喉，那朵花仿佛拿自己的光华来感谢太阳前来探望它而绽放的明亮，那一片欢腾的乐声——它们告诉这个迷途而不幸的汉子，他的同类正与万物一道，在苍穹之下共同欢庆——这一切喜悦的芬芳，这一派蓬勃涌现的幸福，并不能给蒂博的心头带回半分宁静，反倒更添了他胸中的怒火与苦涩。他巴不得整个天下都像他自己的灵魂一般阴沉晦暗。一开头听见那支乡野乐队渐近的声响时，他还想过远远地躲开；可有一股力量——在他看来比他的意志更为强大——竟把他钉在了原地，动弹不得；于是他藏身到橡树的树洞里，等着。欢快的嗓音、活泼的歌声，夹杂在小提琴和高音双簧管的乐音里，阵阵传来；时不时地放一声枪，或是炸响一个炮仗；蒂博断定，这诸般欢庆的声响，定是哪家村里办喜事娶亲闹出来的。他猜得不错，因为不多时他便瞧见一队乡民鱼贯而来，个个都穿上了自己最好的衣裳，五颜六色的长绸带在风里飘飘荡荡，有的系在妇人的腰间，有的从汉子的帽檐上或纽扣眼里垂下来。他们从哈姆（Ham）那条长长的小巷尽头现出身来。

打头的是那几个琴师；随后跟着几个庄稼汉，其中几张面孔，蒂博凭他们身上的号衣认出是韦兹领主（the lord of Vez）麾下的看林人。再往后是昂古勒旺（Engoulevent），第二号猎手，正挽着一位双目失明的老妇人，那老妇人也和别人一样浑身上下缀满了彩带；接着是韦兹城堡（the Castle of Vez）的总管，大约是代这位小猎手的父亲出席的，正挽着新娘。

而新娘本人——蒂博拿一双凶狠发直的眼睛死死盯着她；他竭力想叫自己相信认不出她来，却是徒劳——待她走到离他藏身之处不过几步远时，要装作不认得她是断断办不到的。那新娘正是阿涅莱特（Agnelette）。

阿涅莱特！

===Original===
And to crown his humiliation, as if to give a final blow to his pride, no pale and trembling Agnelette dragged reluctantly to the altar, casting looks behind her of regret or remembrance, but an Agnelette as bright and happy as the bird that was singing, the snowdrop that flowered, the sunlight that was shining; an Agnelette, full of delighted pride in her wreath of orange flowers, her tulle veil and muslin dress; an Agnelette, in short, as fair and smiling as the virgin in the church at Villers-Cotterets, when dressed in her beautiful white dress at Whitsuntide.

She was, no doubt, indebted for all this finery to the Lady of the Castle, the wife of the lord of Vez, who was a true Lady Bountiful in such matters.

But the chief cause of Agnelette’s happiness and smiles was not the great love she felt towards the man who was to become her husband, but her contentment at having found what she so ardently desired, that which Thibault had wickedly promised to her without really wishing to give her﻿—someone who would help her to support her blind old grandmother.

===Chinese===
更叫他无地自容的是——仿佛是对他的自尊再补上最后的一击——这个阿涅莱特，并不是一个面色苍白、浑身发抖、被人不情不愿地拖到圣坛前、一步三回头地留着眷恋或追忆之情的阿涅莱特，而是一个像那唱歌的鸟儿、那绽放的雪花莲、那照耀着的阳光一般明媚而快活的阿涅莱特；一个为自己的橙花花冠、薄纱面纱和细布新裙满心欢喜、颇为得意的阿涅莱特；一句话，一个如维莱-科特雷（Villers-Cotterets）教堂里那位圣处女般——在圣灵降临节穿上她那身美丽的白裙子时——一般姣好、一般含笑的阿涅莱特。

她这一身华美的打扮，想必都出自城堡夫人的恩赐——那位韦兹领主的夫人，在这等事上素来是一位名副其实的大善夫人。

可阿涅莱特之所以这般幸福、这般含笑，其缘由并非她对她未来的丈夫怀着多深的情爱，而是因为她终于得到了她梦寐以求的那样东西——那样蒂博曾恶毒地答应给她、却压根儿没打算给她的东西——一个肯帮她一同赡养她那瞎眼老祖母的人。

===Original===
The musicians, the bride and bridegroom, the young men and maidens, passed along the road within twenty paces of Thibault, without observing the head with its flaming hair and the eyes with their fiery gleam, looking out from the hollow of the tree. Then, as Thibault had watched them appear through the undergrowth, so he watched them disappear. As the sounds of the violins and hautboy has gradually become louder and louder, so now they became fainter and fainter, until in another quarter of an hour the forest was as silent and deserted as ever, and Thibault was left alone with his singing bird, his flowering snowdrop, his glittering ray of sunlight. But a new fire of hell had been lighted in his heart, the worst of the fires of hell; that which gnaws at the vitals like the sharpest serpent’s tooth, and corrodes the blood like the most destructive poison﻿—the fire of jealousy.

On seeing Agnelette again, so fresh and pretty, so innocently happy, and, worse still, seeing her at the moment when she was about to be married to another, Thibault, who had not given a thought to her for the last three month, Thibault, who had never had any intention of keeping the promise which he made her, Thibault now brought himself to believe that he had never ceased to love her.

===Chinese===
乐师、新郎新娘、少男少女们，从离蒂博不过二十步开外的大路上鱼贯而过，竟谁也没有发觉，从那树洞里探出来的一颗头颅上燃烧着火红的头发、一双眼睛里射出灼灼的光。于是，正如蒂博方才瞧着他们从灌木丛后冒出来一般，此刻他又瞧着他们从眼前消失。正如小提琴和高音双簧管的乐声方才一点点地响起来、响起来，如今便一点点地弱下去、弱下去，直到又过了一刻钟的光景，整座森林又恢复了先前的死寂与空旷，蒂博重又孤零零地留下来，与他那唱歌的鸟儿、那绽放的雪花莲、那一缕闪亮的阳光作伴。然而，他心里却已点燃了一团新的地狱之火，那是诸般地狱之火中最凶险的一团；它如最锋利的毒蛇之牙，噬咬人的心肝五脏，又如最烈性的毒药，腐蚀人的血液——这团火，便是嫉妒之火。

一重又见到阿涅莱特，瞧她是那般鲜嫩娇美、那般天真无邪地欢喜，更糟糕的是，偏偏是在她将要嫁给另一个男人的当口上撞见她——蒂博，这个整整三个月来不曾把她放在心上的蒂博，这个从来就没打算履行对她许下的诺言的蒂博，此刻竟自己说服了自己，认定自己从未中断过对她的爱。

===Original===
He persuaded himself that Agnelette was engaged to him by oath, that Engoulevent was carrying off what belonged to him, and he almost leaped from his hiding place to rush after her and reproach her with her infidelity. Agnelette, now no longer his, at once appeared to his eyes as endowed with all the virtues and good qualities, all in short that would make it advantageous to marry her, which, when he had only to speak the word and everything would have been his, he had not even suspected.

After being the victim of so much deception, to lose what he looked upon as his own particular treasure, to which he had imagined that it would not be too late to return at any time, simply because he never dreamed that anyone would wish to take it from him, seemed to him the last stroke of ill fortune. His despair was no less profound and gloomy that it was a mute despair. He bit his fists, he knocked his head against the sides of the tree, and finally began to cry and sob. But they were not those tears and sobs which gradually soften the heart and are often kindly agents in dispersing a bad humour and reviving a better one; no, they were tears and sobs arising rather from anger than from regret, and these tears and sobs had no power to drive the hatred out of Thibault’s heart. As some of his tears fell visibly adown his face, so it seemed that others fell on his heart within like drops of gall.

===Chinese===
他竟哄骗自己说，阿涅莱特是起了誓许配给他的，昂古勒旺这是抢走了本该属于他的人；他险些要从藏身之处一跃而出，追上去痛责她的负心。阿涅莱特，如今已不再属于他了，反倒在他眼里一下子拥有了种种美德和好处，一句话，拥有了种种娶她便大有好处的好处——而当初他只要一开口、这一切便都是他的之时，他却连想都未曾想到过。

他做了这许许多多骗局的牺牲品已不算，到头来竟还要失去这件他视为自家独有的宝贝，他原以为这件宝贝什么时候回头来取都为时不晚——只因为他做梦也没想过会有人把它从自己手里夺走——这在他看来，已是命运所降的最后、最狠的一击。他的绝望既深沉又阴郁，而这份绝望更叫他咬牙的是，它是一份说不出、喊不出的哑绝望。他咬自己的拳头，拿脑袋去撞树壁，到末了竟放声大哭、抽噎起来。然而这眼泪和抽噎，却不是那种能一点点化开人心肠、常常是驱散坏脾性、唤起好心性的善意之泪；不是的，这眼泪和抽噎，与其说是出于悔恨，不如说是出于愤恨，而这眼泪和抽噎丝毫也无力把蒂博心头的仇恨驱走。他脸上有些泪珠分明地滚落下来，似乎与此同时，还有些泪珠落进了他的心里头，如同苦胆的汁水一般。

===Original===
He declared that he loved Agnelette; he lamented at having lost her; nevertheless, this furious man, with all his tender love, would gladly have been able to see her fall dead, together with her bridegroom, at the foot of the altar when the priest was about to join them. But happily, God, who was reserving the two children for other trials, did not allow this fatal wish to formulate itself in Thibault’s mind. They were like those who, surrounded by storm, hear the noise of the thunder and see the forked flashes of the lightning, and yet remain untouched by the deadly fluid.

Before long the shoemaker began to feel ashamed of his tears and sobs; he forced back the former, and made an effort to swallow the latter.

He came out of his lair, not quite knowing where he was, and rushed off in the direction of his hut, covering a league in a quarter of an hour; this mad race, however, by causing him to perspire, somewhat calmed him down. At last he recognised the surroundings of his home; he went into his hut as a tiger might enter its den, closed the door behind him, and went and crouched down in the darkest corner he could find in his miserable lodging. There, his elbows on his knees, his chin on his hands, he sat and thought. And what thoughts were they which occupied this unhappy, desperate man? Ask of Milton what were Satan’s thoughts after his fall.

===Chinese===
他口口声声说自己爱阿涅莱特；他哀叹自己失去了她；然而这个满腔柔情蜜意的狂人，却巴不得能亲眼看着她和她的新郎，在神甫将要为他们主婚的那一刻，双双倒毙在圣坛脚下。所幸的是，上帝既然把这两个孩子留给别样的磨难，便没有容许这致命的愿望在蒂博的脑海里成形。他们便如同那些身处暴风雨之中的人一般：听得见雷声轰隆，看得见电光闪闪，却始终未被那致命的雷电沾身。

不多时，这鞋匠便为自己的眼泪和抽噎感到羞愧起来；他把前者强忍了回去，又竭力把后者吞进了肚里。

他从藏身之处钻出来，迷迷糊糊地不知身在何处，便朝着自家小屋的方向一阵狂奔，一刻钟的工夫便跑完了一里格的路；这一番疯狂的奔跑倒叫他出了一身汗，稍稍平静了一些。他终于认出了自家屋前那一片熟悉的光景；他钻进小屋，活像一头猛虎归穴，反手关上门，便走到他这间破屋子里所能寻见的最阴暗的角落，蜷缩下去。他两肘撑在膝盖上，下巴搁在手掌里，坐在那里想心事。这个不幸而绝望的汉子，满脑子盘算的究竟是什么心事？不妨去问弥尔顿（Milton），撒旦堕天之后想的是什么。

===Original===
He went over again all the old questions which had upset his mind from the beginning, which had brought despair upon so many before him, and would bring despair to so many that came after him.

Why should some be born in bondage and others be born to power?

Why should there be so much inequality with regard to a thing which takes place in exactly the same way in all classes﻿—namely birth?

By what means can this game of nature’s, in which chance forever holds the cards against mankind, be made a fairer one?

And is not the only way to accomplish this, to do what the clever gamester does﻿—get the devil to back him up? he had certainly thought so once.

To cheat? He had tried that game himself. And what had he gained by it? Each time he had held a good hand, each time he had felt sure of the game, it was the devil after all who had won.

===Chinese===
他又把那些自一开始便搅得他心神不宁的老问题，重新在脑子里翻来覆去地过了一遍——这些问题，在他之前已把不知多少人逼入绝境，在他之后还将把不知多少人逼入绝境。

为什么有的人生来便要受苦受难，有的人却生来便要大权在握？

在一桩在各个阶层里发生方式都一模一样的事——也就是降生这件事——上，为什么竟有如此悬殊的不平？

大自然玩的这一局牌，永远是运气跟人类作对，究竟有什么法子，才能叫它公平些呢？

而要办成这件事，唯一的一条路，岂不正是那个聪明的赌客所走的路——请魔鬼来替他撑腰么？他从前确也曾这样想过。

作弊么？这场把戏他自己是亲手试过的。可他又得到了什么好处？每一回他抓了一手好牌、每一回他自以为稳操胜券之时，到头来赢钱的，终究还是魔鬼。

===Original===
What benefit had he reaped from this deadly power that had been given him of working evil to others?

None.

Agnelette had been taken from him; the owner of the mill had driven him away; the Bailiff’s wife had made game of him.

His first wish had caused the death of poor Marcotte, and had not even procured him a haunch of the buck that he had been so ambitious to obtain, and this had been the starting point of all his disappointed longings, for he had been obliged to give the buck to the dogs so as to put them off the scent of the black wolf.

And then this rapid multiplication of devil’s hairs was appalling! He recalled the tale of the philosopher who asked for a grain of wheat, multiplied by each of the sixty-four squares of the chess board﻿—the abundant harvests of a thousand years were required to fill the last square. And he﻿—how many wishes yet remained to him?﻿—seven or eight at the outside. The unhappy man dared not look at himself either in the spring which lurked at the foot of one of the trees in the forest, or in the mirror that hung against the wall. He feared to render an exact account to himself of the time still left to him in which to exercise his power; he preferred to remain in the night of uncertainty than to face that terrible dawn which must rise when the night was over.

===Chinese===
这股能降灾于旁人的致命力量赋予了他，他究竟从中得了什么好处？

一无所得。

阿涅莱特被人夺了去；磨坊主把他撵了出来；执达吏的太太拿他开了心。

他的头一个愿望送了可怜的马尔科特（Marcotte）的命，却甚至连他觊觎已久的那头雄鹿的一条后腿都没能替他弄到手——而这正是他日后一切落空的贪念的开端，因为他不得不把那头鹿丢给猎犬们，好叫它们不去追踪那头黑狼。

再者，这一头魔鬼之发如此飞快地成倍滋生，实在叫人胆寒！他想起那个故事：一位哲人要人在棋盘六十四个方格上，每格所放的麦粒数都是前一格的一倍——要把最后一个方格填满，非得有一千年里最丰盛的麦收不可。而他——他还剩下多少个愿望了呢？——充其量不过七八个罢了。这不幸的人既不敢照林中某棵树脚下藏着的泉水，也不敢照墙上挂着的那面镜子，去端详自己一眼。他害怕对自己清清楚楚地交代一声：他施展这股力量的时日，还剩下多少；他宁可留在那蒙昧无知的黑夜里，也不愿去面对那一夜过后必然要升起的那可怖的黎明。

===Original===
But still, there must be a way of continuing matters, so that the misfortunes of others should bring him good of some kind. He thought surely that if he had received a scientific education, instead of being a poor shoemaker, scarcely knowing how to read or cipher, he would have found out, by the aid of science, some combinations which would infallibly have procured for him both riches and happiness.

Poor fool! If he had been a man of learning, he would have known the legend of Doctor Faust. To what did the omnipotence conferred on him by Mephistopheles lead Faust, the dreamer, the thinker, the preeminent scholar? To the murder of Valentine! to Margaret’s suicide! to the pursuit of Helen of Troy, the pursuit of an empty shadow!

And, moreover, how could Thibault think coherently at all of ways and means while jealousy was raging in his heart, while he continued to picture Agnelette at the altar, giving herself for life to another than himself.

===Chinese===
可总该有个法子，把这桩事继续下去，好叫旁人的灾祸多少替他换来一点好处才是。他暗想，倘若自己当初受的是科学的教养，而不是做一个连读书识字、算账打码都勉强得很的穷鞋匠，那么凭着科学的指点，他一定能寻出几样门道来，万无一失地替自己谋来财富与幸福。

可怜的傻子！倘若他当真是个有学问的人，他便该晓得浮士德博士（Doctor Faust）的传说。梅菲斯托费勒斯（Mephistopheles）所赐予浮士德——那个梦想家、思想家、卓绝的学者——的全能之力，究竟把他引到了什么田地？引到了谋杀瓦伦丁（Valentine）！引到了玛格丽特（Margaret）的自尽！引到了对特洛伊的海伦（Helen of Troy）的追逐——追逐一个空洞的影子！

况且，嫉妒既在他心头熊熊燃烧，他既一遍又一遍地在脑海里描摹着阿涅莱特站在圣坛前、把自己的一生一世交付给另一个不是他的男人的情景，蒂博又怎能条理分明地去盘算什么门道和法子呢？

===Original===
And who was that other? That wretched little Engoulevent, the man who had spied him out when he was perched in the tree, who had found his boar-spear in the bush, which had been the cause of the stripes he had received from Marcotte.

Ah! if he had but known! to him and not to Marcotte would he have willed that evil should befall! What was the physical torture he had undergone from the blows of the strap compared to the moral torture he was enduring now!

And if only ambition had not taken such hold upon him, had not borne him on the wings of pride above his sphere, what happiness might have been his, as the clever workman, able to earn as much as six francs a day, with Agnelette for his charming little housekeeper! For he had certainly been the one whom Agnelette had first loved; perhaps, although marrying another man, she still loved him. And as Thibault sat pondering over these things, he became conscious that time was passing, that night was approaching.

===Chinese===
那么，那另一个男人又是谁呢？便是那个卑鄙的小人昂古勒旺——正是他当初蒂博蹲在树上时把他给瞅了出来，又是他在灌木丛里寻见了那杆野猪矛，这才害得蒂博挨了马尔科特好一顿皮鞭。

啊！早知如此！他当初发愿降灾时，要降的灾祸便该落在昂古勒旺身上，而不是马尔科特身上！他从前受那皮鞭抽打的皮肉之苦，比起他此刻所煎熬的这份心头之苦来，又算得了什么！

倘若野心不曾这般紧紧攫住他，不曾借骄傲的翅膀把他捧到本不属于他的高位上去，那他本可以过上何等快活的日子——做一个手艺精巧、一天能挣六法郎的匠人，让阿涅莱特做他那娇小可人的当家娘子！因为阿涅莱特头一个爱过的人，确实是他；说不定她如今虽嫁作他人妇，心里头还是爱着他的呢。蒂博一面坐在那里翻来覆去地想着这些，一面渐渐觉察到时光正在流逝，夜色正一步步逼近。

===Original===
However modest might be the fortune of the wedded pair, however limited the desires of the peasants who had followed them, it was quite certain that bride, bridegroom and peasants were all at this hour feasting merrily together.

And he, he was sad and alone. There was no one to prepare a meal for him; and what was there in his house to eat or drink? A little bread! a little water! and solitude! in place of that blessing from heaven which we call a sister, a mistress, a wife.

But, after all, why should not he also dine merrily and abundantly? Could he not go and dine wheresoever he liked? Had he not money in his pocket from the last game he had sold to the host of the Boule-d’Or? And could he not spend on himself as much as the wedded couple and all their guests together? He had only himself to please.

“And, by my faith!” he exclaimed, “I am an idiot indeed to stay here, with my brain racked by jealousy, and my stomach with hunger, when, with the aid of a good dinner and two or three bottles of wine, I can rid myself of both torments before another hour is over. I will be off to get food, and better still, to get drink!”

===Chinese===
任凭那对新人的家当再怎么寒微，任凭跟随他们的那些乡民的口腹之欲再怎么有限，此刻新娘、新郎和一众乡民，必定正团团坐在一起，欢欢喜喜地大吃大喝，这是断然无疑的。

而他自己，却孤零零地悲苦着。没有一个人替他预备一口饭；他那屋子里，又有什么可吃可喝的呢？一点面包！一点清水！还有一片孤寂！——代替了那从天而降、我们名之为姊妹、情人与妻的福分。

可说到底，凭什么他自己就不可以也吃得欢天喜地、酒足饭饱呢？难道他不能随自己高兴，上哪儿去吃喝便上哪儿去吃喝么？他口袋里不是揣着钱么——那是他上回卖给金球客栈（the Boule-d’Or）老板的那头猎物所得？难道他不能把那对新人连同他们全体宾客的酒饭钱统统加在一处，都花在自己一个人身上？他可只需讨自己一个人的欢心便够了。

“凭我的信心起誓！”他失声叫道，“我留在这里可真是个十足的大傻瓜——脑壳子叫嫉妒搅得生疼，肚皮子又叫饥饿啃得发慌——其实呢，只要一顿好饭、两三瓶酒下肚，不出一个钟头，这两桩苦楚我便都能一齐打发掉。我这便动身去找吃的，更要紧的是去找喝的！”

===Original===
In order to carry this determination into effect, Thibault took the road to Ferté-Milon, where there was an excellent restaurant, known as the Dauphin d’Or, able it was said to serve up dinners equal to those provided by his head cook for his Highness, the Duke of Orleans.

===Chinese===
为把这一决心付诸实施，蒂博便取道往费尔泰-米隆（Ferté-Milon）去了。那里有一家顶呱呱的饭馆，字号叫金王太子（the Dauphin d’Or），据说端上席来的酒席，堪与奥尔良公爵（the Duke of Orleans）殿下的主厨亲手办出来的酒席相媲美。

## III Agnelette / III 阿涅莱特

===Original===
The Baron took the weapon which Engoulevent handed him, and carefully and deliberately examined the boar-spear from point to handle, without saying a word. On the handle had been carved a little wooden shoe, which had served as Thibault’s device while making the tour of France, as thereby he was able to recognise his own weapon. The Baron now pointed to this, saying to Thibault as he did so:
“Ah, ah, Master Simpleton! there is something which witnesses terribly against you! I must confess this boar-spear smells to me uncommonly of venison, by the devil it does! However, all I have now to say to you is this: You have been poaching, which is a serious crime; you have perjured yourself, which is a great sin; I am going to enforce expiation from you for the one and for the other, to help towards the salvation of that soul by which you have sworn.”
Whereupon turning to the pricker, he continued: “Marcotte, strip off that rascal’s vest and shirt, and tie him up to a tree with a couple of the dog leashes﻿—and then give him thirty-six strokes across the back with your shoulder belt, a dozen for his perjury, and two dozen for his poaching; no, I make a mistake, a dozen for poaching and two dozen for perjuring himself, God’s portion must be the largest.”

===Chinese===
男爵接过昂古勒旺（Engoulevent）递来的那件兵器，仔仔细细、不慌不忙地把这杆野猪矛从矛尖到矛柄审视了一遍，一句话也没说。原来那矛柄上刻着一只小小的木鞋，那是蒂博（Thibault）周游法兰西期间给自己立的标记，凭此便可认出这是自家的兵器。这时男爵指了指那只小木鞋，对蒂博说道：
“啊哈，傻大哥！这可是个要命的见证，对你大大的不利啊！我得承认，这杆野猪矛在我闻起来有一股子鹿肉味儿，好浓的一股子鹿肉味儿，凭魔鬼起誓！也罢，我如今要对你说的只有这几句话：你私猎，这可是重罪；你又发伪誓，这是大罪；这两桩我都要从你身上讨个抵偿，也好为你那拿灵魂起誓的灵魂的得救尽上一份力。”
说罢他转向猎犬长，接着吩咐道：“马尔科特（Marcotte），把那无赖的背心衬衫都扒下来，拿两根牵狗的皮绳把他捆到一棵树上去——然后用你的肩带在他背上抽三十六鞭，一打是赏他的伪誓，两打是赏他的私猎；不，我说错了，一打赏私猎，两打赏伪誓，归上帝的那一份可得是最大的。”

===Original===
This order caused great rejoicing among the menials, who thought it good luck to have a culprit on whom they could avenge themselves for the mishaps of the day.
In spite of Thibault’s protestations, who swore by all the saints in the calender, that he had killed neither buck, nor doe, neither goat nor kidling, he was divested of his garments and firmly strapped to the trunk of a tree; then the execution commenced.
The pricker’s strokes were so heavy that Thibault, who had sworn not to utter a sound, and bit his lips to enable himself to keep his resolution, was forced at the third blow to open his mouth and cry out.

===Chinese===
这道命令惹得那班仆从个个欢喜不迭，他们觉着总算撞上了好运，有个犯事的人好叫他们把这一日里种种窝囊气狠狠地出一出。
任凭蒂博指着日历上众圣徒一一起誓，说他既没宰过雄鹿也没宰过雌鹿，既没碰过山羊也没碰过羊羔，到底还是被扒去了衣裳，结结实实地捆在了一棵树干上；行刑就此开始。
那猎犬长落鞭极重，蒂博原本发了狠咬紧牙关一声不吭，还咬破了嘴唇好撑住自己这股子狠劲，可打到第三下，他便再也忍不住，张嘴叫出声来。

===Original===
The Baron, as we have already seen, was about the roughest man of his class for a good thirty miles round, but he was not hard-hearted, and it was a distress to him to listen to the cries of the culprit as they became more and more frequent. As, however, the poachers on His Highness’s estate had of late grown bolder and more troublesome, he decided that he had better let the sentence be carried out to the full, but he turned his horse with the intention of riding away, determined no longer to remain as a spectator.
As he was on the point of doing this, a young girl suddenly emerged from the underwood, threw herself on her knees beside the horse, and lifting her large, beautiful eyes, all wet with tears, to the Baron, cried:
“In the name of the God of mercy, my Lord, have pity on that man!”

===Chinese===
前文已说过，男爵在方圆足足三十英里之内，算得上是他那一类人里头最粗暴的一个，可他倒并非铁石心肠，听着犯人一声接一声越来越密的哀号，心里也老大不忍。然而殿下这片领地上的偷猎者近来越发胆大妄为、难以管束，他便决意还是让这一场责罚照数行到底，只是拨转马头打算离去，拿定了主意不再留下旁观。
正当他欲行未行之际，一个少女忽然从灌木丛里钻了出来，扑通一声跪倒在马旁，把那一双含满泪水的美丽大眼睛抬起来望着男爵，叫道：
“看在慈悲上帝的份上，老爷，求您开恩可怜可怜那个人吧！”

===Original===
The Lord of Vez looked down at the young girl. She was indeed a lovely child; hardly sixteen years of age, of a slender and exquisite figure, with a pink and white complexion, large blue eyes, soft and tender in expression, and a crown of fair hair, which fell in luxuriant waves over neck and shoulders, escaping from underneath the shabby little grey linen cap, which endeavoured in vain to imprison them.
All this the Baron took in with a glance, in spite of the humble clothing of the beautiful suppliant, and as he had no dislike to a pretty face, he smiled down on the charming young peasant girl, in response to the pleading of her eloquent eyes.

===Chinese===
韦兹领主（the Lord of Vez）低头打量这个少女。这果真是个可爱的孩子，年纪不过十六，身段纤秀玲珑，面容白里透红，一双蓝色的大眼睛神情温柔脉脉，一头金色的秀发如波似浪地披泻在颈上肩头，从那顶灰布小帽下纷纷挣将出来，那帽子怎么也拢它们不住。
男爵只一眼便把这些都看在眼里，全不在意这位美丽的哀求者身上穿着何等寒素，他本不讨厌一张俏脸蛋，便含笑俯看这个动人的乡村小女子，回应她那一双会说话的眼睛的恳求。

===Original===
But, as he looked without speaking, and all the while the blows were still falling, she cried again, with a voice and gesture of even more earnest supplication.
“Have pity, in the name of Heaven, my Lord! Tell your servants to let the poor man go, his cries pierce my heart.”
“Ten thousand fiends!” cried the Grand Master; “you take a great interest in that rascal over there, my pretty child. Is he your brother?”
“No, my Lord.”
“Your cousin?”
“No, my Lord.”
“Your lover?”
“My lover! My Lord is laughing at me.”
“Why not? If it were so, my sweet girl, I must confess I should envy him his lot.”

===Chinese===
可他只管看着不说话，那一鞭一鞭却还在不停落下，少女便又喊了起来，声音与神气越发恳切。
“求您开恩吧，看在上天的份上，老爷！叫您的仆人们放了这个苦命人吧，他这叫声句句扎在我心上。”
“一万个小鬼！”那位猎狼大将（the Grand Master）叫道，“我那漂亮的小丫头，你倒对那边那个无赖上心得很哪。他是你哥哥？”
“不是，老爷。”
“是你表兄？”
“不是，老爷。”
“是你的情郎？”
“我的情郎！老爷这是拿我开心呢。”
“怎么不是呢？要真是这样，我的好姑娘，我得承认我倒要妒忌他的好福气了。”

===Original===
The girl lowered her eyes.
“I do not know him, my Lord, and have never seen him before today.”
“Without counting that now she only sees him wrong side before,” Engoulevent ventured to put in, thinking that it was a suitable moment for a little pleasantry.
“Silence, sirrah!” said the Baron sternly. Then, once more turning to the girl with a smile.
“Really!” he said. “Well, if he is neither a relation nor a lover, I should like to see how far your love for your neighbour will let you go. Come, a bargain, pretty girl!”
“How, my Lord?”
“Grace for that scoundrel in return for a kiss.”

===Chinese===
姑娘垂下了眼睛。
“我不认得他，老爷，直到今日以前从未见过他。”
“还不算她这会儿瞧见的只是他的反面儿呢，”昂古勒旺斗胆插了一句，心想这会儿正是个凑趣的好当口。
“住嘴，你这厮！”男爵厉声道。随即又转过脸来，含笑对那姑娘。
“当真！”他说，“也罢，他既不是你的亲戚又不是你的情郎，我倒要瞧瞧，你这份爱邻如己的心肠能把你带到多远。来，咱们做笔买卖，漂亮姑娘！”
“什么买卖，老爷？”
“饶了那个恶棍，换你一个香吻。”

===Original===
“Oh! with all my heart!” cried the young girl. “Save the life of a man with a kiss! I am sure that our good Curé himself would say there was no sin in that.”
And without waiting for the Baron to stoop and take himself what he had asked for, she threw off her wooden-shoe, placed her dainty little foot on the tip of the wolf-hunter’s boot, and taking hold of the horse’s mane, lifted herself up with a spring to the level of the face of the hardy huntsman, and there of her own accord offered him her round cheek, fresh, and velvety as the down of an August peach.

===Chinese===
“噢！我满心愿意！”少女叫道，“用一个吻救一条人命！我敢保就连我们那位好神父（the good Curé）亲来，也要说这桩事不算犯罪的。”
她也不等男爵俯身来自取他所讨的那个香吻，便蹬掉一只木鞋，把那一只俏生生的小脚尖往这位猎狼人的靴头上一踩，一手攀住马鬃，纵身一跃便弹到了这位结实猎人的脸旁，就在那儿，自动自发地把自己那张圆圆的脸蛋儿送了过去——那脸蛋儿鲜嫩极了，毛茸茸的，活像八月里水蜜桃上的一层绒毛。

===Original===
The Lord of Vez had bargained for one kiss, but he took two; then, true to his sworn word, he made a sign to Marcotte to stay the execution.
Marcotte was religiously counting his strokes; the twelfth was about to descend when he received the order to stop, and he did not think it expedient to stay it from falling. It is possible that he also thought it would be as well to give it the weight of two ordinary blows, so as to make up good measure and give a thirteenth in; however that may be, it is certain that it furrowed Thibault’s shoulders more cruelly than those that went before. It must be added, however, that he was unbound immediately after.
Meanwhile the Baron was conversing with the young girl.

===Chinese===
韦兹领主讲定的是一个吻，他却讨了两个；随后，既已发了誓，便不失信，向马尔科特一摆手，叫他停了刑。
马尔科特正一板一眼地数着他的鞭数；第十二鞭正要落下时他接到停刑的命令，却也觉得这一鞭还是落下为妥。说不定他还想着这一鞭不妨抵得上平常两鞭的分量，好凑个整数再饶进第十三鞭去；无论真相如何，可以肯定的是，这一鞭在蒂博肩头犁出的血痕，比先前任何一鞭都更狠毒。不过还得补上一句：他随即就被解了绑。
与此同时，男爵正与那少女攀谈。

===Original===
“What is your name, my pretty one?”
“Georgine Agnelette, my Lord, my mother’s name! but the country people are content to call me simply Agnelette.”
“Ah, that’s an unlucky name, my child,” said the Baron.
“In what way my Lord?” asked the girl.
“Because it makes you a prey for the wolf, my beauty. And from what part of the country do you come, Agnelette?”
“From Préciamont, my Lord.”
“And you come alone like this into the forest, my child? that’s brave for a lambkin.”
“I am obliged to do it, my Lord, for my mother and I have three goats to feed.”

===Chinese===
“你叫什么名字，我的小美人儿？”
“乔治娜·阿涅莱特（Georgine Agnelette），老爷，跟了我母亲的名字！不过乡下人只管我叫阿涅莱特（Agnelette）罢了。”
“啊呀，这名字可不吉利啊，我的孩子，”男爵道。
“怎么不吉利呢，老爷？”姑娘问。
“因为这么一来你可就成了狼嘴里的食儿了，我的美人儿。你是打哪一处乡下来的呢，阿涅莱特？”
“打普雷西亚蒙（Préciamont）来，老爷。”
“你就这么一个人进林子来，我的孩子？这对一只小羊羔来说，胆子可真不小哇。”
“我没法子呀，老爷，我跟我母亲还得喂三只山羊呢。”

===Original===
“So you come here to get grass for them?”
“Yes, my Lord.”
“And you are not afraid, young and pretty as you are?”
“Sometimes, my Lord, I cannot help trembling.”
“And why do you tremble?”
“Well, my Lord, I hear so many tales, during the winter evenings, about werewolves, that when I find myself all alone among the trees, and can hear no sound but the west wind, and the branches creaking as it blows through them, I feel a kind of shiver run through me, and my hair seems to stand on end; but when I hear your hunting horn and the dogs crying, then I feel at once quite safe again.”

===Chinese===
“所以你到这儿来是给它们割草的？”
“是的，老爷。”
“你这般年轻貌美，就不害怕？”
“有时候，老爷，我忍不住要发抖呢。”
“那你怎么个发抖法？”
“这个嘛，老爷，冬天的晚上我听了那么多狼人（werewolves）的故事，所以每当我孤身一人在树林里头，四下里除了西风、除了风吹得树枝咯吱咯吱作响之外再听不见别的声响，我便觉得有一股子寒颤顺着脊梁骨往下窜，头发也像要一根根竖起来似的；可只要一听见您的猎号和狗叫声，我便立时安心了。”

===Original===
The Baron was pleased beyond measure with this reply of the girl’s, and stroking his beard complaisantly, he said:
“Well, we give Master Wolf a pretty rough time of it; but, there is a way, my pretty one, whereby you may spare yourself all these fears and tremblings.”
“And how, my Lord?”
“Come in future to the Castle of Vez; no werewolf, or any other kind of wolf, has ever crossed the moat there, except when slung by a cord on to a hazel-pole.”
Agnelette shook her head.
“You would not like to come? and why not?”
“Because I should find something worse there than the wolf.”

===Chinese===
男爵听了姑娘这番回答，欢喜得不知如何是好，便得意地捋着胡子说道：
“这个嘛，咱们倒叫狼大人着实吃了些苦头；不过嘛，我的小美人儿，倒有一个法子，叫你这许多担惊受怕全都省了去。”
“什么法子，老爷？”
“往后你到韦兹城堡（the Castle of Vez）来便是；从来没有什么狼人，也没有别的什么狼，能越过那儿护城河的，除非是用绳子拴着、吊在一根榛木竿子上送进去的。”
阿涅莱特摇了摇头。
“你不肯来？为什么不肯？”
“因为我在那儿会碰见比狼还要糟糕的东西。”

===Original===
On hearing this, the Baron broke into a hearty fit of laughter, and, seeing their Master laugh, all the huntsmen followed suit and joined in the chorus. The fact was, that the sight of Agnelette had entirely restored the good humour of the Lord of Vez, and he would, no doubt, have continued for some time laughing and talking with Agnelette, if Marcotte, who had been recalling the dogs, and coupling them, had not respectfully reminded my Lord that they had some distance to go on their way back to the Castle. The Baron made a playful gesture of menace with his finger to the girl, and rode off followed by his train.
Agnelette was left alone with Thibault. We have related what Agnelette had done for Thibault’s sake, and also said that she was pretty.

===Chinese===
一听这话，男爵便纵声大笑起来，仆从们见主子发笑，也个个跟着哄笑附和。原来，瞧见阿涅莱特这一面，韦兹领主那一肚子的火气已消散得干干净净，倘若不是马尔科特——他方才把猎犬们重新唤拢、两两拴成一对——恭恭敬敬地提醒老爷回城堡还有好一段路要走，他必定还会和阿涅莱特说说笑笑好一阵子。男爵朝姑娘开玩笑地伸出一根手指做了个吓唬的手势，便策马而去，身后跟着他那一众人马。
阿涅莱特便和蒂博单独留在了一处。我们方才已叙述过阿涅莱特为蒂博做了什么，也说过她生得标致。

===Original===
Nevertheless, for all that, Thibault’s first thoughts on finding himself alone with the girl, were not for the one who had saved his life, but were given up to hatred and the contemplation of vengeance.
Thibault, as you see, had, since the morning, been making rapid strides along the path of evil.
“Ah! if the devil will but hear my prayer this time,” he cried, as he shook his fist, cursing the while, after the retiring huntsmen, who were just out of view, “if the devil will but hear me, you shall be paid back with usury for all you have made me suffer this day, that I swear.”

===Chinese===
虽说如此，蒂博一旦发觉自己与这姑娘单独相对，心头浮起的头一个念头，竟不是落在救了他性命的那个人身上，反倒满心都被仇恨填满，盘算着如何报仇雪恨。
诸位且看，蒂博从清晨到此刻，在这条作恶的邪路上，已是迈开了大步，飞也似地往前赶了。
“啊！这一回魔鬼若是肯听我的祷告，”他挥着拳头冲那行将隐没的猎人们背影咒骂着叫道，“魔鬼若是肯听我的话，你们今日叫我受的这许多苦，我定要叫你们连本带利地偿还回来，我发这个誓。”

===Original===
“Oh, how wicked it is of you to behave like that!” said Agnelette, going up to him. “The Baron is a kind Lord, very good to the poor, and always gently behaved with women.”
“Quite so, and you shall see with what gratitude I will repay him for the blows he has given me.”
“Come now, frankly, friend, confess that you deserved those blows,” said the girl, laughing.
“So, so!” answered Thibault, “the Baron’s kiss has turned your head, has it, my pretty Agnelette?”
“You, I should have thought, would have been the last person to reproach me with that kiss, Monsieur Thibault. But what I have said, I say again; my Lord Baron was within his rights.”

===Chinese===
“哎呀，你这般行事可真是作孽！”阿涅莱特说着走到他跟前，“男爵是个宽厚的老爷，待穷人极好，对女人向来都是和和气气的。”
“不错不错，我自会叫他瞧瞧，我是怎样一番感激涕零地报答他赏我的那些鞭子的。”
“得了得了，老老实实说一句吧，朋友，你自己承认这些鞭子是你该受的，”姑娘笑着说。
“嚯，嚯！”蒂博答道，“男爵那一个吻可把你的魂儿都勾走了，是不是啊，我漂亮的阿涅莱特？”
“蒂博先生（Monsieur Thibault），我原以为最不该拿那个吻来数落我的，便是您呢。可我说过的话，我再说一遍：男爵老爷做的没错，是在理的。”

===Original===
“What, in belabouring me with blows!”
“Well, why do you go hunting on the estates of these great lords?”
“Does not the game belong to everybody, to the peasant just as much as to the great lords?”
“No, certainly not; the game is in their woods, it is fed on their grass, and you have no right to throw your boar-spear at a buck which belongs to my lord the Duke of Orleans.”
“And who told you that I threw a boar-spear at his buck?” replied Thibault, advancing towards Agnelette in an almost threatening manner.
“Who told me? why, my own eyes, which, let me tell you, do not lie. Yes, I saw you throw your boar-spear, when you were hidden there, behind the beech-tree.”

===Chinese===
“没错？拿鞭子狠狠抽我便是在理？”
“那么，你为何要到这些大老爷的领地上去打猎呢？”
“猎物难道不是人人有份的吗？种地的庄稼汉和大老爷们不是一样有份吗？”
“不对，当然不对；猎物长在他们家的树林里，吃的是他们家的草，你根本没资格拿你的野猪矛去掷奥尔良公爵大人（my lord the Duke of Orleans）的雄鹿。”
“谁告诉你我拿野猪矛掷他的鹿了？”蒂博答道，一边朝阿涅莱特逼了过来，那架势几乎是在威胁。
“谁告诉我？自然是我自己的眼睛，我得跟您说一声，我这双眼睛可不说谎。没错，我就瞧见您掷出了那杆野猪矛，那会儿您正藏在山毛榉树背后呢。”

===Original===
Thibault’s anger subsided at once before the straightforward attitude of the girl, whose truthfulness was in such contrast to his falsehood.
“Well, after all,” he said, “supposing a poor devil does once in a way help himself to a good dinner from the superabundance of some great lord! Are you of the same mind, Mademoiselle Agnelette, as the judges who say that a man ought to be hanged just for a wretched rabbit? Come now, do you think God created that buck for the Baron more than for me?”
“God, Monsieur Thibault, has told us not to covet other men’s goods; obey the law of God, and you will not find yourself any the worse off for it!”
“Ah, I see, my pretty Agnelette, you know me then, since you call me so glibly by my name?”

===Chinese===
蒂博这一腔怒火，在那姑娘坦荡荡的态度面前顿时消了下去——她的坦率诚实，正与他自己的虚谎作伪成了截然的反照。
“也罢，”他说，“就算一个穷光蛋偶尔从哪位大老爷铺天盖地的丰盛里头，给自己讨一顿好饭吃！阿涅莱特小姐（Mademoiselle Agnelette），莫非你也跟那些法官老爷们是一个心思，说一个人仅仅为了一只区区兔子便该上绞架？你倒说说看，你以为上帝造那头鹿，是单为男爵造的，不是为我造的？”
“蒂博先生，上帝早已吩咐我们不可贪图他人的财物；遵行上帝的诫命，你便不会吃什么亏的！”
“啊，我明白了，我漂亮的阿涅莱特，原来你认得我呀，瞧你叫我的名字叫得这般顺口？”

===Original===
“Certainly I do; I remember seeing you at Boursonnes, on the day of the fête; they called you the beautiful dancer, and stood round in a circle to watch you.”
Thibault, pleased with this compliment, was now quite disarmed.
“Yes, yes, of course,” he answered, “I remember now having seen you; and I think we danced together, did we not? but you were not so tall then as you are now, that’s why I did not recognise you at first, but I recall you distinctly now. And I remember too that you wore a pink frock, with a pretty little white bodice, and that we danced in the dairy. I wanted to kiss you, but you would not let me, for you said that it was only proper to kiss one’s vis-á-vis, and not one’s partner.”

===Chinese===
“自然认得；我还记得在布尔松（Boursonnes）过节那天瞧见过您；他们都叫您美舞郎，还围成一个圈子瞧您跳舞呢。”
蒂博听了这句恭维话很是受用，这会儿算是彻底卸下了火气。
“是啦，是啦，自然是的，”他答道，“我如今也记起瞧见过你了；我想咱们还一块儿跳过舞呢，是不是？不过那会儿你个头没如今这么高，所以我头一遭没把你认出来，这会儿我可清清楚楚地记起你来了。我还记得你穿一件粉红的罩衫，配一件挺好看的小白胸衣，咱们是在那间奶房里跳的舞。我那时想亲亲你，你却不依，你说只能亲对面的舞伴，不能亲自己的搭档。”

===Original===
“You have a good memory, Monsieur Thibault!”
“And do you know, Agnelette, that during these last twelve months, for it is a year since that dance, you have not only grown taller, but grown prettier too; I see you are one of those people who understand how to do two things at once.”
The girl blushed and lowered her eyes, and the blush and the shy embarrassment only made her look more charming still.
Thibault’s eyes were now turned towards her with more marked attention than before, and, in a voice, not wholly free from a slight agitation, he asked:
“Have you a lover, Agnelette?”

===Chinese===
“您的记性可真好，蒂博先生！”
“你可知道，阿涅莱特，这十二个月以来——离那回跳舞可有一年了——你不光长高了，还出落得更俏了；我看你呀，就是那种一心能二用、两头都不耽误的人儿。”
姑娘红了脸，垂下了眼睛，这一红脸、这一羞怯的局促，反倒把她衬得越发动人了。
蒂博的目光这会儿比先前更着意地落在她身上，他用一种不无几分激动的嗓音问道：
“你可有情郎么，阿涅莱特？”

===Original===
“No, Monsieur Thibault,” she answered, “I have never had one, and do not wish to have one.”
“And why is that? Is Cupid such a bad lad that you are afraid of him?”
“No, not that, but a lover is not at all what I want.”
“And what do you want?”
“A husband.”
Thibault made a movement, which Agnelette either did not, or pretended not to see.

===Chinese===
“没有，蒂博先生，”她答道，“我从来没有过，也不想有。”
“那是为何？莫非丘比特（Cupid）这小子这般讨人嫌，叫你怕了他？”
“不是这个，只是情郎这东西，我所要的压根儿不是这个。”
“那你要的是什么？”
“一个丈夫。”
蒂博动了一动，这动作阿涅莱特要么没瞧见，要么是装作没瞧见。

===Original===
“Yes,” she repeated, “a husband. Grandmother is old and infirm, and a lover would distract my attention too much from the care which I now give her; whereas, a husband, if I found a nice fellow who would like to marry me﻿—a husband would help me to look after her in her old age, and would share with me the task which God has laid upon me, of making her happy and comfortable in her last years.”
“But do you think your husband,” said Thibault, “would be willing that you should love your grandmother more than you loved him? and do you not think he might be jealous at seeing you lavish so much tenderness upon her?”

===Chinese===
“没错，”她重复道，“一个丈夫。外祖母年迈体弱，要是有个情郎，便会叫我分心，把如今侍奉她的那些心力分去太多；可一个丈夫呢，倘若我能寻着一个好小伙子愿意娶我——一个丈夫便能帮我一道照料她的晚年，与我分担上帝加在我肩头的那桩差事，好叫她老人家在风烛残年里过得舒坦、过得安乐。”
“可是你想，”蒂博说道，“你的丈夫会乐意你爱外祖母胜过爱他么？你就不怕他瞧着你把那般多的柔情都倾在她身上，心里要犯醋意？”

===Original===
“Oh,” replied Agnelette, with an adorable smile, “there is no fear of that, for I will manage so as to let him have such a large share of my love and attention that he will have no cause to complain; the kinder and the more patient he is for the dear old thing, the more I shall devote myself to him, the harder shall I work that there may be nothing wanting to our little household. You see me looking small and delicate, and you doubt that I should have strength for this; but I have plenty of spirit and energy for work, and then, when the heart gives consent, one can work day and night without fatigue. Oh! how I should love the man who loved my grandmother! I promise you, that she, and my husband, and I, we should be three happy folks together.”

===Chinese===
“噢，”阿涅莱特带着一个可爱极了的微笑答道，“这倒不必担忧，因为我会安排得当，叫他分得的那份情意与照拂足够大，大到他没有半点怨言的由头；他待我那可怜的老人家越是和善、越是耐烦，我便越发一心一意地待他，越发拼命操持，好叫咱们那个小家里什么都不缺。您瞧我个头小小的、身子单薄，便疑心我没这份力气；可我做工的心气和精神头足得很呢，再者说，只要心里头愿意，一个人便是日以继夜地干活也不知疲倦的。噢！谁要是爱我的外祖母，我便会怎样地爱他呀！我敢跟您打保票，她老人家，还有我丈夫，还有我，我们三个凑在一处，定是三个有福气的人。”

===Original===
“You mean that you would be three very poor folks together, Agnelette!”
“And do you think the loves and friendships of the rich are worth a farthing more than those of the poor? At times, when I have been loving and caressing my grandmother, Monsieur Thibault, and she takes me on her lap and clasps me in her poor weak trembling arms, and puts her dear old wrinkled face against mine, and I feel my cheek wet with the loving tears she sheds, I begin to cry myself, and, I tell you, Monsieur Thibault, so soft and sweet are my tears, that there is no woman or girl, be she queen or princess, who has ever, I am sure, even in her happiest days, known such a real joy as mine. And, yet, there is no one in all the country round who is so destitute as we two are.”

===Chinese===
“你是说你们三个凑在一处，定是三个穷光蛋吧，阿涅莱特！”
“难道您以为，富人们的情爱与友谊，便比穷人们的多值半个子儿吗？有些时候，蒂博先生，我正爱着外祖母、依偎着她，她便把我搂到膝上，用她那双可怜的、虚弱的、颤抖着的胳膊把我紧紧一抱，把她那张亲爱的、满是皱纹的老脸贴在我的脸上，我觉得自己的脸蛋儿被她洒下的慈爱泪水打湿了，我便也哭了起来——我跟您说，蒂博先生，我流的那泪又软又甜，天底下无论哪个女人或姑娘，便是王后也好公主也罢，我敢说，便是在她最快活的日子里，也断未曾尝过我这般实实在在的欢喜。然而，方圆几百里之内，再没有谁比我们祖孙俩更一贫如洗的了。”

===Original===
Thibault listened to what Agnelette was saying without answering; his mind was occupied with many thoughts, such thoughts as are indulged in by the ambitious; but his dreams of ambition were disturbed at moments by a passing sensation of depression and disillusionment.
He, the man who had spent hours at a time watching the beautiful and aristocratic dames belonging to the Court of the Duke of Orleans, as they swept up and down the wide entrance stairs; who had often passed whole nights gazing at the arched windows of the Keep at Vez, when the whole place was lit up for some festivity, he, that same man, now asked himself, if what he had so ambitiously desired to have, a lady of rank and a rich dwelling, would, after all, be so much worth possessing as a thatched roof and this sweet and gentle girl called Agnelette. And it was certain that if this dear and charming little woman were to become his, that he would be envied in turn by all the earls and barons in the countryside.

===Chinese===
蒂博听阿涅莱特说着这一番话，并不接茬；他心头盘旋着许许多多的念头，都是那等胸怀大志之人所耽溺的念头；只是他那些野心的迷梦，时不时会掠过一阵消沉与幻灭之感，把梦境扰得断断续续。
他这个人，曾经一连好几个钟头地瞧着奥尔良公爵宫廷里那些美丽而高贵的主妇们，在一道宽阔的进门楼梯上款款地上上下下；又曾一次次通宵达旦地凝望着韦兹那座城堡主塔（the Keep at Vez）的拱窗——每逢节庆，整座城堡灯火辉煌——便是这样一个人，如今却扪心自问：他当初那般野心勃勃地渴求到手的，一位贵妇、一处华厦，到底是不是当真比一间茅草屋顶的小屋、比这个名叫阿涅莱特的温柔甜蜜的姑娘更值得拥有。况且可以断言，倘若这个亲爱而迷人的小女子当真成了他的，那么乡间所有的伯爵和男爵，反倒要回过头来妒忌他了。

===Original===
“Well, Agnelette,” said Thibault, “and suppose a man like myself were to offer himself as your husband, would you accept him?”
It has been already stated that Thibault was a handsome young fellow, with fine eyes and black hair, and that his travels had left him something better than a mere workman. And it must further be borne in mind that we readily become attached to those on whom we have conferred a benefit, and Agnelette had, in all probability, saved Thibault’s life; for, under such strokes as Marcotte’s, the victim would certainly have been dead before the thirty-sixth had been given.

===Chinese===
“那么，阿涅莱特，”蒂博说道，“倘若有一个像我这样的人，自荐要做你的丈夫，你可肯应承他？”
前文已经交代过，蒂博是个英俊的小伙子，生着一双好看的眼睛和一头黑发，他那番游历也使他超越了区区一个工匠的格局。此外还须记着一点：我们往往容易对那些受过我们恩惠的人心生情意，而阿涅莱特极有可能救过蒂博一命；因为挨马尔科特那般重的鞭子，受刑之人断然挨不到第三十六下便已一命呜呼了。

===Original===
“Yes,” she said, “if it would be a good thing for my grandmother?”
Thibault took hold of her hand.
“Well then, Agnelette,” he said, “we will speak again about this, dear child, and that as soon as may be.”
“Whenever you like, Monsieur Thibault.”
“And you will promise faithfully to love me if I marry you, Agnelette?”
“Do you think I should love any man besides my husband?”
“Never mind, I want you just to take a little oath, something of this kind, for instance; Monsieur Thibault, I swear that I will never love anyone but you.”

===Chinese===
“肯的，”她说，“只要这件事对外祖母有好处。”
蒂博握住了她的手。
“那么，阿涅莱特，”他说，“咱们这件事回头再细谈，好孩子，越快越好。”
“您什么时候愿意都成，蒂博先生。”
“那么你可得跟我立个誓，要是我娶了你，你必真心真意地爱我，阿涅莱特，成不成？”
“您以为我除了自己的丈夫之外，还会爱别的什么男人吗？”
“那不管，我要你就这么立上一个小小的誓，比方说这么一句：蒂博先生，我发誓这辈子只爱你一个，再不爱旁人。”

===Original===
“What need is there to swear? the promise of an honest girl should be sufficient for an honest man.”
“And when shall we have the wedding, Agnelette?” and in saying this, Thibault tried to put his arm round her waist.
But Agnelette gently disengaged herself.
“Come and see my grandmother,” she said, “it is for her to decide about it; you must content yourself this evening with helping me up with my load of heath, for it is getting late, and it is nearly three miles from here to Préciamont.”

===Chinese===
“何必要立誓呢？一个正经姑娘的一句允诺，对一个正经汉子来说，便该是足够了的。”
“那咱们什么时候办喜事呢，阿涅莱特？”蒂博一边说着，一边便想伸手去揽她的腰。
可阿涅莱特轻轻地挣开了身子。
“去看望我外祖母吧，”她说，“这件事由她老人家做主定夺；今儿晚上您就先帮我顶这一捆石楠花上路，便算尽了心了，时候不早啦，从这儿到普雷西亚蒙可还有足足三英里地呢。”

===Original===
So Thibault helped her as desired, and then accompanied her on her way home as far as the Forest-fence of Billemont, that is until they came in sight of the village steeple. Before parting, he so begged of pretty Agnelette to give him one kiss as an earnest of his future happiness, that at last she consented, and then, far more agitated by this one kiss than she had been by the Baron’s double embrace, Agnelette hastened on her way, in spite of the load which she was carrying on her head, and which seemed far too heavy for so slender and delicate a creature.

===Chinese===
于是蒂博便依言帮了她的忙，又陪她一路走回家去，直走到比勒蒙（Billemont）的森林栅栏那儿，也就是一直走到望得见村里教堂尖顶的地方。临别之际，他再三央求漂亮的阿涅莱特给他一个吻，好当作他日后幸福的订金，到底把她求得应允了。于是，比男爵那双倍的拥抱还要令她心慌得多的这么一个吻之后，阿涅莱特便匆匆上了路，全不顾头顶上还顶着她那一捆、对于她这般纤瘦单薄的人儿来说显然太过沉重的负荷。

===Original===
Thibault stood for some time looking after her as she walked away across the moor. All the flexibility and grace of her youthful figure were brought into relief as the girl lifted her pretty rounded arms to support the burden upon her head, and thus silhouetted against the dark blue of the sky she made a delightful picture. At last, having reached the outskirts of the village, the land dipping at that point, she suddenly disappeared, passing out of sight of Thibault’s admiring eyes. He gave a sigh, and stood still, plunged in thought; but it was not the satisfaction of thinking that this sweet and good young creature might one day be his that had caused his sigh. Quite the contrary; he had wished for Agnelette, because Agnelette was young and pretty, and because it was part of his unfortunate disposition to long for everything that belonged or might belong to another. His desire to possess Agnelette had been quickened by the innocent frankness with which she had talked to him; but it had been a matter of fancy rather than of any deeper feeling, of the mind, and not of the heart. For Thibault was incapable of loving as a man ought to love, who, being poor himself, loves a poor girl; in such a case there should be no thought, no ambition on his part beyond the wish that his love may be returned. But it was not so with Thibault; on the contrary, I repeat, the farther he walked away from Agnelette, leaving it would seem his good genius farther behind him with every step, the more urgently did his envious longings begin again as usual to torment his soul. It was dark when he reached home.

===Chinese===
蒂博驻足许久，目送她穿过那片荒原走远。少女抬起那一双圆润好看的手臂，顶住头上的重负，她那一身年轻身段的全部柔韧与婀娜便都凸显了出来，衬着深蓝深蓝的天幕，凝成了一幅赏心悦目的剪影。末了，她走到了村子的边缘，那一段地势往下低将下去，她便忽地一闪不见了，从蒂博那双赏玩的眼睛里消失了踪影。他叹了一口气，怔怔地立在当地，陷入了沉思；然而这一声叹息，并非因为想到这个甜美纯善的小人儿有朝一日可能归他所有而心满意足。恰恰相反；他之所以想要阿涅莱特，是因为阿涅莱特年轻貌美，更因为他这不幸的性情里，天生有一股子贪图一切属于他人、或可能属于他人之物的毛病。他那占有阿涅莱特的欲念，被少女对他倾诉时那一份天真的坦率撩拨得更旺了；可说到底，那不过是一时兴起，算不得什么更深的情意，是起于头脑，而非发自心底的。因为蒂博这个人，压根儿不懂得一个男人本当如何去爱——一个男人，自己既贫苦，又爱上一个贫苦的姑娘，在这种情形之下，他便不该再有什么别的念头、什么别的野心，只除了那唯一的一桩心愿：但愿自己的这一片情意能得着回报。可蒂博偏偏不是这样的人；恰恰相反，我再说一遍，他离阿涅莱特走得越远，仿佛每走一步便把自己的守护神（good genius）远远抛在身后一步，他那好妒的渴念便照旧开始变本加厉地啮噬起他的灵魂来。等他走到家门时，天已经黑了。

## XI David and Goliath / XI 大卫与歌利亚

===Original===
After walking the whole length of the village, they stopped before an imposing looking house at the junction of the roads leading to Longpré and Haramont. As they neared the house—the little host, with all the gallantry of a *preux chevalier* went on ahead, mounted the flight of five or six steps with an agility which one could not have expected, and, by dint of standing on tiptoe, managed to reach the bell with the tips of his fingers. It should be added, that having once got hold of it, he gave it a pull which unmistakeably announced the return of the master. It was, in short, no ordinary return, but a triumphal one, for the Bailiff was bringing home a guest.

===Chinese===
他们走遍了整座村子，在通往龙普雷（Longpré）与阿拉蒙（Haramont）两条路的交叉口前，一座气派非凡的宅子跟前停了下来。将近门口时——这位身材矮小的主人，拿出一副 *preux chevalier*（侠义骑士）的全副殷勤，抢在前头，登上有五六级之多的台阶，那股子身手之矫捷，实出人意料，又踮起脚尖，好歹用指尖够到了门铃。还得补上一句：他既把那铃绳抓到手里，便使劲拽了一下，那动静分明是在向人宣告——老爷回家了。简而言之，这一趟回家并非寻常回家，而是一趟凯旋，因为执达吏（the Bailiff）这回带了一位客人回来。

===Original===
A maid, neatly dressed in her best clothes, opened the door. The Bailiff gave her an order in a low voice, and Thibault, whose adoration of beautiful women did not prevent him from liking a good dinner, gathered that these few whispered words referred to the menu which Perrine was to prepare. Then turning round, his host addressed Thibault:

“Welcome, my dear guest, to the house of Bailiff Népomucène Magloire.”

===Chinese===
一名衣裳齐整、穿得极体面的女佣开了门。执达吏压低嗓子吩咐了她几句，蒂博虽素来崇拜美人，却也不耽误他喜爱一顿好饭，他据此揣度，这几句耳语关乎的，正是佩琳（Perrine）待办的菜谱。随后主人转过身来，对蒂博说道：

“欢迎，亲爱的客人，欢迎来到执达吏内波米塞纳·马格卢瓦（Népomucène Magloire）的府上。”

===Original===
Thibault politely allowed Madame to pass in before him, and was then introduced into the drawing-room.

But the shoemaker now made a slip. Unaccustomed as yet to luxury, the man of the forest was not adroit enough to hide the admiration which he felt on beholding the bailiff’s home. For the first time in his life he found himself in the midst of damask curtains and gilt armchairs; he had not imagined that anyone save the King, or at least his Highness the Duke of Orleans, had curtains and armchairs of this magnificence. He was unconscious that all the while Madame Magloire was closely watching him, and that his simple astonishment and delight did not escape her detective eye. However, she appeared now, after mature reflection, to look with greater favour on the guest whom her husband had imposed upon her, and endeavoured to soften for him the glances of her dark eyes. But her affability did not go so far as to lead her to comply with the request of Monsieur Magloire, who begged her to add to the flavour and bouquet of the champagne by pouring it out herself for her guest. Notwithstanding the entreaties of her august husband, the Bailiff’s wife refused, and under the pretext of fatigue from her walk, she retired to her own room. Before leaving the room, however, she expressed a hope to Thibault, that, as she owed him some expiation, he would not forget the way to Erneville, ending her speech with a smile which displayed a row of charming teeth. Thibault responded with so much lively pleasure in his voice that it rendered any roughness of speech less noticeable, swearing that he would sooner lose the power to eat and drink than the remembrance of a lady who was as courteous as she was beautiful.

===Chinese===
蒂博彬彬有礼地让太太先他一步进门，随后被引进了客厅。

可这位鞋匠这会儿却露了怯。他不曾见识过这般奢华，这位林中人到底不够圆滑，没能藏住目睹执达吏府邸时心头涌起的赞叹。他这辈子头一遭置身于锦缎帷幔与镀金扶手椅之间；他原以为，除了国王，再不济也得是奥尔良公爵殿下（his Highness the Duke of Orleans），谁也不配有这般华丽的帷幔和座椅。他却浑然不觉，马格卢瓦太太（Madame Magloire）一双眼睛正一刻不离地打量着他，他这份质朴的惊诧与欢喜，没能逃过她那如探子般锐利的目光。不过，她似乎在深思熟虑之后，反倒对这位丈夫硬塞给她的客人多瞧上了几分，于是努力用她那双黑眼睛的柔光来对他缓和颜色。可她的和颜悦色，倒还没到肯依从马格卢瓦先生所请的地步——后者央求她亲手为客人斟香槟，好为那酒再添几分滋味与芬芳。任凭她那位威严的丈夫百般苦求，执达吏太太就是不肯，只推说一路走乏了，便回自己房里去了。临出客厅之前，她却对蒂博表示了一番指望：既然她欠他一番赔情，但愿他莫要忘了通往埃纳维尔（Erneville）的路，话音以一抹微笑作结，露出一排楚楚动人的皓齿。蒂博回话时嗓音里那股子热切劲儿，倒把他言语中粗糙的腔调冲淡了几分，他信誓旦旦地说，宁可失了吃喝的本事，也断不肯忘却一位既美貌又多礼的夫人。

===Original===
Madame Magloire gave him a curtsey which would have made her known as the Bailiff’s wife a mile off, and left the room.

She had hardly closed the door behind her, when Monsieur Magloire went through a pirouette in her honour, which though less light, was not less significant than the caper a schoolboy executes when once he has got rid of his master.

“Ah! my dear friend,” he said, “now that we are no longer hampered by a woman’s presence, we will have a good go at the wine! Those women, they are delightful at mass or at a ball; but at table, heaven defend me, there is nobody like the men! What do you say, old fellow?”

===Chinese===
马格卢瓦太太朝他行了一个屈膝礼——单是这一礼，便足教人在一里开外认出她是执达吏的太太，行罢便离了客厅。

她前脚才把门带上，马格卢瓦先生后脚便为她献上了一个旋转礼，那身段虽说不如学童摆脱了先生之后所蹦的那一跳来得轻巧，意味却一点儿不少。

“啊！亲爱的朋友，”他说，“这会儿再没有女人在跟前碍手碍脚，咱们可得好好跟这酒较较劲了！那些个女人，做弥撒也罢、跳舞也罢，倒也赏心悦目；可一上了餐桌，老天保佑，再没人比得上咱们男人！你说是不是，老兄？”

===Original===
Perrine now came in to receive her master’s orders as to what wine she was to bring up. But the gay little man was far too fastidious a judge of wines to trust a woman with such a commission as this. Indeed, women never show that reverential respect for certain old bottles which is their due, nor that delicacy of touch with which they love to be handled. He drew Perrine down as if to whisper something in her ear; instead of which he gave a good sound kiss to the cheek which was still young and fresh, and which did not blush sufficiently to lead to the belief that the kiss was a novelty to it.

“Well, sir,” said the girl laughing, “What is it?”

“This is it, Perrinette, my love,” said the Bailiff, “that I alone know the good brands, and as they are many in number, you might get lost among them, and so I am going to the cellar myself.” And the good man disappeared trundling off on his little legs, cheerful, alert and fantastic as those Nuremberg toys mounted on a stand, which you wind up with a key, and which, once set going, turn round and round, or go first one way and then the other, till the spring has run down; the only difference being, that this dear little man seemed wound up by the hand of God himself, and gave no sign of ever coming to a standstill.

===Chinese===
这时佩琳进来，听候主人吩咐该取什么酒上来。可这位快活的小个子是位挑剔得过分的品酒行家，断不肯把这样一桩差使放心交给一个女人。说实在的，女人家对那些上了年头的陈瓶，从来不曾表现出它们所应得的那份敬意，也不曾拿那爱酒之人所乐于承受的轻柔手势去侍弄它们。他把佩琳拉到跟前，像是有什么话要附在她耳边低语；谁知却在姑娘那张还年轻水灵的脸蛋上结结实实亲了一口——那脸蛋红都没怎么红，倒让人猜这亲嘴对她而言算不上什么新鲜事。

“哎，老爷，”姑娘笑着说道，“怎么啦？”

“是这么回事，我的小佩琳内特（Perrinette），我的乖乖，”执达吏说，“只有我一个人摸得清那些个佳酿的底细，偏偏牌子又多，你一进去保管挑花了眼，所以这回我亲自下地窖去。”说罢这位好人便没了影儿，迈着他那两条小短腿骨碌碌地奔了出去，那份快活、机灵、新奇劲儿，活像那种装在座子上、拿钥匙拧紧发条的纽伦堡（Nuremberg）玩具，一旦上足了劲，便滴溜溜地转个不停，一会儿朝这边、一会儿又朝那边，直到发条跑完为止；唯一的分别只在于：这位可爱的小个子，仿佛是由上帝亲手上足了发条，丝毫没有要停下来的意思。

===Original===
Thibault was left alone. He rubbed his hands together, congratulating himself on having chanced upon such a well-to-do house, with such a beautiful wife, and such an amiable husband for host and hostess. Five minutes later the door again opened, and in came the bailiff, with a bottle in either hand, and one under each arm. The two under his arms were bottles of sparkling Sillery, of the first quality, which, not being injured by shaking, were safe to be carried in a horizontal position. The two which he carried in his hands, and which he held with a respectful care which was a pleasure to behold, were, one a bottle of very old Chambertin, the other a bottle of Hermitage.

===Chinese===
蒂博被独自留在客厅里。他搓着双手，暗自庆幸竟撞进了这么一户殷实人家，有这么美貌的一位太太、这么和蔼的一位老爷做男女东家。五分钟过后，门又开了，执达吏走了进来，一手一瓶，腋下又各夹一瓶。腋下夹着的两瓶，是头等的起泡西勒里（Sillery），这酒不怕晃，横着夹走也万无一失。他双手各捧一瓶，那一份敬之重之的小心劲儿，瞧着就叫人舒坦——一瓶是极陈年的香贝丹（Chambertin），另一瓶是埃尔米塔日（Hermitage）。

===Original===
The supper hour had now come; for it must be remembered, that at the period of which we are writing, dinner was at midday, and supper at six. Moreover, it had already been dark for some time before six o’clock, in this month of January, and whether it be six, or twelve o’clock at night, if one has to eat one’s meal by candle or lamplight, it always seems to one like supper.

The Bailiff put the bottles tenderly down on the table and rang the bell. Perrinette came in.

“When will the table be ready for us, my pretty?” asked Magloire.

“Whenever Monsieur pleases,” replied Perrine. “I know Monsieur does not like waiting; so I always have everything ready in good time.”

“Go and ask Madame, then, if she is not coming; tell her, Perrine, that we do not wish to sit down without her.”

Perrine left the room.

===Chinese===
用晚饭的时辰已到；须知在我们所述的那个年代，午饭摆在正午，晚饭则开在六点。况且在正月里，六点未到天色早已黑了一阵子了，而无论是傍晚六点还是夜里十二点，但凡要就着烛光或油灯吃饭，便总归像是在吃晚饭。

执达吏把那几瓶酒小心翼翼地放到桌上，按了铃。佩琳内特走了进来。

“桌子什么时候能给咱们备好呀，我的小美人？”马格卢瓦问道。

“老爷什么时候高兴，就什么时候好，”佩琳回道，“我知道老爷不爱等；所以我一向把什么都早早备齐了。”

“那你去问问太太，来是不来；告诉她，佩琳，我们不坐下开席，是要等她的。”

佩琳出去了。

===Original===
“We may as well go into the dining-room to wait,” said the little host; “you must be hungry, my dear friend, and when I am hungry, I like to feed my eyes before I feed my stomach.”

“You seem to me to be a fine gourmand, you,” said Thibault.

“Epicure, epicure, not gourmand—you must not confuse the two things. I go first, but only in order to show you the way.”

And so saying, Monsieur Magloire led his guest into the dining-room.

===Chinese===
“咱们不如先到饭厅里候着，”这位矮小的主人说，“你准是饿了，亲爱的朋友，我自个儿一饿起来，就爱先拿眼睛过过瘾，再拿肚子受用。”

“依我看，您可真是个了不得的馋嘴。”蒂博说。

“是美食家，美食家，可不是馋嘴——这两样万不可混为一谈。我先头里走，不过是给你带个路罢了。”

马格卢瓦先生一面这样说，一面把客人引进了饭厅。

===Original===
“Ah!” he exclaimed gaily as he went in, patting his corporation, “tell me now, do you not think this girl of mine is a capital cook, fit to serve a Cardinal? Just look now at this little supper she has spread for us; quite a simple one, and yet it pleases me more, I am sure, than would have Belshazzar’s feast.”

“On my honour, Bailiff,” said Thibault, “you are right; it is a sight to rejoice one’s heart.” And Thibault’s eyes began to shine like carbuncles.

===Chinese===
“啊！”他一进门便兴冲冲地拍着自己那圆鼓鼓的大肚皮叫了起来，“你倒说说，我这丫头是不是一把做菜的好手，够格给一位红衣主教掌厨？你且瞧瞧她给咱们张罗的这一桌小晚饭；再简单不过了，可我敢说，它叫我看了心里舒坦，只怕比伯沙撒（Belshazzar）的那场大宴还强呢。”

“凭我的名誉起誓，执达吏先生，”蒂博说，“您说得一点不错；这景儿瞧着就叫人心里欢喜。”蒂博那双眼睛，这会儿已像红宝石似的闪闪发亮了。

===Original===
And yet it was, as the Bailiff described it, quite an unpretentious little supper, but withal so appetising to look upon, that it was quite surprising. At one end of the table was a fine carp, boiled in vinegar and herbs, with the roe served on either side of it on a layer of parsley, dotted about with cut carrots. The opposite end was occupied by a boar-ham, mellow-flavoured, and deliciously reposing on a dish of spinach, which lay like a green islet surrounded by an ocean of gravy.

===Chinese===
然而正如执达吏所言，这确是一顿毫不铺张的小晚饭，可瞧着又是那般吊人胃口，简直叫人称奇。桌子一头摆着一条上好的鲤鱼，用醋和香料煮过，鱼子分作两堆摆在两旁，底下一层欧芹，上头零零星星缀着胡萝卜丁。桌子的另一头则由一只野猪腿占着，滋味醇厚，舒舒服服地卧在一盘菠菜之上，那菠菜活像一座碧绿的小岛，被一片肉汁的汪洋环绕着。

===Original===
A delicate game-pie, made of two partridges only, of which the heads appeared above the upper crust, as if ready to attack one another with their beaks, was placed in the middle of the table; while the intervening spaces were covered with side-dishes holding slices of Arles sausage, pieces of tunny-fish, swimming in beautiful green oil from Provence, anchovies sliced and arranged in all kinds of strange and fantastic patterns on a white and yellow bed of chopped eggs, and pats of butter that could only have been churned that very day. As accessories to these were two or three sorts of cheese, chosen from among those of which the chief quality is to provoke thirst, some Reims biscuits, of delightful crispness, and pears just fit to eat, showing that the master himself had taken the trouble to preserve them, and to turn them about on the storeroom shelf.

===Chinese===
桌子正中摆着一只精致的山鹑肉馅饼，只用了两只山鹑做成，两只鸟的头从上层酥皮里探出来，一副要用喙互啄的架势；中间的空当则摆满了各色小碟：盛着阿尔勒（Arles）香肠切片的，盛着金枪鱼肉块、浸在普罗旺斯（Provence）所产的碧绿清油里的，还有凤尾鱼切片，在白黄相间的碎蛋末铺成的底子上摆出种种稀奇古怪、花样百出的图形，再加上一块块只可能是当天才打出来的黄油。配在这些菜色旁边的，是两三种奶酪——都是挑的那种最要紧的本事是叫人口渴的——还有一些松脆可口的兰斯（Reims）饼干，和几只正到了好吃时辰的梨，看得出是主人亲自动手费心保存、在储藏室的架子上一个个翻弄过来的。

===Original===
Thibault was so taken up in the contemplation of this little amateur supper, that he scarcely heard the message which Perrine brought back from her mistress, who sent word that she had a sick-headache, and begged to make her excuses to her guest, with the hope that she might have the pleasure of entertaining him when he next came.

The little man gave visible signs of rejoicing on hearing his wife’s answer, breathed loudly and clapped his hands, exclaiming:

“She has a headache! she has a headache! Come along then, sit down! sit down!” And thereupon, besides the two bottles of old Mâcon, which had already been respectively placed within reach of the host and guest, as *vin ordinaire*, between the Hors-d’œuvres and the dessert plates, he introduced the four other bottles which he had just brought up from the cellar.

===Chinese===
蒂博一心一意地端详着这桌外行却丰盛的小晚饭，竟几乎没听清佩琳从太太那边带回的话——太太传话说自己犯了头风，恳请向客人告罪，并指望下回他再来时，能有款待他的荣幸。

这小个子一听太太的回话，那一份欢喜便明明白白地摆在了脸上，他大声喘着气，拍着手，叫道：

“她犯了头风！她犯了头风！那好，快请坐！快请坐！”说罢，除了已经分别摆在主客二人手边、权作 *vin ordinaire*（家常佐餐酒）的那两瓶陈年马孔（Mâcon），搁在前菜碟与水果碟之间的位置之外，他又把方才从地窖里搬上来的另外四瓶也请上了桌。

===Original===
Madame Magloire had, I think, acted not unwisely in refusing to sup with these stalwart champions of the table, for such was their hunger and thirst, that half the carp and the two bottles of wine disappeared without a word passing between them except such exclamations as:

“Good fish! isn’t it?”

“Capital!”

“Fine wine! isn’t it?”

“Excellent!”

The carp and the Mâcon being consumed, they passed on to the game pie and the Chambertin, and now their tongues began to be unloosed, especially the Bailiff’s.

===Chinese===
我想，马格卢瓦太太拒绝与这两位餐桌上的健将共进晚餐，倒也不算失策，因为他们那一份饥渴之甚，竟叫半条鲤鱼和那两瓶酒下肚之时，彼此间没说上一句话，只有这样的赞叹：

“好鱼！是不是？”

“妙极！”

“好酒！是不是？”

“再好不过！”

鲤鱼和马孔酒既已了结，他们便转向山鹑馅饼和香贝丹，到了这会儿，他们的舌头才渐渐松活起来，尤其是执达吏的那一条。

===Original===
By the time half the game pie, and the first bottle of Chambertin were finished, Thibault knew the history of Népomucène Magloire; not a very complicated one, it must be confessed.

Monsieur Magloire was son to a church ornament manufacturer who had worked for the chapel belonging to his Highness the Duke of Orleans, the latter, in his religious zeal having a burning desire to obtain pictures by Albano and Titian for the sum of four to five thousand francs.

Chrysostom Magloire had placed his son Népomucène Magloire, as head-cook with Louis’ son, his Highness the Duke of Orleans.

===Chinese===
等到半只山鹑馅饼下了肚、第一瓶香贝丹也喝完，蒂博已对内波米塞纳·马格卢瓦的生平了如指掌了——不得不说，这段生平倒算不得多么曲折复杂。

马格卢瓦先生是一位教堂饰物制造商的儿子，他父亲曾为奥尔良公爵殿下的私人礼拜堂供过货，而殿下凭其一片虔诚，正心急火燎地想以四五千法郎的价钱，弄到阿尔巴诺（Albano）和提香（Titian）的画作。

克里索斯托姆·马格卢瓦（Chrysostom Magloire）把儿子内波米塞纳·马格卢瓦安插到了路易之子——即奥尔良公爵殿下——的身边，做了大厨。

===Original===
The young man had, from infancy almost, manifested a decided taste for cooking; he had been especially attached to the Castle at Villers-Cotterets, and for thirty years presided over his Highness’s dinners, the latter introducing him to his friends as a thorough artist, and from time to time, sending for him to come upstairs to talk over culinary matters with Marshal Richelieu.

When fifty-five years of age, Magloire found himself so rounded in bulk, that it was only with some difficulty he could get through the narrow doors of the passages and offices. Fearing to be caught some day like the weasel of the fable, he asked permission to resign his post.

The Duke consented, not without regret, but with less regret than he would have felt at any other time, for he had just married Madame de Montesson, and it was only rarely now that he visited his castle at Villers-Cotterets.

===Chinese===
这年轻人几乎是自幼便对烹饪显出了一股子不可移易的天分；他尤其被留在维莱-科特雷（Villers-Cotterets）的城堡里效命，主持殿下的筵席长达三十年之久，殿下把他引荐给友人时，称他为一位地道的艺术家，还不时把他唤到楼上，与黎塞留元帅（Marshal Richelieu）商榷烹调之道。

到了五十五岁上，马格卢瓦发觉自己身躯已圆滚到了这般地步：要挤过走廊与办事处的那些窄门，竟颇为吃力。他生怕有朝一日像寓言里的黄鼠狼那样卡住脱身不得，便求请辞了差事。

公爵应允了，不无惋惜，可这份惋惜比起从前任何时节都要轻些——因为他刚娶了蒙特森夫人（Madame de Montesson），这会儿难得再到他维莱-科特雷的城堡里来走动了。

===Original===
His Highness had fine old-fashioned ideas as regards superannuated retainers. He, therefore, sent for Magloire, and asked him how much he had been able to save while in his service. Magloire replied that he was happily able to retire with a competence; the Prince, however, insisted upon knowing the exact amount of his little fortune, and Magloire confessed to an income of nine thousand livres.

“A man who has provided me with such a good table for thirty years,” said the Prince, “should have enough to live well upon himself for the remainder of his life.” And he made up the income to twelve thousand, so that Magloire might have a thousand livres a month to spend. Added to this, he allowed him to choose furniture for the whole of his house from his own old lumber-room; and thence came the damask curtains and gilt armchairs, which, although just a little bit faded and worn, had nevertheless preserved that appearance of grandeur which had made such an impression on Thibault.

===Chinese===
殿下对于年迈的老仆，怀着一副古道热肠的旧式体面想法。于是他把马格卢瓦召来，问他在自己手下这些年，攒下了多少家当。马格卢瓦回说，所幸他能带着一份颇为宽裕的进项告老；亲王却非要问清这笔小小家财的确切数目不可，马格卢瓦只好承认自己的进项是每年九千里弗（livres）。

“一个为我操持了三十年好餐桌的人，”亲王说，“自己下半辈子也该有足以过好日子的进项才是。”说罢便把他的进项凑足到一万二千里弗，好叫马格卢瓦每月有一千里弗可供花销。除此之外，还准他从自家那间堆旧货的库房里，挑拣整套屋子的家具；那锦缎帷幔和镀金扶手椅，便是打这儿来的——虽说稍稍褪了点色、磨损了些，到底还留着一派气派，正是这一派气派，给了蒂博那样深的触动。

===Original===
By the time the whole of the first partridge was finished, and half the second bottle had been drunk, Thibault knew that Madame Magloire was the host’s fourth wife, a fact which seemed in his own eyes to add a good foot or two to his height.

He had also ascertained that he had married her not for her fortune, but for her beauty, having always had as great a predilection for pretty faces and beautiful statues, as for good wines and appetising victuals, and Monsieur Magloire further stated, with no sign of faltering, that, old as he was, if his wife were to die, he should have no fear in entering on a fifth marriage.

===Chinese===
等到第一只山鹑整只吃尽、第二瓶酒也喝去了一半，蒂博已知晓马格卢瓦太太是主人的第四任妻子——这一层事实，在主人自己眼里，仿佛给他平添了一两尺的个头。

蒂博还打听得，他娶她并非图其钱财，而是贪其美貌，因为他一向对姣好的容颜与精美的塑像，与对好酒佳馔怀着同等的偏爱；马格卢瓦先生更毫不含糊地接着说，他虽已上了年纪，可倘若太太没了，再娶第五房他也毫无惧色。

===Original===
As he now passed from the Chambertin to the Hermitage, which he alternated with the Sillery, Monsieur Magloire began to speak of his wife’s qualities. She was not the personification of docility, no, quite the reverse; she was somewhat opposed to her husband’s admiration for the various wines of France, and did everything she could, even using physical force, to prevent his too frequent visits to the cellar; while, for one who believed in living without ceremony, she on her part was too fond of dress, too much given to elaborate head-gears, English laces, and suchlike gewgaws, which women make part of their arsenal; she would gladly have turned the twelve hogsheads of wine, which formed the staple of her husband’s cellar, into lace for her arms, and ribbons for her throat, if Monsieur Magloire had been the man to allow this metamorphosis. But, with this exception, there was not a virtue which Suzanne did not possess, and these virtues of hers, if the Bailiff was to be believed, were carried on so perfectly shaped a pair of legs, that, if by any misfortune she were to lose one, it would be quite impossible throughout the district to find another that would match the leg that remained. The good man was like a regular whale, blowing out self-satisfaction from all his air-holes, as the former does seawater. But even before all these hidden perfections of his wife had been revealed to him by the Bailiff, like a modern King Candaules ready to confide in a modern Gyges, her beauty had already made such a deep impression on the shoemaker, that, as we have seen, he could do nothing but think of it in silence as he walked beside her, and since he had been at table, he had continued to dream about it, listening to his host—eating the while of course—without answering, as Monsieur Magloire, delighted to have such an accommodating audience, poured forth his tales, linked one to another like a necklace of beads.

===Chinese===
这会儿他从香贝丹转到了埃尔米塔日，又拿西勒里交替着喝，马格卢瓦先生便开始谈起他太太的种种品性来。她可算不得温顺的化身，不，恰恰相反；她对丈夫那一份推崇法兰西各色葡萄酒的雅好，颇有些不以为然，千方百计——甚至动起手来——要拦阻他太勤快地往地窖里跑；而她这样一个口口声声主张过日子不讲排场的人，自己却又太爱穿衣打扮，太沉迷于种种繁复的头饰、英格兰花边，以及诸如此类被女人们当作自家军火库一部分的小玩意儿；倘若马格卢瓦先生是那等肯依着她的人，她巴不得把丈夫地窖里那十二大桶酒，一股脑儿变成缠在手臂上的花边、系在脖子上的缎带。可除此之外，再没有哪一样美德是苏珊娜（Suzanne）所不具备的；而这些美德——倘若执达吏的话可信——又是立在一双生得十全十美的腿上的，万一她不幸失去其一，那便踏遍这一方乡里，也再寻不出一条能与剩下那条相配的腿来。这好人活像一条正经的鲸鱼，从浑身上下所有的气孔里喷出心满意足之气，一如鲸鱼喷出海水。可其实在执达吏把太太这些深藏的妙处一一说与他听之前——其情其状，恰如一位现代的坎道列斯王（King Candaules），急于向一位现代的古格斯（Gyges）倾吐心曲——她的美貌已在这鞋匠心头刻下了极深的印记，以至于，如我们所见，他走在她身边时，除了默默想她之外什么也做不了，而自打入了席，他仍在梦里神游，一边听主人滔滔不绝——当然嘴上照吃不误——一边并不搭腔，由着马格卢瓦先生因得了这么个随和的听客而满心欢喜，把一段又一段的故事，像一串念珠似的一粒粒连着倒了出来。

===Original===
But the worthy Bailiff, having made a second excursion to the cellar, and this second excursion having produced, as the saying is, a little knot at the tip of his tongue, he began to be rather less appreciative of the rare quality which was required in his disciples by Pythagons. He, therefore, gave Thibault to understand that he had now said all that he wished to tell him concerning himself and his wife, and that it was Thibault’s turn to give him some information as regards his own circumstances, the amiable little man adding that wishing often to visit him, he wished to know more about him. Thibault felt that it was very necessary to disguise the truth; and accordingly gave himself out as a man living at ease in the country, on the revenues of two farms and of a hundred acres of land, situated near Vertefeuille.

===Chinese===
可这位可敬的执达吏，既已二度下了一趟地窖，而这第二趟又如同俗话所说，在他舌尖上打了个小小的结，他便渐渐不那么看重毕达哥拉斯（Pythagoras）所要求于其门徒的那桩难得的品质了——缄默。于是他让蒂博明白，关于自己和太太，他要说的都已说尽了，这回该轮到蒂博把自己的情形讲一讲给他听了；这位和蔼的小个子还补上一句，说他既想着时常登门走动，自然想对蒂博多知道些。蒂博心知这事非得把真相遮掩一番不可；于是便把自己说成一个在乡间过得挺舒坦的人，靠着两处田庄和一百亩地的出息过活，那地就坐落在韦尔特弗耶（Vertefeuille）附近。

===Original===
There was, he continued, a splendid warren on these hundred acres, with a wonderful supply of red and fallow deer, boars, partridges, pheasants and hares, of which the bailiff should have some to taste. The bailiff was astonished and delighted. As we have seen, by the menu for his table, he was fond of venison, and he was carried away with joy at the thought of obtaining his game without having recourse to the poachers, and through the channel of this new friendship.

And now, the last drop of the seventh bottle having been scrupulously divided between the two glasses, they decided that it was time to stop.

===Chinese===
他又接着说，那一百亩地上有一片顶呱呱的猎苑，红鹿、扁角鹿、野猪、山鹑、野鸡和野兔，要多少有多少，回头定要请执达吏尝上几味。执达吏又惊又喜。如我们所见，从他那一桌的菜单便瞧得出，他嗜好野味，一想到能不必再仰仗偷猎者、单凭这段新交情的门路便能弄到野味，他便欢喜得忘乎所以。

到了这时，第七瓶酒里的最后几滴，已被一丝不苟地均分到两只杯子里，他们便议定：是时候打住了。

===Original===
The rosy champagne—prime vintage of Aï, and the last bottle emptied—had brought Népomucène Magloire’s habitual good nature to the level of tender affection. He was charmed with his new friend, who tossed off his bottle in almost as good style as he did himself; he addressed him as his bosom friend, he embraced him, he made him promise that there should be a morrow to their pleasant entertainment; he stood a second time on tiptoe to give him a parting hug as he accompanied him to the door, which Thibault on his part, bending down, received with the best grace in the world.

===Chinese===
那玫瑰色的香槟——阿伊（Aï）上好的年份酒，也是喝空的最后一瓶——已把内波米塞纳·马格卢瓦素日的那份好脾气，推到了柔情脉脉的地步。他满心欢喜这位新朋友，这人干掉一瓶酒的做派几乎不输他自己；他把他唤作知心好友，与他拥抱，逼他应允这场快活的款待还要有个明日；他送他到门口，又二次踮起脚尖给他一个临别拥抱，蒂博这边则弯下腰去，以世上最和善的姿态受了。

===Original===
The church clock of Erneville was striking midnight as the door closed behind the shoemaker. The fumes of the heady wine he had been drinking had begun to give him a feeling of oppression before leaving the house, but it was worse when he got into the open air. He staggered, overcome with giddiness, and went and leant with his back against a wall. What followed next was as vague and mysterious to him as the phantasmagoria of a dream. Above his head, about six or eight feet from the ground, was a window, which, as he moved to lean against the wall, had appeared to him to be lighted, although the light was shaded by double curtains. He had hardly taken up his position against the wall when he thought he heard it open. It was, he imagined, the worthy bailiff, unwilling to part with him without sending him a last farewell, and he tried to step forward so as to do honour to this gracious intention, but his attempt was unavailing. At first he thought he was stuck to the wall like a branch of ivy, but he was soon disabused of this idea. He felt a heavy weight planted first on the right shoulder and then on the left, which made his knees give way so that he slid down the wall as if to seat himself. This manoeuvre on Thibault’s part appeared to be just what the individual who was making use of him as a ladder wished him to do, for we can no longer hide the fact that the weight so felt was that of a man. As Thibault made his forced genuflection, the man was also lowered; “That’s right, l’Eveillé! that’s right!” he said, “So!” and with this last word, he jumped to the ground, while overhead was heard the sound of a window being shut.

===Chinese===
埃纳维尔教堂的钟正敲着午夜十二点，门在鞋匠身后合上了。他在出门之前，那几杯上头烈酒腾起的酒气，已叫他胸口有些发闷，可一出露天，更觉难受。他脚下踉跄，一阵天旋地转，便走过去背靠一堵墙撑着。接下来发生的事，在他只觉得既朦胧又离奇，恍如一场梦里的幻影。在他头顶上方，离地约莫六到八尺的地方，有一扇窗子——他凑到墙根时，便瞧见那窗子亮着灯，虽说那灯光被双层帘子遮得暗暗的。他刚在墙根站定，便觉得自己听见窗子开了。他想，必定是那位好执达吏，舍不得与他分别，要再送他一声道别，他便想向前挪一步，好回应这一番盛意，可这番努力却全然无用。起先他以为自己像一枝常春藤似的粘在了墙上，可这念头很快就给打消了。他只觉一沉甸甸的分量，先压上了他的右肩，继而又压上左肩，压得他双膝一软，整个人顺着墙往下滑，像是要坐到地上去。蒂博这被迫的一蹲，倒正合了那个把他当作梯子来用的人的心意——因为我们再瞒不下去了：他所感到的那一重重分量，正是一个人的分量。蒂博被迫屈膝的当口，那人也被放了下来；“对，就这样，勒韦耶（l’Eveillé）！对！”那人说，“好！”话音这一落，他便跳到了地上，头顶上随之传来一扇窗子合上的声响。

===Original===
Thibault had sense enough to understand two things: first, that he was mistaken for someone called l’Eveillé, who was probably asleep somewhere about the premises; secondly, that his shoulders had just served some lover as a climbing ladder; both of which things caused Thibault an undefined sense of humiliation.

Accordingly, he seized hold mechanically of some floating piece of stuff which he took to be the lover’s cloak, and, with the persistency of a drunken man, continued to hang on to it.

“What are you doing that for, you scoundrel?” asked a voice, which did not seem altogether unfamiliar to the shoemaker. “One would think you were afraid of losing me.”

“Most certainly I am afraid of losing you,” replied Thibault, “because I wish to know who it is has the impertinence to use my shoulders for a ladder.”

“Phew!” said the unknown, “it’s not you then, l’Eveillé?”

“No, it is not,” replied Thibault.

“Well, whether it is you or not you, I thank you.”

“How, thank you? Ah! I dare say! thank you, indeed! You think the matter is going to rest like that, do you?”

“I had counted upon it being so, certainly.”

“Then you counted without your host.”

===Chinese===
蒂博还有那么几分清醒，足以明白两件事：其一，他被人错当成一个唤作勒韦耶的人，那人多半正睡在这宅子什么角落里；其二，他这两肩方才给某个情人当作攀墙下地的梯子使了——这两件事，都叫蒂博心头浮起一种说不清道不明的屈辱。

于是他下意识地一把攥住一片飘拂的料子，只当那是那情人的斗篷，又拿出醉汉的那一股子拧劲儿，死死攥着不肯撒手。

“你这是做什么，你这无赖？”一个声音问道——这嗓音在鞋匠听来，倒也不算全然陌生。“瞧这架势，倒像你是怕把我丢了似的。”

“我当然是怕把你丢了，”蒂博回道，“因为我倒要看看，是谁这般放肆，敢拿我这两肩当梯子使。”

“咄！”那陌生人道，“那么说，你不是勒韦耶了？”

“不错，我不是。”蒂博回道。

“也罢，不管是不是你，我都多谢了。”

“怎么，多谢？哈！可不是吗！多谢你哩！你以为这事就这么了结了，是不是？”

“我原本确是这般盘算的。”

“那你这盘算是没把东家算在里头。”

===Original===
“Now, you blackguard, leave go of me! you are drunk!”

“Drunk! What do you mean? We only drank seven bottles between us, and the Bailiff had a good four to his share.”

“Leave go of me, you drunkard, do you hear!”

“Drunkard! you call me a drunkard, a drunkard for having drunk three bottles of wine!”

“I don’t call you a drunkard because you drank three bottles of wine, but because you let yourself get tipsy over those three unfortunate bottles.”

And, with a gesture of commiseration, and trying for the third time to release his cloak, the unknown continued:

“Now then, are you going to let go my cloak or not, you idiot?”

Thibault was at all times touchy as to the way people addressed him, but in his present state of mind his susceptibility amounted to extreme irritation.

“By the devil!” he exclaimed, “let me tell you, my fine sir, that the only idiot here is the man who gives insults in return for the services of which he has made use, and seeing that is so, I do not know what prevents me planting my fist in the middle of your face.”

===Chinese===
“行了，你这无赖，撒手！你喝醉了！”

“喝醉！什么话？我们俩统共才喝了七瓶，执达吏一人就独吞了足足四瓶。”

“撒手，你这醉汉，听见没有！”

“醉汉！你管我叫醉汉，喝了三瓶酒就成了醉汉了！”

“我管你叫醉汉，倒不是因为你喝了三瓶酒，而是因为你区区三瓶倒霉酒下肚，便由着自个儿醉成这副德行。”

那陌生人说着，做一个怜悯的手势，第三次试着把斗篷从对方手里夺回来，接着道：

“那么，你到底撒不撒手，你这白痴？”

蒂博向来对别人跟他说话的腔调格外敏感，而此刻这心境之下，他那一份多心已激成了极度的火气。

“凭魔鬼起誓！”他嚷道，“我倒要告诉你，我这位好老爷，这儿唯一的白痴，是那个受人好处反倒出言不逊的家伙，既是这般，我竟不知有什么能拦着我不一拳揍到你那张脸正中去的。”

===Original===
This menace was scarcely out of his mouth, when, as instantly as a cannon goes off once the flame of the match has touched the powder, the blow with which Thibault had threatened his unknown adversary, came full against his own cheek.

“Take that, you beast,” said the voice, which brought back to Thibault certain recollections in connection with the blow he received. “I am a good Jew, you see, and pay you back your money before weighing your coin.”

Thibault’s answer was a blow in the chest; it was well directed, and Thibault felt inwardly pleased with it himself.
