But it had no more effect on his antagonist than the fillip from a child’s finger would have on an oak tree. It was returned by a second blow of the fist which so far exceeded the former in the force with which it was delivered, that Thibault felt certain if the giant’s strength went on increasing in the same ratio, that a third of the kind would level him with the ground.

===Chinese===
这番威吓才一出口，便如火星一触火药、大炮应声而发那般迅捷——蒂博方才拿来吓唬那陌生对手的一拳，结结实实地反落在了他自个儿的脸颊上。

“给你这一下，你这畜生，”那声音说道——这一嗓音，连同落在他脸上的这一拳，唤回了蒂博心头几许旧事的记忆。“你瞧，我是个讲信用的犹太人，先把你这枚铜钱还清了，再来掂你的成色。”

蒂博的回敬是照着对方胸口一拳；这一拳打得准，蒂博自个儿心里也暗暗得意。可这一拳打在对手身上，却如孩童一个弹指弹在橡树上，毫无作用。对方回敬过来的第二拳，力道之大远胜头一拳，蒂博心下断定：倘若这巨人的力气照这般比率涨下去，再来同样一下第三拳，便要叫他平平地贴到地上去。

===Original===
But the very violence of his blow brought disaster on Thibault’s unknown assailant. The former had fallen on to one knee, and so doing, his hand, touching the ground, came in contact with a stone. Rising in fury to his feet again, with the stone in his hand, he flung it at his enemy’s head. The colossal figure uttered a sound like the bellowing of an ox, turned round on himself, and then, like an oak tree cut off by the roots, fell his whole length on the ground, and lay there insensible.

Not knowing whether he had killed, or only wounded his adversary, Thibault took to his heels and fled, not even turning to look behind him.

===Chinese===
可正是他这一拳的凶猛，反倒给蒂博那位不知名的对头招来了祸事。原来蒂博方才单膝跪倒在地，跪下去时，一只手触到了地上，摸着了一块石头。他怒不可遏地翻身站起，攥着那块石头，便朝敌人的脑袋掷了过去。那巨人般的身躯发出一声如公牛般的哞吼，整个人在原地转了一圈，随即像一棵被连根斩断的橡树，直挺挺地全身栽倒在地，不省人事地躺在那里。

蒂博也弄不清自己到底是结果了对手，还是只打伤了他，他撒开两腿便跑，连头也不回一下。

## XVIII Death and Resurrection / XVIII 死而复活

===Original===
The cold morning air brought Thibault back to consciousness; he tried to rise, but the extremity of his pain held him bound. He was lying on his back, with no remembrance of what had happened, seeing only the low grey sky above him. He made another effort, and turning managed to lift himself on his elbow. As he looked around him, he began to recall the events of the previous night; he recognised the breach in the wall; and then there came back to him the memory of the love meeting with the Countess and the desperate duel with the Count. The ground near him was red with blood, but the Count was no longer there; no doubt, Lestocq, who had given him this fine blow that was nailing him to the spot, had helped his master indoors; Thibault they had left there, to die like a dog, as far as they cared. He had it on the tip of his tongue to hurl after them all the maledictory wishes wherewith one would like to assail one’s cruellest enemy. But since Thibault had been no longer Thibault, and indeed during the remainder of the time that he would still be the Baron Raoul, or at least so in outward appearance, his demoniacal power had been and would continue in abeyance.

===Chinese===
清晨寒凉的空气将蒂博从昏迷中激醒；他想要撑起身子，可那剧痛到了极处，把他牢牢钉在了原地。他仰面躺着，全然记不得出了什么事，眼前只看得见头顶那一派低垂的灰天。他又使了一把劲，翻过身，勉强用胳膊肘撑着抬起了上半身。他四下一望，昨夜种种便渐渐回到记忆里来；他认出了墙上那道缺口；继而与伯爵夫人幽会、又与伯爵那场拼死决斗的情景，也一齐涌上心头。他身边的地上血迹殷红，可伯爵早已不在那里；不用问，那一刀把他钉在原地的勒斯托克（Lestocq），准是扶着他主子回屋里去了；至于蒂博，他们便丢在那儿——照他们的心思，让他像条狗一样死掉算了。他满心想把一个人恨不得加诸最狠毒仇敌的那一整套咒愿，统统追在他们身后骂出口去。可蒂博既已不再是蒂博，而且在此后他仍要做男爵拉乌尔（Raoul）——或至少外表上仍是——的剩余时日里，他那魔鬼的法力已然、且将继续处于搁置不灵的状态。

===Original===
He had until nine o’clock that evening; but would he live till then? This question gave rise in Thibault to a very uneasy state of mind. If he were to die before that hour, which of them would die, he or the Baron? It seemed to him as likely to be one as the other. What, however, disturbed and angered him most was his consciousness that the misfortune which had befallen him was again owing to his own fault. He remembered now that before he had expressed the wish to be the Baron for four and twenty hours, he had said some such words as these:

“I should laugh, Raoul, if the Comte de Mont-Gobert were to take you by surprise; you would not get off so easily as if he were the Bailiff Magloire; there would be swords drawn, and blows given and received.”

===Chinese===
他还有时间，可以撑到当晚九点；可他到底能不能活到那个时辰呢？这个问题叫蒂博心里七上八下、不得安宁。倘若他在这之前便断了气，死的究竟会是哪一个——是他，还是男爵？在他看来，两者皆有可能。然而，最叫他既恼且恨的，是他心里明白：这场落到他头上的横祸，又得怪他自己。他这才想起，在他发愿要做二十四个小时男爵之前，他分明说过这样的话：

“拉乌尔，若是蒙-戈贝尔伯爵（the Comte de Mont-Gobert）打你个措手不及，我可要笑了；你脱身绝不会像碰上执达吏马格卢瓦那般容易；那可就是刀剑出鞘、你一拳我一脚的场面了。”

===Original===
At last, with a terrible effort, and suffering the while excruciating pain, Thibault succeeded in dragging himself on to one knee. He could then make out people walking along a road not far off on their way to market, and he tried to call to them, but the blood filled his mouth and nearly choked him. So he put his hat on the point of his knife and signalled to them like a shipwrecked mariner, but his strength again failing, he once more fell back unconscious. In a little while, however, he again awoke to sensation; he appeared to be swaying from side to side as if in a boat. He opened his eyes; the peasants, it seemed, had seen him, and although not knowing who he was, had had compassion on this handsome young man lying covered with blood, and had concocted a sort of handbarrow out of some branches, on which they were now carrying him to Villers-Cotterets. But by the time they reached Puiseux, the wounded man felt that he could no longer bear the movement, and begged them to put him down in the first peasant’s hut they came to, and to send a doctor to him there. The carriers took him to the house of the village priest, and left him there, Thibault before they parted, distributing gold among them from Raoul’s purse, accompanied by many thanks for all their kind offices. The priest was away saying mass, but on returning and finding the wounded man, he uttered loud cries of lamentation.

===Chinese===
终于，蒂博拼了命地一使力，一边痛得钻心彻骨，一边到底撑着跪起了一条腿。这时他已能瞧见不远处的路上有人走过，是去赶集的，他便想出声唤他们，可血涌进嘴里，险些把他呛死。于是他把帽子挑在刀尖上，像落难的水手那样朝他们打信号，但气力再一次不济，他又昏了过去。不过片刻之后，他又恢复了知觉；只觉得自己左摇右晃，仿佛坐在船上。他睁开眼来；原来那些庄稼人瞧见了他，虽不认得他是谁，却怜悯这个血泊里躺着的英俊青年，便用树枝扎成一副简易担架，正抬着他往维莱-科特雷（Villers-Cotterets）去。可待抬到皮瑟勒（Puiseux），伤者已觉得再也受不住这一路颠簸，便求他们就近找头一家农舍把他放下，再替他请个医生来。抬担架的人把他送到了本堂神父的宅子里，安顿下来；临别之际，蒂博从拉乌尔的钱袋里掏出金币，散给他们每人一枚，对这一路上种种照应千恩万谢。神父出门做弥撒去了，待他回来瞧见这个伤者，顿时发出了大声的哀号。

===Original===
Had he been Raoul himself, Thibault could not have found a better hospital. The priest had at one time been Curé of Vauparfond, and while there had been engaged to give Raoul his first schooling. Like all country priests, he knew, or thought he knew, something about doctoring; so he examined his old pupil’s wound. The knife had passed under the shoulder-blade, through the right lung, and out between the second and third ribs.

He did not for a moment disguise to himself the seriousness of the wound, but he said nothing until the doctor had been to see it. The latter arrived and after his examination, he turned and shook his head.

===Chinese===
蒂博就算真是拉乌尔本人，也再找不到一处比这更好的养伤之地了。这位神父从前曾在沃帕丰（Vauparfond）任本堂神父，在那任上时，曾被请去教过拉乌尔最初的启蒙功课。与所有乡间神父一样，他也懂得——或自以为懂得一点医道；于是他便动手查看这位旧日学生的伤口。那刀子是从肩胛骨底下刺进去的，穿过右肺，又从第二根与第三根肋骨之间穿了出来。

他一刻也不曾瞒过自己这伤势有多凶险，但他什么也没说，直等到医生来诊视过了。医生到了，查看了一番，转过身来，摇了摇头。

===Original===
“Are you going to bleed him?” asked the priest.

“What would be the use?” asked the doctor. “If it had been done at once after the wound was given, it might perhaps have helped to save him, but it would be dangerous now to disturb the blood in any way.”

“Is there any chance for him?” asked the priest, who was thinking that the less there was for the doctor to do, the more there would be for the priest.

“If his wound runs the ordinary course,” said the doctor, lowering his voice, “he will probably not last out the day.”

“You give him up then?”

“A doctor never gives up a patient, or at least if he does so, he still trusts to the possibility of nature mercifully interfering on the patient’s behalf; a clot may form and stop the hemorrhage; a cough may disturb the clot, and the patient bleed to death.”

===Chinese===
“您要给他放血吗？”神父问。

“放血又有何用？”医生反问，“若是刚一受伤便放，或许还能帮他捡回一条命，可眼下再怎么动那血，都是凶险的事。”

“他还有没有指望？”神父问道——他心里盘算的是，医生无能为力之处越多，留给神父的活计便越多。

“要是这伤口照常理发展下去，”医生压低了声音说，“他多半熬不过今天。”

“那么您是放弃他了？”

“医生从不放弃病人；或者就算放弃了，也总还指望着造化慈悲，肯出头替病人帮一把忙：或有一块血块结起，止住了出血；又或一声咳嗽冲开了血块，那病人便血尽而亡。”

===Original===
“You think then that it is my duty to prepare the poor young man for death,” asked the curate.

“I think,” answered the doctor, shrugging his shoulders, “you would do better to leave him alone; in the first place because he is, at present, in a drowsy condition and cannot hear what you say; later on, because he will be delirious, and unable to understand you.” But the doctor was mistaken; the wounded man, drowsy as he was, overheard this conversation, more reassuring as regards the salvation of his soul than the recovery of his body. How many things people say in the presence of sick persons, believing that they cannot hear, while all the while, they are taking in every word! In the present case, this extra acuteness of hearing may perhaps have been due to the fact that it was Thibault’s soul which was awake in Raoul’s body; if the soul belonging to it had been in this body, it would probably have succumbed more entirely to the effects of the wound.

===Chinese===
“那么您以为，我该去为这可怜的年轻人预备后事了？”本堂神父问道。

“依我看，”医生答道，耸了耸肩，“您还是别去招惹他为好；一来因为他眼下正昏昏沉沉，听不见您说什么；二来待会儿他就要发起谵妄来，也听不懂您的话。”可医生这回却是判断错了；那伤者虽昏昏沉沉，却将这段对话听得一清二楚——这番话论及他灵魂得救的把握，倒比论及他肉身康复的指望更叫他安心。世人有多少话是在病人床前说的，总以为病人听不见，殊不知人家一字一句都听进了耳朵里！就说眼下这一桩，这份格外敏锐的听觉，或许正是因为醒着的，是蒂博的灵魂——寄居在拉乌尔身子里；倘若这身子里装的是它自己原配的那副灵魂，多半早已更彻底地向这伤势的后果缴械投降了。

===Original===
The doctor now dressed the wound in the back, but left the front wound uncovered, merely directing that a piece of linen soaked in iced water should be kept over it. Then, having poured some drops of a sedative into a glass of water, and telling the priest to give this to the patient whenever he asked for drink, the doctor departed, saying that he would come again the following morning, but that he much feared he should take his journey for nothing.

Thibault would have liked to put in a word of his own, and to say himself what he thought about his condition, but his spirit was as if imprisoned in this dying body, and, against his will, was forced to submit to lying thus within its cell. But he could still hear the priest, who not only spoke to him, but endeavoured by shaking him to arouse him from his lethargy. Thibault found this very fatiguing, and it was lucky for the priest that the wounded man, just now, had no superhuman power, for he inwardly sent the good man to the devil, many times over.

===Chinese===
医生这便料理起背部的伤口来，胸前的伤口却没盖什么，只吩咐拿一块浸了冰水的亚麻布覆在上头。随后，他又往一杯水里滴了几滴镇定药水，嘱托神父：病人若讨水喝，便把这水给他。做完这些，医生告辞而去，说次日早晨再来一趟，却只怕自己这一趟是白跑了。

蒂博本想插上一句嘴，亲自说说自己对自身状况的判断，可他的魂魄仿佛被囚禁在这具垂死的躯壳里，身不由己，只得乖乖躺在这牢笼之中。但他仍听得见神父的声音——那神父不但同他说话，还不住地摇晃他，想把他从昏沉里摇醒。蒂博觉得这一通摇晃实在叫人疲惫不堪；幸而这位伤者眼下没有超自然的力量，否则那好神父非得被他暗地里打发到魔鬼那儿去不可，且不止打发一回。

===Original===
Before long it seemed to him that some sort of hot burning pan was being inserted under the soles of his feet, his loins, his head; his blood began to circulate, then to boil, like water over a fire. His ideas became confused, his clenched jaws opened; his tongue which had been bound became loosened; some disconnected words escaped him.

“Ah, ah!” he thought to himself, “this no doubt is what the good doctor spoke about as delirium;” and, for the while at least, this was his last lucid idea.

===Chinese===
不多时，他便觉得脚下、腰间、头顶，仿佛都塞进了烧得滚烫的烙铁；他的血开始流动，继而便像炉子上的水一般沸腾起来。他的思绪乱作一团，紧咬的牙关松开了；原先被缚住的舌头也活动起来；一些支离破碎的字眼从他嘴里溜了出来。

“啊，啊！”他暗自想道，“这想必就是那位好医生所说的谵妄了；”至少在那一刻，这是他最后一个清醒的念头。

===Original===
His whole life﻿—and his life had really only existed since his first acquaintance with the black wolf﻿—passed before him. He saw himself following, and failing to hit the buck; saw himself tied to the oak-tree, and the blows of the strap falling on him; saw himself and the black wolf drawing up their compact; saw himself trying to pass the devil’s ring over Agnelette’s finger; saw himself trying to pull out the red hairs, which now covered a third of his head. Then he saw himself on his way to pay court to the pretty Madame Polet of the mill, meeting Landry, and getting rid of his rival; pursued by the farm servants, and followed by his wolves. He saw himself making the acquaintance of Madame Magloire, hunting for her, eating his share of the game, hiding behind the curtains, discovered by Maître Magloire, flouted by the Baron of Vez, turned out by all three. Again he saw the hollow tree, with his wolves couching around it and the owls perched on its branches, and heard the sounds of the approaching violins and hautboy and saw himself looking, as Agnelette and the happy wedding party went by. He saw himself the victim of angry jealousy, endeavouring to fight against it by the help of drink, and across his troubled brain came the recollection of François, of Champagne, and the Innkeeper; he heard the galloping of Baron Raoul’s horse, and he felt himself knocked down and rolling in the muddy road. Then he ceased to see himself as Thibault; in his stead arose the figure of the handsome young rider whose form he had taken for a while. Once more he was kissing Lisette, once more his lips were touching the Countess’s hand; then he was wanting to escape, but he found himself at a crossroad where three ways only met, and each of these was guarded by one of his victims: the first, by the spectre of a drowned man, that was Marcotte; the second, by a young man dying of fever on a hospital bed, that was Landry; the third, by a wounded man, dragging himself along on one knee, and trying in vain to stand up on his mutilated leg, that was the Comte de Mont-Gobert.

===Chinese===
他这一生——而他这一生，其实是从与那头黑狼相识之后才算真正开始的——一幕幕在眼前掠过。他看见自己追赶那头雄鹿，却失了手；看见自己被绑在橡树上，皮带一下下抽在身上；看见自己与那头黑狼订下盟约；看见自己想把那枚魔鬼的戒指套上阿涅莱特（Agnelette）的手指；又看见自己拼命想拔掉那一头红发——那红发如今已占去了他大半个脑袋。随后，他看见自己前去向磨坊那位俏丽的波莱太太（Madame Polet）献殷勤，遇见朗德里（Landry），又除掉了这个情敌；看见自己被庄户人家追赶，身后跟着他那群狼。他看见自己结识了马格卢瓦太太（Madame Magloire），替她打猎，分吃猎物，躲在帘子后面，被马格卢瓦师傅当场撞破，遭韦兹男爵（the Baron of Vez）一顿奚落，终被那三个人一并轰出门去。他又一次看见那棵中空的老树，群狼伏在四周，猫头鹰栖在枝桠上，他听得渐渐近来的小提琴与高音双簧管的乐声，又看见自己站在那里望着——望着阿涅莱特与那一队快活的送亲队伍走过。他看见自己被怒火中烧的嫉妒所折磨，想借酒浇愁来与之对抗；在那纷乱的脑海中，又掠过弗朗索瓦（François）、香槟（Champagne）和那客栈老板的影子；他听见了男爵拉乌尔的坐骑疾驰而来的马蹄声，觉得自己被撞倒在地，在泥泞的路上打着滚。随后，他便不再以蒂博的身份看见自己了；取而代之的，是那个英俊青年骑手的身影——他这副皮囊，是他暂时借来一用的。他又一次在亲吻莉赛特（Lisette），又一次将嘴唇贴上了伯爵夫人的手；继而他想要逃走，却发觉自己置身于一个三岔路口，三条路各由一个被他害过的人把守：第一条路，由一个淹死鬼的幽魂把守，那是马尔科特（Marcotte）；第二条路，由一个年轻人把守，他正发着高烧、死在医院病床上，那是朗德里；第三条路，由一个伤者把守，他正单膝跪地拖着身子往前挪，那条残腿怎么也撑不起身来——那便是蒙-戈贝尔伯爵。

===Original===
He fancied that as all these things passed before him, he told the history of them one by one, and that the priest, as he listened to this strange confession, looked more like a dying man, was paler and more trembling than the man whose confession he was listening to; that he wanted to give him absolution, but that he, Thibault, pushed him away, shaking his head, and that he cried out with a terrible laugh: “I want no absolution! I am damned! damned! damned!”

===Chinese===
他恍惚觉得，这些景象一幕幕从眼前过的时候，他已将桩桩件件的原委都一五一十地说了出来；而那神父，听罢这场古怪的告解，看上去倒比那个临终的人更像临终之人——脸色比方才向他告解的那个人还要惨白，身子抖得还厉害；他想要赦免他，可他蒂博却一把将他推开，摇着头，发出一声可怕的狞笑，喊道：“我不要赦免！我是该死的！该死的！该死的！”

===Original===
And in the midst of all this hallucination, this delirious madness, the spirit of Thibault could hear the priest’s clock striking the hours, and as they struck he counted them. Only this clock seemed to have grown to gigantic proportions and the face of it was the blue vault of heaven, and the numbers on it were flames; and the clock was called eternity, and the monstrous pendulum, as it swung backwards and forwards called out in turn at every beat: “Never! Forever!” And so he lay and heard the long hours of the day pass one by one; and then at last the clock struck nine. At half past nine, he, Thibault, would have been Raoul, and Raoul would have been Thibault, for just four and twenty hours. As the last stroke of the hour died away, Thibault felt the fever passing from him, it was succeeded by a sensation of coldness, which almost amounted to shivering. He opened his eyes, all trembling with cold, and saw the priest at the foot of the bed saying the prayers for the dying, and the hands of the actual clock pointing to a quarter past nine.

===Chinese===
在这一片幻象与谵狂之中，蒂博的魂魄仍能听见神父屋里那座钟敲点的声音，每敲一下他便默数一下。只不过，这座钟仿佛胀成了硕大无朋的庞然巨物，钟面便是那一穹蓝色的苍天，上面的数字都是熊熊烈焰；这钟，名叫“永恒”；那条怪异的钟摆，一来一回地摆动，每摆一下便轮流喊出：“永不再来！永永远远！”他便这样躺着，听见白日里那漫长的时辰一个接一个地过去；末了，钟终于敲响了九点。九点半，他蒂博就要做满整整二十四个钟头的拉乌尔，而拉乌尔也要做满整整二十四个钟头的蒂博了。当最后一声钟响消歇之际，蒂博只觉那场热病正从身上退去，取而代之的是一阵冷意，几乎要发起抖来。他睁开眼来，冷得浑身打战，只见神父正立在床尾念着临终的祷文，而那座实物钟的指针，正指着九点一刻。

===Original===
His senses had become so acute, that, imperceptible as was their double movement, he could yet see both the larger and smaller one slowly creeping along; they were gradually nearing the critical hour; half past nine! Although the face of the clock was in darkness, it seemed illuminated by some inward light. As the minute hand approached the number six, a spasm becoming every instant more and more violent shook the dying man; his feet were like ice, and the numbness slowly, but steadily, mounted from the feet to the knees, from the knees to the thighs, from the thighs to the lower part of the body. The sweat was running down his forehead, but he had no strength to wipe it away, nor even to ask to have it done. It was a sweat of agony which he knew every moment might be the sweat of death. All kinds of strange shapes, which had nothing of the human about them, floated before his eyes; the light faded away; wings as of bats seemed to lift his body and carry it into some twilight region, which was neither life nor death, but seemed a part of both. Then the twilight itself grew darker and darker; his eyes closed, and like a blind man stumbling in the dark, his heavy wings seemed to flap against strange and unknown things. After that he sank away into unfathomable depths, into bottomless abysses, but still he heard the sound of a bell.

===Chinese===
他的感官已敏锐到了这般地步：那两根指针一长一短、任凭移动得如何难以察觉，他仍能瞧见它们一寸寸地缓缓爬行；它们正一点一点地逼近那个生死攸关的时辰——九点半！钟面虽浸在黑暗里，却仿佛被一种发自内里的光照得通明。待那分针趋近数字六时，一阵比一刻紧似一刻的痉挛，便把垂死之人猛地攫住；他的双脚冷如冰块，那麻木之感缓缓地、却笃定地，自脚向膝、自膝向股、自股向身躯下方蔓延上去。汗水顺着他额头往下淌，他却连擦一擦的力气都没有了，甚至求人代擦都做不到。那是临终的冷汗，他心里明白，这一刻的汗，随时都可能变成断气的死汗。种种与人无涉的怪诞形影，在他眼前飘忽来去；光亮渐次暗淡下去；仿佛蝙蝠的双翼托起了他的身躯，将他载入一片朦胧之境——那地方既非生、亦非死，却像是生死两界各占一半。继而那朦胧本身也越发幽暗了；他的眼皮合上，有如盲人在暗中跌跌撞撞，他那一双沉翼仿佛扑打着种种陌生而未知的事物。此后他便沉了下去，坠入不可测的深处，坠入无底的深渊——可他仍旧听得见一声钟响。

===Original===
The bell rang once, and scarcely had it ceased to vibrate when the dying man uttered a cry. The priest rose and went to the side of the bed; with that cry the Baron Raoul had breathed his last: it was exactly one second after the half hour after nine.

===Chinese===
那钟只响了一声，余音未绝，垂死之人便发出一声喊。神父站起身来，走到床边；这一声喊里，男爵拉乌尔咽下了最后一口气：此刻正是九点半过后一秒。

## XXIV Hunting Down the Werewolf / XXIV 追猎狼人

===Original===
Thibault had got well ahead of the dogs, thanks to the precaution he had taken of making good his escape at the first note of the bloodhound. For some time he heard no further sound of pursuit; but, all at once, like distant thunder, the baying of the hounds reached his ears, and he began to feel some anxiety. He had been trotting, but he now went on at greater speed, and did not pause till he had put a few more leagues between himself and his enemies. Then he stood still and took his bearings; he found himself on the heights at Montaigu. He bent his head and listened—the dogs still seemed a long way off, somewhere near the Tillet coppice.

It required a wolf's ear to distinguish them so far off. Thibault went down the hill again, as if to meet the dogs; then, leaving Erneville to the left, he leaped into the little stream which rises there, waded down its course as far as Grimancourt, dashed into the woods of Lessart-l'Abbesse, and finally gained the forest of Compiègne. He was somewhat reassured to find that, in spite of his three hours' hard running, the steel-like muscles of his wolf legs were not in the least fatigued. He hesitated, however, to trust himself in a forest which was not so familiar to him as that of Villers-Cotterets.

===Chinese===
蒂博早已把那群猎犬远远甩在后头，全亏他预先有了防备——寻血猎犬才一开腔，他便早早溜之大吉。有一阵子他再没听见追兵的声息；可是蓦地，一阵有如远方闷雷般的犬吠传到了他耳中，他心里开始有些发毛。他本是边走边小跑的，这会儿便加快了脚程，一口气又拉开了几里格的路程，把自己和敌人之间的距离拉得更远，这才站住脚。他四面一望，认了认方向，发觉自己已到了蒙塔居（Montaigu）的高地上。他低下头侧耳细听——那些猎犬似乎还在很远的地方，大概在蒂莱树林那一带。

要在那么远的距离上分辨出犬吠，非得有一双狼的耳朵不可。蒂博又从高地下来，仿佛是要迎着那群狗走去；随后，他把埃纳维尔（Erneville）撇在左边，纵身跳进了那儿发源的一条小溪，顺水流一路蹚到格里芒库尔，一头钻进勒萨尔-拉贝斯（Lessart-l'Abbesse）的树林，末了奔进了贡比涅森林。他发觉自己虽说拼死拼活地跑了三个钟头，那双狼腿上钢筋铁骨般的肌肉却竟毫不疲倦，心里多少踏实了些。可这林子终归不如维莱-科特雷那片森林叫人熟稔，他到底不敢轻易在这陌生的林子里托大。

===Original===
After another dash of a mile or so, he decided that by doubling boldly he would be most likely to put the dogs off the scent. He crossed at a gallop all the stretch of plain between Pierrefond and Mont-Gobert, took to the woods at the Champ Meutard, came out again at Vauvaudrand, regained the stream by the Sancères timber floatage, and once more found himself in the forest near Longpont. Unfortunately for him, just as he reached the end of the Route du Pendu, he came across another pack of twenty dogs, which Monsieur de Montbreton's huntsman was bringing up as a relay, for the Baron had sent his neighbour news of the chase. Instantly the hounds were uncoupled by the huntsman as he caught sight of the wolf, for seeing that the latter kept its distance, he feared it would get too far ahead if he waited for the others to come up before loosing his dogs. And now began the struggle between the werewolf and the dogs in very earnest. It was a wild chase, which the horses, in spite of their skilled riders, had great difficulty in following, a chase over plains, through woods, across heaths, pursued at a headlong pace. As the hunt flew by, it appeared and disappeared like a flash of lightning across a cloud, leaving behind a whirlwind of dust, and a sound of horns and cries which echo had hardly time to repeat. It rushed over hill and dale, through torrents and bogs, and over precipices, as if horses and dogs had been winged like Hippogriffs and Chimeras. The Baron had come up with his huntsmen, riding at their head, and almost riding on the tails of his dogs, his eye flashing, his nostrils dilated, exciting the pack with wild shouts and furious blasts, digging his spurs into his horse's sides whenever an obstacle of any kind caused it to hesitate for a single instant. The black wolf, on his side, still held on at the same rapid pace; although sorely shaken at hearing the fresh pack in full pursuit only a short way behind him, just as he had got back to the forest, he had not lost an inch of ground. As he retained to the full all his human consciousness, it seemed to him impossible, as he still ran on, that he should not escape in safety from this ordeal; he felt that it was not possible for him to die before he had taken vengeance for all the agony that others made him suffer, before he had known those pleasures that had been promised him, above all—for at this critical moment his thoughts kept on running on this—before he had gained Agnelette's love. At moments he was possessed by terror, at others by anger. He thought at times that he would turn and face this yelling pack of dogs, and, forgetting his present form, scatter them with stones and blows. Then, an instant after, feeling mad with rage, deafened by the death-knell the hounds were ringing in his ears, he fled, he leaped, he flew with the legs of a deer, with the wings of an eagle. But his efforts were in vain; he might run, leap, almost fly, the sounds of death still clung to him, and if for a moment they became more distant, it was only to hear them a moment after nearer and more threatening still. But still the instinct of self-preservation did not fail him; and still his strength was undiminished; only, if by ill luck, he were to come across other relays, he felt that it might give way. So he determined to take a bold course so as to outdistance the dogs, and to get back to his lairs, where he knew his ground and hoped to evade the dogs. He therefore doubled for the second time. He first ran back to Puiseux, then skirted past Viviers, regained the forest of Compiègne, made a dash into the forest of Largue, returned and crossed the Aisne at Attichy, and finally got back to the forest of Villers-Cotterets at the low lands of Argent. He trusted in this way to baffle the strategical plans of the Lord of Vez, who had, no doubt, posted his dogs at various likely points.

===Chinese===
又猛窜了一英里光景，他拿定主意：只要大胆地兜个圈子折回去，便最有把握叫那群猎犬把气味跟丢。他放开四蹄飞驰，横穿过皮埃尔丰与蒙-戈贝尔之间的那片平原，在默塔尔草场钻进树林，又在沃沃德朗钻出来，顺着桑塞尔木材浮运道那条小溪回到水边，再度来到了龙蓬附近的森林。可惜他运气不好——才奔到吊死人路尽头，便迎头撞上了另一群二十条猎犬，这是蒙布雷东先生家的猎手牵来作接力的，原来男爵早已把围猎的消息派人知会了这位邻居。那猎手一瞧见狼，立刻便把猎犬解了索，因为他见这畜生只顾远远地跑，生怕等大队人马到齐再放狗，它便要跑得太远了。于是，狼人与群犬之间的较量就此当真开了场。那是一场惊心动魄的追逐，任骑手骑术如何了得，胯下的马也极难跟上——这一路追逐，越过平原，穿过树林，跨过荒原，一概是不要命的狂奔。围猎的队伍飞驰而过，恰似一道闪电掠过乌云，忽隐忽现，身后只留下一阵卷地的尘土，一片号角声与呐喊声，连回声都来不及应和。这一行人马冲过山冈与溪谷，蹚过激流与沼泽，越过悬崖峭壁，仿佛那些马和狗都如飞马与吐火兽一般生出了翅膀。男爵已带着手下的猎手们赶了上来，他冲在最前头，几乎踩着自家猎犬的尾巴，双目炯炯，鼻翼大张，以狂野的呼喝与猛烈的号角声激励着群犬，坐骑只要因任何障碍稍有迟疑，他便一马刺扎进马肋。那黑狼这一边呢，却仍以同样的高速奔逃；虽说刚奔回森林便听得身后不远处这一群新上来的生力军穷追不舍，心中着实一凛，到底没让出半寸地盘。他既仍全须全尾地保有着人的神志，便一边跑一边只觉得：要他从这场劫难中安然脱身是不可能的，他不可能就这么死了——他还没有为旁人加诸于他的种种苦楚报过仇，还没有尝到那许诺给他的种种快活，尤其要紧的是——因为这生死关头他的念头总在这一点上打转——他还没有赢得阿涅莱特的爱。他时而为恐惧所攫，时而又为愤怒所制。他有时真想掉过头来，迎着这一群嗥叫的猎犬扑上去，忘却自己眼下的这副模样，拿石头和拳脚把它们打散。可一转眼，他又狂怒起来，被那群犬在耳边敲响的丧钟之声震得发聋，便撒腿逃窜、纵跃、飞翔起来，有如鹿的腿、鹰的翅膀。可他的种种挣扎终归徒劳；他跑也好、跳也好、几乎要飞起来也好，那催命的声音始终缠着他不放，纵使有一刻远去了些，下一刻便又听得它更近、更凶险。然而那求生的本能不曾抛弃他，他的力气也不曾衰减分毫；只不过，万一他再撞上别的接力犬群，这股力气怕就撑不住了。于是他决意行一着险棋，以便把狗群远远甩开，奔回自己的老窝去——那儿地形他熟，他指望能把猎犬甩脱。因此他第二次兜了个圈子。他先奔回皮瑟，接着从维维耶尔旁边绕过去，重又奔回贡比涅森林，一头扎进拉尔格森林，折回来在阿蒂希渡过埃纳河，末了终于回到了维莱-科特雷森林的阿尔让低地。他指望以此搅乱韦兹领主的部署——这位领主想必早已在各处要冲布下了犬队。

===Original===
Once back in his old quarters Thibault breathed more freely. He was now on the banks of the Ourcq between Norroy and Trouennes, where the river runs at the foot of deep rocks on either side; he leaped up on to a sharp-pointed crag overhanging the water, and from this high vantage ground he sprang into the waves below, then swam to a crevice at the base of the rock from which he had leapt, which was situated rather below the ordinary level of the water, and here, at the back of this cave, he waited. He had gained at least three miles upon the dogs; and yet, scarcely another ten minutes had elapsed, when the whole pack arrived and stormed the crest of the rock. Those who were leading, mad with excitement, did not see the gulf in front of them, or else, like their quarry they thought they would leap safely into it, for they plunged, and Thibault was splashed, far back as he was hidden, by the water that was scattered in every direction as they fell into it one by one. Less fortunate, however, and less vigorous than he was, they were unable to fight against the current, and after vainly battling with it, they were borne along out of sight before they had even got scent of the werewolf's retreat. Overhead he could hear the tramping of the horses' feet, the baying of the dogs that were still left, the cries of men, and above all these sounds, dominating the other voices, that of the Baron as he cursed and swore. When the last dog had fallen into the water, and been carried away like the others, he saw, thanks to a bend in the river, that the huntsmen were going down it, and persuaded that the Baron, whom he recognised at the head of his hunting-train, would only do this with the intention of coming up it again, he determined not to wait for this, and left his hiding-place. Now swimming, now leaping with agility from one rock to the other, at times wading through the water, he went up the river to the end of the Crêne coppice. Certain that he had now made a considerable advance on his enemies, he resolved to get to one of the villages near and run in and out among the houses, feeling sure that they would not think of coming after him there. He thought of Préciamont; if any village was well known to him, it was that; and then, at Préciamont, he would be near Agnelette. He felt that this neighbourhood would put fresh vigour into him, and would bring him good fortune, and that the gentle image of the innocent girl would have some influence on his fate. So he started off in that direction. It was now six o'clock in the evening; the hunt had lasted nearly fifteen hours, and wolf, dogs and huntsmen had covered fifty leagues at least. When, at last, after circling round by Manereux and Oigny, the black wolf reached the borders of the heath by the lane of Ham, the sun was already beginning to sink, and shedding a dazzling light over the flowery plain; the little white and pink flowers scented the breeze that played caressingly around them; the grasshopper was singing in its little house of moss, and the lark was soaring up towards heaven, saluting the eve with its song, as twelve hours before it had saluted the morn. The peaceful beauty of nature had a strange effect on Thibault. It seemed enigmatical to him that nature could be so smiling and beautiful, while anguish such as his was devouring his soul. He saw the flowers, and heard the insects and the birds, and he compared the quiet joy of this innocent world with the horrible pangs he was enduring, and asked himself, whether after all, notwithstanding all the new promises that had been made him by the devil's envoy, he had acted any more wisely in making this second compact than he had in making the first. He began to doubt whether he might not find himself deceived in the one as he had been in the other.

===Chinese===
一回到老地盘，蒂博便松了口气。这会儿他正待在诺鲁瓦与特吕埃纳之间的乌尔克河边，此处的河水夹在两岸高耸的岩壁之间奔流；他纵身跃上一块突出于水面之上的尖削岩石，从这高处纵身一跳，扎进了下头的波浪里，随后又游到他所跳下的那块岩壁底部的裂缝中——那裂缝恰好位于水面寻常水位之下一点，他便躲在这岩洞的最深处等着。他至少已把猎犬甩开了三英里；然而不过又过了十分钟，整群猎犬便已赶到，一窝蜂涌上了岩顶。跑在前头的那几条狗兴奋得发了狂，不是没瞧见眼前是一道深渊，便是和它们的猎物一样，以为自己也能安然跳将下去——它们一条接一条地栽了下来，蒂博虽藏身在最深处，也被那四下飞溅的水花溅了一身。不过它们到底没有他这般走运、没有他这般健壮，敌不过那湍急的水流，徒自挣扎了一阵，便一个个被冲得没了踪影——它们甚至还没嗅出狼人藏身之处的气味呢。头顶上方，他听得见马蹄声、剩下那几条狗的吠声、人们的呐喊声，而在这一切声响之上，压过所有嗓门的，是男爵咒骂与赌咒的声音。待到最后一条狗也跌进水里、像其余的狗一样被冲走，他借着河道一弯，瞧见猎手们正顺流而下，心知他认出来的那位策马走在队伍前头的男爵，此举无非是为了再从下游绕上来，便拿定主意不再坐等，离开了藏身之处。他一会儿游水，一会儿身手矫健地从一块岩石跳到另一块岩石，时而又蹚水前行，就这样沿河而上去，一直到了克雷恩树林的尽头。他确信这一来已把追兵甩下了一大截，便决意到附近的某个村庄去，在那些房屋之间穿来穿去地兜圈子，笃定他们想不到会追到那儿去找他。他想到了普雷西亚蒙；要说哪个村庄他最熟，非此地莫属；况且，到了普雷西亚蒙，他便离阿涅莱特近了。他觉得这邻近之地会为他注入新的气力，会给他带来好运，那无辜少女温柔的形象也会对他的命运有所影响。于是他便朝那个方向奔去。此时已是傍晚六点；这场围猎已持续了近十五个钟头，狼、狗与猎手们少说也跑足了五十里格的路。末了，黑狼绕过马讷勒与瓦尼，终于来到了哈姆小巷旁的荒原边上，此时太阳已开始西沉，将一片耀眼的光辉洒在繁花盛开的平原上；那小小的白花与粉花散发出阵阵幽香，弥漫在它们身边亲昵地嬉戏的微风里；蚱蜢在它那小小的苔藓小屋里歌唱着，云雀正展翅直上云霄，以歌声向黄昏致意，一如十二个钟头之前它曾以歌声向清晨致意。大自然这般宁静的美景，在蒂博身上生出了一种奇异的效果。他百思不得其解：自己这般撕心裂肺的苦痛正吞噬着他的灵魂，大自然却怎能如此笑容满面、如此明媚娇好。他望着那些花儿，听着那些虫鸣鸟啼，将这无辜世界恬静的欢欣与自己正承受的万般煎熬两相对照，扪心自问：归根结底，纵使魔鬼的使者又向他许下了一堆新的诺言，他这第二次立约，难道就比头一次立约来得更明智些么？他开始怀疑，自己在这第二份契约上，会不会也和头一份一样，落得个被人愚弄的下场。

===Original===
As he went along a little footpath nearly hidden under the golden broom, he suddenly remembered that it was by this very path that he had taken Agnelette home on the first day of their acquaintance; the day, when inspired by his good angel, he had asked her to be his wife. The thought that, thanks to this new compact, he might be able to recover Agnelette's love, revived his spirits, which had been saddened and depressed by the sight of the universal happiness around him. He heard the church bells at Préciamont ringing in the valley below; its solemn, monotonous tones recalled the thought of his fellow men to the black wolf, and of all he had to fear from them. So he ran boldly on, across the fields, to the village, where he hoped to find a refuge in some empty building. As he was skirting the little stone wall of the village cemetery, he heard a sound of voices, approaching along the road he was in. He could not fail to meet whoever they might be who were coming towards him, if he himself went on; it was not safe to turn back, as he would have to cross some rising ground whence he might easily be seen; so there was nothing left for it but to jump over the wall of the cemetery, and with a bound he was on the other side. This graveyard as usual adjoined the church; it was uncared for, and overgrown with tall grass, while brambles and thorns grew rankly in places. The wolf made for the thickest of these bramble bushes; he found a sort of ruined vault, whence he could look out without being seen, and he crept under the branches and hid himself inside. A few yards away from him was a newly-dug grave; within the church could be heard the chanting of priests, the more distinctly that the vault must at one time have communicated by a passage with the crypt. Presently the chanting ceased, and the black wolf, who did not feel quite at ease in the neighbourhood of a church, and thought that the road must now be clear, decided that it was time to start off again and to find a safer retreat than the one he had fled to in his haste.

===Chinese===
他沿着一条几乎被金黄金雀花掩没的小径走着，忽然想起：正是这条小径，他与阿涅莱特相识的头一天，他就是顺着它送她回家的；那一天，他在守护天使的感召之下，开口求她做自己的妻子。一想到多亏这新立的契约，他或许还能重新赢得阿涅莱特的爱，他那因目睹周遭人人幸福而黯然低落的精神便又重新振作起来。他听见普雷西亚蒙的教堂钟声在下方山谷里响着；那庄严而单调的音调，把这狼人重又唤回了对他同类——也就是他的同胞——的思念，以及他须得提防他们的种种。于是他放开胆子，穿过田野，直奔村庄而去，指望在那儿哪座空屋子里寻个藏身之处。他正沿着村里公墓那道矮石墙走，便听得一阵人声，正顺着他在的那条路走来。他自己若继续往前，必然要与迎面而来的人撞个正着；折回去又不稳妥，因为回头还得翻过一段高坡，在那儿极易被人瞧见；于是别无他法，唯有翻过公墓的墙——纵身一跃，便到了墙的另一边。这座坟场照例紧挨着教堂；它无人照料，长满了高高的荒草，有些地方荆棘与蒺藜长得密密匝匝。这狼朝那片最密的荆棘丛奔去；他找见了一个坍塌的墓穴，从里头可以往外张望却不致被人瞧见，便钻到枝桠底下，藏身其中。离他不几步远便是一座新挖的坟穴；教堂里头传出神父们诵经的吟唱声，听得格外真切，想必这墓穴从前曾有一条通道与教堂的地下墓室相通。不久吟唱声止息了，黑狼在教堂左近本就有些心神不宁，心想这会儿路上想必已清静下来，便拿定主意是时候再动身，另寻一处比他慌不择路逃进来的这儿更稳当的藏身之地了。

===Original===
But he had scarcely got his nose outside the bramble bush when the gate of the cemetery opened, and he quickly retreated again to his hole, in great trepidation as to who might now be approaching. The first person he saw was a child dressed in a white alb and carrying a vessel of holy water; he was followed by a man in a surplice, bearing a silver cross, and after the latter came a priest, chanting the psalms for the dead.

Behind these were four peasants carrying a bier covered with a white pall over which were scattered green branches and flowers, and beneath the sheet could be seen the outline of a coffin; a few villagers from Préciamont wound up this little procession. Although there was nothing unusual in such a sight as this, seeing that he was in a cemetery, and that the newly-dug grave must have prepared him for it, Thibault, nevertheless, felt strangely moved as he looked on. Although the slightest movement might betray his presence and bring destruction upon him, he anxiously watched every detail of the ceremony.

===Chinese===
可他才把鼻子探出荆棘丛，公墓的大门便开了，他赶紧又缩回洞里，心惊胆战地揣测这回来的又是些什么人。他瞧见的头一个人是个孩子，身着白色白袍，捧着一只圣水钵；其后跟着一个穿白罩衫的男子，擎着一架银十字架；再后头便是一位神父，正吟唱着亡者诗篇。

这几个人身后，是四个庄稼汉，抬着一副覆着白布的担架，白布上散着些青枝与花朵，布单底下能看出一具棺木的轮廓；普雷西亚蒙的几个村民缀在这小小的送葬队伍末尾。虽说身在公墓之中，又瞧见那座新挖的坟穴，本该对此早有准备，这样的场面原也不足为奇，可蒂博在一旁看着，心头竟涌起一阵奇异的激动。尽管他深知，自己稍有动静便会暴露行迹、招来杀身之祸，却还是目不转睛、忧心如焚地注视着这场仪式的每一个细节。

===Original===
The priest having blessed the newly-made grave, the peasants laid down their burden on an adjoining hillock. It is the custom in our country when a young girl, or young married woman, dies in the fullness of her youth and beauty, to carry her to the graveyard in an open coffin, with only a pall over her, so that her friends may bid her a last farewell, her relations give her a last kiss. Then the coffin is nailed down, and all is over. An old woman, led by some kind hand, for she was apparently blind, went up to the coffin to give the dead one a last kiss; the peasants lifted the pall from the still face, and there lay Agnelette. A low groan escaped from Thibault's agonised breast, and mingled with the tears and sobs of those present. Agnelette, as she lay there so pale in death, wrapped in an ineffable calm, appeared more beautiful than when in life, beneath her wreath of forget-me-nots and daisies. As Thibault looked upon the poor dead girl, his heart seemed suddenly to melt within him. It was he, as he had truly realised, who had really killed her, and he experienced a genuine and overpowering sorrow, the more poignant since for the first time for many long months he forgot to think of himself, and thought only of the dead woman, now lost to him forever.

===Chinese===
神父祝圣了那座新坟，庄稼汉们便把担架搁在旁边的一座小土丘上。在我们乡间有个风俗：但凡年轻姑娘或新婚少妇，正当青春美貌的大好年华便香消玉殒，人们便用敞着盖的棺木将她抬往坟场，只覆上一层尸布，好让她的朋友能与之作最后的诀别，让她的亲人能给她最后一个吻。之后棺木钉上钉，一切便算尘埃落定。一位老妇人，由一只慈善的手牵着——她瞧着分明是个瞎子——走上前去，要给死者最后的一吻；庄稼汉们揭开那纹丝不动的脸上的白布——躺在那里的，竟是阿涅莱特。蒂博那苦痛难当的胸膛里，不觉迸出一声低沉的哀号，融进了在场众人的泪水与啜泣之中。阿涅莱特那般苍白地长眠着，笼罩在一层言语难描的宁静之中，倒显得比生前更加美丽——她头戴着勿忘我与雏菊编成的花冠。蒂博望着这可怜的死者，心里仿佛忽然有什么东西一下子化开了。是他——这一点他心知肚明——真正地害死了她；他感到了一阵真切而不可抑止的悲恸，这悲恸格外锥心，因为这许多漫长的月份以来，他头一遭忘了挂念自己，满心只想的便是这从此与他永诀的亡女。

===Original===
As he heard the blows of the hammer knocking the nails into the coffin, as he heard the earth and stones being shovelled into the grave and falling with a dull thud on to the body of the only woman he had ever loved, a feeling of giddiness came over him. The hard stones he thought must be bruising Agnelette's tender flesh, so fresh and sweet but a few days ago, and only yesterday still throbbing with life, and he made a movement as if to rush out on the assailants and snatch away the body, which dead, must surely belong to him, since, living, it had belonged to another.

But the grief of the man overcame this instinct of the wild beast at bay; a shudder passed through the body hidden beneath its wolf skin; tears fell from the fierce bloodred eyes, and the unhappy man cried out: "O God! take my life, I give it gladly, if only by my death I may give back life to her whom I have killed!"

The words were followed by such an appalling howl, that all who were in the cemetery fled, and the place was left utterly deserted. Almost at the same moment, the hounds, having recovered the scent, came leaping in over the wall, followed by the Baron, streaming with sweat as he rode his horse, which was covered with foam and blood.

The dogs made straight for the bramble bush, and began worrying something hidden there.

"Halloo! halloo!! halloo!!!" cried the Lord of Vez, in a voice of thunder, as he leapt from his horse, not caring if there was anyone or not to look after it, and drawing out his hunting-knife, he dashed towards the vault, forcing his way through the hounds. He found them fighting over a fresh and bleeding wolf-skin, but the body had disappeared.

There was no mistake as to its being the skin of the werewolf that they had been hunting, for with the exception of one white hair, it was entirely black.

What had become of the body? No one ever knew. Only as from this time forth Thibault was never seen again, it was generally believed that the former sabot-maker and no other was the werewolf.

Furthermore, as the skin had been found without the body, and, as, from the spot where it was found a peasant reported to have heard someone speak the words: "O God! take my life! I give it gladly, if only by my death I may give back life to her whom I have killed," the priest declared openly that Thibault, by reason of his sacrifice and repentance, had been saved!

And what added to the consistency of belief in this tradition was, that every year on the anniversary of Agnelette's death, up to the time when the Monasteries were all abolished at the Revolution, a monk from the Abbey of the Premonstratensians at Bourg-Fontaine, which stands half a league from Préciamont, was seen to come and pray beside her grave.

---

Such is the history of the black wolf, as it was told me by old Mocquet, my father's keeper.

===Chinese===
他听见锤子一下一下把钉子敲进棺木，听见一锹锹的泥土与石块被铲进坟穴、闷声闷气地砸在他此生唯一爱过的女人身上，一阵天旋地转的晕眩袭上心头。他想，那硬邦邦的石头砸下去，定要把阿涅莱特那娇嫩的皮肉磕得青紫——那皮肉不过几天之前还那样鲜嫩甘美，就在昨天还在鲜活地搏动着——他身子一动，仿佛要冲出去扑向那些施害的人，把那遗体夺回来；这遗体既已死了，理当属于他，因为活着的时候，它是属于别人的。

然而，为人的悲恸终究压过了这困兽犹搏的野兽本能；那藏身于狼皮之下的身躯猛地一颤；泪水从那双凶狠的血红眼睛里滚落下来，这不幸的人高声喊道："噢，上帝啊！拿走我的命吧，我心甘情愿地奉上，只要我的死能换回我所害死的她的一条命！"

话音落处，紧随而来的是一声惨绝人寰的嚎叫，直叫得坟场里所有的人四散奔逃，那地方霎时变得空空荡荡、阒无一人。几乎就在同一刻，那群猎犬重又嗅着了气味，翻过墙头纵身跳了进来，其后紧跟着男爵——他骑在马上，浑身大汗淋漓，那匹马则浑身是沫、遍体血污。

群犬直扑那丛荆棘，开始撕咬着藏在里头的什么东西。

"嗬！嗬！！嗬！！！"韦兹领主如雷般地吼道，他翻身跳下马——也顾不上有没有人替他照看坐骑——抽出猎刀，朝着那墓穴冲了过去，拨开群犬挤了进去。他只见它们正为一张鲜血淋漓、尚是新鲜的狼皮争抢撕扯，可那躯身却不翼而飞了。

这确确实实是他们一路追猎的那头狼人的皮，无可置疑——因为除了一根白毛之外，通体乌黑。

那躯身究竟怎么了？谁也无从知晓。只不过，自打这以后蒂博便再也没人见过，于是大伙儿便都认定：当初那个木屐匠不是别人，正是这狼人。

再者，既然这皮是没了躯身寻获的，又既然有庄稼汉报称，在那皮被发现之处听见有人说了这么一句话："噢，上帝啊！拿走我的命吧！我心甘情愿地奉上，只要我的死能换回我所害死的她的一条命"——神父便公开宣告：蒂博既已作了那样的牺牲与忏悔，他的灵魂已经得救了！

而更叫人坚信这一传闻的，还有一桩事：此后年年阿涅莱特的忌日，直到大革命时期所有修道院尽被废止之前，总有人瞧见布尔丰泉的普雷蒙特雷修会修道院——那修道院离普雷西亚蒙不过半里格路——派出的一位修士前来，在她的坟旁祷告。

---

这便是那头黑狼的故事，是我父亲的看林人老莫凯讲给我听的。

## X Maître Magloire / X 马格卢瓦师傅

===Original===
In this reckless state of mind Thibault, who had not as yet decided on any special course of action, spent the last days of the old year and the first of the new. Still, remembering the heavy expenses entailed on each and all by New Year’s Day, he had exacted double rations from his usual purveyor’s, as the trying time drew nearer and nearer, simultaneously, drawing double profits from the landlord of the Boule-d’Or.

Thus it came about that, apart from the disquieting fact that his mesh of red hair was getting larger and larger almost every day, Thibault entered upon the New Year in a better condition as to material matters than he had ever known before. Observe, I say, as to *material* matters, and material matters only; for albeit the body might seem in good plight, the soul was already alarmingly compromised. The body, at any rate was well clothed, and ten crowns or more made a merry jingling in his waistcoat pocket; and so dressed, and so accompanied by this silvery music, Thibault no longer appeared like a wooden shoemaker’s apprentice, but like some well-to-do farmer, or even a comfortable citizen, carrying on a trade maybe, but simply for his own pleasure. Looking such as he now did, Thibault went to one of those village functions, which are fête-days for the whole province. The magnificent ponds of Berval and Poudron were to be drawn. Now the drawing of a pond is a grand affair for the owner, or for the one who farms it, not to mention the great pleasure it affords to the spectators. Such an event therefore is advertised a month in advance, and people come from thirty miles round to enjoy this fine entertainment. And to those of my readers who are not accustomed to the manners and customs of the provinces, let me explain that the fishing which takes place is not a fishing with the line, baited with worms, or scented wheat, or with the cast-net, or the sweep-net, nothing of the kind; this fishing consists in emptying a pond, sometimes nearly a mile, or even three miles long, of every fish from the largest pike to the smallest minnow. This is how the thing is managed. In all probability, not a single one of my readers has ever seen the kind of pond to which I refer. I will describe it; to begin with, it always has two issues, that by which the water flows in, that by which the water flows out; that by which the water enters has no particular name, that by which it is let out is called the sluice. The water as it leaves the sluice falls into a large reservoir whence it escapes through the meshes of a strong net; the water flows away, but the fish remain. Everyone knows that it takes several days to empty a pond, therefore those who wish to take a share in the fishing, and the onlookers, are not summoned to attend before the second, third, or fourth day, according to the volume of water which the pond has to disgorge before it is ready for the final act, and this takes place as soon as the fish appear at the sluice.

===Chinese===
蒂博（Thibault）便在这种天不怕地不怕的心境里，送走了旧年的最后几天，又迎来了新年的头几天，他至今尚未拿定任何一条具体的行止。不过，他记得元旦这天人人都免不了要大破费一笔，于是随着这难熬的关头一天天逼近，他先是从平日采买的店家那里榨来了双份的口粮，与此同时，又从金球客栈（the Boule-d’Or）的老板那里攫取了双倍的进项。

因此，且不说他那绺红发几乎一天比一天越长越大这桩叫人忧心的事，蒂博步入新年之时，论起身外之物，境况竟比从前哪一年都好。请诸位注意，我说的是论起*身外*之物，而且单指身外之物；因为身子骨看着虽还光鲜，那灵魂却早已败坏到了令人心惊的地步。至少，这身子的衣着是体面的，马甲口袋里有十来枚埃居叮当作响，煞是悦耳；蒂博这副打扮，又有这银铃般的乐声相伴，便再不像是哪个做木屐的鞋匠学徒，倒像个殷实的庄稼汉，甚至像个体面的市民，纵使也操持一桩营生，也不过是为着自家消遣罢了。蒂博便以如今这副模样，去赶一场村里的盛会——这种场面，是全省一同欢度的节日。贝尔瓦尔（Berval）和普德朗（Poudron）那两口气派非凡的大塘，要开塘起鱼了。须知开塘起鱼，对塘主或承包塘面的人来说，乃是一桩了不得的大事，更不必说它给看客们带来的那一份大乐趣了。这样的盛事因此往往提前一个月便四下张榜，方圆三十英里的人都赶来同享这桩好消遣。对那些不谙外省风土人情的读者，容我说明一句：这里所说的捉鱼，可不是拿钓竿钓——不论饵上挂的是蚯蚓还是喷了香味的麦粒——也不是撒旋网、拖曳网，全不是那么回事；这种捉鱼，是要把一口塘——有时将近一英里长，甚至有三英里长的——里头大大小小的鱼，从最大的狗鱼到最小的鲦鱼，统统掏个干净。事情是这么个办法的。十之八九，我的读者里没有一个人见过我所说的这种池塘。我来描摹一番：头一样，它总有上下两个口子，一个是水流入的口子，一个是水流出的口子；水流入的那个口子并无特别的名称，水放出去的那个口子，叫做水闸。水一出闸，便落进一个大蓄水池，再从一张结实大网的网眼里漏出去；水流走了，鱼却留了下来。谁都知道，要放干一口塘总得花上好几天，因此那些想参与起鱼的人和看客们，并不在第二天、第三天乃至第四天之前被传唤到场——具体是第几天，要看这口塘在最后一幕上演之前，还得吐出多少水，而这一幕，是等鱼一在水闸露面便立即开始的。

===Original===
At the hour announced for the fishing, a crowd assembles, varying in number according to the size and the celebrity of the pond, but comparatively as large and as fashionable as that to be seen at the Champ de Mars or Chantilly on race days when favourite horses are to run and favourite jockeys to ride. Only here the spectators do not look on from grand stands and carriages; on the contrary, they come as they can, or as they like, in gigs, pleasure vans, phaëtons, carts, on horseback, on donkey-back, but once on the spot everyone rushes to find a place, stationing him or herself either in order of arrival, or according to the amount of elbowing and pushing of which each is capable, always, however, with that due respect for authority which is observed even in the least civilised districts. A sort of stout trellis-work, however, firmly sunk into the ground, prevents the onlookers from falling into the reservoir.

===Chinese===
到了宣布起鱼的时辰，人群便聚拢来，人数多少视这口塘的大小和名气而定，可那份热闹和讲究劲儿，比起战神广场（Champ de Mars）或香榭丽舍（Chantilly）赛马日上名马要跑、名骑师要骑时的光景，也不遑多让。只不过这里的看客并不坐在大看台和马车里观瞧；恰恰相反，他们各显其能、各随其便地赶来——坐双轮轻便马车的，坐游玩篷车的，坐四轮敞篷马车的，坐大板车的，骑马的，骑驴的——可一到地方，人人都争着抢个位置，或按先来后到，或看各人胳膊肘顶撞推搡的能耐大小，自寻落脚之处，不过，纵在最不开化的地方也有的那份对官长的敬畏，在这里始终不曾或缺。倒是有一道结实的木栅栏，牢牢地埋在地里，挡住了看客，不叫他们栽进蓄水池去。

===Original===
The colour and the smell of the water betoken the arrival of the fish. Every kind of show has its drawbacks: the larger and grander the audience at the opera house, the more carbonic acid is there to draw into the lungs; at the drawing of a pond, the nearer the supreme moment approaches, the more marsh-gas is there to inhale.

When the sluice is first opened, the water that pours through is beautifully clear, and slightly green in colour, like the water of a brook; this is the upper layer, which, carried along by its weight, is the first to appear. By degrees the water becomes less transparent, and takes on a greyish hue; this is the second layer, emptying itself in turn, and every now and then, more frequently as the water becomes muddier, a ray of silver is seen to dart through it; it is some fish, too small and weak to resist the current, which flashes past as if acting as scout for its stronger brethren. Nobody troubles to pick it up; it is allowed to lie gasping, and trying to find some little stagnant puddle of water at the bottom of the pond, flapping, floundering and capering like an acrobat going through his antics.

Then the black water comes pouring through; this is the last act, the final catastrophe.

===Chinese===
水的颜色和气味，宣告着鱼群将至。凡是看热闹的场合都有它的缺憾：歌剧院里的听众越多越大场面，吸进肺里的碳酸气也就越多；开塘起鱼，至高无上的时刻越逼近，要吸进肺里的沼气也就越多。

水闸刚打开时，倾泻而出的水清亮极了，带着一点淡绿，如同溪水一般；这是最上面的一层，被自身的重量裹挟着，头一个涌了出来。渐渐地，水不那么透亮了，泛起一种灰蒙蒙的色调；这是第二层水，轮到它往外排了，这会儿时不时地——水越浑，这情形便越频繁——只见一道银光在里头一闪而过；那是一条鱼，小而弱，抵挡不住水流，被冲了过去，活像是给那些强壮的同族打前站的探子。谁也懒得去捡它；任由它喘着气躺在那儿，拼命在塘底找那么一汪死水洼，扑腾着、翻滚着、蹦跶着，活像一个耍把式的杂技演员在走他的套子。

接着，黑水倾泻而出；这便是最后一幕，最终的浩劫。

===Original===
Each fish, according to its power of resistance, struggles against the current which is bearing it along in this unusual manner. Instinctively they feel there is danger, and each strives its hardest to swim in an opposite direction; the pike struggles beside the carp which it was yesterday pursuing so hard; the perch is reconciled to the tench, and as they swim side by side, does not so much as think of taking a bite out of the flesh he finds so palatable at other times. So the Arabs at times find huddled together in the pits they dig to catch game, gazelles and jackals, antelopes and hyenas, the jackals and hyenas having grown as gentle and as timid as the gazelles and antelopes.

But the strength of the struggling and dying fish begins at last to fail. The scouts that we noticed a few minutes ago become more numerous; the size of the fish becomes more respectable, which is proved to them by the attention they receive from the pickers-up. These pickers-up are men, clad in plain linen trousers and cotton shirts; the trousers are rolled up to above the knee, and the shirt sleeves turned up to the shoulders. The fish are gathered up in baskets; those destined to be sold alive, or kept for restocking the pond, are poured off into tanks; those condemned to death are simply spread out on the grass, and will be sold before the day is out. As the fish grow more and more abundant the cries of delight from the spectators become louder and more frequent; for these onlookers are not like the audiences in our theatres; they have no idea of stifling their feelings, or showing good taste by appearing indifferent. No, they come to amuse themselves, and every fine tench, or fine carp, or fine pike, calls forth loud, undisguised and delighted applause. As in a well-ordered review, the troops file past in order, according to their weight, if we may use the expression, first the light sharpshooters, then the somewhat heavier dragoons, and finally the ponderous cuirassiers and heavy artillery to bring up the rear, so the fish sweep by according to their several species; the smallest, that is the weakest, first, the heaviest, that is the strongest, last.

===Chinese===
每一条鱼，各凭自己抵抗的本事，与这股以如此反常的方式裹挟它们而去的水流抗争。它们本能地觉察到危险，各自拼命朝相反的方向游；狗鱼和它昨天还穷追不舍的鲤鱼并排挣扎着；河鲈与丁鲷握手言和，并肩游过时，压根儿想不到去咬一口它在别的时候觉得那样可口的那块肉。正如阿拉伯人有时会在他们为猎兽而挖的陷坑里发现，瞪羚和豺、羚羊和鬣狗挤作一团，那些豺和鬣狗，竟也变得和瞪羚、羚羊一般温顺胆怯。

然而，那些挣扎着、奄奄一息的鱼，力气到底开始不济了。方才我们留意到的那些探子越来越多；鱼的个头也越发可观了——捡鱼人对它们那份殷勤便是明证。这些捡鱼人都是汉子，下身一条素麻布裤子，上身一件棉布衬衫；裤腿卷到膝盖以上，袖子挽到肩头。鱼用筐子收拢起来；那些定了要活卖、或留着补塘的，倒进盛水的木槽里；那些定了死罪的，便往草地上一摊，赶不得天黑就要卖掉。鱼越来越多，看客们的欢呼声也越来越响、越来越密；因为这些看客可不似我们戏园子里的看客；他们全然不懂得压抑自己的心绪，更不懂得以装作淡漠来显出好品味。不，他们是来找乐子的，每一条肥硕的丁鲷，每一条肥硕的鲤鱼，每一条肥硕的狗鱼，都要博得一片响亮、毫不掩饰、喜形于色的喝彩。好比一场井然有序的阅兵，队伍按各自的分量鱼贯而过——姑且这么说罢——先是轻装的散兵，接着是稍重一些的龙骑兵，末了压阵的是沉重的胸甲骑兵和重炮；鱼群也依着各自的种类依次冲过；最小的，亦即最弱的，打头阵，最重的，亦即最强的，压后队。

===Original===
At last the moment comes when the water ceases to flow; the passage is literally obstructed by the remainder of the fish, the bigwigs of the pond, and the pickers-up have veritable monsters to fight with. This is the supreme moment. Now comes the climax of applause, the last vociferous bravos. Then, the play being over, everyone goes to examine the actors; the latter are mostly lying gasping to death on the grass of the field, while a certain number are recovering themselves in the water. You look about for the eels; where are the eels you ask? Then three or four eels, about as big round as your thumb and half the length of your arm, are pointed out to you; for the eels, thanks to their peculiar organization, have momentarily at least, escaped the general carnage. The eels have taken a header into the mud and disappeared; and this is the reason why you may see men with guns walking up and down at the edge of the pond, and hear a report from time to time. If you ask the reason for this shooting, you will be told that it is to bring the eels out of their hiding-places. But why do eels come out of the mud when they hear the report of a gun? Why do they make for the water which still runs in little rivulets at the bottom of the pond? Why, in short, being safe at the bottom of the mud, like other good friends of our acquaintance who have the good sense to remain there, do the eels not stay there, instead of wriggling back into a stream of water, which carries them along with it, and finally lands them in the reservoir, that is in the common grave? The Collège de France would find nothing easier than to answer this question, under existing circumstances; so I put this question to its learned members: Is not the idea of the gun a pure superstition, and is not the following solution the right and simple one? The mud in which the eel takes refuge is at first liquid, but gradually becomes drier and drier, like a sponge when squeezed, and so becomes more and more uninhabitable for it, and so, in the long run, it is obliged to get back to its natural element﻿—the water. The water once reached, the eel is lost; but it is not till the fifth or sixth day after the emptying of the pond, that the eels are caught.

===Chinese===
终于到了水不再流的那一刻；水道简直被剩下的鱼——那些塘中的头面人物——堵得水泄不通，捡鱼人便当真要和怪物们搏斗一番了。这便是至高无上的时刻。这时，掌声到了顶点，最后一片声的叫好。随后，戏散了场，人人都去围观那些演员；后者大多奄奄一息地喘着气，躺在场地的草地上，另有少数正在水里缓过劲来。你四下里找鳗鱼；你问，鳗鱼在哪儿呢？于是有人指给你看三四条鳗鱼，粗细约莫跟你拇指差不多，长度有你半条胳膊；原来这些鳗鱼，多亏它们那奇特的体格，至少暂时逃过了这场大屠杀。鳗鱼一头扎进淤泥里，便不见了踪影；这便是你为什么会看见一些手持猎枪的人在塘边走来走去，并不时听见一声枪响的缘故。你若问起这放枪的缘由，人家会告诉你，那是为了把鳗鱼从藏身之处轰出来。可是，鳗鱼为什么一听见枪响便会从淤泥里出来呢？为什么它们要奔向塘底仍如涓涓细流般淌着的那点儿水呢？简而言之，鳗鱼明明在淤泥底下安安稳稳——如同我们相识的另一些好朋友，很有见地地待在原地不动——为什么偏不肯待着，反倒要扭动着钻回一股水流里，被水裹挟而去，末了落进蓄水池，也就是那共同的坟场里呢？在眼下这种情形，法兰西公学院（Collège de France）要回答这个问题，再没有比这更容易的了；那么我把这个问题摆到那些博学的院士们面前：放枪这一招，难道不只是一种纯粹的迷信，而下面这个解答，岂不才是又对又简单的？鳗鱼藏身的那层淤泥，起初是稀的，可渐渐地越来越干，好比一块被拧过的海绵，对它来说越来越不宜栖身，这么一来，日久天长，它就不得不回到它本来的元素里去——水。一旦回到水里，鳗鱼便在劫难逃；不过，要等塘放干了第五天或第六天之后，鳗鱼才被捕上来。

===Original===
It was to a fête of this kind that everyone at Villers-Cotterets, at Crespy, at Mont-Gobert, and in the surrounding villages had been invited. Thibault went like everybody else; he had now no need to work, finding it simpler to allow the wolves to work for him. From a workman he had risen to be a man at ease, it now only remained to make himself a gentleman, and Thibault counted upon being able to do this. He was not a man to allow himself to remain in the rear, and he therefore made good use of his arms and legs so as to secure a place in the front row. In the course of this manoeuvre he happened to rumple the dress of a tall, fine woman, next to whom he was trying to install himself. The lady was fond of her clothes, and no doubt, also, she was in the habit of commanding, which naturally produces an attitude of disdain, for, turning to see who had brushed past her, she let fall the uncompromising word, “lout!” Notwithstanding the rudeness of the remark, the mouth that uttered the words was so beautiful, the lady so pretty, and her momentary anger in such ugly contrast to the charming expression of her face, that Thibault, instead of retorting in similar, or even more objectionable style, only drew back, stammering some sort of excuse.

===Chinese===
维莱-科特雷（Villers-Cotterets）、克雷斯皮（Crespy）、蒙-戈贝尔（Mont-Gobert）以及周围各村庄的人，受的就是这样一场盛会的邀请。蒂博和别人一样去了；他如今已不必再做活，觉得让群狼替他卖力要省事得多。从一个做活的匠人，他已爬到了一个清闲之人的地步，眼下剩下的，便是把自个儿弄成一位老爷了，蒂博满心指望着这事也能办成。他可不是肯落在人后的人，于是把两条胳膊两条腿使得十分灵光，好抢到前排一个位置。在这番挤挪之中，他偏偏揉皱了身边一位高挑俊俏妇人的衣裳——他正想在她旁边安顿下自个儿。这位夫人爱惜她的衣裳是无疑的，再者，她想必也惯于发号施令，这自然养出一种颐指气使的神气，因为她回过头来看是谁擦了她，便甩出了那句不留情面的话：“粗坯！”这话虽粗鲁，吐出这话的那张嘴却美极了，这位夫人也漂亮极了，她这一时的恼怒，与她脸上那副迷人的神情形成了极不谐调的对照，蒂博非但没有以同样难听、甚至更加不堪的话回敬，反倒直往后缩，结结巴巴地赔了几句不是。

===Original===
There is no need to remind the reader that of all aristocracies, beauty is still the chief. If the woman had been old and ugly, she might have been a Marquise, but Thibault would certainly have called her by some opprobrious title. It is possible also that Thibault’s ideas were somewhat distracted by the strange appearance of the man who served as knight to this lady, He was a stout man of about sixty years of age, dressed entirely in black, and of a perfectly dazzling exactness of toilet; but therewith, so extremely short, that his head scarcely reached the lady’s elbow, and as she would have been unable to take his arm, without positive torture to herself, she was content to lean majestically upon his shoulder. Seeing them thus together, one might have taken her for an ancient Cybele leaning on one of those grotesque little modern figures of Chinese idols. And what a fascinating idol it was with those short legs, that bulgy stomach, those little fat podgy arms, those white hands under the lace ruffles, that plump, rubicund head and face, that well-combed, well-powdered, well-curled head of hair, and that tiny pigtail, which with every movement of its wearer’s, went bobbing up and down with its neat bow of ribbon against the coat collar. It reminded one of those black beetles of which the legs seem so little in harmony with the body, that the insects seem rather to roll than to walk. And with it all, the face was so jovial, the little eyes level with the forehead, were so full of kindness, that one felt involuntarily drawn towards him; one could be sure that the pleasant little man was too intent on giving himself a good time, by every means in his power, to think of quarrelling with that vague and indefinite person known as one’s neighbour. Wherefore, on hearing his companion speak so cavalierly to Thibault, the good fat little man appeared to be in despair.

===Chinese===
不必提醒读者诸君，在一切门第的高贵之中，美貌仍是头一等的高贵。倘若这妇人是又老又丑的，那么她便是个侯爵夫人，蒂博也定要骂她一句什么难听的称呼。再者，蒂博的心思，说不定也被充当这位夫人护花使者的那个男人的怪模样岔开了几分。那是个约莫六十岁的胖子，通体着黑，衣饰之考究齐整，简直叫人眼花缭乱；可同时，他个子又极矮，脑袋勉强够得到那位夫人的胳膊肘，她若要挽他的胳膊，不啻是活受罪，便也知足地威仪凛凛地把身子倚在他肩头上。瞧他俩这般并排走着，倒叫人把她看作一尊古代的众神之母西布莉（Cybele），倚靠在一尊近代中国偶像那种怪模怪样的小人像上。而这又是何等迷人的一尊偶像啊——那两条短腿，那个鼓鼓囊囊的肚子，那两条胖乎乎圆滚滚的小胳膊，那双在花边褶裥下露出来的白手，那个丰润红润的脑袋和面庞，那梳得一丝不乱、扑得喷香、卷得齐整的一头假发，还有那根细小的辫子，随着主人的每一个动作，系着整洁缎带蝴蝶结的辫子一翘一翘地在上衣领子上蹦跳。这倒叫人想起那种黑色的甲虫，腿和身子看上去是那样不相称，这虫子与其说在走，倒不如说在滚。纵然如此，那张脸却是那样和气，那双与额头齐平的小眼睛里盛满了善意，叫人情不自禁地想亲近他；你尽可以放心，这位讨人喜欢的小个子一心只想用尽法子让自个儿舒坦快活，断断不会去和那个含糊暧昧、被称作"旁人"的家伙过不去。正因如此，听得同伴这般倨傲地对待蒂博，这好心肠的胖小个一脸的过意不去。

===Original===
“Gently, Madame Magloire! gently, Madame Bailiff!” he said, contriving in these few words to let his neighbours know what and who he was; “gently! those were ugly words to use to the poor fellow, who is more sorry than you are for the accident.”

“And may I ask, Monsieur Magloire,” replied the lady, “if I am not at liberty to thank him for so nicely crumpling my beautiful blue damask dress, which is now entirely spoilt, not taking into consideration that he also trod on my little toe?”

“I beg you, Madame, to pardon my clumsiness,” replied Thibault; “when you turned your face towards me, its wonderful beauty dazzled me like a ray of May sunshine, so that I could not see where I was treading.”

===Chinese===
“轻着点儿，马格卢瓦太太（Madame Magloire）！轻着点儿，执达吏太太（Madame Bailiff）！”他说道，就这么三言两语，便让左右邻里都晓得了他是何许人、做何营生；“轻着点儿！对这可怜虫说这种话可不好听，这场祸事他比你还懊恼呢。”

“那么请问，马格卢瓦先生（Monsieur Magloire），”那位夫人答道，“他把我会那身漂亮的蓝锦缎裙子揉得这般齐整，如今全毁了，我难道连谢他一声都不许吗？更何况他还踩了我的小脚趾头，这且不算在内呢。”

“夫人，恳请您原谅我的莽撞，”蒂博答道，“您把脸转向我时，您那惊人的美貌，如同一道五月的阳光，晃得我睁不开眼，我便瞧不见脚下踩到哪儿去了。”

===Original===
It was not a badly turned compliment for a man who for three months past had been in the daily society of a pack of wolves; nevertheless it did not produce any great effect upon the lady, who only responded with a haughty little pouting of the mouth. The truth was, that in spite of Thibault being so decently dressed, she had, with the curious insight which women of all ranks possess in these matters, detected at once to what class he belonged.

Her stout little companion however, was more indulgent, for he clapped loudly with his podgy hands, which the pose adopted by his wife left him free to use as he liked.

“Ah! bravo, bravo!” he said, “you have hit the mark, Monsieur; you are a clever young fellow, and seem to have studied the style to address women in. My love, I hope you appreciated the compliment as I did, and to prove to this gentlemen that we are good Christians and bear no ill-will towards him, he will, I hope, if he is living in this neighbourhood and it would not be too far out of his way, accompany us home, and we will drink a bottle of old wine together, if Perrine will get one out for us from the back of the wood shed.”

===Chinese===
对一个三个月来日日与一群狼为伍的人来说，这恭维话说得倒也不算太蹩脚；可它在那位夫人身上却没起什么大作用，她只傲慢地撇了撇嘴作为回答。实则，尽管蒂博穿戴得如此体面，她却凭着各色妇人在这方面一概具有的那份敏锐眼力，一眼就看穿了他出身于哪一等阶层。

然而，她那位胖小个同伴却宽厚得多，因为他那双胖乎乎的手呱呱地鼓起掌来——他太太所摆的那副姿态，倒腾出了这双手，任他随意使用。

“啊！好哇，好哇！”他说，“您说到了点子上，先生；您是个机灵的年轻人，看样子是研究过对妇人说话的辞令的。我的心肝，但愿您和我一样领了这份恭维的情，为着向这位先生证明我们是好基督徒、对他并无恶意，我盼着——如果他住在这左近、顺路不至于太绕远的话——他能陪我们回家去，咱们一道喝上一瓶陈年老酒，只要佩琳（Perrine）肯替咱们从柴棚后头拿一瓶出来。”

===Original===
“Ah! there I know you, Master Népomucène; any excuse serves you to be clinking glasses with somebody, and when no genuine occasion offers, you are very clever at ferreting out one, it does not matter where. But you know, Monsieur Magloire, that the doctor has expressly forbidden you to drink between meals.”

“True, Madame Bailiff, true,” replied her husband, “but he did not forbid me to show politeness to an agreeable young fellow such as Monsieur appears to me to be. Be lenient, I pray, Suzanne; give up this surly manner, which suits you so ill. Why, Madame, those who do not know you, would think, to hear you, that we had nearly got to quarrelling over a gown. However, to prove the contrary to Monsieur, I promise that if you can get him to go back with us, I will, the very minute we get home, give you the money to buy that figured silk dress, which you have been wishing for so long.”

===Chinese===
“啊！内波米塞纳师傅（Master Népomucène），我算把你看透了；不论什么由头都够你和什么人碰上几杯，万一连个像样的由头也寻不着，你又能耐，不知打哪儿也要挖出一个来。可您是知道的，马格卢瓦先生，大夫明令不许您在两顿饭之间喝酒的。”

“不错，执达吏太太，不错，”她丈夫答道，“可他并没有不许我对一位可心的年轻人表示礼数——这位先生在我看来便是这样一位。求您宽和些吧，苏珊（Suzanne）；收起这副凶巴巴的样子吧，它跟您这样不相称。哎呀，夫人，那些不知您为人的人，听您这么一说，还当我们险些为一件袍子吵起来了呢。也罢，为了向这位先生证明并非如此，我答应您：只要您能请得动他同我们回去，咱们一到家，我立马便把钱给您，让您去买那身您盼了这许久的提花绸裙子。”

===Original===
The effect of this promise was like magic. Madame Magloire was instantly mollified, and as the fishing was now drawing to a close, she accepted with less ungraciousness the arm which Thibault, somewhat awkwardly we must confess, now offered her.

As to Thibault himself, struck with the beauty of the lady, and gathering from words which had fallen from her and her husband, that she was the wife of a magistrate, he parted the crowd before him with an air of command, holding his head high the while, and making his way with as much determination as if he were starting on the conquest of the Golden Fleece.

And in truth, Thibault, the bridegroom elect of Agnelette, the lover who had been so ignominiously expelled her house by the mistress of the mill, was thinking not only of all the pleasure he could enjoy, but of the proud position he would hold as the beloved of a bailiff’s wife, and of all the advantages to be drawn from the good fortune which had so unexpectedly befallen him, and which he had so long desired.

===Chinese===
这一应允的效验，简直如有神助。马格卢瓦太太当即软了下来，起鱼这会儿也已近尾声，她便不那么刁蛮地接受了蒂博——我们不得不说，多少有些笨拙地——此时伸过来的胳膊。

至于蒂博自己，他为这位夫人的美貌所倾倒，又从她和她丈夫吐露的话里听出她是一位法官太太，便一副发号施令的神气拨开身前的人群，高高昂着头，一往无前地走着，那股子劲头，仿佛他是去夺取金羊毛的。

说实在的，蒂博——阿涅莱特（Agnelette）那曾被许下的新郎，那位被磨坊主母那么不体面地赶出门去的情人——心里盘算的，不仅是他能享到的那种种乐趣，还有他身为一位执达吏太太的意中人将身居的那种体面地位，以及从这天上掉下来的、他又盼望了这许久的好运里，可以榨取的种种好处。

===Original===
As on her side also, Madame Magloire was not only very much preoccupied with her own thoughts, but also paid very little attention to him, looking to right and left, first in front of her and then behind, as if in search of someone, the conversation would have lagged terribly as they walked along if their excellent little companion had not been at the expense of the best part of it, as he jogged along now beside Thibault and now beside Suzanne, waddling like a duck jogging home after a big feed.

And so with Thibault engaged in his calculations, and the Bailiff’s wife with her dreams, the Bailiff trotting beside them talking and wiping his forehead with a fine cambric pocket-handkerchief, they arrived at the village of Erneville, which is situated about a mile and a half from the Poudron ponds. It was here, in this charming village, which lies halfway between Haramont and Bonneuil, within a stone’s throw or two of the Castle of Vez, the dwelling of my lord the Baron, that Monsieur Magloire sat as magistrate.

===Chinese===
而马格卢瓦太太这边呢，她不仅一心想着自己的心事，对他也全然没几分留意，左顾右盼，时而看前面，时而望身后，仿佛在找什么人，他们这一路走着，谈话本会冷场得可怕，幸而有他们那位出色的小个同伴，独自扛下了这场谈话的大半费用——他一会儿颠到蒂博身旁，一会儿颠到苏珊身旁，摇摇摆摆地走着，活像一只饱餐一顿后颠着回家的鸭子。

就这样，蒂博沉浸在他的盘算里，执达吏太太沉浸在她的美梦里，那位执达吏在他们身边一路小跑，一边说着话，一边拿一方细白麻纱手帕抹着脑门——他们一行人便到了埃讷维尔（Erneville）村，这村子坐落在离普德朗塘约莫一英里半的地方。便是在这里，在这座宜人的村子里——它位于阿拉蒙（Haramont）与博纳伊（Bonneuil）之间，离男爵（the Baron）老爷的居所韦兹城堡（the Castle of Vez）不过一两个投石之遥——马格卢瓦先生就任法官之职。

## XVI My Lady’s Lady / XVI 夫人的侍女

===Original===
Thibault was delighted at seeing what had happened to the young Baron, whose hand, anything but light, had so shortly before made use of his whip on Thibault’s shoulders, which still smarted with the blow. The latter now ran at full speed to see how far Monsieur Raoul de Vauparfond was injured; he found a body lying insensible, stretched across the road, with the horse standing and snorting beside it.

But Thibault could hardly believe his senses on perceiving that the figure lying in the road was not the same as had, but five minutes previously, ridden past him and given him the lash with the whip. In the first place, this figure was not in the dress of a gentleman, but clothed like a peasant, and, what was more, the clothes he had on seemed to Thibault to be the same as he himself had been wearing only a moment before. His surprise increased more and more and amounted almost to stupefaction on further recognising, in the inert, unconscious figure, not only his own clothes, but his own face. His astonishment naturally led him to turn his eyes from this second Thibault to his own person, when he became aware that an equally remarkable change had come over his costume. Instead of shoes and gaiters, his legs were now encased in an elegant pair of hunting boots, reaching to the knee, as soft and smooth as a pair of silk stockings, with a roll over the instep, and finished off with a pair of fine silver spurs. The knee-breeches were no longer of corduroy, but of the most beautiful buckskin, fastened with little gold buckles. His long coarse olive-coloured coat was replaced by a handsome green hunting-coat, with gold lace facings, thrown open to display a waistcoat of fine white jean, while over the artistically pleated shirt hung the soft wavy folds of a cambric cravat. Not a single article of dress about him but had been transformed, even to his old lantern-shaped hat, which was now a three-cornered one, trimmed with gold lace to match the coat. The stick also, such as workmen carry partly for walking and partly for self-defence, and which he had been holding in his hand a minute before, had now given place to a light whip, with which he gave a cut through the air, listening with a sense of aristocratic pleasure to the whistling sound it made. And finally, his slender figure was drawn in at the waist by a belt, from which hung a hunting-knife, half-sword, half-dagger.

===Chinese===
瞧见年轻的男爵这副光景，蒂博心里十分痛快——方才还那么不留情面的一只手，拿马鞭抽在蒂博肩上，那一鞭至今还火辣辣地疼着。这会儿他撒腿飞奔过去，要看看拉乌尔·德·沃帕丰先生（Monsieur Raoul de Vauparfond）到底伤成了什么样子；只见一具身子不省人事地横在大路当中，那匹马站在一旁，打着响鼻。

可蒂博一眼望去，简直不敢相信自己的眼睛：躺在路上的那个人影，分明不是五分钟前骑马从他身边掠过、赏了他一马鞭的那个。头一件，这身形穿的并非绅士打扮，倒像个庄稼汉的装束；再者，他身上这套衣裳，蒂博越看越觉得眼熟，竟像是他自家方才穿在身上的那一套。这一惊非同小可，越看越奇，到后来几乎化作了呆若木鸡——因为他在那毫无知觉、一动不动的人身上，不仅认出了自家的衣裳，更认出了自家的脸。这一惊自然引着他把目光从这第二个蒂博身上移开，落到自己身上来，这才发现自家的穿戴也已起了同样天翻地覆的变化。脚下的鞋子和绑腿不见了，换上了一双雅致的猎靴，齐膝而止，柔软光滑得如同一双丝袜，脚背上翻着一道卷边，末尾配着一对精巧的银马刺。及膝短裤不再是大粗灯芯绒，而是最上等的鹿皮所制，以小小的金搭扣扣紧。那件又长又粗的橄榄色旧外套也不见了，换成了一件漂亮的绿色猎装，镶着金线绲边，衣襟敞开着，露出一件细白帆布的坎肩，而那件打褶打得十分讲究的衬衫之上，垂着一条麻纱领巾，褶纹柔软地起着波浪。浑身上下没有一样不是变过了的，连他那顶灯笼似的旧毡帽，也变成了一顶三角帽，缀着与猎装相称的金线绲边。还有那根短棍——做工的人带着它，一半为着走路，一半为着防身，方才还握在他手里——如今也换成了一条轻巧的马鞭；他随手朝空中抽了一鞭，听得那鞭梢破风的锐响，竟生出一种贵族式的惬意来。末了，他那颀长的腰身被一条腰带束着，腰带上挂着一把猎刀，半像剑，半像匕首。

===Original===
Thibault was pleased beyond measure at finding himself clothed in such a delightful costume, and with a feeling of vanity, natural under the circumstances, he was overcome with the desire to ascertain without delay how the dress suited his face. But where could he go to look at himself, out there in the midst of pitch darkness? Then, looking about him, he saw that he was only a stone’s throw from his own hut.

“Ah! to be sure!” he said, “nothing easier, for I have my glass there.”

And he made haste towards his hut, intending, like Narcissus, to enjoy his own beauty in peace and all to himself. But the door of the hut was locked, and Thibault felt vainly for the key. All he could find in his pockets was a well-filled purse, a sweetmeat box containing scented lozenges, and a little mother-of-pearl and gold penknife. What could he have done then with his door-key? Then suddenly a bright thought occurred to him—possibly the key was in the pocket of that other Thibault who was lying out there in the road. He went back and felt in the breeches pocket, where he discovered the key at once, in company with a few sous. Holding the rough clumsy thing in the tips of his fingers, he returned to open the door. The inside of the hut was even darker that the night outside, and Thibault groped about to find the steel, the tinder and flint, and the matches, and then proceeded to try and light the candle, which consisted of an end stuck into an empty bottle. In a second or two this was accomplished, but in the course of the operation Thibault was obliged to take hold of the candle with his fingers.

“Pah!” he said, “what pigs these peasants are! I wonder how they *can* live in this dirty sort of way!”

===Chinese===
蒂博发觉自个儿竟穿上了这么一身叫人欢喜的行头，乐得心花怒放；那一股理所当然的虚荣心一起，他便迫不及待地想立刻瞧瞧，这身装扮配在自个儿脸上到底是何等模样。可这是黑灯瞎火的野地里，到哪儿去照镜子呢？他四下一望，瞧见离自家的小屋不过一箭之地。

“啊！对啦！”他说，“再没有比这更便当的了，我那儿有面镜子呢。”

他三步并作两步朝小屋奔去，一心要学那喀索斯（Narcissus）的样，独享自家的俊俏模样，从从容容地看个够。谁知小屋的门是锁着的，蒂博摸来摸去也寻不见钥匙。他把口袋掏了个遍，只摸出一只沉甸甸的钱袋、一只装着几粒香锭的糖盒，还有一把珍珠母贝嵌金的小铅笔刀。他把房门钥匙弄哪儿去了呢？忽然间他灵光一闪——莫非钥匙就在另一个蒂博的口袋里，就是那个还横在大路上的人。他折回去，伸手往那条短裤的口袋里一摸，果不其然，钥匙就在里头，还伴着几个苏（sous）。他用指尖尖捏着那把又粗又笨的家伙，回身去开门。屋子里头比外头的夜色还要黑上几分，蒂博摸黑去找火镰、火绒和火石，又寻着了火柴，便动手去点那根蜡烛——说是蜡烛，其实不过是一截蜡烛头，插在一只空酒瓶口上。一两下便点着了，可在这番工夫里头，蒂博免不得要用手指去捏那蜡烛。

“呸！”他说，“这些乡下人真是猪猡！我真纳闷他们怎能过这种脏日子！”

===Original===
However, the candle was alight, which was the chief matter, and Thibault now took down his mirror, and bringing it to the light, looked at himself in it. His eye had scarcely caught sight of the reflected image, than he uttered a cry of astonishment, it was no longer himself that he saw, or rather, although it was still Thibault in spirit, it was no longer Thibault in body. His spirit had entered into the body of a handsome young man of twenty-five or twenty-six years of age, with blue eyes, pink fresh cheeks, red lips, and white teeth; in short, it had entered into the body of the Baron Raoul de Vauparfond. Then Thibault recalled the wish that he had uttered in his moment of anger after the blow from the whip and his collision with the horse. His wish had been that for four and twenty hours he might be the Baron de Vauparfond, and the Baron de Vauparfond be Thibault, which now explained to him what had at first seemed inexplicable, why the unconscious man now lying in the road was dressed in his clothes and had his face.

“But I must not forget one thing,” he said, “that is, that although I seem to be here, I am not really here, but lying out there, so I must be careful to see that during the twenty-four hours, during which I shall be imprudent enough to be away from myself, no irreparable harm comes to me. Come now, Monsieur de Vauparfond, do not be so fastidious; carry the poor man in, and lay him gently on his bed here.” And, although with his aristocratic instincts Monsieur de Vauparfond found the task very repugnant to him, Thibault, nevertheless, courageously took up his own body in his arms and carried himself from the road to the bed. Having thus placed the body in safety, he blew out the light, for fear that any harm should come to this other self before he came to; then, carefully locking the door, he hid the key in the hollow of a tree, where he was in the habit of leaving it when not wishing to take it with him.

===Chinese===
好在蜡烛是点着了，这才是头等要紧的事。蒂博当下把镜子摘下来，端到烛光底下，对着镜子端详起自个儿来。目光才一触到镜中的影像，他便失声惊呼起来——他瞧见的已不再是他自个儿了，或者更准确地说，镜中人精神上仍是蒂博，身子上却已不是蒂博了。他的魂魄已经钻进了一个二十五六岁的俊俏少年的躯壳里，那少年生着一双蓝眼睛、两瓣桃花似的鲜嫩脸颊、红红的嘴唇、雪白的牙齿；一句话，他已钻进了沃帕丰男爵拉乌尔（the Baron Raoul de Vauparfond）的身体里。这一来，蒂博便记起自个儿挨了那一马鞭、又撞了那一马之后，在心头火起之际所发下的那个愿来。他那时许的愿是：但愿二十四个钟头里，他自家能做沃帕丰男爵，而沃帕丰男爵做蒂博。如今这桩原以为讲不通的怪事，便一下子讲通了——为什么那昏倒在大路上的人，穿着的是他的衣裳，顶着的是他的脸。

“可有一桩事我不能忘，”他自言自语道，“那就是，我虽像是站在这儿，其实并不真在这儿，而是躺在外头那儿的，所以我得小心着些，这二十四个钟头里，我既冒冒失失地离开了自家，可不能让自家吃上什么无可挽回的亏。得了吧，沃帕丰先生，别这么娇气了；把这个可怜人抬进去，把他轻轻地搁在这张床上吧。”虽说凭着那一身贵族的 instinct，沃帕丰先生觉得这活计实在叫他作呕，蒂博却鼓起勇气，一把将自家的肉身抱了起来，从大路上抱到了床上。把这条肉身安顿妥当之后，他吹灭了蜡烛——生怕这另一个自家在转醒之前出什么差池；接着小心把门锁上，把钥匙藏进一个树洞里——他往常不愿随身带钥匙时，总把它搁在那儿。

===Original===
The next thing to do was to get hold of the horse’s bridle and mount into the saddle. Once there, Thibault had a preliminary moment of some uneasiness, for, having travelled more on foot than on horseback, he was not an accomplished rider, and he naturally feared that he might not be able to keep his seat when the horse began to move. But it seemed, that, while inheriting Raoul’s body, he also inherited his physical qualities, for the horse, being an intelligent beast, and perfectly conscious of the momentary want of assurance on the part of his rider, made an effort to throw him, whereupon Thibault instinctively gathered up the reins, pressed his knees against the horse’s sides, dug his spurs into them, and gave the animal two or three cuts of the whip, which brought it to order on the spot.

Thibault, perfectly unknown to himself, was a past master in horsemanship. This little affair with the horse enabled Thibault more fully to realise his duality. As far as the body was concerned, he was the Baron Raoul de Vauparfond from top to toe; but as far as the spirit was concerned, he was still Thibault. It was, therefore, certain that the spirit of the young lord who had lent him his body was now sleeping in the form of the unconscious Thibault which he had left behind in the hut.

===Chinese===
接下来要做的，便是抓住马笼头，翻身上了鞍子。一坐上去，蒂博心里先打了个小鼓，因为他往常赶路多是靠两条腿走路，骑马的时候少，算不得什么好骑手，自然生怕马一动起来，他便坐不稳当。可看样子，他既承继了拉乌尔的身子，便也承继了他那一身本事——原来那匹马是个灵性十足的畜生，察觉出骑马的人这一时三刻心里有点发虚，便存心要颠他下来；蒂博见状，不由自主地一收缰绳，两膝一夹马腹，马刺往里一扎，又赏了那畜生两三鞭，登时将它治得服服帖帖。

蒂博自个儿全然不知，他竟已是一位精于骑术的大行家了。这一场与马的小小较量，更叫蒂博深切体味到自个儿这副"一分为二"的光景。论身子，他上上下下、彻头彻尾都是沃帕丰男爵拉乌尔；可论魂魄，他到底还是蒂博。如此说来，那位把身子借给他的少年老爷，其魂魄此刻正昏昏沉睡在那个不省人事的蒂博的躯壳里——也就是他留在小屋床上的那一个。

===Original===
The division of substance and spirit between himself and the Baron, however, left him with a very vague idea of what he was going, or would have, to do. That he was going to Mont-Gobert in answer to the Countess’s letter, so much he knew. But what was in the letter? At what hour was he expected? How was he to gain admission to the Castle? Not one of these questions could he answer, and it only remained for him to discover what to do, step by step, as he proceeded. Suddenly it flashed across him that probably the Countess’s letter was somewhere on his person. He felt about his dress, and, sure enough, inside the side pocket of his coat was something, which by its shape, seemed to be the article he wanted. He stopped his horse, and putting his hand into his pocket, drew out a little scented leather case lined with white satin. In one side of the case were several letters, in the other only one; no doubt the latter would tell him what he wanted to know, if he could once get to read it. He was now only a short distance from the village of Fleury, and he galloped on hoping that he might find a house still lighted up. But villagers go to bed early, in those days even earlier than they do now, and Thibault went from one end of the street to the other without seeing a single light. At last, thinking he heard some kind of movement in the stables of an Inn, he called. A stable boy sallied out with a lantern, and Thibault, forgetting for the moment that he was a lord, said: “Friend, could you show me a light for a moment? You would be doing me a service.”

“And that’s what you go and call a chap out of bed for?”—answered the stable-boy rudely. “Well, you are a nice sort of young’un, you are!” and turning his back on Thibault he was just going to reenter the stable, when Thibault, perceiving that he had gone on a wrong tack, now raised his voice, calling out:

“Look here, sirrah, bring your lantern here and give me a light, or I’ll lay my whip across your back!”

“Ah! pardon, my lord!” said the stable-boy. “I did not see who it was I was speaking to.” And he immediately stood on tiptoe holding the lantern up as Thibault directed him:

===Chinese===
然而，他与男爵这身子与魂魄的一分为二，叫他对自个儿要去干什么、该怎么干，心里头一片糊涂。他只知道，自个儿这是要上蒙-戈贝尔（Mont-Gobert）去，为的是回伯爵夫人那封信——这点他是明白的。可那信里写了些什么？约的是几时？他该怎样进那座城堡才好？这些问题他一个也答不上来，只可走一步看一步，边走边摸出门道来。忽然他心头一亮：说不定伯爵夫人那封信就在他身上哪个地方。他在衣裳上上下下摸了一遍，果不其然，外套侧面的口袋里有样东西，凭那形状，正像是他要找的物件。他勒住马，伸手进口袋一掏，摸出一只熏了香的小皮匣子，里子是白缎子的。匣子一面放着好几封信，另一面却只一封；不用说，只要他能读到这封信，它定能把他想知道的事告诉他。这会儿他离弗勒里（Fleury）村已没多远了，便策马飞奔过去，指望能撞见一户还亮着灯的人家。可庄稼人睡得早，那年头比现在睡得更早，蒂博从街头跑到街尾，一盏灯也没瞧见。末了，他以为听见一家客栈的马厩里有点响动，便高声叫唤起来。一个小马夫提着盏灯笼钻了出来，蒂博一时忘了自个儿如今是个老爷了，便道：“朋友，借个火使使成不成？这可就帮了我大忙了。”

“就为这点子事，你把人家从被窝里叫起来？”小马夫粗声粗气地回道，“你可真是个好后生，你！”

说罢便背过身去，正要钻回马厩，蒂博一看不对路，连忙提高嗓门喝道：

“听着，小子！把灯笼给我提过来借个火，不然我叫你尝尝我马鞭的滋味！”

“哎哟！恕罪，老爷！”小马夫说道，“我没瞧清跟谁说话呢。”说罢立刻踮起脚尖，把灯笼高高举着，照蒂博吩咐的方向举好。

===Original===
Thibault unfolded the letter and read:

> “My dear Raoul,
> “The goddess Venus has certainly taken us under her protection. A grand hunt of some kind is to take place tomorrow out in the direction of Thury; I know no particulars about it, all I do know is, that *he* is going away this evening. You, therefore, start at nine o’clock, so as to be here at half-past ten. Come in by the way you know; someone whom you know will be awaiting you, and will bring you, you know where. Last time you came, I don’t mean to upbraid you, but it did seem to me you stayed a long time in the corridors.
> “Jane.”

“Devil take it!” muttered Thibault.

“I beg your pardon, my lord?” said the stable-boy.

“Nothing, you lout, except that I do not require you any longer and you can go.”

“A good journey to you, my lord!” said the stable-boy, bowing to the ground, and he went back to his stable.

===Chinese===
蒂博把信展开，读道：

> “我亲爱的拉乌尔：
> “维纳斯女神定是收了我们做她的庇护之人。明日往蒂里（Thury）那一带要举行一场什么大猎会；详情我也不知，我只晓得，*他*今儿晚上就要出门去了。所以呢，你九点钟动身，好赶在十点半到这儿。从你认得的那条路进来；有个你认得的人会等着你，把你带到——你知道的那个地方。上回你来，我并非要怪你，可你那回在走廊里耽搁的工夫，瞧着可着实不短。
> “简（Jane）。”

“见鬼！”蒂博咕哝了一声。

“老爷您说什么？”小马夫问。

“没什么，你这蠢材，不过是我不再用你了，你走吧。”

“祝老爷一路顺风！”小马夫说道，一揖到地，便回了他的马厩。

===Original===
“Devil take it!” repeated Thibault, “the letter gives me precious little information, except that we are under the protection of the Goddess Venus, that *he* goes away this evening, that the Comtesse de Mont-Gobert expects me at half-past ten, and that her Christian name is Jane. As for the rest, I am to go in by *the way I know*, I shall be awaited by *someone I know*, and taken *where I know*.” Thibault scratched his ear, which is what everybody does, in every country of the world, when plunged into awkward circumstances. He longed to go and wake up the Lord of Vauparfond’s spirit, which was just now sleeping in Thibault’s body on Thibault’s bed; but, apart from the loss of time which this would involve, it might also cause considerable inconvenience, for the Baron’s spirit, on seeing its own body so near to it, might be taken with the desire of reentering it. This would give rise to a struggle in which Thibault could not well defend himself without doing serious harm to his own person; some other way out of the difficulty must therefore be found. He had heard a great deal about the wonderful sagacity of animals, and had himself, during his life in the country, had occasion more than once to admire their instinct, and he now determined to trust to that of his horse. Riding back into the main road, he turned the horse in the direction of Mont-Gobert, and let it have its head. The horse immediately started off at a gallop; it had evidently understood. Thibault troubled himself no further, it was now the horse’s affair to bring him safely to his destination. On reaching the corner of the park wall the animal stopped, not apparently because it was in doubt as to which road to take, but something seemed to make it uneasy, and it pricked its ears. At the same time, Thibault also fancied that he caught sight of two shadows; but they must have been only shadows, for although he stood up in his stirrups and looked all around him, he could see absolutely nothing. They were probably poachers he thought, who had reasons like himself for wishing to get inside the park. There being no longer anything to bar his passage, he had only, as before, to let the horse go its own way, and he accordingly did so.
