The horse followed the walls of the park at a quick trot, carefully choosing the soft edge of the road, and not uttering a single neigh; the intelligent animal seemed as if it knew that it must make no sound or at least as little sound as possible.

===Chinese===
“见鬼！”蒂博又咕哝了一遍，“这封信给我的消息可真够少的，无非是说我们受了维纳斯女神的庇护，说*他*今儿晚上出门去了，说蒙-戈贝尔伯爵夫人（the Comtesse de Mont-Gobert）十点半等着我，还有她的教名叫简。至于别的呢，叫我*从认得的那条路*进去，有*认得的人*等着我，再把我带到*认得的那个地方*去。”蒂博伸手抓了抓耳朵——人陷进难处的时候，全世界无论哪国的人，都是这副光景。他恨不得立刻回去，把沃帕丰老爷的魂魄唤醒，那魂魄此刻正睡在蒂博床上蒂博的身子里；可这一来，一来耽搁工夫，二来怕还要惹出许多不便——男爵的魂魄一见自家身子近在眼前，说不定会动了重新钻进去的念头。这便免不了一场争夺，蒂博要自卫，便不能不伤着自家这条肉身；因而得另想一条出路才好。他往常听说过不少关于畜生灵性的奇事，自个儿在乡下过活的这些年里，也不止一回领教过它们那本能之妙，如今便决意把宝押在这匹马的身上。他骑回大路，把马头掉向蒙-戈贝尔那边，由着它自个儿走。那马立刻撒开蹄子飞奔起来；它显然是懂的。蒂博不再操心，这会儿把自个儿平安送到地头，乃是马的事了。跑到园墙拐角处，那畜生停了下来，倒不像是拿不准该走哪条路，倒像是有什么叫它不安，它竖起了耳朵。与此同时，蒂博也恍惚觉得瞥见了两个人影；可那想必只是影子罢了，因为他在马镫上立起身来，四下里张望了一圈，什么也没瞧见。他想，那大约是些偷猎的人，跟他一样，也有缘故想钻进这园子里去。眼下既无人拦他的路，他便像方才那样，由着马自个儿走，于是便由它去了。那马迈着快步沿着园墙前行，特意挑着路边松软的那一沿走，一声也不嘶；这匹灵性十足的畜生，仿佛晓得它绝不能出声，或者至少也得尽可能少出声。

===Original===
In this way, they went along the whole of one side of the park, and on reaching the corner, the horse turned as the wall turned, and stopped before a small breach in the same. “It’s through here, evidently,” said Thibault, “that we have to go.”

The horse answered by sniffing at the breach, and scraping the ground with its foot; Thibault gave the animal the rein, and it managed to climb up and through the breach, over the loose stones which rolled away beneath its hoof. Horse and rider were now within the park. One of the three difficulties had been successfully overcome: Thibault had got in by the *way he knew*; it now remained to find the person *whom he knew*, and he thought it wisest to leave this also to his horse. The horse went on for another five minutes, and then stopped at a short distance from the Castle, before the door of one of those little huts of rough logs and bark and clay, which are built up in parks, as painters introduce buildings into their landscapes, solely for the sake of ornament.

On hearing the horse’s hoofs, someone partly opened the door, and the horse stopped in front of it.

A pretty girl came out, and asked in a low voice, “Is it you, Monsieur Raoul?”

“Yes, my child, it is I,” answered Thibault, dismounting.

“Madame was terribly afraid that drunken fool of a Champagne might not have given you the letter.”

“She need not have been afraid; Champagne brought it me with the most exemplary punctuality.”

===Chinese===
他们就这样沿着园墙走完了一整边，到了拐角处，马随着墙一起转了弯，在一处小小的缺口前停了下来。“显然是从这儿进去的喽，”蒂博说，“我们得打这儿走。”

那马用鼻子在缺口上嗅了嗅，又用蹄子刨了刨地，算作回答；蒂博把缰绳一松，那畜生便攀上缺口，从那些在它蹄下骨碌碌滚动的碎石上翻了进去。人与马此刻都进了园子。三道难关已顺利闯过一道：蒂博已从*认得的那条路*进来了；剩下便是要找那个*认得的人*，他觉得这事还是托付给马最为稳妥。那马又往前走了五分钟光景，便在离城堡不远的地方停了下来，停在一间小屋门前——那种用圆木、树皮和泥巴搭成的小屋，专为装点园子而建，正如画家在山水画里点染几间楼阁，不过是为着好看罢了。

听见马蹄声，有人把门开了一条缝，那马便在门前停住了脚。

一个俏丽的姑娘走了出来，低声问道：“是您吗，拉乌尔先生？”

“是的，好孩子，是我。”蒂博答道，翻身下了马。

“夫人方才可担心了，生怕那个醉鬼香槟（Champagne）没把信交给您。”

“她大可不必担心；香槟把信送到了，准时得挑不出一点毛病。”

===Original===
“Leave your horse then and come.”

“But who will look after it?”

“Why Cramoisi, of course, the man who always does.”

“Ah, yes, to be sure,” said Thibault, as if these details were familiar to him, “Cramoisi will look after it.”

“Come, come,” said the maid, “we must make haste or Madame will complain again that we loiter in the corridors.” And as she spoke these words, which recalled a phrase in the letter which had been written to Raoul, she laughed, and showed a row of pearly white teeth, and Thibault felt that he should like to loiter in the park, before waiting to get into the corridors.

Then the maid suddenly stood still a moment with her head bent, listening.

“What is it?” asked Thibault.

“I thought I heard the sound of a branch creaking under somebody’s foot.”

“Very likely,” said Thibault, “no doubt Cramoisi’s foot.”

“All the more reason that you should be careful what you do… at all events out here.”

“I don’t understand.”

“Do you not know that Cramoisi is the man I am engaged to?”

===Chinese===
“那您把马搁这儿，跟我来吧。”

“可谁来看着它呢？”

“嗨，自然是克拉莫瓦西（Cramoisi）了，一向都是他管的。”

“啊，是了，没错，”蒂博说道，装出这些关节他都烂熟于胸的样子，“克拉莫瓦西会照看好它的。”

“来呀，来呀，”侍女说道，“我们得赶紧才是，不然夫人又要怪我们在走廊里磨磨蹭蹭的了。”她说出这话——正好应了写给拉乌尔那封信里的一句——便笑了起来，露出一口珍珠似的皓齿；蒂博只觉得，比起钻进走廊里去，他倒宁可先在这园子里磨蹭一会儿。

这时，侍女忽然站住了脚，侧着头听了一听。

“怎么了？”蒂博问。

“我好像听见一根树枝叫谁踩得嘎吱一响。”

“多半吧，”蒂博说，“想必是克拉莫瓦西的脚。”

“那您就更该当心着些才是……不管怎么说，在外头这儿。”

“我不懂你的意思。”

“您不知道克拉莫瓦西是我的未婚夫吗？”

===Original===
“Ah! to be sure! But when I am alone with you, my dear Rose, I always forget that.”

“I am called Rose now, am I! I never knew such a forgetful man as you are, Monsieur Raoul.”

“I call you Rose, my pretty one, because the rose is the queen of flowers, as you are the queen of waiting-maids.”

“In good truth, my Lord,” said the maid, “I have always found you a lively, witty gentleman, but you surpass yourself this evening.”

Thibault drew himself up, flattered by this remark—really a letter addressed to the Baron, but which it had fallen to the shoemaker to unseal.

“Let us hope your mistress will think the same!” he said.

“As to that,” said the waiting maid, “any man can make one of these ladies of fashion think him the cleverest and wittiest in the world, simply by holding his tongue.”

“Thank you,” he said, “I will remember what you say.”

“Hush!” said the woman to Thibault, “there is Madame behind the dressing-room curtains; follow me now staidly.”

For they had now to cross an open space that lay between the wooded part of the park and the flight of steps leading up to the Castle. Thibault began walking towards the latter.

“Now, now,” said the maid, catching hold of him by the arm, “what are you doing, you foolish man?”

“What am I doing? well, I confess Suzette, I don’t know in the least what I *am* doing!”

“Suzette! so that’s my name now, is it? I think Monsieur does me the honour of calling me in turn by the name of all his mistresses. But come, this way! You are not dreaming I suppose of going through the great reception rooms. That would give a fine opportunity to my lord the Count, truly!”

===Chinese===
“啊！是啦！可我一跟你单独在一起，我亲爱的罗丝（Rose），便总把这事给忘了。”

“我这会儿又叫罗丝了，是吧！我可从没见过像您这么健忘的人呢，拉乌尔先生。”

“我把你唤作罗丝，我的俏人儿，那是因为玫瑰是百花之王，正如你是众侍女之王啊。”

“说实在话，老爷，”侍女说道，“我一向觉得您是位风趣俏皮的绅士，可今儿晚上您更是超常发挥了。”

蒂博把腰板一挺，这话听得他心里十分受用——这本是写给男爵的一封恭维信，偏巧落到了鞋匠手里来拆封。

“但愿你的女主人也这般想吧！”他说道。

“这个嘛，”侍女说道，“随便哪个男人，只要管住嘴不说话，都能叫这些时兴夫人们觉得他是世上最聪明最风趣的人。”

“多谢指教，”他说道，“你说的话我记下了。”

“嘘！”那妇人朝蒂博说道，“夫人就在梳妆室的窗帘后头呢；这会儿放稳重点儿，跟着我走。”

原来他们这会儿得穿过一片开阔地——那片地夹在园子树木葱茏的那一头与通往城堡的那一段台阶之间。蒂博抬脚便往台阶那边走去。

“哎哎，哎哎，”侍女一把抓住他的胳膊说道，“您这是做什么呀，您这傻汉子？”

“我做什么？好吧，我承认，苏泽特（Suzette），我压根儿不知道我*在*做什么！”

“苏泽特！这会儿我又叫这个名字了，是不是？我看哪，先生赏脸，是把您那一位位相好的名字，挨个儿安到我头上来了。得了吧，这边走！您总不至于做着梦要从那些大客厅里穿过吧。那可真要给伯爵老爷一个大好机会了，可不是！”

===Original===
And the maid hurried Thibault towards a little door, to the right of which was a spiral staircase.

Halfway up, Thibault put his arm round his companion’s waist, which was as slender and supple as a snake.

“I think we must be in the corridors, now, eh?” he asked, trying to kiss the young woman’s pretty cheek.

“No, not yet,” she answered; “but never mind that.”

“By my faith,” he said, “if my name this evening were Thibault instead of Raoul, I would carry you up with me to the garrets, instead of stopping on the first floor!”

At that moment a door was heard grating on its hinges.

“Quick, quick, Monsieur!” said the maid, “Madame is growing impatient.”

And drawing Thibault after her, she ran up the remaining stairs to the corridor, opened a door, pushed Thibault into a room, and shut the door after him, firmly believing that it was the Baron Raoul de Vauparfond or, as she herself called him, the most forgetful man in the world, whom she had thus secured.

===Chinese===
说着，那侍女便催着蒂博奔向一扇小门，门右手边是一道螺旋楼梯。

上到半途，蒂博伸手搂住同伴的腰——那腰肢纤细柔韧，如同一条蛇。

“我想这会儿我们该到走廊里了吧，嗯？”他问，一面想去亲那少妇俏丽的脸颊。

“还没呢，”她答道，“不过别在意。”

“我凭信仰起誓，”他说道，“今儿晚上我若叫蒂博，不叫拉乌尔，我便把你抱上阁楼去，才不在这儿二楼上就停下呢！”

正在这时，只听得一扇门在铰链上"吱呀"一声开了。

“快，快，先生！”侍女说道，“夫人等得不耐烦了。”

她拽着蒂博跟在身后，三步并作两步奔上余下的几级楼梯，进了走廊，推开一扇门，把蒂博推进一间屋里，随手把门关紧——她满心以为，自个儿这么一关，关进来的便是沃帕丰男爵拉乌尔，或者照她的叫法，世上顶健忘的那个人了。

## XXIII The Anniversary / XXIII 周年

===Original===
As soon as Thibault ceased to hear the furious cries of his pursuers behind him, he slackened his pace, and the usual silence again reigning throughout the forest, he paused and sat down on a heap of stones. He was in such a troubled state of mind that he did not recognise where he was, until he began to notice that some of the stones were blackened, as if they had been licked by flames; they were the stones of his own former hearth.

Chance had led him to the spot where a few months previously his hut had stood.

The shoemaker evidently felt the bitterness of the comparison between that peaceful past and this terrible present, for large tears rolled down his cheeks and fell upon the cinders at his feet. He heard midnight strike from the Oigny church clock, then one after the other from the other neighbouring village towers. At this moment the priest was listening to Agnelette's dying prayers.

===Chinese===
蒂博听不见身后追兵愤怒的喊叫声之后，便放慢了脚步。森林里重新恢复了往日的那片寂静，他停下脚，在一堆石头上坐了下来。他心绪纷乱极了，一时竟没认出自己身在何处；直到他开始注意到有几块石头被熏得乌黑，像是被火焰舔过似的——那是他自己那座旧炉灶上的石头。

机缘竟引他到了这么个所在：几个月前，他的小屋就立在这儿。

这鞋匠分明是痛感到往昔那份安宁与此刻这份可怕之间那说不出的苦涩对比，因为豆大的泪珠顺着他两颊滚落下来，掉在他脚下的灰烬上。他听见瓦尼（Oigny）教堂的钟敲了午夜十二点，接着邻近一座座村庄钟楼里的钟也跟着一下一下地响了起来。就在这当口，那位神父正在聆听阿涅莱特（Agnelette）临终的祷告。

===Original===
"Cursed be the day!" cried Thibault, "when I first wished for anything beyond what God chooses to put within the reach of a poor workman! Cursed be the day when the black wolf gave me the power to do evil, for the ill that I have done, instead of adding to my happiness, has destroyed it forever!"

A loud laugh was heard behind Thibault's back.

He turned; there was the black wolf himself, creeping noiselessly along, like a dog coming to rejoin its master. The wolf would have been invisible in the gloom but for the flames shot forth from his eyes, which illuminated the darkness; he went round the hearth and sat down facing the shoemaker.

"What is this!" he said. "Master Thibault not satisfied? It seems that Master Thibault is difficult to please."

===Chinese===
"该诅咒啊，那个日子！"蒂博叫道，"我头一回盼求上帝所肯放进一个穷苦匠人手边的东西之外的事物，便是那一天！"该诅咒啊，黑狼（the black wolf）赐我作恶之力的那一天！因为我所作下的那些恶，非但没有添我半分福分，反倒把我的福气永远毁了个干净！"

蒂博身后响起一阵响亮的笑声。

他回过头去；只见那黑狼本尊，正像一条狗归来寻主似的，悄没声儿地爬了过来。若不是它双眼里射出的火焰照亮了周遭的黑暗，它在幽暗中本会隐没无形；它绕着那炉灶转了一圈，在鞋匠对面坐了下来。

"这是怎么说的！"它说道，"蒂博师傅不称心？看来蒂博师傅还真难伺候啊。"

===Original===
"How can I feel satisfied," said Thibault. "I, who since I first met you, have known nothing but vain aspirations and endless regrets? I wished for riches, and here I am in despair at having lost the humble roof of bracken under shelter of which I could sleep in peace without anxiety as to the morrow, without troubling myself about the rain or the wind beating against the branches of the giant oaks.

"I wished for position, and here I am, stoned and hunted down by the lowest peasants, whom formerly I despised. I asked for love, and the only woman who loved me and whom I loved became the wife of another, and she is at this moment cursing me as she lies dying, while I, notwithstanding all the power you have given me, can do nothing to help her!"

===Chinese===
"我怎么能称心呢？"蒂博说，"我这个人，自打头一回遇见你，就再没尝过什么滋味，净是些空落落的贪求和无穷无尽的懊悔！我盼的是富贵，可如今，我连往日那一顶朴素的蕨草屋棚都丢了，正绝望得要命——往日在它遮蔽之下，我能安安稳稳地睡上一觉，不必为明日发愁，也不必理会那风雨敲打着巨橡的枝桠。

"我盼的是体面，可如今，我却叫那些从前我瞧不上眼的最低贱的乡下人，拿石头砸我、四下追捕我。我求的是爱情，可那唯一爱我、我也爱她的女子，竟作了别人的妻，此刻正躺在那里奄奄一息地咒骂着我，而我呢，纵有你赐我的那些神通，却半点也救不了她！"

===Original===
"Leave off loving anybody but yourself, Thibault."

"Oh! yes, laugh at me, do!"

"I am not laughing at you. But did you not cast envious eyes on other people's property before you had set eyes on me?"

"Yes, for a wretched buck, of which there are hundreds just as good browsing in the forest!"

"You thought your wishes were going to stop at the buck, Thibault; but wishes lead on to one another, as the night to the day, and the day to night. When you wished for the buck, you also wished for the silver dish on which it would be served; the silver dish led you on to wish for the servant who carries it and for the carver who cuts up its contents. Ambition is like the vault of heaven; it appears to be bounded by the horizon, but it covers the whole earth. You disdained Agnelette's innocence, and went after Madame Poulet's mill; if you had gained the mill, you would immediately have wanted the house of the Bailiff Magloire; and his house would have had no further attraction for you when once you had seen the Castle of Mont-Gobert.

"You are one in your envious disposition with the fallen Angel, your master and mine; only, as you were not clever enough to reap the benefit that might have accrued to you from your power of inflicting evil, it would perhaps have been more to your interest to continue to lead an honest life."

===Chinese===
"蒂博啊，别再爱别人，只爱你自个儿就是了。"

"噢！好啊，你尽管笑我吧，笑吧！"

"我并非笑你。可你在遇见我之前，难道不曾拿妒忌的眼光，去瞟旁人的财物么？"

"不错，为了一头区区的雄鹿，那样的鹿，林子里正有成百头同样好的，在啃着草呢！"

"你以为你的那些愿望会止于那头鹿罢了，蒂博；可愿望是一个连着一个的，正如夜连着昼，昼连着夜。你盼那头鹿的时候，也连带盼上了要盛它的那一只银盘；那银盘又勾着你，盼上了捧盘子的仆役，盼上了分割鹿肉的那位切肉人。野心好比那苍穹，看上去像被地平线圈住了，其实它罩着整个大地。你鄙弃了阿涅莱特的清纯，转去追逐波莱太太（Madame Polet）的磨坊；你若得了磨坊，立刻便要想要执达吏马格卢瓦（the Bailiff Magloire）的那座宅子；等他的宅子你看过了，蒙-戈贝尔城堡（the Castle of Mont-Gobert）又再不能叫你动心。

"你这好妒的性子，与那位堕落的天使——你的主子、也是我的主子——原是一脉相承；只不过，你既不够精明，捞不到你这作恶之力本可带给你的好处，那么，你与其如此，倒不如接着过你那清白日子，许还更合算些。"

===Original===
"Yes, indeed," replied the shoemaker, "I feel the truth of the proverb, 'Evil to him who evil wishes.' But," he continued, "can I not become an honest man again?"

The wolf gave a mocking chuckle.

"My good fellow, the devil can drag a man to hell," he said, "by a single hair. Have you ever counted how many of yours now belong to him?"

"No."

"I cannot tell you that exactly either, but I know how many you have which are still your own. You have one left! You see it is long past the time for repentance."

===Chinese===
"是啊，一点不错，"鞋匠答道，"那句谚语说得真不差：'心怀恶念者，恶报必临头。'可是，"他接着说，"我就不能再做回一个清白人么？"

那狼嘲弄地哼了一声笑。

"我的好朋友，魔鬼拽一个人下地狱，"它说道，"只消一根头发就成了。你可曾数过，你那脑袋上有多少根已经归他所有了？"

"没有。"

"我也说不上准确的数，可我知道还有多少根仍是你自个儿的。你只剩下一根了！你瞧，悔改的日子早就过啦。"

===Original===
"But if a man is lost when but one of his hairs belongs to the devil," said Thibault, "why cannot God likewise save a man in virtue of a single hair?"

"Well, try if that is so!"

"And, besides, when I concluded that unhappy bargain with you, I did not understand that it was to be a compact of this kind."

"Oh, yes! I know all about the bad faith of you men! Was it no compact then to consent to give me your hairs, you stupid fool? Since men invented baptism, we do not know how to get hold of them, and so, in return for any concessions we make them, we are bound to insist on their relinquishing to us some part of their body on which we can lay hands. You gave us the hairs of your head; they are firmly rooted, as you have proved yourself and will not come away in our grasp.… No, no, Thibault, you have belonged to us ever since, standing on the threshold of the door that was once there, you cherished within you thoughts of deceit and violence."

===Chinese===
"可要是人只消有一根头发归了魔鬼，便算是没救了，"蒂博说，"那上帝怎么就不能照样凭一根头发来拯救一个人呢？"

"那么，你就试试看是不是这样吧！"

"再说，当初我跟你做成那桩不幸的交易时，我可没料到它会是这样的一份契约（compact）。"

"哦，是啦！你们人类的不守信我还不清楚么！你答应把你的头发给我，这难道不算契约么，你这蠢货？自从人类发明了洗礼这玩意儿，我们就再也抓不住他们了，所以，作为我们对他们所作让步的回报，我们非要他们舍弃身上某一处我们能下手抓住的部分不可。你把你的头发给了我们；它们的根扎得牢牢的，你自己已经试过了，在我们爪子里也揪不下来。……不，不，蒂博，你早就是我们的了——打从你站在那道曾经存在过的门槛上、心里藏着那些欺瞒与暴力的念头那一刻起，你就归我们了。"

===Original===
"And so," cried Thibault passionately, rising and stamping his foot, "and so I am lost as regards the next world without having enjoyed the pleasures of this!"

"You can yet enjoy these."

"And how, I pray."

"By boldly following the path that you have struck by chance, and resolutely determining on a course of conduct which you have adopted as yet only in a halfhearted way; in short, by frankly owning yourself to be one of us."

"And how am I to do this?"

"Take my place."

"And what then?"

"You will then acquire my power, and you will have nothing left to wish for."

===Chinese===
"这么说来，"蒂博激愤地叫道，一边站起身来直跺脚，"这么说来，来世我是毁了，可这一世的种种快活我却连半点也没尝到！"

"这些快活你倒还尝得到。"

"请问怎么个尝法？"

"那就是大胆地顺着那条你不意间闯出来的路走下去，铁了心照着那套你至今还只是三心二意去做的做派做下去；一句话，痛痛快快地承认你是我们一伙的。"

"那要我怎么做呢？"

"顶替我的位子。"

"顶替了又如何？"

"你便能得着我的神通，便再无所可盼了。"

===Original===
"If your power is so great, if it can give you all the riches that I long for, why do you give it up?"

"Do not trouble yourself about me. The master for whom I shall have won a retainer will liberally reward me."

"And if I take your place, shall I also have to take your form?"

"Yes, in the nighttime; by day you will be a man again."

"The nights are long, dark, full of snares; I may be brought down by a bullet from a keeper, or be caught in a trap, and then goodbye riches, goodbye position and pleasure."

"Not so; for this skin that covers me is impenetrable by iron, lead or steel. As long as it protects your body, you will be not only invulnerable, but immortal; once a year, like all werewolves, you will become a wolf again for four and twenty hours, and during that interval, you will be in danger of death like any other animal. I had just reached that dangerous time a year ago today, when we first met."

"Ah!" said Thibault, "that explains why you feared my Lord Baron's dogs."

===Chinese===
"若你的神通真这么厉害，若它能给你一切我所眼热的富贵，那你为何要把它拱手让出？"

"这个你就不必替我操心了。我替那位主子拉到了一个仆从，他自会重重地赏我。"

"我若顶替你的位子，是不是也得顶替你的模样？"

"不错，到了夜间便得如此；一到白天，你便又变回人。"

"夜长、夜黑、夜里满是机关陷阱；我说不定叫哪个看守一枪打翻，说不定落进陷阱里被捉，到那时候，富贵啦，体面和快活啦，全都别提了。"

"不然；因为我身上这一层皮，刀枪不入，铁、铅、钢通通扎不进。只要它护着你的肉身，你不但刀枪不入，而且是长生不老的；一年一度，跟所有的狼人（werewolf）一样，你会重新化作狼二十四个钟头；而在那段工夫里，你便会跟任何一头畜生一样，有送命的危险。一年前的今天，我正到了那段险期，便遇见了你。"

"啊！"蒂博说，"这便说明了，你当初为何那么怕男爵老爷（my Lord Baron）的猎犬。"

===Original===
"When we have dealings with men, we are forbidden to speak anything but the truth, and the whole truth; it is for them to accept or refuse."

"You have boasted to me of the power that I shall acquire; tell me, now, in what that power will consist?"

"It will be such that even the most powerful king will not be able to withstand it, since his power is limited by the human and the possible."

"Shall I be rich?"

"So rich, that you will come in time to despise riches, since, by the mere force of your will, you will obtain not only what men can only acquire with gold and silver, but also all that superior beings get by their conjurations."

===Chinese===
"我们与人类打交道时，是被严禁说一句假话、或者只说半截真话的；信与不信，全凭他们自个儿做主。"

"你既向我夸下了我所能得的神通，那你如今且告诉我，这神通究竟到了何等地步？"

"它将大到连最有权势的国王也抵挡不住，因为他的权柄，到底还受着人力与可能之力的限制。"

"我会富么？"

"富到将来你迟早会鄙薄富贵；因为单凭你意志之力，你不单能弄到旁人非得用金银才能到手的东西，更能弄到那些超凡之灵凭咒语才能弄到的一切。"

===Original===
"Shall I be able to revenge myself on my enemies?"

"You will have unlimited power over everything which is connected with evil."

"If I love a woman, will there again be a possibility of my losing her?"

"As you will have dominion over all your fellow creatures, you will be able to do with them what you like."

"There will be no power to enable them to escape from the trammels of my will?"

"Nothing, except death, which is stronger than all."

"And I shall only run the risk of death myself on one day out of the three hundred and sixty-five?"

"On one day only; during the remaining days nothing can harm you, neither iron, lead, nor steel, neither water, nor fire."

"And there is no deceit, no trap to catch me, in your words?"

"None, on my honour as a wolf!"

"Good," said Thibault, "then let it be so; a wolf for four and twenty hours, for the rest of the time the monarch of creation! What am I to do? I am ready."

===Chinese===
"我能向我的仇人们报仇雪恨么？"

"凡与恶相关的一切，你都有着无边无际的权柄。"

"我若爱了一个女子，难道还会有失掉她的可能么？"

"你既对一切同类都有统治之权，那对他们要怎样便怎样，悉听尊便。"

"那便没有任何力量能叫他们从我意志的桎梏里挣脱出去么？"

"没有，除了死亡——死亡强过一切。"

"而我自己，只在三百六十五天里头的一天，才有送命的危险？"

"只一天而已；其余那些日子里，任何东西都伤不着你，铁也好、铅也好、钢也好，水也好、火也好，都伤不着。"

"你这番话里头，没有欺瞒，也没有设下什么套子要我钻吧？"

"绝无半点，凭我身为狼的荣誉起誓！"

"好，"蒂博说，"那就一言为定；二十四个钟头做狼，余下的时日做造物之主！要我做什么？我预备好了。"

===Original===
"Pick a holly-leaf, tear it in three pieces with your teeth, and throw it away from you, as far as you can."

Thibault did as he was commanded.

Having torn the leaf in three pieces, he scattered them on the air, and although the night till then had been a peaceful one, there was immediately heard a loud peal of thunder, while a tempestuous whirlwind arose, which caught up the fragments and carried them whirling away with it.

"And now, brother Thibault," said the wolf, "take my place, and good luck be with you! As was my case just a year ago, so you will have to become a wolf for four and twenty hours; you must endeavour to come out of the ordeal as happily as I did, thanks to you, and then you will see realised all that I have promised you. Meanwhile, I will pray the lord of the cloven hoof that he will protect you from the teeth of the Baron's hounds, for, by the devil himself, I take a genuine interest in you, friend Thibault."

===Chinese===
"摘一片冬青叶子，用牙齿把它撕成三片，再使劲往远处一甩。"

蒂博依言而行。

他把那片叶子撕成三片，往空中一撒；虽说方才那夜还是宁静的，可这一下子便听得一声响雷，又卷起一阵暴烈的旋风，将那几片碎叶卷起来，滴溜溜地旋着带走了。

"那么，蒂博兄弟，"那狼说道，"顶我的位子吧，愿你走运！正如我一年前的处境那样，你也得化作狼二十四个钟头；你可得拼命熬过这一关，像我当初托你的福那么圆满地熬过来才好；熬过了，你便能瞧见我答应你的一桩桩都兑现。这其间，我自会向那位分蹄大老爷祷告，求他护佑你，叫男爵的猎犬伤不着你；因为，凭魔鬼本尊起誓，我是真心关照你的，蒂博老兄。"

===Original===
And then it seemed to Thibault that he saw the black wolf grow larger and taller, that it stood up on its hind legs and finally walked away in the form of a man, who made a sign to him with his hand as he disappeared.

We say *it seemed to him*, for Thibault's ideas, for a second or two, became very indistinct. A feeling of torpor passed over him, paralysing his power of thought. When he came to himself, he was alone. His limbs were imprisoned in a new and unusual form; he had, in short, become in every respect the counterpart of the black wolf that a few minutes before had been speaking to him. One single white hair on his head alone shone in contrast to the remainder of the sombre coloured fur; this one white hair of the wolf was the one black hair which had remained to the man.

===Chinese===
接着，蒂博只觉得自己眼见着那黑狼越长越高大，越长越魁伟，它以后腿立了起来，末了竟化作一个人形走了开去；那人一边消失，一边还朝他抬手打了个招呼。

我们之所以说*他只觉得*，是因为蒂博那一两秒间的神志已模糊极了。一阵麻痹之感漫过他全身，叫他再不能思索。待他再清醒过来时，他已是孤零零一个人了。他的四肢被一种新奇而陌生的形体所拘束；一句话，他浑身上下，已成了几分钟前那个对他说话的黑狼的翻版。唯独他脑袋上有一根白毛，在那一片深色的毛皮中格外醒目；这一根狼身上的白毛，便是那个人身上仅存的那一根黑发。

===Original===
Thibault had scarcely had time to recover himself when he fancied he heard a rustling among the bushes, and the sound of a low, muffled bark.… He thought of the Baron and his hounds, and trembled. Thus metamorphosed into the black wolf, he decided that he would not do what his predecessor had done, and wait till the dogs were upon him. It was probably a bloodhound he had heard, and he would get away before the hounds were uncoupled. He made off, striking straight ahead, as is the manner of wolves, and it was a profound satisfaction to him to find that in his new form he had tenfold his former strength and elasticity of limb.

"By the devil and his horns!" the voice of the Lord of Vez was now heard to say to his new huntsman a few paces off, "you hold the leash too slack, my lad; you have let the bloodhound give tongue, and we shall never head the wolf back now."

"I was in fault, I do not deny it, my Lord; but as I saw it go by last evening only a few yards from this spot, I never guessed that it would take up its quarters for the night in this part of the wood and that it was so close to us as all that."

===Chinese===
蒂博才刚刚定下神来，便仿佛听见灌木丛里有窸窸窣窣的响动，又听得一声低沉而闷着的吠叫。……他想起男爵和他那群猎犬，不由得直打哆嗦。既已化作这头黑狼，他便打定主意，绝不肯学他前任的样儿，坐等那些狗扑到身上。方才他听见的大概是一条寻血猎犬，那他便赶在那些狗解开绳索之前先溜之大吉。他撒腿就跑，照着狼的做派一路照直冲了出去；而他发觉自己在这副新躯壳里，筋骨之强健、四肢之轻灵，竟十倍于从前，这实在叫他舒坦极了。

"凭魔鬼和他的犄角起誓！"几步开外，韦兹领主（the Lord of Vez）的嗓音此刻正冲着他新来的猎手响起来，"你那绳子牵得也太松啦，小子；你由着那条寻血犬开了腔，这下咱们再也别想把那头狼截回来啦。"

"是小的不是，小的也不抵赖，老爷；可我昨晚亲眼瞧见它打这地儿才几码远的地方经过，哪里猜得到，它竟会在林子这一带过夜，又跟咱们挨得这么近呢。"

===Original===
"Are you sure it is the same one that has got away from us so often?"

"May the bread I eat in your service choke me, my lord, if it is not the same black wolf that we were chasing last year when poor Marcotte was drowned."

"I should like finely to put the dogs on its track," said the Baron, with a sigh.

"My lord has but to give the order, and we will do so, but he will allow me to observe that we have still two good hours of darkness before us, time enough for every horse we have to break its legs."

"That may be, but if we wait for the day, l'Eveillé, the fellow will have had time to get ten leagues away."

"Ten leagues at least," said l'Eveillé, shaking his head.

===Chinese===
"你敢保准就是那头屡屡从咱们手里溜走的畜生么？"

"老爷要不是我去年追的那头黑狼，就叫我吃您家饭的那口面包噎死我；那一年，可怜的马尔科特（Marcotte）就是追它时淹死的。"

"我倒真巴不得把猎犬撒到它踪迹上去，"男爵叹了口气说道。

"老爷只消一声令下，我们便撒狗；可老爷也得容我进一言，咱们前头还有足足两个钟头的好天黑呢，这些马儿要在这当儿跌断腿，工夫是绰绰有余的。"

"那或许不假，可咱们要等到天亮，勒韦耶（l'Eveillé），那家伙便有的是工夫逃出十里格（leagues）开外了。"

"至少十里格，"勒韦耶摇着头说道。

===Original===
"I have got this cursed black wolf on my brain," added the Baron, "and I have such a longing to have its skin, that I feel sure I shall catch an illness if I do not get hold of it."

"Well then, my lord, let us have the dogs out without a moment's loss of time."

"You are right, l'Eveillé; go and fetch the hounds."

L'Eveillé went back to his horse, that he had tied to a tree outside the wood, and went off at a gallop, and in ten minutes' time, which seemed like ten centuries to the Baron, he was back with the whole hunting train. The hounds were immediately uncoupled.

"Gently, gently, my lads!" said the Lord of Vez, "you forget you are not handling your old well-trained dogs; if you get excited with these raw recruits, they'll merely kick up a devil of a row, and be no more good than so many turnspits; let 'em get warmed up by degrees."

===Chinese===
"我满脑子都是这头该死的黑狼，"男爵又说道，"我一心想扒下它那张皮，想得这般厉害，我敢说，我要是抓不到它，非得染上一场大病不可。"

"既然如此，老爷，那就一秒钟也别耽搁，把狗放出来吧。"

"你说得对，勒韦耶；去把猎犬牵来。"

勒韦耶回到林子外面拴在树上的坐骑旁，飞身上马疾驰而去。不出十分钟——在男爵觉着却像是十个世纪——他便带着整套狩猎人马转了回来。猎犬们立时被解了索。

"稳着些，稳着些，伙计们！"韦兹领主说道，"你们忘了，今儿使的可不是你们平日那些驯熟的老狗；你们要是对这帮新兵崽子也跟着起劲，它们不过闹个鸡飞狗跳，跟一窝烤叉狗也强不到哪儿去；得让它们慢慢热起身来。"

===Original===
And, indeed, the dogs were no sooner loose, than two or three got at once on to the scent of the werewolf, and began to give cry, whereupon the others joined them. The whole pack started off on Thibault's track, at first quietly following up the scent, and only giving cry at long intervals, then more excitedly and of more accord, until they had so thoroughly imbibed the odour of the wolf ahead of them, and the scent had become so strong, that they tore along, baying furiously, and with unparalleled eagerness in the direction of the Yvors coppice.

"Well begun, is half done!" cried the Baron. "You look after the relays, l'Eveillé; I want them ready whenever needed! I will encourage the dogs.… And you be on the alert, you others," he added, addressing himself to the younger keepers, "we have more than one defeat to avenge, and if I lose this view-halloo through the fault of anyone among you, by the devil and his horns! he shall be the dogs' quarry in place of the wolf!"

After pronouncing these words of encouragement, the Baron put his horse to the gallop, and although it was still pitch dark and the ground was rough, he kept the animal going at top speed so as to come up with the hounds, which could be heard giving tongue in the low lands about Bourg-Fontaine.

===Chinese===
果不其然，那些狗一松开，便有两三条立刻嗅上了那狼人的气味，开口吠了起来；其余的随即也跟着凑上来。整群猎犬循着蒂博的踪迹追了出去，起初还不慌不忙地循着气味走，老半天才叫上一声，渐渐地越追越起劲、叫得也越齐整，到末了它们把前头那头狼的气味吸得透透的，那气味也浓烈到了极点，它们便咆哮着疯狂地冲了出去，以一种前所未见的急切劲儿，朝着伊沃尔（Yvors）矮树林的方向狂奔而去。

"良好的开端是成功的一半！"男爵叫道，"勒韦耶，你照应着那些接力犬去；我要它们随时待命！我来给猎犬们鼓劲。……你们几个也给我警醒着点儿，"他又转向那几个年轻的看守说道，"咱们败在它手里的次数可不只一回了，这回要是有谁坏了事，叫我丢了这一声追猎的号角，凭魔鬼和他的犄角起誓，我便叫他替那头狼去喂狗！"

男爵发表完这番鼓劲的话，便策马飞奔起来；虽说天还黑得伸手不见五指，地面又高低不平，他却仍纵马全速疾驰，好追上那群猎犬——只听得它们正在布尔丰泉（Bourg-Fontaine）一带的低地里此起彼伏地吠叫着。

## XVII The Baron de Mont-Gobert / XVII 蒙-戈贝尔男爵

===Original===
Thibault found himself in the Countess’s room. If the magnificence of Bailiff Magloire’s furniture rescued from the lumber-room of his Highness the Duke of Orleans, had astonished Thibault, the daintiness, the harmony, the taste of the Countess’s room filled him with intoxicating delight. The rough child of the forest had never seen anything like it, even in dreams; for one cannot even dream of things of which we have no idea.

Double curtains were drawn across the two windows, the one set of white silk trimmed with lace, the other of pale china blue satin, embroidered with silver flowers. The bed and the toilet table were draped to match the windows, and were nearly smothered in clouds of Valenciennes lace. The walls were hung with very light rose-coloured silk, over which thick folds of Indian muslin, delicate as woven air, undulated like waves of mist at the slightest breath of air from the door. The ceiling was composed of a medallion painted by Boucher, and representing the toilet of Venus; she was handing her cupids the various articles of a woman’s apparel, and these were now all distributed, with the exception of the goddess’s girdle.

The central medallion was surrounded by a series of panels, on which were painted supposed views of Cnidos, Paphos, and Amathus. All the furniture﻿—chairs, armchairs, settees, sociables﻿—was covered with China satin similar to that of the curtains; over the groundwork of the carpet, of the colour of pale green water, were scattered bouquets of blue cornflowers, pink poppies, and white daisies. The tables were of rosewood; the corner-pieces of Indian lacquer; and the whole room was softly lighted by pink wax candles held in two candelabra. A vague and indescribably delicate perfume pervaded the air, one could not say from what sweet essence, for it was scarcely even a perfume, but rather an emanation, the same kind of odorous exhalation whereby Aeneas, in the *Aeneid*, recognised the presence of his mother.

===Chinese===
蒂博发觉自己已置身于伯爵夫人的房中。倘若说，执达吏马格卢瓦师傅那些从奥尔良公爵（the Duke of Orleans）殿下杂物间里搬来的华美家具，曾叫蒂博惊叹不已，那么，伯爵夫人这间屋子里的精巧、和谐与雅致，便使他满心沉醉的欢喜了。这个林子里长大的粗野汉子，便是做梦也未曾见过这般景象；因为凡是人心中了无概念的事物，做梦也是梦不出来的。

两扇窗子都挂着双层帘子，一层是镶着花边的白绸，另一层是浅淡的瓷青蓝缎，上头绣着银色的花朵。床铺与梳妆台的帷幔与窗帘同一式样，几乎埋在瓦朗西安花边的云堆里。四壁挂着极淡的玫瑰色丝绸，外面又罩着一层厚厚的印度细纱褶皱，轻妙如织就的空气，门缝里透进一丝风，便如薄雾的波涛般荡漾起伏。天花板上绘着一幅布歇（Boucher）所作的圆形画，画的是维纳斯梳妆；她正将女人各式衣饰一件件递给她那些小爱神，如今这些衣饰都已分发完毕，只除下女神的那条腰裙。

这幅中央圆画四周环着一圈嵌板，板上画着尼多斯（Cnidos）、帕福斯（Paphos）和阿玛图斯（Amathus）的假想风光。全套家具——靠背椅、扶手椅、长榻、对坐椅——全蒙着与窗帘相同的瓷青缎子；地毯底色作浅碧水色，上头撒满一束束蓝矢车菊、粉红的罂粟和白色的雏菊。桌子是花梨木的，墙角柜是印度漆器的；整间屋子由两座烛台上粉色蜡烛的柔光轻轻照亮。一股若有若无、妙不可言的幽香弥漫在空气里，说不清出自哪一种香精，因为它几乎算不得是一股香气，倒更像一缕芳息——正如《埃涅阿斯纪》（*Aeneid*）里的埃涅阿斯，便是凭着这一类芬芳的气息，认出了他母亲的临在。

===Original===
Thibault pushed into the room by the waiting-maid, made one step forward, and then stopped. He had taken everything in at a glance, inhaled everything at a breath. For a second there passed before his mind’s eye like a vision﻿—Agnelette’s little cottage, Madame Polet’s dining-room, the bedchamber of the Bailiff’s wife; but they disappeared as quickly to give place to this delicious paradise of love into which he had been transported as by magic. He could scarcely believe that what he looked upon was real. Were there really men and women in the world, so blessed by fortune as to live in such surroundings as these? Had he not been carried to some wizard’s castle, to some fairy’s palace? And those who enjoyed such favour as this, what special good had they done? what special evil had they done, who were deprived of these advantages? Why, instead of wishing to be the Baron for four and twenty hours, had he not wished to be the Countess’s lapdog all his life? How would he bear to be Thibault again after having seen all this? He had just reached this point in his reflections, when the dressing-room door opened and the Countess herself appeared, a fit bird for such a nest, a fit flower for such a sweet scented garden.

===Chinese===
蒂博被那侍女推进屋里，往前迈了一步，便站住了。这一切，他只一眼便尽收眼底，只一呼吸便吸了个透彻。一刹那间，他心头眼前掠过幻影一般——阿涅莱特（Agnelette）的小屋，波莱太太（Madame Polet）的饭厅，执达吏太太的卧房；可这些影子转瞬即逝，让位给了这座他像着了魔法一般被送入的、这般甜美的爱情乐园。他几乎不敢相信，眼前所见竟是真实。世上果真有这样的男男女女，被命运如此眷顾，竟住在这样的地方么？他莫不是被摄到了哪位术士的城堡，哪位仙人的宫殿里？而那些享有这般福分的人，究竟做过什么特别的善事？那些被剥夺了这些好处的人，又究竟做过什么特别的恶事？他当初为何只发愿做二十四个小时的男爵，而不是发愿一辈子做伯爵夫人的膝上哈巴狗呢？见过这一切之后，他怎能再忍受重新做回蒂博？他正盘算到这一节，梳妆室的门开了，伯爵夫人本人走了出来——这般香巢，正该配这样一只鸟儿；这般芳香的花园，正该配这样一朵花儿。

===Original===
Her hair, fastened only by four diamond pins, hung down loosely to one side, while the rest was gathered into one large curl that hung over the other shoulder and fell into her bosom. The graceful lines of her lithe and well-formed figure, no longer hidden by puffings of dress, were clearly indicated beneath her loose pink silk gown, richly covered with lace; so fine and transparent was the silk of her stockings, that it was more like pearl-white flesh than any texture, and her tiny feet were shod in little slippers made of cloth of silver, with red heels. But not an atom of jewellery﻿—no bracelets on the arms, no rings on the fingers; just one row of pearls round the throat, that was all﻿—but what pearls! worth a king’s ransom!

As this radiant apparition came towards him, Thibault fell on his knees; he bowed himself, feeling crushed at the sight of this luxury, of this beauty, which to him seemed inseparable.

===Chinese===
她的头发只以四枚钻石发钗绾住，松松地垂向一边，余下的则绾成一绺大卷发，搭在另一边肩头，一直垂到胸前。她那袅娜身段的优美线条，不再为鼓胀的衣衫所遮掩，在那件缀满花边的宽松粉绸袍子底下勾勒得分明；她袜子的绸料精细透明，看着竟不像织造，倒像珠白的皮肉，一双小脚趿着银布做成的小拖鞋，红红的鞋跟。却不见半分珠翠——腕间无镯，指上无环；颈间只挂了一串珍珠，仅此而已——可那是怎样的珍珠啊！抵得过一位国王的赎金！

这一道光彩照人的身影向他走来，蒂博双膝一软，跪了下去；他深深俯首，被眼前这份奢华、这份美貌所压倒——在他看来，这两者仿佛本就是不可分离的一体。

===Original===
“Yes, yes, you may well kneel﻿—kneel lower, lower yet﻿—kiss my feet, kiss the carpet, kiss the floor, but I shall not any the more forgive you﻿ ﻿… you are a monster!”

“In truth, Madame, if I compare myself with you, I am even worse than that!”

“Ah! yes, pretend that you mistake my words and think I am only speaking of your outward appearance, when you know I am speaking of your behaviour﻿ ﻿… and, indeed, if your perfidious soul were imaged in your face, you would verily and indeed be a monster of ugliness. But yet it is not so, for Monsieur, for all his wickedness and infamous doings, still remains the handsomest gentleman in all the country round. But, come now, Monsieur, ought you not to be ashamed of yourself?”

“Because I am the handsomest gentleman in the neighbourhood?” asked Thibault, detecting by the tone of the lady’s voice that his crime was not an irremediable one.

“No, Monsieur, but for having the blackest soul and the falsest heart ever hidden beneath such a gay and golden exterior. Now, get up, and come and give an account of yourself to me.”

And the Countess so speaking held out a hand to Thibault which offered pardon at the same time that it demanded a kiss.

===Chinese===
“是啦，是啦，你跪倒也跪得应当——跪得再低些，再低些——亲我的脚，亲这地毯，亲这地板，可我也还是不能饶你……你是个怪物！”

“说真的，夫人，若是拿我跟您相比，我比怪物还要不如呢！”

“哈！是了，你偏要装作误会我的话，装作我只说的是你的相貌，可你心里明白我说的是你的行径……不过，话又说回来，倘若你那颗背信弃义的灵魂当真照映在脸上，那你确确实实就是个丑陋的怪物了。好在还不至于如此，因为这位先生尽管恶毒，尽管尽做些下作的勾当，在这一方圆之内，到底还数他长得最俊。好了，好了，先生，你这就不觉得惭愧么？”

“惭愧我竟是左近最英俊的绅士？”蒂博问道，他已从这位夫人说话的腔调里听出，自己犯下的罪还不至于无可挽回。

“不是的，先生，惭愧的是，你竟把一颗最黑的心、一副最虚假的肝胆，藏在这般风流金贵的皮囊底下。起来罢，过来，向我交代交代你的所作所为。”

伯爵夫人这样说着，向蒂博伸出一只手来，那手一边递过宽恕，一边却又讨一个亲吻。

===Original===
Thibault took the soft, sweet hand in his own and kissed it; never had his lips touched anything so like satin. The Countess now seated herself on the settee and made a sign to Raoul to sit down beside her.

“Let me know something of your doings, since you were last here,” said the Countess to him.

“First tell me, dear Countess,” replied Thibault, “when I last was here.”

“Do you mean you have forgotten? One does not generally acknowledge things of that kind, unless seeking for a cause of quarrel.”

“On the contrary, dear friend, it is because the recollection of that last visit is so present with me, that I think it must have been only yesterday we were together, and I try in vain to recall what I have done, and I assure you I have committed no other crime since yesterday but that of loving you.”

===Chinese===
蒂博握住那只柔软香甜的手，在上面吻了一下；他的嘴唇还从未触碰过这般缎子般的东西。伯爵夫人于是坐到长榻上，向拉乌尔（Raoul）示意，让他坐在自己身旁。

“跟我说说，自打你上次来过之后，你都干了些什么。”伯爵夫人对他说。

“您得先告诉我，亲爱的伯爵夫人，”蒂博答道，“我上回是什么时候来的。”

“你是说你忘了？这一类事，寻常人是不肯承认的，除非是存心要寻衅吵架。”

“恰恰相反，亲爱的朋友，正因为上回那一面在我心头记挂得太真切，我竟觉得咱俩分明不过是昨天才在一起的，我拼命回想这几天都做了些什么也是白搭，我可以向您保证，自打昨天以来，我除了爱您这一桩罪名之外，再没犯过别的罪。”

===Original===
“That’s not a bad speech; but you will not get yourself out of disgrace by paying compliments.”

“Dear Countess,” said Thibault, “supposing we put off explanations to another time.”

“No, you must answer me now; it is five days since I last saw you; what have you been doing all that time?”

“I am waiting for you to tell me, Countess. How can you expect me, conscious as I am of my innocence, to accuse myself?”

“Very well then! I will not begin by saying anything about your loitering in the corridors.”

“Oh, pray, let us speak of it! how can you think, Countess, that knowing you, the diamond of diamonds, was waiting for me, I should stop to pick up an imitation pearl?”

“Ah! but I know how fickle men are, and Lisette is such a pretty girl!”

===Chinese===
“这番话说得倒不坏；可你光拿好话奉承，也休想从我这儿把失宠的处境摘出去。”

“亲爱的伯爵夫人，”蒂博说，“咱们不如把解释的事往后搁一搁，好不好？”

“不行，你这会儿就得回我的话；我有五天没见着你了；这五天你都做什么来着？”

“我正等着您来告诉我呢，伯爵夫人。我自知清白无辜，您怎能指望我来自我指控呢？”

“好罢！那我在走廊里磨蹭那一桩，便不先提了。”

“噢，求您了，咱们偏要提提这一桩！您怎么想得到，伯爵夫人，我明知您这钻石中的钻石在等着我，反倒会停下脚步去捡一颗假珠子呢？”

“啊！可我晓得男人都是何等薄情，而莉赛特（Lisette）又是那样标致的一个丫头！”

===Original===
“Not so, dear Jane, but you must understand that she being our confidante, and knowing all our secrets, I cannot treat her quite like a servant.”

“How agreeable it must be to be able to say to one’s-self ‘I am deceiving the Comtesse de Mont-Gobert and I am the rival of Monsieur Cramoisi!’ ”

“Very well then, there shall be no more loiterings in the corridors, no more kisses for poor Lisette, supposing of course there ever have been any!”

“Well, after all, there is no great harm in that.”

“Do you mean that I have done something even worse?”

“Where had you been the other night, when you were met on the road between Erneville and Villers-Cotterets?”

===Chinese===
“并非如此，亲爱的简（Jane），可您总该明白，她既是咱们的心腹，又知晓咱们所有的秘密，我总不能把她当成寻常下人一般对待。”

“能跟自己说一句‘我在欺骗蒙-戈贝尔伯爵夫人（the Comtesse de Mont-Gobert），我又是克拉莫西先生（Monsieur Cramoisi）的情敌！’——这事想来多么惬意！”

“那好罢，往后走廊里再不磨蹭了，再不亲吻可怜的莉赛特了——当然啦，前提是从前当真有过这等事！”

“也罢，到底这也不是什么大不了的过错。”

“您是说我还有更不像话的行径？”

“前儿个夜里你在埃纳维尔（Erneville）和维莱-科特雷（Villers-Cotterets）之间的大路上被人撞见，你是打哪儿回来的？”

===Original===
“Someone met me on the road?”

“Yes, on the Erneville Road; where were you coming from?”

“I was coming home from fishing.”

“Fishing! what fishing?”

“They had been drawing the Berval ponds.”

“Oh! we know all about that; you are such a fine fisher, are you not, Monsieur? And what sort of an eel were you bringing back in your net, returning from your fishing at two o’clock in the morning!”

“I had been dining with my friend, the Baron, at Vez.”

“At Vez? ha! I fancy you went there mainly to console the beautiful recluse, whom the jealous Baron keeps shut up there a regular prisoner, so they say. But even that I can forgive you.”

===Chinese===
“有人在那条路上撞见了我？”

“是的，在埃纳维尔那条路上；你是打哪儿来的？”

“我是钓鱼回来。”

“钓鱼！钓什么鱼？”

“那几口贝尔瓦尔（Berval）塘不是刚起了鱼么。”

“噢！这事我们肚里明白；先生真是好一位钓鱼的高手，是不是？而且凌晨两点钟钓鱼回来，网里往回带的是怎样一条鳗鱼啊！”

“我是跟我的朋友男爵在韦兹（Vez）一道用了晚饭。”

“在韦兹？哈！我想你上那儿去，多半是为着去安慰那位幽居的美人儿罢——听人说，那善妒的男爵把她当作正经囚犯一般关在那儿呢。不过，这一桩我倒也饶得下你。”

===Original===
“What, is there a blacker crime still,” said Thibault, who was beginning to feel quite reassured, seeing how quickly the pardon followed on the accusation; however serious it appeared at first.

“Yes, at the ball given by his Highness the Duke of Orleans.”

“What ball?”

“Why, the one yesterday! it’s not so very along ago, is it?”

“Oh, yesterday’s ball? I was admiring you.”

“Indeed; but I was not there.”

“Is it necessary for you to be present, Jane, for me to admire you; cannot one admire you in remembrance as truly as in person? and if, when absent, you triumph by comparison, the victory is only so much the greater.”

“I daresay, and it was in order to carry out the comparison to its utmost limits that you danced four times with Madame de Bonneuil; they are very pretty, are they not, those dark women who cover themselves with rouge, and have eyebrows like the Chinese mannikins on my screens and moustaches like a grenadier.”

===Chinese===
“什么，难道还有一桩更黑的罪名不成？”蒂博说道——眼见这指控之后赦免来得如此之快，无论起先看着多严重，他心里已渐渐踏实下来了。

“有的，在奥尔良公爵殿下办的那场舞会上。”

“哪场舞会？”

“嗐，就是昨天那场嘛！这也不算多久以前的事，是不是？”

“噢，昨天的舞会？我那时正在赞赏您呢。”

“当真；可我并不在场呀。”

“难道非要您本人在场，简，我才能赞赏您么？难道不能凭着记忆来赞赏您，与亲见您本人一般真切么？况且，您不在场时反倒能从比较中胜出，这胜利岂不更见辉煌。”

“我想也是；而您之所以与博纳伊夫人（Madame de Bonneuil）连跳四回舞，正是为了把这番比较推到极处罢；那些抹了一脸胭脂的深色皮肤女人，生得倒是俊俏，是不是，那眉毛活像我屏风上的中国小人儿，唇髭又像掷弹兵一般。”

===Original===
“Do you know what we talked about during those four dances.”

“It is true then, that you danced four times with her?”

“It is true, no doubt, since you say so.”

“Is that a proper sort of answer?”

“What other could I give? could anyone contradict what was said by so pretty a mouth? not I certainly, who would still bless it, even though it were pronouncing my sentence of death.”

And, as if to await this sentence, Thibault fell on his knees before the Countess, but at that moment, the door opened, and Lisette rushed in full of alarm.

“Ah! Monsieur, Monsieur,” she cried, “save yourself! here comes my master the Count!”

“The Count!” exclaimed the Countess.

“Yes, the Count in person, and his huntsman Lestocq, with him.”

“Impossible!”

“I assure you, Madame, Cramoisi saw them as plain as I see you; the poor fellow was quite pale with fright.”

===Chinese===
“您可知道这四支舞的工夫我们都谈了些什么。”

“那么说，您当真与她跳了四回？”

“想必是真的，既然您这么说。”

“这样的回话，可算得体么？”

“我还能怎样回呢？从这么漂亮的一张嘴里说出的话，谁还能反驳不成？我自然更不能了，哪怕它正宣判我的死刑，我也仍要祝福这张嘴。”

蒂博仿佛是要等候这判决一般，在伯爵夫人跟前跪了下去，可正在这时，门开了，莉赛特满脸惊慌地冲了进来。

“啊！先生，先生，”她叫道，“快逃罢！我家老爷伯爵来了！”

“伯爵！”伯爵夫人失声道。

“是的，伯爵本人，还有他的猎手勒斯托克（Lestocq）跟着呢。”

“不可能！”

“我向您保证，夫人，克拉莫西瞧得清清楚楚，就像我此刻瞧见您一样清楚；那可怜的小伙子吓得脸都白了。”

===Original===
“Ah! then the meet at Thury was all a pretence, a trap to catch me?”

“Who can tell, Madame? Alas! alas! men are such deceiving creatures!”

“What is to be done?” asked the Countess.

“Wait for the Count and kill him,” said Thibault resolutely, furious at again seeing his good fortune escaping from him, at losing what above all things it had been his ambition to possess.

“Kill him! kill the Count? are you mad, Raoul? No, no, you must fly, you must save yourself.﻿ ﻿… Lisette! Lisette! take the Baron through my dressing-room.” And in spite of his resistance, Lisette by dint of pushing got him safely away. Only just in time! steps were heard coming up the wide main staircase. The Countess, with a last word of love to the supposed Raoul, glided quickly into her bedroom, while Thibault followed Lisette. She led him rapidly along the corridor, where Cramoisi was keeping guard at the other end; then into a room, and through this into another, and finally into a smaller one which led into a little tower; here, the fugitives came again on to a staircase corresponding with the one by which they had gone up, but when they reached the bottom they found the door locked. Lisette, with Thibault still following, went back up a few steps into a sort of office in which was a window looking over the garden; this she opened. It was only a few feet from the ground, and Thibault jumped out, landing safely below.

“You know where your horse is,” called Lisette, “jump on its back, and do not stop till you get to Vauparfond.”

===Chinese===
“啊！那么蒂里（Thury）那场聚猎全是幌子，是设了圈套来捉我的了？”

“谁知呢，夫人？唉！唉！男人家最是会骗人的了！”

“这可怎么办？”伯爵夫人问。

“等着伯爵，把他杀了。”蒂博斩钉截铁地说——他眼睁睁看着到手的福气又要从他手边溜走，眼看要失去他这凌驾一切之上的、野心勃勃一心想弄到手的东西，恨得直咬牙。

“杀了他！杀了伯爵？你疯了吗，拉乌尔？不行，不行，你赶快逃，赶快救你自己。……莉赛特！莉赛特！领男爵打我梳妆室那边出去。”蒂博虽还挣扎，莉赛特连推带搡，到底把他安然送走了。真是分秒不差！只听得宽大的正楼梯上已传来上楼的脚步声。伯爵夫人对那她以为是拉乌尔的人丢下最后一句情话，便一溜烟闪进了自己的卧房，蒂博则跟着莉赛特去了。她领着他飞快穿过走廊——克拉莫西正在走廊那一头望风；接着进了一间屋子，穿过这间又进另一间，末了进了一间小些的，通向一座小塔楼；到了这儿，逃命的二人又踏上一道楼梯，与他们方才上来的那道相对应，可赶到楼下，却发现门锁着。莉赛特，蒂博仍跟着她，折回几级，进了一间像是账房的小屋，里头有一扇对着花园的窗子；她把窗子推开。窗子离地不过几尺，蒂博纵身一跃，安然落在下头。

“你知道你的马在哪儿，”莉赛特喊道，“翻身上马，到了沃帕丰（Vauparfond）之前别停下。”

===Original===
Thibault would have liked to thank her for all her kindly warnings, but she was some six feet above him and he had no time to lose. A stride or two brought him to the clump of trees under which stood the little building which served as stable for his horse. But was the horse still there? He heard a neigh which reassured him: only the neigh sounded he thought more like a cry of pain. Thibault went in, put out his hand, felt the horse, gathered up the reins, and leaped on to its back without touching the stirrups; Thibault, as we have already said, had suddenly become a consummate horseman. But the horse no sooner felt the weight of the rider on its back than the poor beast began to totter on its legs. Thibault dug his spurs in savagely, and the horse made a frantic effort to stand. But in another instant, uttering one of those pitiful neighs which Thibault had heard when he approached the stable, it rolled helplessly over on its side. Thibault quickly disengaged his leg from under the animal, which, as the poor thing struggled to rise, he had no difficulty in doing, and he found himself again on his feet. Then it became clear to him, that in order to prevent his escape, Monsieur le Comte de Mont-Gobert had hamstrung his horse.

Thibault uttered an oath: “If I ever meet you, Monsieur Comte de Mont-Gobert,” he said, “I swear that I will hamstring you, as you have hamstrung this poor beast!”

===Chinese===
蒂博本想感谢她这一番好心的示警，可她在他头顶六尺来高的地方，他又一刻也耽搁不起。一两步便赶到了那丛树木底下，树荫中立着那座充作马厩的小屋。可那马还在不在里头呢？他听见一声马嘶，心便放下了：只是那嘶声听来，他觉得更像一声哀鸣。蒂博走进去，伸手一摸，摸着了马，拢起缰绳，也不踩镫便跃上了马背——如前所说，蒂博这会儿已成了一位出神入化的骑手。可那马刚一觉出背上骑手的分量，这可怜的畜生四条腿便打起晃来。蒂博狠狠一夹马刺，那马拼命挣扎着想站稳。可转眼之间，它便发出一声凄厉的嘶鸣——正是蒂博走近马厩时所听见的那一种——身子一歪，无可奈何地翻倒在地。蒂博赶忙把腿从畜生身子底下抽出来，那可怜的东西正挣扎着想站起来，他抽腿倒也并不费力，便又重新站稳了脚跟。到这时他才明白，蒙-戈贝尔伯爵先生（Monsieur le Comte de Mont-Gobert）为了不叫他逃脱，竟将他这匹马的腿筋挑断了。

蒂博咒骂了一声：“蒙-戈贝尔伯爵先生，我若有一日撞见你，”他说，“我发誓也要照样挑断你的腿筋，就像你挑断这可怜畜生的腿筋一样！”

===Original===
Then he rushed out of the little building, and remembering the way he had come, turned in the direction of the breach in the wall, and walking quickly towards it, found it, climbed over the stones, and was again outside the park. But his further passage was barred, for there in front of him was the figure of a man, who stood waiting, with a drawn sword in his hand. Thibault recognised the Comte de Mont-Gobert, the Comte de Mont-Gobert thought he recognised Raoul de Vauparfond.

“Draw, Baron!” said the Count; further explanation was unnecessary. Thibault, on his side, equally enraged at having the prey, on which he had already set tooth and claw, snatched away from him, was as ready to fight as the Count. He drew, not his sword, but his hunting-knife, and the two men crossed weapons.

Thibault, who was something of an adept at quarterstaff, had no idea of fencing; what was his surprise therefore, when he found, that he knew by instinct how to handle his weapon, and could parry and thrust according to all the rules of the art. He parried the first two or three of the Count’s blows with admirable skill.

“Ah, I heard, I remember,” muttered the Count between his clenched teeth, “that at the last match you rivalled Saint-Georges himself at the foils.”

Thibault had no conception who Saint-Georges might be, but he was conscious of a strength and elasticity of wrist, thanks to which he felt he might have rivalled the devil himself.

===Chinese===
说罢他便冲出那座小屋，记得来时的路径，便朝围墙缺口那一边拐去，快步赶过去，寻着了缺口，攀过乱石，又到了园子外头。可再往前去的路已被挡住，只见前头立着一个人影，手执出鞘的长剑，正等着他。蒂博认出是蒙-戈贝尔伯爵（the Comte de Mont-Gobert），蒙-戈贝尔伯爵却自以为认出的是拉乌尔·德·沃帕丰（Raoul de Vauparfond）。

“拔剑罢，男爵！”伯爵说道；别的解释都属多余。蒂博这边呢，眼看那已经咬上爪子、扑上去的猎物又要被人夺走，同样怒不可遏，与伯爵一样巴不得一战。他拔出的不是剑，而是猎刀，两人便交起手来。

蒂博耍短棍倒还算是把好手，剑术却一窍不通；因此，当他发觉自己竟凭着本能便会使弄手中家伙，进退招架无不合乎这一门功夫的规矩，那份诧异可想而知。伯爵头两三剑，都被他以令人叹服的巧劲儿挡了开去。

“啊，我听人说过，我也记得，”伯爵咬紧牙关咕哝道，“上回那场比试，你的花剑功夫竟连圣乔治（Saint-Georges）本人在你面前也要甘拜下风。”

蒂博压根儿不知道这圣乔治是何许人也，他只觉得腕子里有一股子力气与韧劲，凭这股劲儿，他相信便是那魔鬼本尊亲自来比，他也能与之一较高下。

===Original===
So far, he had only been on the defensive; but the Count having aimed one or two unsuccessful lunges at him, he saw his opportunity, struck out, and sent his knife clean through his adversary’s shoulder. The Count dropped his sword, tottered, and falling on to one knee, cried “Help, Lestocq!”

Thibault ought then to have sheathed his knife and fled; but, unfortunately, he remembered the oath he had taken as regards the Count, when he had found that his horse had been hamstrung. He slipped the sharp blade of his weapon under the bent knee and drew it towards him; the Count uttered a cry; but as Thibault rose from his stooping posture, he too felt a sharp pain between his shoulder-blades, followed by a sensation as of extreme cold over the chest, and finally the point of a weapon appeared above his right breast. Then he saw a cloud of blood, and knew no more. Lestocq, called to his master’s aid as the latter fell, had run to the spot, and, as Thibault rose from hamstringing the Count, had seized that moment to dig his hunting knife into his back.

===Chinese===
方才他一味只是招架；可伯爵朝他刺了一两剑都未得手，被他觑见破绽，便还了一手，一刀干干净净地捅穿了对手的肩膀。伯爵的长剑落地，身子一个趔趄，单膝跪倒，喊了一声：“勒斯托克，快来！”

蒂博这时候原该收刀逃走的；可惜，他记起了方才发现马被挑断腿筋时对伯爵所发的那一誓。他把锋利的刀刃顺那曲着的膝盖底下塞过去，朝自己这边一拉；伯爵惨叫一声；可蒂博从那弯腰的姿势里直起身子时，自己也觉出两块肩胛骨之间一阵剧痛，紧接着胸口掠过一阵彻骨的寒意，末了，一柄兵器的尖头从他的右胸上方探了出来。随后他眼前涌起一片血雾，便人事不省了。原来，勒斯托克听见主人倒地时那声呼救，已飞奔赶到，趁着蒂博挑断伯爵腿筋、直起身来的那一瞬，一猎刀捅进了他的后背。

## VI The Bedevilled Hair / VI 中了邪的头发

===Original===
The huntsmen, being reassured with regard to their master’s health, now went in search of the dogs, which had been left to carry on the chase alone. They were found lying asleep, the ground around them stained with blood. It was evident that they had run down the buck and eaten it; if any doubt on the matter remained, it was done away with by the sight of the antlers, and a portion of the jaw bone, the only parts of the animal which they could not crunch up, and which had therefore not disappeared. In short, they were the only ones who had cause to be satisfied with the day’s work. The huntsmen, after shutting up the hounds in Thibault’s shed, seeing that their master was still sleeping, began to turn their thoughts to getting some supper. They laid hands on everything they could find in the poor wretch’s cupboard, and roasted the goat, politely inviting Thibault to take a share in the meal towards the cost of which he had not a little contributed. He refused, giving as a plausible excuse, the great agitation he still was under, owing to Marcotte’s death and the Baron’s accident.

===Chinese===
猎手们见主子的身子骨已无大碍，这才放心，转去找寻那群被撇下独自追猎的猎犬。只见那些狗正趴在地上睡大觉，周遭的地皮被血污得斑斑点点。显而易见，它们已把那头雄鹿追倒吃掉了；倘若对此还有什么疑心，一瞧那对鹿角和一截颌骨便尽消了——那是整头畜生身上猎犬们嚼不烂、故而没能下肚的唯一部分。总而言之，这一整天下来，唯有它们几个是心满意足的。猎手们把猎犬关进蒂博的披屋，见主子还在酣睡，便动起了张罗晚饭的念头。他们把能从这可怜虫碗橱里翻出来的东西一概拿下手来，把那只山羊也烤了，倒还算客气，邀请蒂博也来入席分享——这顿饭的用度，他可是出了不小的力。蒂博推辞不去，托了一个说得过去的由头：说马尔科特（Marcotte）丧了命、男爵又遭了意外，他至今还惊魂未定，心绪难平。

===Original===
He gathered up the fragments of his beloved drinking-cup, and seeing that it was useless to think of ever being able to put it together again, he began turning over in his mind what it might be possible for him to do, so as to free himself from the miserable existence which the events of the last two days had rendered more insupportable than ever. The first image that appeared to him, was that of Agnelette. Like the beautiful angels that pass before the eyes of children in their dreams, he saw her figure, dressed all in white, with large white wings, floating across a blue sky. She seemed happy and beckoned to him to follow, saying the while “Those who come with me will be very happy.” But the only answer which Thibault vouchsafed to this charming vision was a movement of the head and shoulders, which interpreted, meant, “Yes, yes, Agnelette, I see you, and recognise you; yesterday, it would have been all very well to follow you; but today I am, like a king, the arbiter of life and death, and I am not the man to make foolish concessions to a love only born a day ago, and which has hardly learnt to stammer out its first words. To marry you, my poor child, far from lessening the bitter hardships of our lives, would only double or treble the burden under which we are both borne down. No, Agnelette, no! You would make a charming mistress; but, a wife﻿—she must be in a position to bring money to support the household, equal in proportion to the power which I should contribute.”

===Chinese===
他把那只心爱酒杯的碎瓷片一一拾起，眼见想再把它拼回原样已是痴心妄想，便在肚里盘算起来：自己到底还能做些什么，好摆脱这桩苦日子——过去两日发生的种种，已把这苦日子熬得比从前更叫人难以忍受了。头一个浮现在他心头的，是阿涅莱特（Agnelette）的身影。如同孩童梦中那翩翩掠过眼前的美丽天使一般，他看见她的身形，通体缟素，生着一双硕大的白翅膀，悠悠飘过一片湛蓝的天空。她满面喜色，招手唤他跟上来，一边说道：“随我同去的人，必定快活。”可蒂博对这迷人的幻象唯一的回礼，不过是把头和肩膀那么一耸，这动作译作言语，便是：“是啦，是啦，阿涅莱特，我瞧见你了，也认出你了；换作昨日，跟着你走倒也罢了；可今日的我，已如帝王一般，操着生杀予夺之权，我可不是那种会为一段昨日才落地、才咿呀着学会吐出第一个字的情爱，便做出糊涂让步的人。娶了你，我可怜的孩子，非但减轻不了咱俩生计上的苦楚，反倒要把你我肩头都压垮了的那副重担再加倍、再加三倍。不，阿涅莱特，不！你做个可人的情人倒还使得；可做起妻子来——她总该带得出一笔与我所贡献的权力相称的银钱，好撑持这个家才是。”

===Original===
His conscience told him plainly that he was engaged to marry Agnelette; but he quieted it with the assurance that if he broke the engagement, it would be for the good of that gentle creature.

“I am an upright man,” he murmured to himself, “and it is my duty to sacrifice my personal pleasure to the welfare of the dear child. And more than that, she is sufficiently young and pretty and good, to find a better fate than what would await her as the wife of a plain sabot maker.” And the end of all these fine reflections was, that Thibault felt himself bound to allow his foolish promises of the day before to melt away into air, and to forget the betrothal, of which the only witnesses had been the quivering leaves of the birch trees, and the pink blossom of the heather. It should be added that there was another mental vision, not wholly irresponsible for the resolution at which Thibault had arrived﻿—the vision of a certain young widow, owner of the mill at Croyolles, a woman between twenty-six and twenty-eight, fresh and plump, with fine, rolling eyes, not devoid of mischief. Moreover, she was credibly supposed to be the richest match in all the country side, for her mill was never idle, and so, for all reasons, as one can clearly see, it was the very thing for Thibault.

===Chinese===
良心明明白白地告诉他，他已与阿涅莱特有了婚约在身；可他却拿一套说辞把它安抚住了：倘若他悔了这亲事，那也是为这温柔人儿的好处着想。

“我是个正直的人，”他暗自咕哝道，“为这好孩子的福祉牺牲我一己的快乐，原是我分内之事。再者说，她又年轻、又漂亮、又心地好，尽可以找到一条比我这么个粗鄙木屐匠的妻子强得多的命路。”这一通冠冕堂皇的思量到了末了，便是蒂博认定自己不得不由着昨日那些痴傻的誓言化作一缕青烟散去，不得不把那桩订婚忘个干净——那订婚的唯一见证，不过是白桦树簌簌颤动的叶片，和石楠花粉红的细碎花朵罢了。还得补上一句：另有一样心头的影像，对蒂博所下的这个决心也不能说全无干系——那便是某位年轻寡妇的倩影，克鲁瓦约尔磨坊（the mill at Croyolles）的老板娘，年方二十六到二十八之间，白嫩丰腴，一双水灵灵会说话的眼睛，眼角眉梢透着点儿促狭劲儿。况且，众人皆信她是方圆一带最有钱的亲事，因她那磨坊从不停歇；如此种种缘由，谁都能看得明白，这于蒂博正是再合适不过的了。

===Original===
Formerly, it would never have occurred to Thibault to aspire to anyone in the position of the rich and beautiful Madame Polet, for such was the name of the owner of the mill; and this will explain why her name is introduced here for the first time. And, in truth, it was the first time that she had ever occurred as a subject of serious consideration to our hero. He was astonished at himself for not having thought of her before, but then, as he said to himself, he *had* often thought about her, but without hope, while now, seeing that he was under the protection of the wolf, and that he had been endowed with a supernatural power, which he had already had occasion to exercise, it seemed to him an easy matter to get rid of all his rivals and achieve his purpose. True there were evil tongues that spoke of the owner of the mill as having something of an ill-temper and a hard heart; but the shoemaker came to the conclusion that, with the devil up his sleeve, he need not trouble himself about any wicked spirit, any petty little second-class demon that might find a corner in Widow Polet’s disposition. And so, by the time the day broke, he had decided to go to Croyolles, for all these visions had of course visited him during the night.

===Chinese===
从前，蒂博是断断不敢奢望一位地位如又富又美的波莱太太（Madame Polet）那样的女子——这便是那位磨坊老板娘的名字；这也便解释了她的名字何以到此处才头一回登场。说句实在话，这也是她头一遭作为一桩正经盘算的题目，进到我们这位主人公的脑海里。他对自己竟不曾早些想到她而诧异不已，可随即又自我宽解道：他从前倒也常常想起她，只是从不敢存那指望罢了；而如今，既有那头狼庇护着他，又得了那般他已然施展过的超自然之力，那么扫清一切情敌、如愿以偿，在他看来便是易如反掌的事了。诚然，也有那等长舌之徒说磨坊老板娘的闲话，道是她脾气有些乖戾、心肠也有些硬；可这位鞋匠心里盘算定了：既有魔鬼给他撑腰，波莱寡妇脾性里万一藏着什么邪性、什么二流的小鬼小妖，他又何须放在心上。于是，等到天色破晓时分，他已决意动身去克鲁瓦约尔了——原来这一幕幕幻象，自是夜里来拜访他的。

===Original===
The Lord of Vez awoke with the first song of the birds; he had entirely recovered from his indisposition of the day before, and woke up his followers with loud slashings of his whip. Having sent off Marcotte’s body to Vez, he decided that he would not return home without having killed something, but that he would hunt the boar, just as if nothing out of the way had taken place on the previous day. At last, about six o’clock in the morning, they all went off, the Baron assuring Thibault that he was most grateful to him for the hospitality that he himself and his men and dogs had met with under his poor roof, in consideration of which he was quite willing, he swore, to forget all the grievances which he had against the shoemaker.

===Chinese===
韦兹领主（the Lord of Vez）在第一声鸟啼里醒了过来；他昨日那点不适已好生利落了，便甩开马鞭噼里啪啦一顿响，把随从们都唤了起来。打发人把马尔科特的遗体送回韦兹之后，他决意总得猎杀点什么才肯回家，便要去围猎野猪，权当头一天什么事也没出过似的。末了，约莫早晨六点钟光景，这一行人马统统离去，男爵向蒂博担保，说自己对这穷檐之下款待他本人及其人马猎犬的这份款待，着实感激不尽；念在这份情分上，他发誓，他倒很情愿把他对这位鞋匠积下的种种旧怨一笔勾销。

===Original===
It will be easily guessed that Thibault experienced little regret at the departure of lord, dogs, and huntsmen. All these having at last disappeared, he stood a few moments contemplating his ransacked home, his empty cupboard, his broken furniture, his empty shed, the ground scattered with fragments of his belongings. But, as he told himself, all this was the ordinary thing to happen whenever one of the great lords went through a place, and the future, as it appeared to him, was far too brilliant to allow him to dwell long on this spectacle. He dressed himself in his Sunday attire, smartening himself as best he could, ate his last bit of bread with the last morsel left of his goat, went to the spring and drank a large glass of water, and started off for Croyolles. Thibault was determined to try his fortune with Madame Polet before the day was over, and therefore set out about nine o’clock in the morning.

===Chinese===
不难想见，领主、猎犬和猎手们的离去，蒂博并未感到多少惋惜。这一干人等终于尽数消失之后，他在原地站了片刻，打量着自己被翻得底朝天的家：空空如也的碗橱，打烂的家具，空荡荡的披屋，地上散落着家什的残片。可正如他自个儿劝慰自己的那样，但凡一位大老爷打一处地方过境，这一切原是家常便饭；而摆在他眼前的那个将来，又实在太过灿烂，不容他在这一幕惨景上多作停留。他换上做礼拜的那身行头，尽可能地把自己收拾齐整，就着山羊身上剩下来的最后那一丁点肉，吃掉了他最后一块面包，又去泉边灌下一大杯水，便动身往克鲁瓦约尔去了。蒂博打定主意，非要趁着这一日还没过完，便去波莱太太那里碰一碰自己的运气，于是早晨九点光景便上了路。

===Original===
The shortest way to Croyolles was round by the rear of Oigny and Pisseleu. Now Thibault knew every in and out of the forest of Villers-Cotterets as well as any tailor knows the pockets he has made; why, therefore, did he take the Chrétiennelle track, seeing that it lengthened his journey by a good mile and a half? Reader, it was because this lane would bring him near to the spot where he had first seen Agnelette, for, although practical considerations were carrying him in the direction of Croyolles Mill, his heart was drawing him towards Préciamont. And there, as fate would have it, just after crossing the road that runs to La Ferté-Milou, he came upon Agnelette, cutting grass by the wayside for her goats. He might easily have passed her without being seen, for her back was turned towards him; but the evil spirit prompted him, and he went straight up to her. She was stooping to cut the grass with her sickle, but hearing someone approaching she lifted her head, and blushed as she recognised that it was Thibault. With the blush a happy smile rose to her face, which showed that the rising colour was not due to any feeling of hostility towards him.

===Chinese===
去克鲁瓦约尔最近的路，是绕过瓦尼（Oigny）和皮瑟勒（Pisseleu）的后面走。蒂博对维莱-科特雷（Villers-Cotterets）森林里每一处犄角旮旯的熟悉，原不亚于任何一个裁缝对自己亲手缝的口袋那般了如指掌；那么，他为何偏偏要取道克雷蒂埃内尔（Chrétiennelle）那条小径，白白把路程多绕出一英里半去呢？看官，那是因为这条小巷能把他带到他头一回遇见阿涅莱特的那处地方附近——原来，虽说现实的盘算正把他往克鲁瓦约尔磨坊那一头拽，他的心却把他往普雷西亚蒙（Préciamont）那边引。而就在那里，命中注定一般，他刚穿过那条通往费尔泰-米卢（La Ferté-Milou）的大路，便撞见了阿涅莱特——她正在路旁割草，给她的山羊预备草料。他本可以轻而易举地从她身边走过去而不被瞧见，因她正背对着他；可那恶灵却在一旁撺掇，他便径直朝她走了过去。她正弯着腰用镰刀割草，听见有人走近，便抬起头来，认出是蒂博，脸上一红。伴随着这阵红晕，一个欢喜的笑意涌上她的面庞，可见这腾起的绯色并非出于对他的什么敌意。

===Original===
“Ah! there you are,” she said. “I dreamt much of you last night, and prayed many prayers for you also.” And as she spoke, the vision of Agnelette passing along the sky, with the dress and wings of an Angel, and her hands joined in supplication, as he had seen her the previous night, returned to him.

“And what made you dream of me and pray for me, my pretty child?” asked Thibault with as unconcerned an air as a young lord at Court. Agnelette looked at him with her large eyes of heavenly blue.

“I dreamed of you, Thibault, because I love you,” she said, “and I prayed for you, because I saw the accident that happened to the Baron and his huntsmen, and all the trouble that you were put to in consequence.﻿ ﻿… Ah! if I had been able to obey the dictates of my heart, I should have run to you at once to give you help.”

===Chinese===
“啊！你在这儿呀，”她说，“我昨夜梦见你好多回，也为你念了好些祷告。”她说话间，蒂博眼前又浮起阿涅莱特掠过天际的身影——一身天使的衣裳、一双天使的翅膀，双手合十作祈求之状，正如他头天夜里见她的模样。

“什么事叫你梦见我、又为我祷告呢，我漂亮的孩子？”蒂博问道，神态之从容，活像个宫廷里的年轻贵胄。阿涅莱特用她那一双天蓝色的、大大的眼睛望着他。

“我梦见你，蒂博，是因为我爱你，”她说，“我为你祷告，是因为我瞧见男爵和他的猎手们遭了祸，又瞧见你为此受了多少连累……啊！倘若我能依着自己心里的念头去做，我早就一口气奔到你身边来帮你了。”

===Original===
“It is a pity you did not come; you would have found a merry company, I can tell you.”

“Oh! it was not for that I should have liked to be with you, but to be of use to you in receiving the Baron and his train. Oh! what a beautiful ring you have, Monsieur Thibault, where did you get it?”

And the girl pointed to the ring which had been given to Thibault by the wolf. Thibault felt his blood run cold. “This ring?” he said.

“Yes, that ring,” and seeing that Thibault appeared unwilling to answer her, Agnelette turned her head aside, and sighed. “A present from some fine lady, I suppose,” she said in a low voice.

“There you are mistaken, Agnelette,” replied Thibault with all the assurance of a consummate liar, “it is our betrothal ring, the one I have bought to put on your finger the day we are married.”

===Chinese===
“可惜你没来；我倒可以告诉你，你准会瞧见一伙快快活活的人马。”

“噢！我之所以想陪在你身边，可不是为了那个，是为了在男爵和他那一行人到来时，能替你帮上一把。咦！好漂亮的一枚戒指啊，蒂博先生，您这是从哪儿得来的？”

姑娘指的，正是那头狼给蒂博的那枚戒指。蒂博只觉浑身的血一凉。“这戒指？”他说。

“是啊，就是那枚戒指。”见蒂博似乎不大情愿回答她，阿涅莱特便把头偏过一边，叹了口气。“想必是哪位体面太太送的礼吧。”她低声说道。

“这回你可猜错了，阿涅莱特，”蒂博答道，拿出了一个撒谎老手的十足镇定，“这是咱俩的订婚戒指，是我特地买来，要在咱俩成亲那天给你戴到手指上去的。”

===Original===
“Why not tell me the truth, Monsieur Thibault?” said Agnelette, shaking her head sadly.

“I am speaking the truth, Agnelette.”

“No,” and she shook her head more sadly than before.

“And what makes you think that I am telling a lie?”

“Because the ring is large enough to go over two of my fingers.” And Thibault’s finger would certainly have made two of Agnelette’s.

“If it is too large, Agnelette,” he said, “we can have it made smaller.”

“Goodbye, Monsieur Thibault.”

“What! Goodbye?”

“Yes.”

“You are going to leave me?”

“Yes, I am going.”

“And why, Agnelette?”

“Because I do not love liars.”

===Chinese===
“您何不跟我说实话呢，蒂博先生？”阿涅莱特说道，伤心地摇了摇头。

“我说的句句是实话，阿涅莱特。”

“不。”她摇着头，比先前越发伤心了。

“那你怎么知道我是在撒谎呢？”

“因为这枚戒指大得能套下我两根手指。”而蒂博的一根手指，管保能抵得上阿涅莱特的两根。

“要是它太大，阿涅莱特，”他说，“咱们可以拿去改小些。”

“再见了，蒂博先生。”

“什么！再见？”

“是的。”

“你这就走了？”

“是的，我走了。”

“这是为什么呀，阿涅莱特？”

“因为我不爱撒谎的人。”

===Original===
Thibault tried to think of some vow he could make to reassure Agnelette, but in vain.

“Listen,” said Agnelette, with tears in her eyes, for it was not without a great effort of self-control that she was turning away, “if that ring is really meant for me.﻿ ﻿…”

“Agnelette, I swear to you that it is.”

“Well then, give it me to keep till our wedding day, and on that day I will give it back to you, that you may have it blessed.”

“I will give it you with all my heart,” replied Thibault, “but I want to see it on your pretty hand. You were right in saying that it was too large for you, and I am going into Villers-Cotterets today, we will take the measure of your finger, and I will get Monsieur Dugué, the goldsmith there, to alter it for us.”

===Chinese===
蒂博想寻出一句什么誓言来安抚阿涅莱特，却是白费力气。

“您听着，”阿涅莱特眼里含着泪说道——她这一转身离去，原是下了好大一番克制的功夫才做到的，“倘若那枚戒指当真是给我的……”

“阿涅莱特，我向你发誓，它是给你的。”

“那么，您就把它交给我收着，留到咱们成亲那天，到那天我再把戒指还给您，好让您拿去请神父祝圣。”

“我心甘情愿把它给你，”蒂博答道，“可我想瞧瞧它戴在你这只俏手上的模样。你说它太大了，这话原不错，我今日就要进维莱-科特雷城里去，咱们把你的指头量一量尺寸，我再拿去找城里的金匠迪盖先生（Monsieur Dugué），请他替咱们改一改。”

===Original===
The smile returned to Agnelette’s face and her tears were dried up at once. She put out her little hand; Thibault took it between his own, turned it over and looked at it, first on the back and then on the palm, and stooping, kissed it.

“Oh!” said Agnelette, “you should not kiss my hand, Monsieur Thibault, it is not pretty enough.”

“Give me something else then to kiss.” And Agnelette lifted her face that he might kiss her on the forehead.

“And now,” she said joyously, and with childish eagerness, “let me see the ring.”

===Chinese===
笑意重又回到阿涅莱特脸上，眼泪也顿时干透了。她伸出那只小手来；蒂博把它攥在自个儿两只手当中，翻来覆去地端详，先看手背，再看手心，又弯下腰去，吻了它一下。

“哎呀！”阿涅莱特说，“您不该亲我的手，蒂博先生，我的手不够好看。”

“那给我亲点别的什么吧。”于是阿涅莱特抬起脸来，好让他亲一亲她的额头。

“这会儿，”她欢欢喜喜地、带着孩子气的急切说道，“让我瞧瞧那枚戒指吧。”

===Original===
Thibault drew off the ring, and laughing, tried to put it on Agnelette’s thumb; but, to his great astonishment, he could not get it over the joint. “Well, well,” he exclaimed, “who would ever have thought such a thing?”

Agnelette began to laugh. “It is funny, isn’t it!” she said.

Then Thibault tried to pass it over the first finger, but with the same result as when he put it on the thumb. He next tried the middle finger, but the ring seemed to grow smaller and smaller, as if fearing to sully this virgin hand; then the third finger, the same on which he wore it himself, but it was equally impossible to get it on. And as he made these vain attempts to fit the ring, Thibault felt Agnelette’s hand trembling more and more violently within his own, while the sweat fell from his own brow, as if he were engaged in the most arduous work; there was something diabolic at the bottom of it, as he knew quite well. At last he came to the little finger and endeavoured to pass the ring over it. This little finger, so small and transparent, that the ring should have hung as loosely upon it as a bracelet on one of Thibault’s, this little finger, in spite of all Agnelette’s efforts, refused to pass through the ring. “Ah! my God, Monsieur Thibault,” cried the child “what does this mean?”

===Chinese===
蒂博褪下戒指，笑着，想把它套到阿涅莱特的大拇指上；可叫他大吃一惊的是，他竟没法把它套过那节骨眼去。“哎，哎，”他叫道，“谁能想到还有这种怪事！”

阿涅莱特笑出声来。“真逗，是不是！”她说。

蒂博便又去试那根食指，可结果跟套大拇指时一模一样。他接着去试中指，可那枚戒指竟像是越缩越小，仿佛不敢亵渎这只处女的手；又去试无名指——正是他自己戴着这戒指的那一根——可同样没法套上去。他这般徒劳地想把戒指套上，蒂博只觉得阿涅莱特那只手在自己掌心里越抖越厉害，而他自己额上的汗珠子直往下淌，仿佛正干着一桩最吃重的活计；这底下藏着什么邪门的玩意儿，他心里再清楚不过了。末了，他到了那根小指头，想方设法要把戒指套过去。这根小指头，又小又透亮，戒指本该套上去松松垮垮，活像一只手镯套在蒂博的手腕上一般——可这根小指头，任凭阿涅莱特如何用力，也怎么都不肯穿过那枚戒指去。“啊！我的天哪，蒂博先生，”这孩子叫道，“这是怎么回事呀？”

===Original===
“Ring of the Devil, return to the Devil!” cried Thibault, flinging the ring against a rock, in the hope that it would be broken. As it struck the rock, it emitted flame; then it rebounded, and in rebounding, fitted itself on to Thibault’s finger. Agnelette who saw this strange evolution of the ring, looked at Thibault in horrified amazement. “Well,” he said, trying to brave it out, “what is the matter?”

Agnelette did not answer, but as she continued to look at Thibault, her eye grew more and more wild and frightened. Thibault could not think what she was looking at, but slowly lifting her hand and pointing with her finger at Thibault’s head, she said, “Oh! Monsieur Thibault, Monsieur Thibault, what have you got there?”

“Where?” asked Thibault.

“There! there!” cried Agnelette, growing paler and paler.

“Well, but where?” cried the shoemaker, stamping with his foot. “Tell me what you see.”

But instead of replying, Agnelette covered her face with her hands, and uttering a cry of terror, turned and fled with all her might.

===Chinese===
“魔鬼的戒指，回到魔鬼那里去吧！”蒂博叫道，把戒指朝一块岩石狠狠掷去，指望能把它摔个粉碎。戒指一撞上岩石，便噗地冒出一团火苗；接着它一弹而起，弹跳之间，竟自己套回了蒂博的手指上。阿涅莱特瞧见戒指这番古怪的变戏法，又惊又惧地望着蒂博。“怎么啦，”他强作镇定地说，“出什么事了？”

阿涅莱特没有作答，可她仍旧盯着蒂博瞧，眼神越发变得狂乱而惊恐。蒂博想不出她到底在看什么，只见她缓缓抬起一只手，用手指点着蒂博的头，说道：“啊！蒂博先生，蒂博先生，您那头上是什么呀？”

“哪儿？”蒂博问。

“那儿！那儿！”阿涅莱特叫道，脸色越来越白。

“到底哪儿呀？”鞋匠跺着脚嚷道，“你告诉我你瞧见什么了。”

可阿涅莱特并不答话，只把两手捂住脸，发出一声惊恐的尖叫，转身拼了命地逃走了。

===Original===
Thibault, stunned by what had happened, did not even attempt to follow her; he stood rooted to the spot, unable to move or speak, as if thunderstruck.

What had Agnelette seen that had alarmed her so? What was it that she pointed to with her finger? Had God branded him, as He branded the first murderer? And why not? Had not he, like Cain, killed a man? and in the last sermon he had heard at Oigny had not the preacher said that all men were brothers? Thibault felt wild with misgivings; what had so terrified Agnelette? That he must find out without delay. At first he thought he would go into the town of Bourg-Fontaine and look at himself in a glass. But then, supposing the fatal mark was upon him, and others, besides Agnelette, were to see it! No, he must think of some other way of finding out. He could, of course, pull his hat over his brow, and run back to Oigny, where he had a fragment of mirror in which he could see himself; but Oigny was a long way off. Then he remembered that only a few paces from where he stood, there was a spring as transparent as crystal, which fed the pond near Baisemont, and those at Bourg; he would be able to see himself in that as clearly as in the finest mirror from Saint-Gobain. So Thibault went to the side of the stream and, kneeling down, looked at himself. He saw the same eyes, the same nose, and the same mouth, and not even the slightest little mark upon the forehead﻿—he drew a breath of relief. But still, there must have been something. Agnelette had certainly not taken fright as she had, for nothing. Thibault bent over closer to the crystal water; and now he saw there was something bright that shone amid the dark curls on his head and fell over his forehead. He leaned closer still﻿—it was a red hair. A red hair, but of a most peculiar red﻿—not sandy coloured or carrotty; neither of a light shade nor a dark; but a red of the colour of blood, with a brightness of the most vivid flame.

===Chinese===
蒂博被方才发生的事惊得呆了，竟连追上去追也没有追；他像被雷劈中了一般，钉在了原地，动弹不得，也作声不得。

阿涅莱特到底瞧见了什么，才吓成那样？她用手指点的又究竟是什么？莫非是上帝在他身上打了记号，就像给头一个杀人凶手打的记号一样？又为什么不会呢？他岂不是跟该隐（Cain）一样，杀过一个人？再说，他上一回在瓦尼听的那篇讲道里，那讲道的人不还说众人都是兄弟么？蒂博心里七上八下，满腹疑惧：究竟是什么把阿涅莱特吓成了那样？这件事他非得马上弄个水落石出不可。他起初想进布尔丰泉（Bourg-Fontaine）城里去，找面镜子照照自个儿。可转念一想，万一这要命的记号真在身上，被阿涅莱特之外的人也瞧见了，那还了得！不行，他得另想法子打听明白。他当然可以把帽子往眉骨上一压，奔回瓦尼去——那儿他有一小片镜子，可以照见自己；可瓦尼路途不近。这时他想起，离他所站之处不过几步开外，便有一汪泉水，清澈如水晶，那泉便是贝斯蒙（Baisemont）附近那口池塘、还有布尔（Bourg）那几口水塘的水源；他尽可以从中照见自己，照得跟圣戈班（Saint-Gobain）出产的上好镜子一般清楚。于是蒂博走到溪边，跪下身来，朝水里照了照。他瞧见的是同一双眼睛、同一个鼻子、同一张嘴，额头上连一丁点儿记号也没有——他长长地舒了一口气。可总该有点什么才是。阿涅莱特断不至于无缘无故吓成那个样子。蒂博把身子更凑近那水晶般的水面；这一回，他瞧见自己头顶那一片黑色的鬈发当中，有一点亮晶晶的东西在闪闪发亮，垂到了额前。他再把脸凑近些——那是一根红发。一根红发，可那红的颜色古怪至极——既不是沙褐色，也不是胡萝卜色；既不算浅，也不算深；而是一种血一般的红，带着最鲜明炽烈的火焰般的亮光。

===Original===
Without stopping to consider how a hair of such a phenomenal colour could possibly have grown there, he began trying to pull it out. He drew forward the curl where gleamed the terrible red hair, that it might hang over the water, and then taking hold of it carefully between his finger and thumb gave it a violent pull; but the hair refused to come away. Thinking that he had not got sufficient hold of it, he tried another way, winding the hair round his finger and again giving it a vigorous jerk﻿—the hair cut into his fingers but remained as firmly rooted as ever. Thibault then turned it round two of his fingers and pulled again; the hair lifted a little bit of his scalp, but as to moving, Thibault might as well have tried to move the oak that threw its shady branches across the stream. Thibault began to think that he would do better to continue his walk to Croyolles; after all, as he remarked to himself, the questionable colour of a single hair would not hardly upset his plans of marriage. Nevertheless, the wretched hair caused him a great deal of worry; he could not get it out of his mind, it seemed to dance before his eyes, dazzling him like flames of running fire, until at last, out of all patience, he stamped his foot, exclaiming, “By all the devils in hell! I am not far from home yet, and I’ll get the better of this confounded hair somehow.” Whereupon he set off running back towards his hut, went in and found his fragment of mirror, got hold of his hair again, seized a carpenter’s chisel, placed it so as to cut off the hair as close to the head as possible, and keeping hair and tool in this position, leant over his bench, and dug the chisel down with as much force as possible. The tool cut deeply into the wood of the bench, but the hair remained intact. He tried the same plan again, only this time he armed himself with a mallet, which he swung over his head and brought down with redoubled blows on the handle of the chisel﻿—but he was as far as ever from carrying out his purpose. He noticed, however, that there was a little notch in the sharp edge of the chisel, just the width of a hair. Thibault sighed; he understood now that this hair, the price he had paid in return for his wish, belonged to the black wolf, and he gave up all further attempts to get rid of it.

===Chinese===
他也顾不得琢磨这么一种颜色奇诡的头发，怎会凭空长到这儿来，便动手要把它拔掉。他把那绺闪着可怖红光的鬈发往前拢了拢，好让它垂到水面上头，然后用拇指和食指小心翼翼地捏住，狠狠一拽——可那根头发就是不肯下来。他只当是自己捏得不紧，便另换一个法子，把那根头发在手指上缠了几圈，又使劲一扯——那头发勒进了他的指头里，却依旧岿然不动，扎得牢牢的。蒂博又把它在两根手指上绕了两绕，再拔一回；这回倒掀起了一小块头皮，可要说把它拔下来，蒂博还不如去试试能不能撼动那棵把浓荫撒在溪面上的橡树。蒂博便盘算着，倒不如还是照旧往克鲁瓦约尔走去；毕竟，他自个儿宽慰自个儿地说，一根头发颜色古怪些，总不至于坏了他的婚事大计。话虽如此，这根要命的头发还是叫他烦心不已；他没法把它从脑子里赶出去，它仿佛在他眼前直晃悠，像一道道流火般晃得他眼花，直到末了，他再也耐不住了，把脚一跺，嚷道：“凭地狱里众小鬼起誓！我家离这儿不算远，我总有法子收拾这根该死的头发。”说罢他便撒腿往自家小屋飞奔回去，一进屋便寻出那片破镜子的碎块，重新把那绺头发抓在手里，又摸出一把木匠用的凿子来，把凿子贴着头皮比好，好尽量贴根把头发切断，就这么一手捏着头发、一手拿着家伙地比定了姿势，俯身在木工台子上，使出吃奶的力气把凿子猛地朝下一摁。那家什深深地切进了台子的木头里，可那根头发却纹丝未损。他又照样来了一回，只是这一回他多备了一把木槌，抡过头顶，一下又一下加倍的力气砸在凿子柄上——可离他的目的还是一样地遥远。然而他注意到，那凿子锋利的刃口上崩出了一个小小的缺口，宽窄恰好是一根头发丝。蒂博叹了口气；他这回算是明白了：这根头发，是他为那个愿望所付的代价，原是属于那头黑狼的，于是他再不作摆脱它的打算了。

## IV The Black Wolf / IV 黑狼

===Original===
Thibault’s first thought was to get himself some supper, for he was terribly tired. The past day had been an eventful one for him, and certain things which had happened to him had evidently been calculated to produce a craving for food. The supper, it must be said, was not quite such a savoury one as he had promised himself, when starting to kill the buck; but the animal, as we know, had not been killed by Thibault, and the ferocious hunger which now consumed him made his black bread taste almost as delicious as venison.

===Chinese===
蒂博头一个念头便是弄点晚饭吃，因为他累得够呛。这一天对他而言真可谓多事之秋，而他所遭遇的某些事情，分明是专门用来勾起人的饥火的。这顿晚饭，得说一句，远不如他动身去猎那头雄鹿时所盘算的那般可口；然而那畜生——如我们所知——并非蒂博所杀，此刻吞噬着他的那阵凶猛饥饿，竟使他觉得黑面包吃起来也几乎和鹿肉一般鲜美。

===Original===
He had hardly, however, begun his frugal repast, when he became conscious that his goat﻿—of which I think we have already spoken﻿—was uttering the most plaintive bleatings. Thinking that she, too, was in want of her supper, he went into the lean-to for some fresh grass, which he then carried to her, but as he opened the little door of the shed, out she rushed with such precipitancy that she nearly knocked Thibault over, and without stopping to take the provender he had brought her, ran towards the house. Thibault threw down the bundle of grass and went after her, with the intention of reinstalling her in her proper place; but he found that this was more than he was able to do. He had to use all his force to get her along, for the goat, with all the strength of which a beast of her kind is capable, resisted all his efforts to drag her back by the horns, arching her back, and stubbornly refusing to move. At last, however, being vanquished in the struggle, it ended by the goat being once more shut up in her shed, but, in spite of the plentiful supper which Thibault left her with, she continued to utter the most lamentable cries. Perplexed, and cross at the same time, the shoemaker again rose from his supper and went to the shed, this time opening the door so cautiously that the goat could not escape. Once inside he began feeling about with his hands in all the nooks and corners to try and discover the cause of her alarm. Suddenly his fingers came in contact with the warm, thick coat of some other animal. Thibault was not a coward, far from it, none the less, he drew back hastily. He returned to the house and got a light, but it almost fell from his hand, when, on reentering the shed, he recognised in the animal that had so frightened the goat, the buck of the Lord of Vez; the same buck that he had followed, had failed to kill, that he had prayed for in the devil’s name, if he could not have it in God’s; the same that had thrown the hounds out; the very same in short which had cost him such hard blows. Thibault, after assuring himself that the door was fastened, went gently up to the animal; the poor thing was either so tired, or so tame, that it did not make the slightest attempt to move, but merely gazed out at Thibault with its large dark velvety eyes, rendered more appealing than ever by the fear which agitated it.

===Chinese===
然而他这份寒酸的饭食才刚开头，便觉察到他那头山羊——我想我们已经提起过这头羊了——正发出最为哀切的咩咩叫声。他以为这畜生也是饿了，便到披屋里去抱了些鲜草来喂它，谁知刚一打开羊棚的小门，那羊便夺路冲出，来势之猛险些把蒂博撞个跟头，它也顾不上吃他带来的草料，径直朝屋子跑去。蒂博把草捆往地上一扔，追了上去，打算把它重新安顿回原处；可他发觉这事自己根本办不到。他得使出浑身力气才把它拽动，因为那山羊拼着一头这种畜生所能有的全部气力，弓着脊背，犟着不肯挪动，死活抗着他揪着它的角往回拖。然而它终究斗不过，末了还是被重新关进了羊棚；可尽管蒂博给它留下了丰盛的晚餐，它仍旧叫个不停，那声音凄惨极了。鞋匠又恼又疑，再次从饭桌前起身走向羊棚，这回开门时小心翼翼，那羊再逃不出去。他一进门便伸手在各个角落、各处缝隙里摸索，想找出它受惊的缘故。忽然，他的手指触到了另一只畜生身上那温暖而厚实的皮毛。蒂博并非胆小之人——远非如此——可他还是慌忙缩回了手。他回屋去取了火来，可等他再走进羊棚，那火把险些从手中脱落：原来吓坏了山羊的这只畜生，正是韦兹领主的那头雄鹿——他追踪过、没能杀死、在求上帝不得时以魔鬼之名祈求过的那一头；正是那头甩开了群犬的鹿；总之，正是那头叫他挨了好一顿毒打的鹿。蒂博先确认门已闩牢，才轻轻走到那畜生跟前；这可怜东西也不知是累极了还是驯顺极了，竟一动不动，只用那双覆着天鹅绒般乌黑的大眼睛望着蒂博，因为心中的惊恐，那目光显得比任何时候都更惹人怜悯。

===Original===
“I must have left the door open,” muttered the shoemaker to himself, “and the creature, not knowing where to hide itself, must have taken refuge here.” But on thinking further over the matter, it came back to him that when he had gone to open the door, only ten minutes before, for the first time, he had found the wooden bolt pushed so firmly into the staple that he had had to get a stone to hammer it back; and then, besides, the goat, which, as we have seen, did not at all relish the society of the newcomer, would certainly have run out of the shed before, if the door had been open. What was, however, still more surprising was that Thibault, looking more closely at the buck, saw that it had been fastened up to the rack by a cord.

===Chinese===
“我准是没关门，”鞋匠自言自语地咕哝道，“这畜生没处藏身，便躲到这儿来了。”可再一想，他又记起：十分钟前他头一回过去开门时，那根木门闩在门鼻里插得死紧，他还得找了块石头才把它砸回去；况且那山羊——如我们所见，对这位不速之客的陪伴半点也不领情——倘若门是开着的，早先就一定逃出羊棚去了。然而更叫人惊奇的是，蒂博再细看那头鹿，只见它竟被一根绳子拴在了草架子上。

===Original===
Thibault, as we have said, was no coward, but now a cold sweat began to break out in large drops on his brow, a curious kind of a shiver ran through his body, and his teeth chattered violently. He went out of the shed, shutting the door after him, and began looking for his goat, which had taken advantage of the moment when the shoemaker had gone to fetch a light, and ran again into the house, where she was now lying beside the hearth, having evidently quite made up her mind this time not to forsake a resting place, which, for that night at least, she found preferable to her usual abode.

===Chinese===
蒂博，如前所说，并非胆小之人，可此刻一颗颗豆大的冷汗却从他额头上冒了出来，一种说不清道不明的寒颤传遍全身，牙齿也咯咯地打得厉害。他走出羊棚，随手关上门，去找他的山羊——那畜生趁他取火的工夫又钻进了屋子，此刻正卧在炉灶旁，显然这回是铁了心不再离开这个栖身之处了，至少今夜，它觉得此地比它惯常的住处可亲得多。

===Original===
Thibault had a perfect remembrance of the unholy invocation he had addressed to Satan, and although his prayer had been miraculously answered, he still could not bring himself to believe that there was any diabolic intervention in the matter.

As the idea, however, of being under the protection of the spirit of darkness filled him with an instinctive fear, he tried to pray; but when he wished to raise his hand to make the sign of the cross on his forehead, his arm refused to bend, and although up to that time he had never missed a day saying his *Ave Maria*, he could not remember a single word of it.

===Chinese===
蒂博对他向撒旦（Satan）所发的那番亵渎祈求记得一清二楚，虽说那祷告竟奇迹般地应验了，他心里仍不肯相信这回事当真有魔鬼插手。

然而，一想到自己竟受着那黑暗之灵的庇护，他便生出一种本能的恐惧，想祷告一番；可当他想举手在额上画个十字时，那条胳膊竟弯不过来；虽说直到那会儿为止，他从来没有一天落下过念《圣母经》（*Ave Maria*），可这会儿他竟连一句也想不起来了。

===Original===
