These fruitless efforts were accompanied by a terrible turmoil in poor Thibault’s brain; evil thoughts came rushing in upon him, and he seemed to hear them whispering all around him, as one hears the murmur of the rising tide, or the laughing of the winter wind through the leafless branches of the trees.

===Chinese===
这番徒劳的挣扎，伴随着可怜的蒂博脑中一阵可怕的骚动；种种恶念纷纷涌来，他仿佛听见它们在他四周窃窃私语，如同人听见涨潮时的低语，或是听见冬日的寒风穿过光秃秃的树枝时那阵狞笑。

===Original===
“After all,” he muttered to himself, as he sat pale, and staring before him, “the buck is a fine windfall, whether it comes from God or the Devil, and I should be a fool not to profit by it. If I am afraid of it as being food sent from the nether regions, I am in no way forced to eat it, and what is more, I could not eat it alone, and if I asked anyone to partake of it with me, I should be betrayed; the best thing I can do is to take the live beast over to the Nunnery of Saint-Rémy, where it will serve as a pet for the Nuns and where the Abbess will give me a good round sum for it. The atmosphere of that holy place will drive the evil out of it, and I shall run no risk to my soul in taking a handful of consecrated crown pieces.

===Chinese===
“说到底，”他自言自语地咕哝道，脸色苍白，两眼直勾勾地瞪着前头，“这头鹿到底是个意外之财，不论它是从上帝那儿来还是从魔鬼那儿来，我要是白白放过，那才是傻子呢。倘若我怕它是冥间送来的吃食，我也大可不必吃它；况且我一个人也吃不完，要是请谁来同吃，反倒会走漏风声；我顶好把这活畜生送到圣雷米女修院（the Nunnery of Saint-Rémy）去，在那儿给修女们当个玩物，院长嬷嬷（the Abbess）自会给我一笔可观的钱。那圣地里的气息自会把它身上的邪气驱散，而我换得一把祝圣过的埃居（crown pieces），灵魂上也不会冒半点风险。”

===Original===
“What days of sweating over my work, and turning my auger, it would take, to earn even the quarter of what I shall get by just leading the beast to its new fold! The devil who helps one is certainly better worth than the angel who forsakes one. If my lord Satan wants to go too far with me, it will then be time enough to free myself from his claws: bless me! I am not a child, nor a young lamb like Georgine, and I am able to walk straight in front of me and go where I like.” He had forgotten, unhappy man, as he boasted of being able to go where and how he liked, that only five minutes before he had tried in vain to lift his hand to his head.

===Chinese===
“我累死累活地埋头做活、拧着钻子，要挣到我不过牵着这畜生去它新圈就能到手的那点钱的四分之一，得花上多少日子啊！肯帮你的魔鬼，到底比丢下你不管的天使强得多。要是撒旦老爷想跟我得寸进尺，到那时再想法子从他爪子底下脱身也不迟：得了吧！我又不是个小孩子，也不是像乔治娜（Georgine）那样的小羊羔，我能挺直腰板朝前走，爱去哪儿就去哪儿。”这个可怜虫吹嘘自己能随心所欲、爱去哪儿就去哪儿的时候，却忘了就在五分钟之前，他连把手举到额上都办不到。

===Original===
Thibault had such convincing and excellent reasons ready to hand, that he quite made up his mind to keep the buck, come whence it might, and even went so far as to decide that the money he received for it should be devoted to buying a wedding dress for his betrothed. For, strange to say, by some freak of memory, his thoughts would keep returning towards Agnelette; and he seemed to see her clad in a long white dress with a crown of white lilies on her head and a long veil. If, he said to himself, he could have such a charming guardian angel in his house, no devil, however strong and cunning he might be, would ever dare to cross the threshold. “So,” he went on, “there is always that remedy at hand, and if my lord Satan begins to be too troublesome, I shall be off to the grandmother to ask for Agnelette; I shall marry her, and if I cannot remember my prayers or am unable to make the sign of the cross, there will be a dear pretty little woman, who has had no traffic with Satan, who will do all that sort of thing for me.”

===Chinese===
蒂博手里攥着这样一些又管用又漂亮的好理由，便铁了心要留下这头鹿——管它是从哪儿来的——甚至拿定了主意：卖鹿所得的钱，要拿去给他的未婚妻添置一身嫁衣。因为说来也怪，不知是记忆出了什么岔子，他的心思总是一再飘回到阿涅莱特（Agnelette）身上；他仿佛看见她身着一袭长长的白裙，头戴白百合花冠，披着长长的面纱。他心想，若是家里能有这样一位可爱的守护天使，那么不论魔鬼有多强、多狡猾，也绝不敢跨过他家门槛一步。“这么着，”他接着盘算，“这条退路到底是在手边的，万一撒旦老爷闹得太凶，我便去找那外祖母，讨阿涅莱特做媳妇；我娶了她，就算我念不出祷告、画不成十字，有这样一个不曾与撒旦打过交道的可亲可爱的小女人，这些事她自会替我做。”

===Original===
Having more or less reassured himself with the idea of this compromise, Thibault, in order that the buck should not run down in value, and might be as fine an animal as possible to offer to the holy ladies, to whom he calculated to sell it, went and filled the rack with fodder and looked to see that the litter was soft and thick enough for the buck to rest fully at its ease. The remainder of the night passed without further incident, and without even a bad dream.

===Chinese===
蒂博用这番妥协之计多少宽慰了自己，又为了让那头鹿别掉了膘、能尽可能肥壮地送到他想卖给的那几位圣洁的夫人面前，便去把草架塞满了草料，又去瞧了瞧垫草够不够软、够不够厚，好让那鹿能舒舒服服地歇着。这一夜余下的时光平平无事地过去了，连一个噩梦也没做。

===Original===
The next morning, my lord Baron again went hunting, but this time it was not a timid deer that headed the hounds, but the wolf which Marcotte had tracked the day before and had again that morning traced to his lair.

===Chinese===
第二天一早，男爵老爷又出猎去了，可这一回领着群犬穷追不舍的，不再是那头怯懦的鹿，而是马尔科特（Marcotte）昨日追踪到、今早又寻到窝里的那头狼。

===Original===
And this wolf was a genuine wolf, and no mistake; it must have seen many and many a year, although those who had that morning caught sight of it while on its track, had noted with astonishment that it was black all over. Black or grey, however, it was a bold and enterprising beast, and promised some rough work to the Baron and his huntsmen. First started near Vertefeuille, in the Dargent covert, it had made over the plain of Meutard, leaving Fleury and Dampleux to the left, crossed the road to Ferté-Milou, and finally begun to run cunning in the Ivors coppices. Then, instead of continuing in the same direction, it doubled, returning along the same track it had come, and so exactly retracing its own steps, that the Baron, as he galloped along, could actually distinguish the prints left by his horse’s hoofs that same morning.

===Chinese===
这头狼是头货真价实的狼，错不了；它想必已活了许多许多年头，虽说今早循着踪迹追它而曾一瞥它身影的人，都诧异地注意到它通体乌黑。可无论是黑是灰，这都是一头胆大包天、生性悍勇的畜生，势必要给男爵和他那一众猎手好一番苦斗。它起初在韦尔特弗耶（Vertefeuille）附近的达尔让林（the Dargent covert）被惊起，接着窜过默塔尔平原（the plain of Meutard），把弗勒里（Fleury）和当普勒（Dampleux）甩在左边，横穿费尔泰-米卢（Ferté-Milou）大道，末了在伊沃尔（Ivors）的矮树林里耍起花招来。随后，它非但不顺着原方向继续跑，反而折了回来，沿着来时的原路返回，原路折返得如此一丝不差，以至于男爵纵马疾驰之际，竟能一一辨认出他那匹坐骑当天早晨留下的蹄印。

===Original===
Back again in the district of Bourg-Fontaine, he ranged the country, leading the hunt right to the very spot where the misadventures of the previous day had had their start, the vicinity of the shoemaker’s hut.

Thibault, we know, had made up his mind what to do in regard to certain matters, and as he intended going over to see Agnelette in the evening, he had started work early.

===Chinese===
它又回到布尔丰泉（Bourg-Fontaine）一带，在那片乡野里纵横驰骋，把这场围猎径直引到了昨日那场倒霉事的起始之地——鞋匠小屋的近旁。

如我们所知，蒂博在几桩事情上已拿定了主意，又打算晚上去看阿涅莱特，便早早地动手做起活来。

===Original===
You will naturally ask why, instead of sitting down to a work which brought in so little, as he himself acknowledged, Thibault did not start off at once to take his buck to the ladies of Saint-Rémy. Thibault took very good care to do nothing of the sort; the day was not the time to be leading a buck through the forest of Villers-Cotterets; the first keeper he met would have stopped him, and what explanation could he have given? No, Thibault had arranged in his own mind to leave home one evening about dusk, to follow the road to the right, then go down the sandpit lane which led into the Chemin du Pendu, and he would then be on the common of Saint-Rémy, only a hundred paces or so from the Convent.

===Chinese===
读者自然要问：蒂博既然自己都承认这活计挣不了几个钱，那他为何不立马动身，把他的鹿送到圣雷米那几位夫人那儿去呢？蒂博可千万个不肯干这种傻事；大白天牵着头鹿穿过维莱-科特雷（Villers-Cotterets）的森林，可不是时候；他碰上的头一个看林人就会把他拦下，到时候他又能拿什么话来搪塞呢？不，蒂博心里早有盘算：他要等到黄昏时分离家出门，顺着右边那条路走，再拐进那条通往吊死人路（the Chemin du Pendu）的沙坑小道，这样他便能到圣雷米的公地，离那座修道院不过百来步路。

===Original===
Thibault no sooner caught the first sound of the horn and the dogs, than he immediately gathered together a huge bundle of dried heather, which he hastily piled up in front of the shed, where his prisoner was confined, so as to hide the door, in case the huntsmen and their master should halt in front of his hut, as they had the day before. He then sat down again to his work, applying to it an energy unknown even to himself before, bending over the shoe he was making with an intentness which prevented him from even lifting his eyes. All at once he thought he detected a sound like something scratching at the door; he was just going through from his lean-to to open it when the door fell back, and to Thibault’s great astonishment an immense black wolf entered the room, walking on its hind legs. On reaching the middle of the floor, it sat down after the fashion of wolves, and looked hard and fixedly at the sabot-maker.

===Chinese===
蒂博一听见那第一声号角和犬吠，便赶忙拢起一大捆干石楠，匆匆堆在关着他那名“俘虏”的羊棚门口，好把那扇门遮起来，以防猎手们和他们主子像昨日那样在他小屋跟前停下脚。随后他又坐下做活，使出一股连他自己都未曾意识到的劲头，埋头在那只正在做的鞋上，专注得连眼皮都顾不上抬一抬。蓦地，他像是听见了什么挠门似的声响；他正要从披屋里穿过去开门，那门却朝里倒了下去，只见一头硕大无朋的黑狼（a black wolf）闯进屋来，竟用后腿直立行走。它走到屋子中央，便照着狼的坐法坐下，目不转睛地死死盯着这位木屐匠。

===Original===
Thibault seized a hatchet which was within reach, and in order to give a fit reception to his strange visitor, and to terrify him, he flourished the weapon above his head.

A curious mocking expression passed over the face of the wolf, and then it began to laugh.

===Chinese===
蒂博顺手抄起一把够得着的手斧（a hatchet），为了好好“招待”这位怪客、吓他一吓，便把那家伙在头顶上抡得呼呼作响。

黑狼脸上掠过一丝古怪的、嘲弄的神情，接着，它竟笑了起来。

===Original===
It was the first time that Thibault had ever heard a wolf laugh. He had often heard tell that wolves barked like dogs, but never that they laughed like human beings. And what a laugh it was! If a man had laughed such a laugh, Thibault would verily and indeed have been scared out of his wits.

He brought his lifted arm down again.

===Chinese===
蒂博有生以来还是头一回听见狼笑。他常听人说狼会像狗一样吠叫，可从没听说狼还能像人一样发笑。这一笑，又是何等样的一笑啊！倘若是个人笑出这么一声来，蒂博准会被吓得魂飞魄散。

他放下了抡起的胳膊。

===Original===
“By my lord of the cloven foot,” said the wolf, in a full and sonorous voice, “you are a fine fellow! At your request, I send you the finest buck from His Royal Highness’s forests, and in return, you want to split my head open with your hatchet; human gratitude is worthy to rank with that of wolves.” On hearing a voice exactly like his own coming forth from a beast’s mouth, Thibault’s knees began to shake under him, and the hatchet fell out of his hand.

===Chinese===
“凭那位分蹄大老爷起誓，”那狼开口道，嗓音浑厚而洪亮，“你可真是个好样的！你一开口，我便把殿下森林里最肥的那头雄鹿给你送来，作为回报，你倒想拿手斧劈开我的脑袋；人类的知恩图报，倒也配跟狼相提并论了。”听见一头畜生嘴里竟吐出与自己一模一样的嗓音，蒂博两条腿直打颤，手斧从手里脱落下来。

===Original===
“Now then,” continued the wolf, “let us be sensible and talk together like two good friends. Yesterday you wanted the Baron’s buck, and I led it myself into your shed, and for fear it should escape, I tied it up myself to the rack. And for all this you take your hatchet to me!”

===Chinese===
“好啦，”那狼接着说道，“咱们还是通情达理些，像两个好朋友那样好好说说话。昨天你想要男爵的鹿，是我亲手把它领进了你的羊棚，又怕它跑了，亲手把它拴在了草架上。你倒好，为这报答我一记手斧！”

===Original===
“How should I know who you were?” asked Thibault.

“I see, you did not recognise me! A nice sort of excuse to give.”

===Chinese===
“我怎知道你是谁？”蒂博问道。

“我明白了，你没认出我来！这倒是个好借口。”

===Original===
“Well, I ask you, was it likely I should take you for a friend under that ugly coat?”

“Ugly coat, indeed!” said the wolf, licking his fur with a long tongue as red as blood. “Confound you! You are hard to please. However, it’s not a matter of my coat; what I want to know is, are you willing to make me some return for the service I have done you?”

===Chinese===
“那么我问你，你披着这么一身丑皮囊，我能把你当朋友看待吗？”

“丑皮囊，好一个丑皮囊！”那狼说道，用一条血一般红的长舌头舔着自己的毛，“你这混账！你可真难伺候。不过，皮囊的事先撇开不提；我只想知道，你愿不愿意为我帮你的这个忙，给我一点回报？”

===Original===
“Certainly,” said the shoemaker, feeling rather uncomfortable! “but I ought to know what your demands are. What is it? What do you want? Speak!”

“First of all, and above all things, I should like a glass of water, for those confounded dogs have run me until I am out of breath.”

===Chinese===
“那自然，”鞋匠说道，心里却觉得颇为不自在，“可我得先弄清你讨的是什么。是什么？你要什么？说吧！”

“头一桩，也是最要紧的一桩，我想要一杯水喝，因为那群该死的狗追得我气都喘不上来了。”

===Original===
“You shall have it in a moment, my lord wolf.”

And Thibault ran and fetched a bowl of fresh, clear water from a brook which ran some ten paces from the hut. The eager readiness with which he complied with the wolf’s request betrayed his feeling of relief at getting out of the bargain so cheaply.

===Chinese===
“狼老爷，您这就有了。”

蒂博便跑去从小屋十步开外流过的一条小溪里，舀了一碗清冽的活水来。他这般巴结地应承狼的要求，倒泄漏了他心头那份如释重负——能这么便宜就脱身，他着实松了一口气。

===Original===
As he placed the bowl in front of the wolf, he made the animal a low bow. The wolf lapped up the contents with evident delight, and then stretched himself on the floor with his paws straight out in front of him, looking like a sphinx.

“Now,” he said, “listen to me.”

===Chinese===
他把碗放到狼跟前，朝这位畜生深深地鞠了一躬。那狼显然喝得心满意足，把碗里的水舔了个干净，随即趴到地上，两只前爪直直地伸在前面，活像一尊斯芬克斯（a sphinx）。

“现在，”它说，“你听我说。”

===Original===
“There is something else you wish me to do,” asked Thibault, inwardly quaking.

“Yes, a very urgent something,” replied the wolf. “Do you hear the baying of the dogs?”

===Chinese===
“您还有别的事要我办？”蒂博问道，心里头直打鼓。

“不错，一桩十万火急的事，”那狼答道，“你听见狗吠没有？”

===Original===
“Indeed I do, they are coming nearer and nearer, and in five minutes they will be here.”

“And what I want you to do is to get me out of their way.”

===Chinese===
“听是听见了，它们越来越近，再过五分钟便要到这儿了。”

“我要你做的，便是把我从它们眼皮底下弄出去。”

===Original===
“Get you out of their way! and how?” cried Thibault, who but too well remembered what it had cost him to meddle with the Baron’s hunting the day before.

“Look about you, think, invent some way of delivering me!”

===Chinese===
“把你弄出它们眼皮底下！怎么个弄法？”蒂博叫了起来，他可太记得昨日插手男爵的围猎让自己吃了什么苦头了。

“你四下里瞧瞧，想想，想个法子救我出来！”

===Original===
“The Baron’s dogs are rough customers to deal with, and you are asking neither more nor less than that I should save your life; for I warn you, if they once get hold of you, and they will probably scent you out, they will make short work of pulling you to pieces. And now supposing I spare you this disagreeable business,” continued Thibault, who imagined that he had now got the upper hand, “what will you do for me in return?”

===Chinese===
“男爵那群狗可不是好惹的主儿，你这要求的不是别的，正是要我救你的命；我先警告你，它们一旦逮住你——而它们多半会嗅出你来——眨眼工夫就能把你撕成碎片。那么，倘若我替你免了这桩麻烦事，”蒂博接着说道，自以为这回占了上风，“你拿什么来回报我呢？”

===Original===
“Do for you in return?” said the wolf, “and how about the buck?”

“And how about the bowl of water?” said Thibault.

===Chinese===
“拿什么回报你？”那狼说，“那头鹿又怎么说？”

“那碗水又怎么说？”蒂博道。

===Original===
“We are quits there, my good sir. Let us start a fresh business altogether; if you are agreeable to it, I am quite willing.”

“Let it be so then; tell me quickly what you want of me.”

===Chinese===
“这一笔咱们算是两清了，我的好先生。咱们另起一桩新买卖；你若愿意，我也求之不得。”

“那就这么着吧；快说你还要我干什么。”

===Original===
“There are folks,” proceeded Thibault, “who might take advantage of the position you are now in, and ask for all kinds of extravagant things, riches, power, titles, and whatnot, but I am not going to do anything of the kind; yesterday I wanted the buck, and you gave it me, it is true; tomorrow, I shall want something else. For some time past I have been possessed by a kind of mania, and I do nothing but wish first for one thing and then for another, and you will not always be able to spare time to listen to my demands. So what I ask for is, that, as you are the devil in person or someone very like it, you will grant me the fulfilment of every wish I may have from this day forth.”

===Chinese===
“有的人家，”蒂博接着说道，“见你这会儿落在我手里，说不定会趁机提出各种各样过分的条件，要富贵、要权势、要头衔，诸如此类，我可不想干这种事；昨天我想要那头鹿，你给了，这是实情；明天，我又会想要别的东西。这一阵子我像中了邪似的犯了一种怪毛病，成天无所事事，就知道一会儿想要这一桩、一会儿想要那一桩，你也未必总有工夫来听我开口。所以我所要的是：你既是个货真价实的魔鬼，或者与之八九不离十的角色，便请你答应我，从今往后我所起的每一个心愿，都能如愿以偿。”

===Original===
The wolf put on a mocking expression of countenance. “Is that all?” he said, “Your peroration does not accord very well with your exordium.”

“Oh!” continued Thibault, “my wishes are honest and moderate ones, and such as become a poor peasant like myself. I want just a little corner of ground, and a few timbers, and planks; that’s all that a man of my sort can possibly desire.”

===Chinese===
那狼脸上挂起一副嘲弄的神气。“就这些吗？”它说，“你这收尾的话，跟开头可不大相称哪。”

“噢！”蒂博接着说，“我的心愿都是些正正经经、本本分分的愿望，与我这般穷苦乡下人相称。我想要的不过是一小块地、几根木料、几块板子罢了；像我这种人，还能图个什么呢。”

===Original===
“I should have the greatest pleasure in doing what you ask,” said the wolf, “but it is simply impossible, you know.”

“Then I am afraid you must make up your mind to put up with what the dogs may do to you.”

===Chinese===
“能依你，我是再乐意不过的，”那狼说，“可这压根儿办不到，你是明白的。”

“那我看你只好认命，听凭那些狗拿你怎么着了。”

===Original===
“You think so, and you suppose I have need of your help, and so you can ask what you please?”

“I do not suppose it, I am sure of it.”

===Chinese===
“你倒是想得美，你以为我离不了你的帮衬，所以你便能漫天要价了？”

“我不是以为，我是拿得稳。”

===Original===
“Indeed! well then, look.”

“Look where,” asked Thibault.

===Chinese===
“当真！那好，你瞧。”

“瞧哪儿？”蒂博问。

===Original===
“Look at the spot where I was,” said the wolf. Thibault drew back in horror. The place where the wolf had been lying was empty; the wolf had disappeared, where or how it was impossible to say. The room was intact, there was not a hole in the roof large enough to let a needle through, nor a crack in the floor through which a drop of water could have filtered.

===Chinese===
“瞧我方才躺着的那块地方，”那狼说。蒂博吓得直往后缩。那狼方才趴着的地方空空如也；那狼不见了，是从哪儿、怎么溜走的，根本无从说起。屋子完好无损，屋顶上连一个能容一根针穿过的窟窿都没有，地板上也没有一条能让一滴水渗下去的裂缝。

===Original===
“Well, do you still think that I require your assistance to get out of trouble,” said the wolf.

“Where the devil are you?”

===Chinese===
“怎么样，你还以为我非得借你的力才能脱身吗？”那狼说道。

“你到底在哪儿？”

===Original===
“If you put a question to me in my real name,” said the wolf with a sneer in his voice, “I shall be obliged to answer you. I am still in the same place.”

“But I can no longer see you!”

===Chinese===
“你若用我真名来问我，”那狼语带讥讽地说道，“我不得不答你。我仍躺在原处。”

“可我再也看不见你了！”

===Original===
“Simply because I am invisible.”

“But the dogs, the huntsmen, the Baron, will come in here after you?”

===Chinese===
“只因我如今隐了形。”

“可那些狗、那些猎手、还有男爵，会进这屋里来找你啊？”

===Original===
“No doubt they will, but they will not find me.”

“But if they do not find you, they will set upon me.”

===Chinese===
“他们自然会来，可他们找不着我。”

“可他们要是找不着你，便会冲我来。”

===Original===
“As they did yesterday; only yesterday you were sentenced to thirty-six strokes of the strap, for having carried off the buck; today, you will be sentenced to seventy-two, for having hidden the wolf, and Agnelette will not be on the spot to buy you off with a kiss.”

===Chinese===
“就像昨日那样；只不过昨日你拐走那头鹿，判了三十六记皮条；今日你窝藏这头狼，可要判七十二记，这回阿涅莱特可不在跟前用一个吻来替你赎身了。”

===Original===
“Phew! what am I to do?”

“Let the buck loose; the dogs will mistake the scent, and they will get the blows instead of you.”

===Chinese===
“咝！那我该怎么办？”

“把那头鹿放出来；狗便会闻岔了气味，挨揍的便是它们，而不是你。”

===Original===
“But is it likely such trained hounds will follow the scent of a deer in mistake for that of a wolf?”

“You can leave that to me,” replied the voice, “only do not lose any time, or the dogs will be here before you have reached the shed, and that would make matters unpleasant, not for me, whom they would not find, but for you, whom they would.”

===Chinese===
“可那么些训练有素的猎犬，会把鹿的气味当成狼的气味去追吗？”

“这个你交给我好了，”那声音答道，“只是别耽搁工夫，否则你还没到羊棚，狗便已到了门口；到那时事情可不妙——不妙的不在我，它们找不着我，倒在你，它们找得着你。”

===Original===
Thibault did not wait to be warned a second time, but was off like a shot to the shed. He unfastened the buck, which, as if propelled by some hidden force, leapt from the house, ran round it, crossing the track of the wolf, and plunged into the Baisemont coppice. The dogs were within a hundred paces of the hut; Thibault heard them with trepidation; the whole pack came full force against the door, one hound after the other.

===Chinese===
蒂博不等再受第二遍叮嘱，便如离弦之箭一般冲向羊棚。他解开那头鹿，那畜生像受了什么暗力驱使似的，从屋里一跃而出，绕屋跑了一圈，蹭过狼留下的气味，一头扎进了贝斯蒙（Baisemont）的矮树林。群犬离小屋已不足百步；蒂博提心吊胆地听着；整群狗一拥而上，一条接一条地撞在小屋的门上。

===Original===
Then, all at once, two or three gave cry and went off in the direction of Baisemont, the rest of the hounds after them.

The dogs were on the wrong scent; they were on the scent of the buck, and had abandoned that of the wolf.

===Chinese===
忽然间，有两三条狗吠了起来，朝贝斯蒙的方向追去，其余的狗也一窝蜂跟在后头。

狗闻岔了气味；它们追的是鹿的气味，把狼的气味丢了。

===Original===
Thibault gave a deep sigh of relief; he watched the hunt gradually disappearing in the distance, and went back to his room to the full and joyous notes of the Baron’s horn.

He found the wolf lying composedly on the same spot as before, but how it had found its way in again was quite as impossible to discover as how it had found its way out.

===Chinese===
蒂博如释重负地长舒了一口气；他望着那场围猎渐渐在远处消失，便在男爵那嘹亮欢快的号角声中，回自己的屋里去了。

他发觉那狼又神态自若地趴在方才那块地方，至于它是怎么又进来的，跟它方才是怎么出去的一样，无从查考。

## VII The Boy at the Mill / VII 磨坊里的男孩

===Original===
Finding it impossible either to cut off or pull out the accursed hair, the only thing left for Thibault to do was to hide it as well as he could, by bringing the other hair over it; everybody would not, he hoped, have such eyes as Agnelette.

As we have already said, Thibault had a fine head of black hair, and by parting it down the side, and giving a certain turn to the front lock, he trusted that the one hair would pass unobserved.

He recalled with envy the young lords whom he had seen at the court of Madame de Maintenon, for, with their powdered wigs to cover it, the colour of their hair, whatever it might be, was of no moment. He, unfortunately, could not make use of powder to hide his, being prohibited from doing so by the sumptuary laws of the period.

However, having successfully managed, by an adroit turn of the comb, to hide his one red hair artistically under the others, Thibault decided to start again on his premeditated visit to the fair owner of the mill. He was careful this time, instead of inclining to the left, to verge towards the right, fearing to meet Agnelette if he followed the same path as he had taken that morning.

===Chinese===
那根该死的头发既剪不掉、又拔不出，蒂博（Thibault）能做的便只有尽量把它藏好——拿别的头发盖在它上头。他指望，世上的人未必个个都长着阿涅莱特（Agnelette）那样一双眼睛。

前头已经说过，蒂博生着一头漂亮的黑发，只要把发缝偏向一边，再把前额那一绺头发顺势一拢，他便信得过那一根头发能瞒天过海、无人觉察。

他满心艳羡地想起自己曾在曼特农夫人（Madame de Maintenon）宫廷里见过的那些年轻贵族——他们戴着一头扑粉的假发盖着，头发的颜色是黑是黄、是红是棕，全不要紧。可怜他这一头发，偏偏没法用扑粉来遮掩——当年的禁奢法令是不许他这种人用发粉的。

不过，蒂博到底是靠梳子那么灵巧地一拢，把那一根红发巧妙地藏在其余头发底下，遮得妥妥帖帖。他便打定主意，照着原先的盘算，再去拜访那位美貌的磨坊主人。这一回他可不敢大意：不再像早上那样往左边走，而是朝右边偏过去，生怕再走同一条路、再撞上阿涅莱特。

===Original===
Emerging, therefore, on to the road leading to La Ferté-Milou, he then took the footpath which runs direct to Pisseleu across the fields. Arriving at Pisseleu, he continued along the valley in the direction of Croyolles, but had scarcely pursued this lower road for more than a few minutes, when, walking just ahead of him, he saw two donkeys being driven by a tall youth, whom he recognised as a cousin of his, named Landry. Cousin Landry was head boy at the mill, in the service of the owner whom Thibault was on his way to visit, and as the latter had but an indirect acquaintance with the widow Polet, he had counted on Landry to introduce him. It was a lucky chance therefore to come across his cousin like this, and Thibault hastened to overtake him.

Hearing footsteps behind him echoing his own, Landry turned and recognised Thibault. Thibault had always found Landry a pleasant and cheerful companion, and he was therefore very much astonished to see him looking sad and troubled. Landry waited for Thibault to come up to him, letting his donkeys go on alone. Thibault was the first to speak:

“Why, Cousin Landry,” he asked, “what’s the meaning of this? Here am I, putting myself out and leaving my work to come and shake hands with a friend and relation that I have not seen for more than six weeks, and you greet me with a face like that!”

===Chinese===
于是他走上通往费尔泰-米卢（La Ferté-Milou）的那条大路，又转上一条穿过田地直通皮瑟勒（Pisseleu）的田间小径。到了皮瑟勒，他顺着山谷继续朝克鲁瓦约尔（Croyolles）的方向走去。可这条低洼小路他才走没几分钟，便瞧见前头正走着的两条驴子，赶驴的是个高高的小伙子——他认出来了，正是自己的表弟朗德里（Landry）。表弟朗德里是磨坊里的大伙计，伺候的正是蒂博此番要去拜访的那位磨坊主人；而蒂博自己与波莱寡妇仅有几面之缘，原本还指望靠朗德里给他引荐引荐呢。这一来撞见表弟，真是天赐良机，蒂博赶紧三步并作两步赶上前去。

听得背后有与自己脚步声相和的另一双脚响，朗德里回头一望，认出是蒂博。蒂博一向觉得朗德里是个讨人喜欢、性情开朗的伙伴，如今见他满脸愁苦、神色大变，不由得十分诧异。朗德里停住脚等蒂博追上来，由着自己的驴子独自往前走。蒂博先开了口：

“怎么啦，朗德里表弟，”他问，“这是唱的哪一出？我撂下活计、劳心费力地跑来，要和一个六七个礼拜没见面的亲朋好友握握手，你倒好，就这么副脸色来迎我！”

===Original===
“Ah, my dear Thibault,” replied Landry, “what would you have of me! I may greet you with a gloomy face, but, believe me or not as you will, I am truly delighted to see you.”

“That may be as you say, but you do not appear so.”

“What do you mean?”

“You tell me you are delighted to see me in a tone of voice fit to bring on the blue-devils. Why, my dear Landry, you are generally as bright and lively as the click-clack of your mill, and singing songs to accompany it, and today you are as melancholy as the crosses in the cemetery. How now then! has the mill stopped for want of water?”

“Oh! not that! there is no want of water; on the contrary, there is more than usual, and the sluice is kept constantly at work. But, you see, instead of corn, it is my heart that is in the mill, and the mill works so well and so incessantly, and my heart is so ground between the stones that there is nothing left of it but a little powder.”

“Indeed! Are you so miserable then at the mill?”

“Ah! would to God I had been dragged under the wheel the first day I put my foot inside it!”

“But what is it? you frighten me, Landry!… tell me all your troubles, my dear lad.”

Landry gave a deep sigh.

“We are cousins,” continued Thibault, “and if I am too poor to give you a few crowns to help you out of any money trouble you are in, well, I can at least give you some words of good advice if it is a matter of the heart that is causing you grief.”

“Thank you, Thibault; but neither money nor advice can do me any good.”

“Well, anyhow, tell me what is the matter; it eases trouble to speak of it.”

“No, no; it would be useless; I will say nothing.”

===Chinese===
“唉，我亲爱的蒂博，”朗德里答道，“你要我怎样呢！我这副愁眉苦脸迎你是迎你了，可你信不信由你，我心里实实在在是高兴见到你的。”

“也许你说是这么说，可你瞧着并不像。”

“这话怎么讲？”

“你说见到我高兴，那声调却听得人直想发愁犯病。哎，我亲爱的朗德里，平日里你这人，就像你那磨坊的咔嗒咔嗒声一样又快活又有精神，还一边唱着歌儿给它伴奏；今儿你却丧得跟坟地里的一排十字架似的。这是怎么了！敢情磨坊缺水停转了不成？”

“噢！不是那么回事！水倒是不缺；非但不缺，还比平常多，水闸一刻不停地开着呢。可是你要知道，磨坊里磨的不是麦子，是我这颗心；磨坊转得又顺当又不停歇，我的心夹在磨盘之间碾得又那么狠，到末了，除了一小撮粉末，就再没剩下什么了。”

“当真！这么说你在磨坊里就这么受罪？”

“唉！但愿我头一天踏进磨坊门槛的时候，就叫那磨盘把我卷到轮子底下去了才好！”

“到底是什么事？你倒吓着了我，朗德里！……把你的烦心事一股脑儿说给我听吧，好伙计。”

朗德里长叹了一声。

“咱们是表兄弟，”蒂博又接着说，“要说是钱上的难处，我穷是穷，拿不出几枚埃居（crown）来帮你度过难关；可若是哪桩心事惹你发愁，我好歹还能给你出几句好主意。”

“多谢你了，蒂博；可无论是钱还是主意，对我都无济于事。”

“也罢，你总该把事情原委说给我听听；说出来心里就好受些。”

“不，不；说了也是白说；我什么也不讲。”

===Original===
Thibault began to laugh.

“You laugh?” said Landry, both angry and astonished, “my trouble makes you laugh?”

“I am not laughing at your trouble, Landry, but at your thinking that you can hide the cause of it from me, when it is as easy as anything to guess what it is.”

“Guess then.”

“Why, you are in love; nothing more difficult than that to guess, I can swear.”

“I, in love!” exclaimed Landry; “why who has been telling you lies like that?”

“It is not a lie, it is the truth.”

Landry again drew a deep sigh, more laden with despair even than his former one.

“Well, yes!” he said, “it is so, I am in love!”

“Ah! that’s right! you have spoken out at last!” said Thibault, not without a certain quickening of the pulse, for he foresaw a rival in his cousin, “and with whom are you in love?”

“With whom?”

“Yes, I ask you with whom?”

“As to that, Cousin Thibault, you will have to drag the heart out of my breast before I tell you.”

“You have told me already.”

“What? I have told you who it is?” cried Landry, staring at Thibault with astonished eyes.

“Certainly you have.”

“Surely you cannot mean it!”

“Did you not say that it would have been better for you to have been dragged under by the mill wheel the first day you entered into the service of Madame Polet, than to have been taken on by her as chief hand? You are unhappy at the mill, and you are in love; therefore, you are in love with the mistress of the mill, and it is this love which is causing your unhappiness.”

“Ah, Thibault, pray hush! what if she were to overhear us!”

“How is it possible that she can overhear us; where do you imagine her to be, unless she is able to make herself invisible, or to change herself into a butterfly or a flower?”

“Never mind, Thibault, you keep quiet.”

===Chinese===
蒂博竟笑了起来。

“你笑？”朗德里又恼又惊，“我的愁事倒逗你笑了？”

“我笑的不是你的愁事，朗德里，我笑的是——你居然以为瞒得过我，这事儿的根由，要猜出来可再容易不过了。”

“那你就猜。”

“嗐，你是害了相思呗；这还用猜，我可以发誓，没有比这更好猜的了。”

“我，害相思！”朗德里叫道，“谁对你造这种谣？”

“这不是谣言，这是实情。”

朗德里又长叹一声，这一声比刚才那声还要绝望。

“好罢，是！”他说，“就是这么回事，我是害了相思！”

“啊！这就对了！你到底说出口了！”蒂博说，心头禁不住一通怦怦乱跳——他已预感到这位表弟便是自己的情敌，“那你相思的是哪一位？”

“哪一位？”

“是啊，我问你的是哪一位？”

“这个嘛，蒂博表哥，”朗德里说，“除非你把我这颗心从胸腔里掏出来，我才能说。”

“你早已告诉我了。”

“什么？我已经告诉你那个人是谁了？”朗德里叫道，瞪大了惊奇的两眼望着蒂博。

“你当然告诉过我了。”

“你总不是当真的吧！”

“你刚才不是说了吗——你头一天进波莱太太（Madame Polet）的磨坊当伙计，倒不如那一日就给磨盘卷到轮子底下去，也比让她收留你当大伙计来得强。你在磨坊里过的是受罪日子，你又害着相思；由此可见，你相思的便是磨坊的女主人，害你这般愁苦的也正是这段相思。”

“唉，蒂博，求你小声点！要是叫她听见了可怎么好！”

“她怎么能听得见咱们说话；你以为她在哪儿，除非她会隐身法，或者能化作一只蝴蝶、一朵花儿？”

“不管那个，蒂博，你只管住嘴就是。”

===Original===
“Your mistress of the mill is hard-hearted then, is she? and takes no pity on your despair, poor fellow?” was Thibault’s rejoinder; but his words, though seemingly expressive of great commiseration, had a shade of satisfaction and amusement in them.

“Hard-hearted! I should think so indeed!” said Landry. “In the beginning, I was foolish enough to fancy that she did not repulse my love.… All day long I was devouring her with my eyes, and now and then, she too would fix her eyes on me, and after looking at me a while, would smile.… Alas! my dear Thibault, what happiness those looks and smiles were to me!… Ah! why did I not content myself with them?”

“Well, there it is,” said Thibault philosophically. “Man is so insatiable.”

“Alas! yes; I forgot that I had to do with someone above me in position, and I spoke. Then Madame Polet flew into a great rage; called me an insolent beggar, and threatened to turn me out of doors the very next week.”

“Phew!” said Thibault, “and how long ago is that?”

“Nearly three weeks.”

“And the following week is still to come?” The shoemaker as he put the question began to feel a revival of the uneasiness which had been momentarily allayed, for he understood women better than his cousin Landry. After a minute’s silence, he continued: “Well, well, you are not so unhappy after all as I thought you.”

“Not so unhappy as you thought me?”

“No.”

===Chinese===
“这么说，你那位磨坊女主人倒是个铁石心肠了？对你这般失魂落魄也不动一动怜悯之心，可怜虫？”蒂博这样回道。可他这话听着虽是十二分的同情，里头却分明夹着一丝称心快意。

“铁石心肠！那还用说！”朗德里道，“起初我傻得很，还当她并不嫌弃我的情意呢……我一整日拿眼睛把她瞧了个够，她呢，偶尔也把眼睛停在我身上，看上一会儿，便冲我一笑……唉！我亲爱的蒂博，那几眼、那几笑，给了我多大的欢喜啊！……唉！我怎么就不安分守己、知足于此呢？”

“是呀，人就是这么回事，”蒂博一副哲人的派头，“人心是填不满的。”

“唉！不错；我忘了与我打交道的身份比我高，竟把心里话说出了口。波莱太太登时大发雷霆，骂我是个放肆的叫花子，还吓唬我说下个礼拜就把我扫地出门。”

“嗬！”蒂博道，“这是多久以前的事？”

“快有三个礼拜了。”

“那这一个礼拜还没过呢？”鞋匠问出这话时，心头那一点方才平下去的不安又翻腾起来——女人这门学问，他到底比他表弟朗德里懂得多些。沉默了一会儿，他接着道：“也罢也罢，你到底不像我以为的那般受罪。”

“不像你想的那般受罪？”

“不像。”

===Original===
“Ah! if you only knew the life I lead! never a look, or a smile! When she meets me she turns away, when I speak to her on matters concerning the mill, she listens with such a disdainful air, that instead of talking of bran and wheat and rye, of barley and oats, of first and second crops, I begin to cry, and then she says to me, *Take care!* in such a menacing tone, that I run away and hide myself behind the bolters.”

“Well, but why do you pay your addresses to this mistress of yours? There are plenty of girls in the country round who would be glad to have you for their wooer.”

“Because I love her in spite of myself, I cannot help it, so there!”

“Take up with someone else; I’d think no more about her.”

“I could not do it.”

“At any rate, you might try. It’s just possible that if she saw you transferring your affections to another, the mistress of the Mill might grow jealous, and might then run after you, as you are now running after her. Women are such curious creatures.”

“Oh, if I was sure of that, I would begin to try at once… although now…” and Landry shook his head.

“Well, what about… now?”

“Although now, after all that has happened, it would be of no use.”

“What has happened then?” asked Thibault, who was anxious to ascertain all particulars.

“Oh! as to that, nothing,” replied Landry, “and I do not even dare speak of it.”

“Why?”

“Because, as they say with us, ‘Best let sleeping dogs lie.’”

===Chinese===
“唉！你要是知道我过的什么日子就好了！再没一个眼神、再没一丝笑了！她遇见我，便扭头过去；我跟她谈磨坊里的事，她那神情鄙夷得很，我本该讲的是麦麸、小麦、黑麦，讲大麦和燕麦，讲头茬二茬的收成，却忍不住哭了起来；这时她便对我说一句“*当心！*”声调那么吓人，吓得我一溜烟跑到筛粉机背后躲起来。”

“可你何苦还去缠着这位女主人呢？四乡里头，肯要你做她们追求者的姑娘有的是。”

“因为我不由自主地爱她，管不住自己，就是这样！”

“你就另外找一个好啦；我才不再想她呢。”

“我做不到。”

“好歹试一试嘛。说不定她瞧见你把心思挪到别人身上，磨坊女主人反倒吃起醋来，那时节就该她追着你不放了，正如你眼下追着她一般。女人就是这么一种古怪东西。”

“噢，要是真能这样，我立马就动手试试……虽说这会儿……”朗德里说着摇了摇头。

“怎么……这会儿怎么了？”

“虽说这会儿，经过这许多事，再试也没用了。”

“那到底出了什么事？”蒂博问，他急着要把底细打听个一清二楚。

“噢！要说这个嘛，倒没什么，”朗德里答道，“我连说都不敢说。”

“为什么？”

“因为，照我们那儿的话说，‘睡着的狗，还是别去惹为妙’。”

===Original===
Thibault would have continued to urge Landry to tell him what the trouble was to which he referred, but they were now near the Mill, and their explanation would have to remain unfinished, even if once begun. What was more, Thibault thought that he already knew enough; Landry was in love with the fair owner of the Mill, but the fair owner of the Mill was not in love with Landry. And, in truth, he feared no danger from a rival such as this. It was with a certain pride and self-complacency that he compared the timid, boyish looks of his cousin, a mere lad of eighteen, with his own five feet six and well-set figure, and he was naturally led into thinking, that, however little of a woman of taste Madame Polet might be, Landry’s failure was a good reason for believing that his own success was assured. The Mill at Croyolles is charmingly situated at the bottom of a cool green valley; the stream that works it forms a little pond, which is shaded by pollard willows, and slender poplars; and between these dwarfed and giant trees stand magnificent alders, and immense walnut trees with their fragrant foliage. After turning the wheel of the mill, the foaming water runs off in a little rivulet, which never ceases its hymn of joy as it goes leaping over the pebbles of its bed, starring the flowers that lean coquettishly over to look at themselves in its clear shallows with the liquid diamonds that are scattered by its tiny waterfalls.

===Chinese===
蒂博本还要逼着朗德里把他影射的那桩事说明白，可这会儿两人已离磨坊不远，话头纵然起了个头，也只得半途作罢。况且蒂博心下想，自己知道的已够多了——朗德里爱着那位美貌的磨坊女主人，而那位美貌的磨坊女主人并不爱朗德里。说实在的，这样一个情敌，他并不引以为患。他带着几分得意、几分自满，把表弟那怯生生的少年模样——不过是个十八岁的毛孩子——与自己五尺六寸、匀称结实的身段一比，自然便生出念头：无论波莱太太算不算得上有品味的女子，朗德里的落败正好给了他理由相信，自己这头定能马到成功。克鲁瓦约尔的磨坊坐落得可真妙——在一道清凉翠绿的山谷尽头；推动磨盘的那道水汇成一方小潭，潭上有截了顶的柳树和细长的白杨遮荫；在这矮树与高树之间，又挺立着一棵棵好看的赤杨，还有那几棵极大的胡桃树，叶子散发着清香。水转过磨坊的轮子之后，便化作一条小溪泛着白沫流走，那溪水一刻不停地唱着欢乐的圣歌，在溪床的卵石上跳跃着，又把它那一道道小瀑布溅出的水钻，点点洒在那些探出身来、对着清澈浅水顾影自怜的花儿身上。

===Original===
The Mill itself lies so hidden in a bower of shrubs, behind the sycamores and weeping willows, that until one is within a short distance of it, nothing is to be seen but the chimney from which the smoke rises against the background of trees like a column of blue tinted alabaster. Although Thibault was familiar with the spot, the sight of it filled him, as he now looked upon it, with a feeling of delight which he had not hitherto experienced; but then he had never before gazed on it under the conditions in which he now found himself, for he was already conscious of that sense of personal satisfaction which the proprietor feels on visiting an estate which has been obtained for him by proxy. On entering the farmyard, where the scene was more animated, he was moved to even greater ecstasy of enjoyment.

The blue and purple-throated pigeons were cooing on the roofs, the ducks quacking, and going through sundry evolutions in the stream, the hens were clucking on the dung-heap, and the turkey cocks bridling and strutting as they courted the turkey-hens, while the brown and white cows came slowly in from the fields, their udders full of milk. Here, on one side, a cart was being unloaded, there, as they were being unharnessed, two splendid horses neighed and stretched their necks, now freed from the collar, towards their mangers; a boy was carrying a sack up into the granary, and a girl was bringing another sack filled with crusts and the refuse water to an enormous pig, that lay basking in the sun waiting to be transformed into salt-pork, sausages, and black puddings; all the animals of the ark were there, from the braying donkey to the crowing cock, mingling their discordant voices in this rural concert, while the mill with its regular click-clack, seemed to be beating time.

===Chinese===
磨坊本身藏得极深，掩在一丛灌木结成的凉棚里，前面又隔着梧桐与垂柳，不到近旁几步之遥，便什么也瞧不见，只看见一根烟囱——那烟囱里升起的烟，衬着树木的背景，宛若一根泛蓝的雪花石膏柱。虽说蒂博对这地方原是熟稔的，这会儿一看，心头却涌上一股从未有过的欢喜；不过话又说回来，他以往可不曾以今日这般身份来打量它——他分明已经尝到了那等做主人的得意滋味，正如一位东家去巡视那由人代为置下的产业时所感到的一般。一进到农家院子里，眼前景象更见热闹，他越发欢喜得如痴如醉。

那一群蓝羽紫颈的鸽子在屋瓦上咕咕地叫，鸭子们嘎嘎地叫着，在溪水里做着种种花样的泅水动作；母鸡们在粪堆上咯咯地唤；雄火鸡们一边向雌火鸡求爱，一边昂头挺胸、大摇大摆地踱着方步；一群棕色白色的奶牛正从地里慢悠悠地踱回来，乳房里涨满了奶。这边，一辆大车正在卸货；那边，两匹骏马卸了套，伸长脖子，摆脱了颈箍的束缚，朝着自己的料槽嘶鸣；一个小伙子正扛着一只口袋往谷仓上走，一个姑娘正提着另一只装满面包皮和泔水的口袋，走到一头其大无比的猪跟前——那猪正摊开身子晒着太阳，等着日后变成腌肉、香肠和黑布丁；方舟里有的禽畜这里一应俱全，从嘶鸣的驴到啼叫的鸡，都在这场田园交响曲里掺和进自己那不协和的嗓音，而那磨坊，以其有规律的咔嗒咔嗒声，仿佛在给这一切打着拍子。

===Original===
Thibault felt quite dazzled; he saw himself the owner of all that he now looked upon, and he rubbed his hands together with such evident pleasure, that Landry, if he had not been so absorbed in his own trouble, which grew ever greater as they drew nearer to the house, would certainly have noticed this apparently causeless emotion of joy on his cousin’s part. As they entered the farmyard, the widow, who was in the dining-room, became aware of their presence, and seemed very inquisitive to know who the stranger was who had returned with her head boy. Thibault, with an easy and confident sort of manner, went up to the dwelling-house, gave his name, and explained to her, that, having a great wish to see his cousin Landry, he had decided to come over and introduce himself to her.

The mistress of the Mill was extremely gracious, and invited the newcomer to spend the day at the Mill, accompanying her invitation with a smile that Thibault took as a most favourable augury.

===Chinese===
蒂博只觉得眼花缭乱；他看见自己成了眼前这一切的主人，两只手禁不住搓了又搓，那份欢喜之情溢于言表——若是朗德里不是那么一心沉浸在自己的烦恼里（离屋子越近，那烦恼便越深重），必定会察觉表哥这无缘无故的欣喜。两人走进院子时，正在饭厅里的寡妇觉察到他们来了，显得十分好奇，想弄清楚跟自家大伙计一同回来的这个陌生人究竟是谁。蒂博一副从容自在的神气，径直走到正屋跟前，报了姓名，对她说明，自己十分想念表弟朗德里，便打定主意过来一趟，顺便在她面前引荐引荐自己。

磨坊女主人殷勤极了，邀这位新客在磨坊盘桓一日，说这话时还带着一笑——蒂博把这一笑当作了最吉利的兆头。

===Original===
Thibault had not come unprovided with a present. He had unhooked some thrushes which he had found caught in a snare set with rowan-berries, as he came through the forest; and the widow sent them at once to be plucked, saying as she did so, that she hoped Thibault would stay to eat his share of them. But he could not help noticing that all the while she was speaking to him, she kept on looking over his shoulder at something which seemed to attract her attention, and turning quickly, he saw that the preoccupation of the fair owner of the Mill had evidently been caused by watching Landry, who was unloading his asses. Becoming conscious that Thibault had noticed the wandering of her looks and attention, Madame Polet turned as red as a cherry, but, immediately recovering herself she said to her new acquaintance:

“Monsieur Thibault, it would be kind of you, who appear so robust, to go and help your cousin; you can see that the job is too heavy a one for him alone,” and so saying, she went back into the house.

“Now, the devil!” muttered Thibault, as he looked first after Madame Polet and then at Landry, “is the fellow after all more fortunate than he suspects himself, and shall I be forced to call the black wolf to my assistance to get rid of him?”

However, he went as the owner of the mill had asked him, and gave the required assistance. Feeling quite sure that the pretty widow was looking at him through some chink or other of the curtain, he put forth all his strength, and displayed to the full his athletic grace, in the accomplishment of the task in which he was sharing.

===Chinese===
蒂博此番倒不是空手而来。他穿过林子时，瞧见一副用花楸果做饵的套子，便把套住的几只鸫鸟摘了下来带来；寡妇当即打发人拿去拔毛，一边说，盼着蒂博留下，好一同尝尝这鸫鸟的滋味。可蒂博忍不住察觉到，她对自己说话的当儿，目光一直越过他的肩头，望着什么吸引她注意的东西；他飞快地一回头，便看出来：这位美貌磨坊女主人方才的心不在焉，分明是看朗德里卸驴子看的。波莱太太发觉蒂博已注意到她眼神与心思的游移，脸一下子红得像颗樱桃，但随即定了定神，对这位新相识说：

“蒂博先生（Monsieur Thibault），您瞧着身强力壮的，劳您大驾去帮帮您表弟好不好；您瞧得见，这活儿他一个人干未免太重了些。”说罢，她便回屋去了。

“好哇，见鬼！”蒂博喃喃道，眼睛先目送着波莱太太，又转向朗德里，“莫非这家伙到底比他自己想的还要走运，难道我非得把黑狼（the black wolf）请出来帮忙，才能打发掉他？”

不过，他还是依着磨坊女主人的吩咐走过去，搭了一把手。他十拿九稳地断定，那标致寡妇正隔着窗帘的哪道缝儿在瞧着他，便使出浑身力气，在那桩他一道儿搭手的活计里，把自己那健美矫捷的身手展露了个淋漓尽致。

===Original===
The unloading finished, they all assembled in the dining-room where a waiting-maid was busy setting the table. As soon as dinner was served, Madame Polet took her place at the head of the table, with Thibault to her right. She was all attention and politeness to the latter, so much so indeed that Thibault, who had been temporarily crestfallen, took heart again, filled with hope. In order to do honour to Thibault’s present, she had herself dressed the birds with juniper-berries, and so prepared, no more delicate or appetising dish could well have been provided. While laughing at Thibault’s sallies, however, she cast stealthy glances now and again at Landry, who she saw had not touched what she herself had placed on the poor boy’s plate, and also that great tears were rolling down his cheeks, and falling into the untasted juniper sauce. This mute sorrow touched her heart; a look almost of tenderness came into her face, as she made a sign to him with her head, which seemed to say, so expressive was it, “Eat, Landry, I beg of you.” There was a whole world of loving promises in this little pantomime. Landry understood the gesture, for he nearly choked himself trying to swallow the bird at one mouthful, so eager was he to obey the orders of his fair mistress.

Nothing of all this escaped Thibault’s eye.

===Chinese===
卸完了货，众人齐集到饭厅里，一名使女正忙着摆桌。饭菜一摆上，波莱太太便在上首落座，让蒂博坐在她右手。她对蒂博关怀备至、礼数周全，竟使得方才一度灰了心的蒂博又壮起胆来，满怀希冀。为了给蒂博这份礼物挣个面子，她亲手用杜松子把那几只鸟调了味，这样一番整治下来，再没有比这更精致诱人的菜肴了。然而她一边因蒂博的俏皮话而发笑，一边又不时偷偷瞟一眼朗德里——她瞧见，自己亲手夹到那可怜孩子盘子里的肉他一口没动，还瞧见豆大的泪珠正顺着他的脸颊往下滚，滴在那没动过的杜松子酱里。这无声的悲苦叫她动了心；她脸上几乎现出一丝温柔的神情，朝他点了点头，那神情那么生动，仿佛在说：“吃吧，朗德里，求求你。”这一出小小的默剧里，藏着一整个世界的柔情许诺。朗德里懂了这一记眼神的意思——他恨不得一口把那只鸟整个儿吞下去，险些把自己噎个半死，他是这般急着要听从这位美貌女主人的吩咐。

这一切，一丝一毫也没逃过蒂博的眼睛。

===Original===
He swore to himself, using an oath that he had heard in the mouth of the Seigneur Jean, and which, now that he was the friend of the devil, he fancied he might use like any other great lord: “Can it be possible,” he thought, “that she is really in love with this slip of a youth? Well, if so, it does not say much for her taste, and more than that, it does not suit my plans at all. No, no, my fair mistress, what you need is a man who will know how to look well after the affairs of the mill, and that man will be myself or the black wolf will find himself in the wrong box.”

Noticing a minute later that Madame Polet had finally gone back to the earlier stage of sidelong glances and smiles which Landry had described to him, he continued, “I see I shall have to resort to stronger measures, for lose her I will not; there is not another match in all the countryside that would suit me equally well. But then, what am I to do with Cousin Landry? his love, it is true, upsets my arrangements; but I really cannot for so small a thing send him to join the wretched Marcotte in the other world. But what a fool I am to bother my brains about finding a way to help myself! It’s the wolf’s business, not mine?” Then in a low voice: “Black wolf,” he said, “arrange matters in such a way, that without any accident or harm happening to my Cousin Landry, I may get rid of him.” The prayer was scarcely uttered, when he caught sight of a small body of four or five men in military uniform, walking down the hillside and coming towards the mill. Landry also saw them; for he uttered a loud cry, got up as if to run away, and then fell back in his chair, as if all power of movement had forsaken him.

===Chinese===
他在心里起了一个誓——用的是他曾听让领主（the Seigneur Jean）嘴里说过的那种咒语，而他如今既是魔鬼的朋友，便觉得自己也可以像任何一位大老爷那样随口说说了：“难道真有这种事，”他想，“她当真爱上了这么个半大的毛孩子？倘若当真如此，那她的品味也未免太差了点，更要紧的是，这与我的盘算全然相左。不成，不成，我美貌的女主人，你需要的，是一个懂得如何照管磨坊事务的汉子，而这汉子，非我莫属——要不然，就叫黑狼自认倒了大霉去吧。”

过了一会儿，他发觉波莱太太到底又回到了朗德里对他描述过的那个地步——又递眼色，又含笑意。他接着想下去：“看来我非得用上更厉害的手段不可了，因为我绝不放手；放眼这一带乡下，再找不出第二门像我这样合算的亲事。可是，朗德里表弟这头又该怎么打发？他这一片痴情，诚然打乱了我的部署；可我也实在犯不着为这点小事就把他打发到另一个世界去，叫他与那可怜的马尔科特（Marcotte）作伴。唉，我这是何苦来，绞尽脑汁地想法子帮自己！这本是那狼的事，又不是我的事？”于是他压低嗓门念道：“黑狼啊，”他说，“把事情安排一下，让我既不遭什么意外、也不受什么伤害地摆脱我的朗德里表弟。”这祷告才一出口，他便瞧见一小队四五个穿军装的人，正从山坡上下来，朝磨坊走来。朗德里也瞧见了他们——他高叫一声，猛地起身像要逃跑，可又跌回椅子里，仿佛浑身的力气都已离他而去。

## XIX The Dead and the Living / XIX 死者与生者

===Original===
At the same moment that the trembling soul of the young Baron passed away, Thibault, awaking as if from an agitated sleep full of terrible dreams, sat up in his bed. He was surrounded by fire, every corner of his hut was in flames; at first he thought it was a continuation of his nightmare, but then he heard cries of, “Death to the wizard! death to the sorcerer! death to the werewolf!” and he understood that some terrible attack was being made upon him.

The flames came nearer, they reached the bed, he felt their heat upon him; a few seconds more and he would be burned alive in the midst of the flaming pile. Thibault leaped from his bed, seized his boar-spear, and dashed out of the back door of his hut. No sooner did his enemies see him rush through the fire and emerge from the smoke than their cries of “death to him!” “death!” were redoubled. One or two shots were fired at him; Thibault heard the bullets whizz past; those who shot at him wore the livery of the Grand Master, and Thibault recalled the menace of the lord of Vez, uttered against him a few days before.

He was then beyond the pale of the law; he could be smoked out of his hole like a fox; he could be shot down like a buck. Luckily for Thibault, not one of the bullets struck him, and as the circle of fire made by the burning hut was not a large one, he was soon safely beyond it, and once again in shelter of the vast and gloomy forest, where, had it not been for the cries of the menials who were burning down his house, the silence would have been as complete as the darkness. He sat down at the foot of a tree and buried his head in his hands. The events of the last forty-eight hours had succeeded each other with such rapidity, that there was no lack of matter to serve as subjects of reflection to the shoemaker.

The twenty-four hours, during which he had lived another existence than his own, seemed to him like a dream, so much so, that he would not have dared to take his oath that all this recent affair between the Baron, and the Countess Jane, and the Comte de Mont-Gobert had really taken place. The church clock of Oigny struck ten, and he lifted his head. Ten o’clock! and only half-an-hour before he had been still in the body of the Baron Raoul, as he lay dying in the house of the Curé of Puiseux.

===Chinese===
年轻男爵那颤抖的灵魂离开躯壳的同一刹那，蒂博像从一场尽是骇人噩梦的躁动睡梦里醒过来似的，在床上坐起了身。他四下里被火围着，小屋的每一个角落都烧着了；起初他还以为这不过是那场噩梦的延续，可随后他听见了那一阵阵喊声：“巫师该死！术士该死！狼人该死！”他便明白，正有一场可怕的袭击落在他头上。

火焰逼了过来，烧到了床边，他已经觉出那热度烤在身上；再过几秒钟，他就要被活活烧死在这熊熊火堆之中了。蒂博从床上一跃而起，抄起他的野猪矛（boar-spear），从小屋后门冲了出去。他的敌人们一见他冲过火光、从浓烟里钻出来，“打死他！”“打死他！”的喊声便加倍地响了起来。一两枪朝他放了过来；蒂博听见子弹从耳边嗖嗖飞过；朝他开枪的人身上穿着猎狼大将（the Grand Master）的号衣，蒂博便记起了韦兹领主（the lord of Vez）几天前对他发出的那一番威吓。

这么说，他已落在法度的庇护之外了；人家可以像熏一只狐狸那样，把他从洞里熏出来；可以像射一头雄鹿那样，把他射倒。算蒂博走运，没有一颗子弹打中他，而那燃烧的小屋围出来的火圈本也不大，他没多会儿便安然冲到了火圈之外，重又躲进了那片广袤阴森的大森林——在这里，若不是那些正放火烧他屋子的下人们嚷个不住，那一片寂静本该和那一片黑暗一样深重的。他在一棵树下坐下来，把头埋进了两只手里。这四十八小时之内发生的事，一桩接一桩来得这样急，倒叫这位鞋匠有的是材料可供反复思量。

那二十四小时里，他过的原是另一个人家的日子，如今回想起来竟像一场梦一般，他甚至不敢起誓说，男爵、简伯爵夫人（the Countess Jane）、蒙-戈贝尔伯爵（the Comte de Mont-Gobert）之间这一桩桩近来之事，果真是发生过。瓦尼（Oigny）教堂的钟敲了十下，他抬起头来。十点钟！而半个钟头之前，他还身在拉乌尔男爵（the Baron Raoul）的躯壳里，躺在皮瑟（Puiseux）本堂神父（the Curé）家中奄奄一息呢。

===Original===
“Ah!” he exclaimed “I must find out for certain what has happened! It is not quite three miles to Puiseux and I shall be there in half-an-hour; I should like to ascertain if the Baron is really dead.” A melancholy howl made answer to his words; he looked round; his faithful bodyguards were back again; he had his pack about him once more.

“Come, wolves! come, my only friends!” he cried, “let us be off!” And he started with them across the forest in the direction of Puiseux. The huntsmen of the Lord of Vez, who were poking up the remaining embers of the ruined hut, saw a man pass, as in a vision, running at the head of a dozen or more wolves. They crossed themselves, and became more convinced than ever that Thibault was a wizard. And anybody else who had seen Thibault, flying along as swiftly as his swiftest wolf, and covering the ground between Oigny and Puiseux in less than a quarter of an hour, would certainly have thought so too.

He stopped at the entrance to the village, and turning to his wolves, he said:

“Friend wolves, I have no further need of you tonight, and indeed, I wish to be alone. Amuse yourselves with the stables in the neighbourhood, I give you leave to do just what you like; and if you chance to come across one of those two-footed animals, called men, forget, friend wolves, that they claim to be made in the image of their Creator, and never fear to satisfy your appetite.” Whereupon the wolves rushed off in different directions, uttering howls of joy, while Thibault went on into the village. The Curé’s house adjoined the church, and Thibault made a circuit so as to avoid passing in front of the Cross. When he reached the presbytery, he looked in through one of the windows, and there he saw a bed with a lighted wax candle beside it; and over the bed itself was spread a sheet, and beneath the sheet could be seen the outlines of a figure lying rigid in death. There appeared to be no one in the house; the priest had no doubt gone to give notice of the death to the village authorities. Thibault went inside, and called the priest, but no one answered. He walked up to the bed, there could be no mistake about the body under the sheet being that of a dead man; he lifted the sheet, there could be no mistaking that the dead body was that of Raoul de Vauparfond. On his face lay the still, unearthly beauty which is born of eternity. His features, which in life had been somewhat too feminine for those of a man, had now assumed the sombre grandeur of death. At the first glance you might have thought he only slept; but on gazing longer you recognised in that immovable calm something more profound than sleep. The presence of one who carries a sickle for sceptre, and wears a shroud for mantle was unmistakeable, and you knew King Death was there.

===Chinese===
“啊！”他失声叫道，“我得弄个明白，究竟出了什么事！到皮瑟不过三英里路，我半个钟头就到了；我倒要查实查实，男爵是不是真的死了。”一声凄凉的嚎叫回应了他的话；他四下一看；他那些忠心耿耿的贴身护卫又回来了；他的狼群又围在他身边了。

“来吧，狼儿们！来吧，我唯一的朋友们！”他喊道，“咱们走罢！”他便带着它们穿过森林，朝皮瑟那一边奔去。韦兹领主（the Lord of Vez）手下的猎手们正拨弄着那座废屋残存的余烬，忽然瞧见一个人跑了过去——恍如幻象一般——身后领着十来头狼。他们赶紧在胸前画了个十字，越发相信蒂博是个巫师了。换了旁人，若是瞧见蒂博这般飞奔——脚程竟与他跑得最快的狼一般快，从瓦尼到皮瑟这段路不到一刻钟便赶完了——也定会作如是想。

他在村口停下脚步，转身对狼群说道：

“狼朋友们，今夜我不再用得着你们了，况且，我倒想独自待一会儿。附近那些马厩，你们只管去寻开心，我许你们爱怎么闹便怎么闹；万一你们撞上哪一种两条腿的畜生——唤作人的，狼朋友们，你们只管忘了他们自夸是照着造物主的模样做的，尽管放开肚皮吃个饱就是。”狼群听了，便四下里嗥叫着欢天喜地散了开去，蒂博则往村子里面走去。本堂神父的屋子紧挨着教堂，蒂博绕了个弯，免得从十字架跟前经过。他走到神父宅（the presbytery）前，从一扇窗子往里张望，只见里头摆着一张床，床头点着一支蜡；床上蒙着一条床单，床单底下，隐约能看出一个僵直地躺着的人身的轮廓。屋里似乎并没有人；那神父想必是去把这死讯报给村里的官府了。蒂博走进屋里，唤了唤神父，却无人答应。他走到床前，床单底下躺着的分明是一具尸首，断不会认错；他揭开床单，那一具尸首是拉乌尔·德·沃帕丰（Raoul de Vauparfond）的，也断不会认错。那张脸上，凝着一种由永恒中生出的、静谧而超乎尘世的美。他的五官，生前作为男子原本略嫌太过柔秀了些，如今却带上了死亡那阴沉的庄严。乍一看去，你也许会当他不过是在酣睡；可再凝神细看，你便会从那纹丝不动的安详里，觉出一种比睡眠更深沉的东西。那一位手执镰刀当作权杖、披着裹尸布当作大氅的王者，其驾临是错不了的——你便知道，死神国王（King Death）就在那里。

===Original===
Thibault had left the door open, and he heard the sound of light footsteps approaching; at the back of the alcove hung a serge curtain, which masked a door by which he could retreat, if necessary, and he now went and placed himself behind it. A woman dressed in black, and covered with a black veil, paused in some hesitation at the door. The head of another woman passed in front of hers and looked carefully round the room.

“I think it is safe for Madame to go in; I see no one about, and besides, I will keep watch.”

The woman in black went in, walked slowly towards the bed, stopped a moment to wipe the perspiration from her forehead, then, without further hesitation, lifted the sheet which Thibault had thrown back over the face of the dead man; Thibault then saw that it was the Countess.

“Alas!” she said, “what they told me was true!”

Then she fell on her knees, praying and sobbing. Her prayer being ended, she rose again, kissed the pale forehead of the dead, and the blue marks of the wound through which the soul had fled.

===Chinese===
蒂博走时没关门，他听见了轻盈的脚步声渐渐近来；床龛后头挂着一条哔叽帷幕，遮住了一扇门，必要的时候他可以从那儿退走，于是他便走过去躲到了帷幕背后。一个一身黑衣、蒙着黑面纱的女子，在门口停下，有些迟疑。另一个女子的头从她身前探出来，细细地朝屋里四下张望了一番。

“我看夫人进去是稳妥的；我没瞧见附近有人，再者，由我替您放哨。”

那黑衣女子走了进去，缓步朝床前走去，停了一停，抹了抹额头上的汗，随后不再犹豫，揭开了蒂博重新盖在死人脸上的那条床单；蒂博这才看清，原来正是伯爵夫人。

“唉！”她说道，“他们对我说的竟是真的！”

说罢她跪倒在地，一边祈祷，一边抽泣。祈祷完毕，她又站起身来，亲吻了死者苍凉的额头，又亲吻了那道伤口的青紫印痕——灵魂便是从那里逃走的。

===Original===
“O my well-beloved, my Raoul;” she murmured, “who will tell me the name of your murderer? who will help me to avenge your death?” As the Countess finished speaking, she gave a cry and started back; she seemed to hear a voice that answered, “I will!” and something had shaken the green serge curtain.

The Countess however was no chickenhearted woman; she took the candle that was burning at the head of the bed and went and looked behind the curtain; but no creature was to be seen, a closed door was all that met her eye. She put back the candle, took a pair of gold scissors from a little pocket case, cut off a curl of the dead man’s hair, placed the curl in a black velvet sachet which hung over her heart, gave one last kiss to her dead lover, laid the sheet over his face, and left the house. Just as she was crossing the threshold, she met the priest, and drawing back, drew her veil more closely over her face.

“Who are you?” asked the priest.

“I am Grief,” she answered, and the priest made way for her to pass.

===Chinese===
“噢，我最心爱的，我的拉乌尔；”她低声呢喃，“谁会告诉我，杀害你的凶手姓甚名谁？谁会帮我为你报仇雪恨？”伯爵夫人的话音刚落，便一声惊叫，直退了回去；她仿佛听见有一个声音答道：“我！”又有一样东西晃动了那条绿色的哔叽帷幕。

不过伯爵夫人并非那种胆小如鼠的女人；她拿起床头燃着的那支蜡烛，走过去朝帷幕背后看了看；却什么活物也没瞧见，映入眼帘的只有一扇紧闭的门。她把蜡烛放回原处，从一只随身小匣里取出一把金剪刀，铰下死者的一绺卷发，放进一只挂在她心口的黑丝绒小袋里，最后又亲了她那死去的情人一下，把床单重新盖到他脸上，便离开了这屋子。她正跨过门槛，迎面撞见了那神父，便退后一步，把面纱更严地遮住了脸。

“您是谁？”神父问道。

“我是哀恸（Grief），”她答道，神父便让到一旁，由她过去了。

===Original===
The Countess and her attendant had come on foot, and were returning in the same manner, for the distance between Puiseux and Mont-Gobert was not much more than half-a-mile. When about halfway along their road, a man, who had been hiding behind a willow tree, stepped forward and barred their further passage. Lisette screamed, but the Countess, without the least sign of fear, went up to the man, and asked: “Who are you?”

“The man who answered ‘I will’ just now, when you were asking who would denounce the murderer to you.”

“And you can help me to revenge myself on him?”

“Whenever you like.”

“At once?”

“We cannot talk here very well.”

“Where can we find a better place?”

“In your own room for one.”

“We must not enter the castle together.”

“No; but I can go through the breach in the park wall: Mademoiselle Lisette can wait for me in the hut where Monsieur Raoul used to leave his horse, she can take me up the winding-stair and into your room. If you should be in your dressing-room, I will wait for you, as Monsieur Raoul waited the night before last.”

The two women shuddered from head to foot.

“Who are you to know all these details?” asked the Countess.

“I will tell you when the time comes for me to tell you.”

The Countess hesitated a moment, then, recovering her resolution, she said:

“Very well then; come through the breach; Lisette will wait for you in the stable.”

“Oh! Madame,” cried the maid, “I shall never dare to go and bring that man to you!”

“I will go myself then,” said the Countess.

“Well said!” put in Thibault, “there spoke a woman worth calling one!” And so saying he slid down into a kind of ravine beside the road, and disappeared. Lisette very nearly fainted.

“Lean on me, Mademoiselle,” said the Countess, “and let us walk on; I am anxious to hear what this man has to say to me.”

===Chinese===
伯爵夫人和她的随行是步行而来的，回去也是步行，因为皮瑟与蒙-戈贝尔（Mont-Gobert）之间的路程，不过半英里出头。走到半路上，一个原本躲在一棵柳树背后的男子闪了出来，挡住了她们的去路。莉赛特（Lisette）尖叫了一声，伯爵夫人却没有半点惧色，径直走到那人跟前，问道：“你是谁？”

“就是方才您问起谁会替您举发那凶手时，答了一声‘我’的那个人。”

“那么你能帮我向他复仇了？”

“随您的便，什么时候都成。”

“眼下就成么？”

“这地方不好说话。”

“上哪儿找个好说话的去处呢？”

“您自己的房里便是一处。”

“咱们不能一道进城堡去。”

“自是不能；可我可以从园子围墙的缺口那儿钻进去：莉赛特小姐可以在拉乌尔先生从前拴马的那座小屋里等我，她可以带我顺着旋梯上您房里去。倘若您在梳妆室里，我便等您，就像拉乌尔先生前儿夜里等您那般。”

两个女人从头到脚打了个寒噤。

“你是什么人，竟知道这许多细情？”伯爵夫人问。

“该告诉您的时候，我自然会告诉您。”

伯爵夫人迟疑了一下，随即又打定了主意，说道：

“那便这样罢；从缺口进来；莉赛特会在马厩里等你。”

“哎呀！夫人，”那丫鬟叫道，“我可不敢去把那样一个男人带到您跟前！”

“那我自己去，”伯爵夫人说。

“说得好！”蒂博插嘴道，“这话才像个有胆量的娘儿们说的！”说罢他便溜到路旁一道沟壑里，不见了踪影。莉赛特险些晕了过去。

“靠着我，小姐，”伯爵夫人说，“咱们往前走罢；我急着要听这人有话对我说呢。”

===Original===
The two women entered the castle by way of the farm; no one had seen them go out, and no one saw them return. On reaching her room, the Countess waited for Lisette to bring up the stranger. Ten minutes had elapsed when the maid hurried in with a pale face.

“Ah! Madame,” she said, “there was no need for me to go to fetch him.”

“What do you mean?” asked the Countess.

“Because he knew his way up as well as I did! And oh! Madame! if you knew what he said to me! That man is the devil, Madame, I feel sure!”

“Show him in,” said the Countess.

“I am here!” said Thibault.

“You can leave us now, my girl,” said the Countess to Lisette. The latter quitted the room and the Countess remained alone with Thibault. Thibault’s appearance was not one to inspire confidence. He gave the impression of a man who had once and for all made up his mind, but it was also easy to see that it was for no good purpose; a Satanic smile played about his mouth, and there was a demoniacal light in his eyes. He had made no attempt to hide his red hairs, but had left them defiantly uncovered, and they hung over his forehead like a plume of flame. But still the Countess looked him full in the face without changing colour.

“My maid says that you know the way to my room; have you ever been here before?”

“Yes, Madame, once.”

“And when was that?”

“The day before yesterday.”

“At what time?”

“From half-past ten till half-past twelve at night.”

The Countess looked steadily at him and said:

“That is not true.”

“Would you like me to tell you what took place?”

“During the time you mention?”

“During the time I mention.”

“Say on,” replied the Countess, laconically.

Thibault was equally laconic.

“Monsieur Raoul came in by that door,” he said, pointing to the one leading into the corridor, “and Lisette left him here alone. You entered the room by that one,” he continued, indicating the dressing-room door, “and you found him on his knees. Your hair was unbound, only fastened back by three diamond pins, you wore a pink silk dressing-gown, trimmed with lace, pink silk stockings, cloth-of-silver slippers and a chain of pearls round your neck.”

“You describe my dress exactly,” said the Countess, “continue.”

“You tried to pick a quarrel with Monsieur Raoul, first because he loitered in the corridors to kiss your waiting-maid; secondly, because someone had met him late at night on the road between Erneville and Villers-Cotterets; thirdly, because, at the ball given at the Castle, at which you yourself were not present, he danced four times with Madame de Bonneuil.”

“Continue.”

“In answer to your accusations, your lover made excuses for himself, some good, some bad; you, however, were satisfied with them for you were just forgiving him when Lisette rushed in full of alarm calling to Monsieur Raoul to escape, as your husband had just returned.”

“Lisette was right, you can be nothing less than the devil,” said the Countess with a sinister laugh, “and I think we shall be able to do business together.… Finish your account.”

“Then you and your maid together pushed Monsieur Raoul, who resisted, into the dressing-room; Lisette forced him along the corridors and through two or three rooms; they then went down a winding staircase, in the wing of the Castle opposite to the one by which they had gone up. On arriving at the foot of the staircase, the fugitives found the door locked; then they ran into a kind of office where Lisette opened the window, which was about seven or eight feet above the ground. Monsieur Raoul leaped down out of this window, ran to the stable, found his horse still there, but hamstrung; then he swore that if he met the Count at any time he would hamstring him as the Count had hamstrung his horse, for he thought it a cowardly act to injure a poor beast so unnecessarily. Then he went on foot to the breach, climbed it, and found the Count awaiting him outside the park, with his sword drawn. The Baron had his hunting-knife with him; he drew it, and the duel began.”

“Was the Count alone?”

“Wait… the Count appeared to be alone; after the fourth or fifth pass the Count was wounded in the shoulder, and sank on one knee, crying: ‘help, Lestocq!’ Then the Baron remembered his oath, and hamstrung the Count as he had hamstrung the horse; but as the Baron rose, Lestocq drove his knife into his back; it passed under the shoulder blade and out through the chest. I need not tell you where… you kissed the wound yourself.”

“And after that?”

“The Count and his huntsman returned to the Castle, leaving the Baron lying helpless; when the latter came to, he made signs to some passing peasants, who put him on a litter, and bore him away, with the intention of taking him to Villers-Cotterets; but he was in such pain, that they could not carry him farther than Puiseux; there they laid him on the bed where you found him, and on which he breathed his last a second after the half hour after nine in the evening.”

===Chinese===
两个女子从农庄那一头进了城堡；她们出来时无人瞧见，回去时也无人瞧见。回到自己房里，伯爵夫人便等莉赛特把那陌生人领上来。过了十分钟，那丫鬟脸色煞白地跑了进来。

“哎呀！夫人，”她说，“我根本不必去接他。”

“此话怎讲？”伯爵夫人问。

“因为那条上来的路，他竟和我一样熟！还有，噢！夫人！要是您知道他对我说了些什么！那个人就是魔鬼，夫人，我敢保！”

“让他进来，”伯爵夫人说。

“我在这里！”蒂博说道。

“你下去罢，丫头，”伯爵夫人对莉赛特说。莉赛特退出房去，伯爵夫人便与蒂博独处了。蒂博这副模样，原不是叫人见了便能安心的。他给人的印象，是一个已经铁了心、再不回头的汉子，可也同样不难看出，他这一条心并非为着什么好勾当；一丝撒旦般的笑意挂在他嘴角，眼里也透着一股恶魔似的光。他并无半点遮掩他那几绺红发的意思，反倒挑衅似地任它们裸露在外，像一撮火焰似的垂在额前。然而伯爵夫人仍旧直直地盯着他的脸，面色不改分毫。

“我的丫鬟说，你认得到我房里来的路；你从前到这儿来过么？”

“来过，夫人，一回。”

“那是何时？”

“前天。”

“什么时候？”

“夜里十点半，到十二点半。”

伯爵夫人定定地瞧着他，说道：

“这不是真的。”

“您要我细说说那时出了什么事么？”

“在我所说的那段时间里？”

“在我所说的那段时间里。”

“说罢，”伯爵夫人言简意赅地答道。

蒂博也同样言简意赅。

“拉乌尔先生是从那扇门进来的，”他说着，指了指通走廊的那一扇，“莉赛特便把他一人丢在这儿。您是从这一扇进来的，”他接着说，指了指梳妆室的门，“您瞧见他正跪着。您那时头发散着，只用三枚钻石别针松松地别在脑后，身穿一件镶花边的粉色丝绸晨衣，脚登粉色丝袜、银缕拖鞋，颈上挂着一串珍珠链子。”

“您把我的穿戴说得一丝不差，”伯爵夫人道，“往下说。”

“您跟拉乌尔先生寻衅找岔，一则为他在走廊里磨蹭，偷吻您的贴身丫鬟；二则因为有人深夜在埃纳维尔（Erneville）与维莱-科特雷（Villers-Cotterets）之间的大路上撞见过他；三则因为城堡里那场舞会——您本人并未到场——他竟与博纳伊夫人（Madame de Bonneuil）连跳了四回。”

“往下说。”

“您的情人听您这一桩桩责问，便替自己分辩起来，有的说辞尚好，有的却蹩脚；您听了却还算满意，正要饶恕他，莉赛特便慌慌张张地奔了进来，叫拉乌尔先生快逃，说是您的丈夫刚刚回来了。”

“莉赛特说得不错，你除了是魔鬼，再不可能是别的了，”伯爵夫人阴恻恻地笑了一声，说道，“我想，咱们是能做得成一桩买卖的。……把你的话说完罢。”

“于是您和您的丫鬟一同把反抗着的拉乌尔先生推进了梳妆室；莉赛特押着他穿过走廊，又过了两三间屋子；随后他们下了一道旋梯，那旋梯在城堡里与他们上来的那一翼相对的另一翼之中。逃命的人下到梯脚，却发现那门锁着；他们便奔进一间像是账房之类的屋子，莉赛特打开了窗，那窗子离地约莫七八英尺高。拉乌尔先生从这窗子里跳了下去，奔到马厩，发觉他的马还在，可腿筋已被人挑断；他便发下誓愿，倘若日后撞见伯爵，定要把伯爵的腿筋也挑断，一如伯爵挑断他这匹马的腿筋，因为他觉得，如此毫无必要地伤害一头可怜的畜生，实是怯懦之举。随后他步行到缺口处，攀了过去，便瞧见伯爵手执出鞘的长剑，在园子外头等着他。男爵随身带着猎刀；他抽了出来，决斗便开始了。”

“伯爵是一个人么？”

“慢着……伯爵瞧着像是一个人；斗到第四五个回合，伯爵肩上挨了一刀，单膝跪倒，叫道：‘来人，勒斯托克（Lestocq）！’男爵便记起了自己的誓言，照着挑断马腿筋的法子，把伯爵的腿筋也挑断了；可男爵刚一欠身站起来，勒斯托克便一刀戳进了他的后背；那刀从肩胛骨底下穿进去，从胸口穿出来。那地方我原不必细说……那伤口您自己都亲过了。”

“那之后呢？”

“伯爵和他的猎手回了城堡，任男爵无助地躺在那里；后来男爵苏醒过来，向几个过路的庄稼汉打手势，他们便把他抬上一副担架，抬着他走，原打算抬到维莱-科特雷去；可他疼得厉害，他们抬到皮瑟便再也抬不动了；他们便把他安顿在您方才见着他的那张床上，晚上九点半过一秒，他就在那张床上咽了最后一口气。”

===Original===
The Countess rose, and without speaking, went to her jewel-case and took out the pearls she had worn two nights before. She handed them to Thibault.

“What are they for?” he asked.

“Take them,” said the Countess, “they are worth fifty thousand livres.”

“Are you still anxious for revenge?”

“Yes,” replied the Countess.

“Revenge will cost more than that.”

“How much will it cost?”

“Wait for me tomorrow night,” said Thibault, “and I will tell you.”

“Where shall I await you?” asked the Countess.

“Here,” said Thibault, with the leer of a wild animal.

“I will await you here,” said the Countess.

“Till tomorrow then.”

“Till tomorrow.”

Thibault went out. The Countess went and replaced the pearls in her dressing case; lifted up a false bottom, and drew from underneath it a small bottle containing an opal-coloured liquid, and a little dagger with a jewelled handle and case, and a blade inlaid with gold. She hid both beneath her pillow, knelt at her prie-dieu, and, her prayer finished, threw herself dressed on to her bed.…

===Chinese===
伯爵夫人站起身来，一言不发，走到她的首饰匣前，取出她前儿夜里戴过的那串珍珠，递给蒂博。

“这是做什么？”他问。

“拿着罢，”伯爵夫人道，“这值五万里弗（livres）。”

“您还急着要复仇么？”

“急着呢，”伯爵夫人答道。

“复仇的价码，可不止这些。”

“要多少？”

“明晚等我，”蒂博说，“届时我告诉您。”

“我在哪儿等您？”伯爵夫人问。

“就在这儿，”蒂博说，那神情活像一头野兽在舔嘴。

“我便在这儿等您，”伯爵夫人说。

“那就明儿见。”

“明儿见。”

蒂博走了出去。伯爵夫人走过去，把那串珍珠放回首饰匣；她掀起匣底一块活动的夹板，从底下取出一只小瓶——里头盛着一种蛋白石般颜色的液体——还有一把镶珠嵌玉、连鞘带柄的小匕首，刀刃上镶着黄金。她把这两样东西都藏在枕头底下，跪到祈祷台（prie-dieu）前，祷告已毕，便连衣裳也不脱，一头扑到床上。……

## XXI The Genius of Evil / XXI 邪灵

===Original===
The next evening, about nine o’clock, a man might be seen walking along the Puits-Sarrasin road and making for the Osiéres forest-path.

It was Thibault, on his way to pay a last visit to the hut, and to see if any remains of it had been left by the fire. A heap of smoking cinders alone marked the place where it had stood; and as Thibault came in sight of it, he saw the wolves, as if he had appointed them to meet him there, forming an immense circle round the ruins, and looking upon them with an expression of mournful anger. They seemed to understand that by destroying this poor hut, made of earth and branches, the one who, by the compact with the black wolf, had been given them for master, had been made a victim. As Thibault entered the circle, all the wolves gave simultaneously a long and sinister sounding howl, as if to make him understand that they were ready to help in avenging him.

===Chinese===
第二天傍晚九点钟光景，可以看见有一个人正顺着普伊-萨拉森（Puits-Sarrasin）大路走着，朝奥西耶尔（Osiéres）林间小径而去。

正是蒂博（Thibault），他要去小屋那边再看最后一眼，瞧瞧大火之后是否还留下点什么。只剩下一堆还在冒烟的灰烬，标示着小屋原本所在的位置；蒂博远远望见那废墟时，只见一群狼——仿佛是他约好了在那儿会面似的——围着断壁残垣围成了一个大大的圆圈，神情悲愤地凝视着那堆焦土。它们似乎明白：摧毁这所泥土树枝搭成的小破屋，也就等于害苦了那个凭与黑狼（the black wolf）所立之约被指派给它们当主子的人。蒂博一走进狼圈，所有的狼便同时发出一声拖长的、阴森的嚎叫，像是要让他知道，它们已准备好助他一臂之力，替他报仇雪恨。

===Original===
Thibault went and sat down on the spot where the hearth had stood; it was recognisable from a few blackened stones still remaining, which were otherwise uninjured, and by a higher heap of cinders just at that spot. He stayed there some minutes, absorbed in his unhappy thoughts. But he was not reflecting that the ruin which he saw around him was the consequence and the punishment of his jealous and covetous desires, which had gone on gathering strength. He felt neither repentance nor regret. That which dominated all other feeling in him was his satisfaction at the thought of being henceforth able to render to his fellow-creatures evil for evil, his pride in having, thanks to his terrible auxiliaries, the power to fight against those who persecuted him.

===Chinese===
蒂博走过去，在灶膛原先所在的位置坐下；从几块尚未全毁、只是烧黑了的石头，再加上那一处堆得更高些的灰烬，便能认出这里。他在那儿坐了几分钟，沉浸在自己那苦涩的思绪里。可他心里丝毫没有反省：眼前这满目疮痍，本是他那日益滋长的嫉妒与贪婪之心所招致的后果与报应。他既无悔意，也无懊恨。压倒他心中一切其他情感的，唯有这么一种满足——想到自己从此能够以眼还眼、以牙还牙地对付同类；又有这么一种骄傲——多亏了他那些可怕的帮手，他如今有了与迫害他的人相抗衡的力量。

===Original===
And as the wolves continued their melancholy howling: “Yes, my friends,” said Thibault, “yes, your howls answer to the cry of my heart.… My fellow-creatures have destroyed my hut, they have cast to the winds the ashes of the tools wherewith I earned my daily bread; their hatred pursues me as it pursues you, I expect from them neither mercy nor pity. We are their enemies as they are ours; and I will have neither mercy nor compassion on them. Come then, let us go from this hut to the Castle, and carry thither the desolation which they have brought home to me.”

And then the master of the wolves, like a chief of banditti followed by his desperadoes, set off with his pack in quest of pillage and carnage.

===Chinese===
听着群狼那凄凉的嚎叫仍在继续，蒂博说道：“是啊，朋友们，是啊，你们的嚎声正应着我心里的呐喊。……我的同类毁了我的屋子，把我挣饭吃的家什的灰烬撒向了四方；他们的仇恨追逐着我，就像追逐你们一样，我不指望从他们那里得到半点怜悯与慈悲。我们是他们的仇敌，正如他们也是我们的仇敌；我也绝不会对他们手下留情、动什么恻隐之心。那么来吧，咱们从小屋开到城堡（the Castle）去，把他们送到我家门口的这场浩劫，原样送回他们那边去。”

于是这位群狼之主，便如一个亡命之徒簇拥着的强盗头子一般，率领他的狼群出动，去寻觅劫掠与杀戮了。

===Original===
This time it was neither red-deer, nor fallow-deer, nor any timid game of which they were in pursuit. Sheltered by the darkness of the night Thibault first directed his course to the Château of Vez, for there was lodged his chief enemy. The Baron had three farms belonging to the estate, stables filled with horses, and others filled with cows, and the park was full of sheep. All these places were attacked the first night, and on the morrow two horses, four cows, and ten sheep were found killed.

The Baron was doubtful at first if this could be the work of the beasts against which he waged so fierce a warfare; there seemed something partaking rather of intelligence and revenge in it than of the mere unreasoning attacks of a pack of wild animals. Still it seemed manifest that the wolves must have been the aggressors, judging by the marks of teeth on the carcases and the footprints left on the ground. Next night the Baron set watchers to lie in wait, but Thibault and his wolves were at work on the farther side of the forest. This time it was the stables and parks of Soucy and of Vivières which were decimated, and the following night those of Boursonnes and Yvors. The work of annihilation, once begun, must be carried out with desperate determination, and the master never left his wolves now; he slept with them in their dens, and lived in the midst of them, stimulating their thirst for blood.

===Chinese===
这一回他们所追逐的，既不再是赤鹿，也不是扁角鹿，更不是任何怯懦的猎物。借着夜色的掩护，蒂博先把矛头指向韦兹城堡（the Château of Vez）——因为他那位头号仇人就住在那里。男爵名下有三处属于产业的农庄，有塞满马的马厩，也有挤满牛的牛棚，苑囿里还放养着成群的羊。头一晚这些地方便尽数遭袭，第二天一早便发现有十只羊、四头牛、两匹马被咬死。

起初男爵还拿不准，这是否就是他正与之激战的那群畜生所为；这事里头似乎掺着某种带智性的、图报仇的味道，倒不大像一群野兽毫无心计的乱扑乱咬。然而，从尸体上的牙印和地上留下的足印来判断，肇事者显然又是狼。第二夜男爵设下埋伏守候，可蒂博和他的狼群却在森林另一头闹得天翻地覆。这一回遭殃的是苏西（Soucy）和维维耶尔（Vivières）的马厩与苑囿，再下一夜便轮到了布尔松（Boursonnes）和伊沃尔（Yvors）。这场毁灭之工一经开端，便要以拼命的狠劲干到底；如今这位主子片刻不离他的狼群，与它们同眠于兽穴之中，与它们朝夕厮混，挑动着它们嗜血的渴望。

===Original===
Many a woodman, many a heath-gatherer, came face to face in the thickets with the menacing white teeth of a wolf, and was either carried off and eaten, or just saved his life by the aid of his courage and his billhook. Guided by a human intelligence, the wolves had become organised and disciplined, and were far more formidable than a band of discontented soldiery let loose in a conquered country.

The terror of them became general; no one dared go beyond the towns and villages unarmed; horses and cattle were all fed inside the stables, and the men themselves, their work done, waited for one another, so as not to go about singly. The Bishop of Soissons ordered public prayer to be made, asking God to send a thaw, for the unusual ferocity of the wolves was attributed to the great quantity of snow that had fallen. But the report also went about that the wolves were incited to their work, and led about by a man; that this man was more indefatigable, more cruel and insatiable than the wolves themselves; that in imitation of his companions he ate raw flesh and quenched his thirst in blood. And the people went further and said that this man was Thibault.

===Chinese===
不知多少樵夫、多少拾柴火的，在林丛里撞见一头狼那白森森的、骇人的獠牙，要么被叼去吃了，要么全凭着胆量和手里的钩镰（billhook）才勉强保住一条性命。在一颗人脑的指挥之下，群狼变得有组织、有纪律，远比一伙被放到征服之国里去撒野的怨军还要可怕得多。

人人谈狼色变，风声鹤唳；没有谁敢不带兵器就走出城镇和村庄一步；马牛一律关在厩里喂养；干完活儿的汉子们也要彼此等着，不肯独自一个落单。苏瓦松（Soissons）主教下令举行公开祈祷，求上帝降下一场解冻——因为人们把群狼这异常的凶暴，都归咎于落得太大的雪。可也另有传言说，这些狼是有人撺掇着干这营生的，还有人领着它们四处转悠；这个人比狼群本身还要不知疲倦、还要凶残、还要贪得无厌；他效法同伴，也吃生肉，也饮血解渴。人们还说得更进一步——这个人，便是蒂博。

===Original===
The Bishop pronounced sentence of excommunication against the former shoemaker. The Lord of Vez, however, had little faith in the thunders of the Church being of much effect, unless supported by some well-conducted hunting. He was somewhat cast down at so much blood being spilt, and his pride was sorely hurt that his, the Grand Master’s, own cattle should have suffered so heavily from the very wolves he was especially appointed to destroy.

At the same time, he could not but feel a secret delight, at the thought of the triumphant view-halloos in store for him, and of the fame which he could not fail to win among all sportsmen of repute. His passion for the chase, excited by the way in which his adversaries the wolves had so openly entered upon the struggle, became absolutely overpowering; he allowed neither respite nor repose; he took no sleep himself and ate his meals in the saddle. All night long he scoured the country in company with l’Eveillé and Engoulevent, who, in consideration of his marriage had been raised to the rank of pricker; and the dawn had no sooner appeared before he was again in the saddle, ready to start and chase the wolf until it was too dark to distinguish the hounds. But alas! all his knowledge of the art of Venery, all his courage, all his perseverance, were lost labour. He occasionally brought down some wretched cub, some miserable beast eaten with mange, some imprudent glutton which had so gorged itself with carnage that its breath would not hold out after an hour or two’s run; but the larger, well-grown wolves, with their thick dark coats, their muscles like steel springs and their long slender feet—not one of these lost a hair in the war that was being made upon them. Thanks to Thibault they met their enemies in arms on nearly equal ground.

===Chinese===
主教对这位昔日的鞋匠宣布了绝罚的判决。然而韦兹领主（the Lord of Vez）对教会那雷霆之威究竟有多大效用，却不怎么信得过——除非再辅以一场调度得当的围猎。看到这么多血流成河，他颇为沮丧；而他身为猎狼大将（the Grand Master），自家牲口竟在他奉旨专门剿灭的狼口下吃了这么大的亏，这可把他的自尊伤得不轻。

可与此同时，一想到那等着他的、凯旋般的号角声，一想到他在一众有头有脸的猎人中必能赢下的赫赫声名，他心里又忍不住暗暗欢喜。他那嗜猎的狂热，被对手群狼如此公然开战的做派撩拨到了无以复加的地步；他不许人喘一口气、歇一刻脚；自己更是不眠不休，连饭都在马背上吃。他整夜整夜地带着勒韦耶（l’Eveillé）和昂古勒旺（Engoulevent）——昂古勒旺因已成婚的缘故，已被擢升为管犬役夫（pricker）——骑马巡游乡间；天一亮，他早已又跨上了马鞍，预备出发，要把那头狼追到天黑得连猎犬都分辨不清才肯罢休。只可惜！他所有的狩猎之术（the art of Venery）、所有的勇气、所有的坚韧，全都成了徒劳。他偶尔能撂倒一两头可怜的狼崽子，一两头被疥癣烂得不像样子的孱畜，或是哪头贪嘴冒失、吃得肚皮撑足了血肉，跑上一两个钟头便喘不上气的货色；至于那些个头大、长成了的壮狼——一身厚实的深色毛皮，筋肉如钢簧，四脚又长又细——在围剿它们这场仗里，没有一头掉过一根毛。多亏有蒂博在，它们几乎是武装齐备地、在近乎势均力敌的阵地上与来犯之敌相周旋。

===Original===
As the Baron of Vez remained forever with his dogs, so did Thibault with his wolves; after a night of sack and pillage, he kept the pack awake on the watch to help the one that the Baron had started. This wolf again, following Thibault’s instructions, had recourse at first to stratagem. It doubled, crossed its tracks, waded in the streams, leaped up into the bending trees so as to make it more difficult still for huntsmen and hounds to follow the scent, and finally when it felt its powers failing, it adopted bolder measures and went straight ahead. Then the other wolves and their master intervened; at the least sign of hesitation on the part of the hounds, they managed so cleverly to put them on the wrong scent, that it required an experienced eye to detect that the dogs were not all following up the same track, and nothing less than the Baron’s profound knowledge could decide which was the right one. Even he sometimes was mistaken.

===Chinese===
韦兹男爵（the Baron of Vez）永远和他的猎犬待在一起，蒂博也同样永远和他的狼群形影不离；一夜劫掠杀戮之后，他便让狼群守着不睡，好去帮那头被男爵放出来追的狼应援。而被追的那头狼，又遵照蒂博的指示，先施诈术：它折回原地、踏乱自己的足迹、涉水涉溪、纵身蹿上弯垂的树枝——好叫猎人和猎犬越发难以循气味追踪；等到它自觉气力将竭，便改用更大胆的法子，径直往前奔去。这时其余的狼和它们的主子便出来接应；猎犬这边一有半点迟疑，它们便施巧计把犬群引到错的气味上去，手段之高明，非得老练的眼睛才能瞧出狗并不全在循同一条迹线；而非得靠男爵那渊博的功底，才能判断哪一条才是真正的踪迹。可即便是他，有时也会看走眼。

===Original===
Again, the wolves in their turn followed the huntsmen; it was a pack hunting a pack; only the one hunted in silence, which made it far the more formidable of the two. Did a tired hound fall behind, or another get separated from the main body, it was seized and killed in an instant, and Engoulevent, whom we have had occasion to mention several times before and who had taken poor Marcotte’s place, having hastened one day to the help of one of his hounds that was uttering cries of distress, was himself attacked and only owed his life to the swiftness of his horse.

===Chinese===
再者，这一回轮到狼群反过来追踪猎人了；这是一群在猎另一群；只不过被追的那一群是悄然无声地行动，这就使它远比对手更为可怕。只要有一只疲惫的猎犬落了后，或是有哪一只与大部队走散了，它便被瞬间扑倒、咬死。昂古勒旺——我们前面已有几次三番提到他，如今他接了可怜的马尔科特（Marcotte）的班——有一日急忙赶去救援自己一只正哀嚎呼救的猎犬，结果自己也遭到袭击，全靠他的马跑得飞快，这才保住了性命。

===Original===
It was not long before the Baron’s pack was decimated; his best hounds were nearly dead with fatigue, and his more second-rate ones had perished by the wolves’ teeth. The stable was in no better condition than the kennel; Bayard was foundered, Tancred had sprained a tendon leaping over a ditch, and a strained fetlock had placed Valourous on the list of invalids. Sultan, luckier than his three companions had fallen honourably on the field of battle, having succumbed to a sixteen hours’ run under the weight of his gigantic master, who never for a moment lost courage notwithstanding the fact that the dead bodies of his finest and most faithful servitors lay heaped around him.

===Chinese===
没过多久，男爵那群猎犬便折损大半；他最好的几条几乎累得半死，二流的那些则已丧于狼牙之下。马厩的情形也不比犬舍强多少：巴雅尔（Bayard）腿蹄崩损，坦克雷德（Tancred）跃过一道沟时扭了筋，瓦勒鲁（Valourous）又因球节扭伤而被列入了伤兵名册。只有苏尔唐（Sultan）比它的三位同伴运气稍好——它是在战场上体面地倒下的：驮着它那巨人般的主人连跑了十六个钟头，终因力竭而毙；它那位主人呢，纵然身边横七竖八堆满了最精锐、最忠心的仆从的尸首，却一刻也未曾灰过心。

===Original===
The Baron, following the example of the noble-hearted Romans who exhausted the resources of military art against the Carthaginians who were forever reappearing as enemies, the Baron, I repeat, changed his tactics and tried what battues could accomplish. He called on all available men among the peasants, and beat up the game throughout the forest with such a formidable number of men, that not so much as a hare was left in its form near any spot which they had passed.

But Thibault made it his business to find out beforehand where these battues were going to take place, and if he ascertained that the beaters were on the side of the forest towards Viviers or Soucy, he and his wolves made an excursion to Boursonnes or Yvors; and if the Baron and his men were busy near Haramont or Longpré, the people of Corcy and Vertefeuille were made painfully aware of Thibault and his wolves.

===Chinese===
男爵效法那些胸怀壮志的罗马人——他们对那一次又一次卷土重来的迦太基敌人，穷尽了兵法上的一切手段——我再说一遍，男爵改变了战术，转而试试大规模围猎（battues）能办到些什么。他把庄户人家中凡用得上的男丁全召集了来，用这样一支声势浩大的人马把整片森林轰了个遍，凡他们经过之处附近，就连一只野兔也别想还在兔窝里趴着。

可蒂博偏偏要事事抢先打听清楚这些围猎要在哪儿进行；他若打听到击鼓哄围的人正在维维耶尔（Viviers）或苏西（Soucy）那一边的林子里，他和他的狼群便远奔布尔松（Boursonnes）或伊沃尔（Yvors）去作案；若男爵一伙正忙着在阿拉蒙（Haramont）或龙普雷（Longpré）附近搜捕，那么科尔西（Corcy）和韦尔特弗耶（Vertefeuille）的百姓便要痛切地领教一番蒂博和他狼群的厉害了。

===Original===
In vain the Lord of Vez drew his cordon at night round the suspected enclosures, so as to begin the attack with daylight; never once did his men succeed in starting a wolf, for not once did Thibault make a mistake in his calculations. If by chance he had not been well informed, and was uncertain in what direction the Baron and his men were going, he called all his wolves together, sending express couriers after them as the night set in; he then led them unobserved down the wooded lane leading to Lessart-l’Abbesse, which at that time ran between the forest of Compiègne and the forest of Villers-Cotterets, and so was able to pass from one to the other. This state of things went on for several months. Both the Baron and Thibault carried out the task each had set before himself, with equally passionate energy; the latter, like his adversary, seemed to have required some supernatural power, whereby he was able to resist fatigue and excitement; and this was the more remarkable seeing that during the short intervals of respite accorded by the Lord of Vez, the Wolf-leader was by no means at peace in himself.

===Chinese===
韦兹领主夜里在那些可疑的围场周围密密地布下人墙，好等天一亮便动手进攻——可全是白费功夫；他的人没有一次能放出一头狼来，因为蒂博的盘算没有一次失误。万一他凑巧消息不灵，拿不准男爵一伙要往哪个方向去，夜色一降临，他便把所有的狼都召拢来，差专使四处去追它们归队；然后他神不知鬼不觉地领它们顺着那条通往勒萨尔-拉贝斯（Lessart-l’Abbesse）的林间小道走——那条道当时正夹在贡比涅（Compiègne）森林和维莱-科特雷（Villers-Cotterets）森林之间，如此他便得以从一片林子穿到另一片林子去。这般局面一连持续了好几个月。男爵和蒂博都以同样炽烈的劲头，各自行着自己立下的那份心思；后者与他的对手一样，仿佛也得着了某种超自然之力，借此得以抵御疲倦与亢奋；这便愈发难得——须知在韦兹领主偶尔赐下的短短喘息之间里，这位狼之领主（the Wolf-leader）的内心，其实一刻也未曾真正安宁过。

===Original===
It was not that the terrible deeds in which he was an active agent, and at which he presided, filled him exactly with horror, for he thought them justifiable; he threw the responsibility of them, he said, on to those who had forced him to commit them; but there were moments of failing spirit, for which he could not account, when he went about in the midst of his ferocious companions, feeling gloomy, morose and heavy hearted.
