Again the image of Agnelette would rise before him, seeming to him like the personification of his own past life, honest and laborious, peaceful and innocent. And more than that, he felt he loved her more than he had ever thought it possible for him to love anybody. At times he would weep at the thought of all his lost happiness, at others he was seized with a wild fit of jealousy against the one to whom she now belonged—she, who at one time, might if he had liked, have been his.

===Chinese===
倒不是他亲身参与、亲自主掌的那些骇人之事，叫他心生恐怖——他自认为这都是理直气壮的；他说，干这些事的责任，该推到那些逼他走到这一步的人头上。可他总有那么些精神低落、自己也说不清道不明的时刻，他在那些凶残的伙伴中间走来走去，满心阴郁、乖戾、沉甸甸的。这时阿涅莱特（Agnelette）的影子又会浮上心头，在他看来，她就像是他自己往昔生活的化身——那生活老实、勤恳、安宁、纯洁。更甚于此的是，他发觉自己爱她，竟比他自以为可能爱上任何人的程度都要深。有时他一想到自己那尽数失去的幸福，便会落下泪来；有时他又妒火中烧，狂性大发，嫉妒起如今已占有她的那个人来——她，曾经，只要他愿意，原本是可以成为他的人的。

===Original===
One day, the Baron in order to prepare some fresh means of destruction, had been forced for the while to leave the wolves in peace. Thibault, who was in one of the moods we have just described, wandered forth from the den where he lived in company with the wolves. It was a splendid summer’s night, and he began to rove about the woodlands, where the moon was lighting up the trunks of the trees, dreaming of the time when he trod the mossy carpet underfoot free from trouble and anxiety, until at last the only happiness which was now left him, forgetfulness of the present, stole over his senses. Lost in this sweet dream of his earlier life, he was all of a sudden aroused by a cry of distress from somewhere near at hand. He was now so accustomed to such sounds, that, ordinarily, he would have paid no attention to it, but his heart was for the moment softened by the recollection of Agnelette, and he felt more disposed than usual to pity; as it happened also he was near the place where he had first seen the gentle child, and this helped to awaken his kinder nature.

===Chinese===
一日，男爵为了筹划几样新的剿杀家伙什，一时之间只好暂时由着狼群安生几日。蒂博正陷在我们方才所说的那种心境里，便从他与狼群同住的那处兽穴里溜达出来。那是个极美的夏夜，他开始在林间四处游荡起来；月光把一株株树干照得通亮，他一面走一面梦着旧日光景——那时他踏着脚下那层苔藓织成的绒毯，无忧无虑；渐渐地，如今唯一还剩下的那点乐事——忘掉眼下的处境——悄悄地袭上了他的感官。他正沉浸在这往昔生活的美梦之中，忽然被近处传来的一声呼救惊醒。他平日里早已听惯了这类声音，本不会去理会，可这会儿他的心正因忆起阿涅莱特而软了下来，比平常多了几分怜悯之情；又凑巧，他正走在当初第一次见到那个温柔孩子的地方附近，这一来也助长了他天性里那点善根。

===Original===
He ran to the spot whence the cry had come, and as he leaped from the underwood into the deep forest-lane near Ham, he saw a woman struggling with an immense wolf which had thrown her on the ground. Thibault could not have said why he was so agitated at this sight, nor why his heart beat more violently than usual; he rushed forward and seizing the animal by the throat hurled it away from its victim, and then lifting the woman in his arms, he carried her to the side of the lane and laid her on the slope. Here a ray of moonlight, breaking through the clouds, fell on the face of the woman he had saved, and Thibault saw that it was Agnelette. Near to the spot was the spring in which Thibault had once gazed at himself, and had seen the first red hair; he ran to it, took up water in his hands, and threw it into the woman’s face. Agnelette opened her eyes, gave a cry of terror, and tried to rise and flee.

“What!” cried the Wolf-leader, as if he were still Thibault the shoemaker, “you do not know me again, Agnelette?”

“Ah! yes indeed, I know you, Thibault; and it is because I know who you are,” cried the young woman, “that I am afraid!”

===Chinese===
他朝那呼救声传来的方向奔去，从矮树丛里一跃而出，跳进哈姆（Ham）附近那条幽深的林间小道——只见一个女人正和一头硕大无朋的狼搏斗，那狼已把她扑倒在地。蒂博说不上来为什么一看见这情形便如此激动，也说不上来为什么心跳得比平日更猛；他一个箭步冲上前去，一把掐住那畜生的喉咙，将它从受害者身上甩了出去，然后双臂将那女子抱起，抱到路边，把她安放在斜坡上。这时一缕月光破云而出，正落在他所救的那张脸上——蒂博一眼看出，这正是阿涅莱特。近处有一眼泉水，蒂博当初正是在这泉里照过自己，也正是在这里瞧见了头上第一根红发（red hair）；他奔过去，双手捧起水，泼到那女子脸上。阿涅莱特睁开眼睛，发出一声惊恐的喊叫，挣扎着想爬起来逃走。

“怎么！”狼之领主叫道，那口吻仿佛他还是当年那个鞋匠蒂博似的，“你认不出我了，阿涅莱特？”

“啊！认得，认得，我认得你，蒂博；可正因为我认得你是谁，”少妇叫道，“我才害怕呀！”

===Original===
Then throwing herself on her knees, and clasping her hands: “Oh do not kill me, Thibault!” she cried, “do not kill me! it would be such dreadful trouble for the poor old grandmother! Thibault, do not kill me!”

The Wolf-leader stood overcome with consternation; up to this hour he had not fully realised the hideous renown which he had gained; but the terror which the sight of him inspired in the woman who had loved him and whom he still loved, filled him with a horror of himself.

“I, kill you, Agnelette!” he said, “just when I have snatched you from death! Oh! how you must hate and despise me for such a thought to enter your head.”

“I do not hate you, Thibault,” said the young woman, “but I hear such things about you, that I feel afraid of you.”

“And do they say nothing of the infidelity which has led Thibault to commit such crimes?”

“I do not understand you,” said Agnelette looking at Thibault with her large eyes, blue as the heavens.

“What!” exclaimed Thibault, “you do not understand that I loved you—that I adored you Agnelette, and that the loss of you sent me out of my mind?”

“If you loved me, if you adored me, Thibault, what prevented you from marrying me?”

“The spirit of evil,” muttered Thibault.

“I too loved you,” continued the young woman, “and I suffered cruelly waiting for you.”

===Chinese===
接着她双膝跪下，十指紧攥合掌哀求道：“噢，别杀我，蒂博！”她哭喊道，“别杀我！那样可苦了可怜的老祖母啦！蒂博，千万别杀我！”

狼之领主站在那里，惊愕得说不出话来；直到此时此刻，他才彻底明白自己挣下了怎样一桩骇人的恶名；可是他曾经爱过、如今也还爱着的那女子，一见他便这般骇得魂飞魄散——这事实叫他对自己生出一股毛骨悚然的厌恶。

“我，杀你，阿涅莱特！”他说，“偏偏是我刚从死神手里把你抢回来的时候！噢！你心里居然冒出这种念头来，那你该有多么恨我、多么瞧不起我啊。”

“我并不恨你，蒂博，”少妇说，“可我听人讲了那么多关于你的事，我不由得不怕你。”

“那他们就没有说起过，是怎样的负心才叫蒂博犯下这许多罪孽的吗？”

“我不懂你的话，”阿涅莱特说，用她那双蓝得像晴空一样的大眼睛望着蒂博。

“怎么！”蒂博失声叫道，“你竟然不懂——我爱过你——我曾经把你当作心头肉一样疼爱，阿涅莱特，而失去你，把我逼得发了疯？”

“你要是爱过我，你要是把我当心头肉一样疼爱，蒂博，那又是什么拦着你不娶我？”

“是那恶灵（the spirit of evil）。”蒂博喃喃地说。

“我也爱过你呀，”少妇接着说，“我等你等得好苦，受尽了煎熬。”

===Original===
Thibault heaved a sigh.

“You loved me, Agnelette?” he asked.

“Yes,” replied the young woman with her soft voice and gentle eyes.

“But now, all is over,” said Thibault, “and you love me no more.”

“Thibault,” answered Agnelette, “I no longer love you, because it is no longer right to love you; but one cannot always forget one’s first love as one would wish.”

“Agnelette!” cried Thibault, trembling all over, “be careful what you say!”

“Why should I be careful what I say, since it is the truth,” said the girl with an innocent shake of the head. “The day you told me that you wished to make me your wife, I believed you, Thibault; for why should I think that you would lie to me when I had just done you a service? Then, later I met you, but I did not go in search of you; you came to me, you spoke words of love to me, you were the first to refer to the promise that you had made me. And it was not my fault either, Thibault, that I was afraid of that ring which you wore, which was large enough for you and yet, oh, it was horrible! not big enough for one of my fingers.”

===Chinese===
蒂博长叹了一声。

“你爱过我，阿涅莱特？”他问。

“是的。”少妇回答，嗓音柔柔的，目光也柔柔的。

“可如今，一切都完了，”蒂博说，“你再也不爱我了。”

“蒂博，”阿涅莱特答道，“我不再爱你，是因为如今再爱你已不合规矩了；可是人对自己的初恋，并不总能像自己所愿的那样把它忘个干净。”

“阿涅莱特！”蒂博浑身发着抖叫道，“你说话可要小心！”

“既然这是实话，我说话为什么要小心呢。”姑娘天真地摇了摇头说道。“那一天你对我说想娶我做妻子，我是信了你的，蒂博；因为我才刚帮过你的忙，怎么会去想你竟然会骗我呢？后来呢，我又见到了你，可不是我去找的你；是你来找的我，是你对我诉说着情话，是你头一个又提起了你对我许下的那个诺言。再说，那也不是我的错呀，蒂博——你手上戴的那枚戒指把我吓坏了，那戒指戴在你手上倒还嫌大，可天哪，真叫人毛骨悚然！它竟连我一根手指都套不进去。”

===Original===
“Would you like me not to wear this ring any more?” said Thibault. “Would you like me to throw it away?” And he began trying to pull it off his finger, but as it had been too small to go on Agnelette’s finger, so now it was too small to be taken off Thibault’s. In vain he struggled with it, and tried to move it with his teeth; the ring seemed rivetted to his finger for all eternity.

Thibault saw that it was no use trying to get rid of it; it was a token of compact between himself and the black wolf, and with a sigh he let his arms fall hopelessly to his sides.

“That day,” went on Agnelette, “I ran away; I know that I was wrong to do so, but I was no longer mistress of myself after seeing that ring and more still.…” She lifted her eyes as she spoke, looking timidly up at Thibault’s hair. Thibault was bareheaded, and, by the light of the moon Agnelette could see that it was no longer a single hair that shone red as the flames of hell, but that half the hair on Thibault’s head was now of this devil’s colour.

===Chinese===
“你要不要我以后不再戴这戒指？”蒂博说，“你要不要我把它扔了？”他便动手想把戒指从手指上褪下来，可正像它当初小得套不上阿涅莱特的手指那样，如今它也小得从蒂博手指上褪不下来了。他白费了半天劲，又用牙去咬着想把它挪动，那戒指却仿佛已被铆在他指上，直至永永远远。

蒂博明白，想把它弄掉是徒劳的；那是他和黑狼之间所立盟约的信物，他叹了口气，无可奈何地垂下了双臂。

“那一天，”阿涅莱特接着说，“我逃走了；我知道我那样做是不对的，可一见了那戒指——还有更叫人害怕的呢——之后，我便再也管不住自己了……”她说话时抬眼怯怯地望向蒂博的头发。蒂博没有戴帽子，月光底下，阿涅莱特看得清清楚楚：如今蒂博头上发着红光的，已不再是一根如地狱之火一般猩红的头发了——而是他整整半个脑袋的头发，都已变成了这魔鬼的颜色。

===Original===
“Oh!” she exclaimed, drawing back, “Thibault! Thibault! What has happened to you since I last saw you?”

“Agnelette!” cried Thibault throwing himself down with his face to the ground, and holding his head between his hands, “I could not tell any human creature, not even a priest what has happened to me since then; but to Agnelette, all I can say is: Agnelette! Agnelette! have pity on me, for I have been most unhappy!”

Agnelette went up to him and took his hands in hers.

“You did love me then? You did love me?” he cried.

“What can I do, Thibault!” said the girl with the same sweetness and innocence as before. “I took you at your word, and every time I heard someone knocking at our hut door, I thought it was you come to say to the old grandmother, ‘Mother, I love Agnelette, Agnelette loves me; will you give her to me for my wife?’ Then when I went and opened the door, and found that it was not you, I used to go into a corner and cry.”

“And now, Agnelette, now?”

“Now,” she answered. “Now, Thibault, it may seem strange, but in spite of all the terrible tales that are told about you, I have not been really frightened; I was sure that you could not wish any harm to me, and I was walking boldly through the forest, when that dreadful beast from which you saved me, suddenly sprang upon me.”

===Chinese===
“噢！”她惊叫一声，倒退几步，“蒂博！蒂博！自打我上次见你之后，你到底出了什么事？”

“阿涅莱特！”蒂博脸朝地扑倒下去，双手抱住头叫道，“从那以后我遭遇的事，我无法告诉任何人，连神父也不能；可对阿涅莱特，我只能说一句：阿涅莱特！阿涅莱特！可怜可怜我吧，因为我是最最不幸的人！”

阿涅莱特走上前来，伸出双手握住了他的手。

“这么说，你那时是爱过我的？你爱过我？”他叫道。

“我能怎么样呢，蒂博！”姑娘说道，语调和神气仍和先前一样甜美、一样天真。“我信了你的话，每回听见有人敲我们小屋的门，我就以为是你来了，来对老祖母说：‘大妈，我爱阿涅莱特，阿涅莱特也爱我；您把她许给我做媳妇吧？’可等我跑去开了门，发现敲门的不是你，我便躲到一个角落里偷偷地哭。”

“那么现在呢，阿涅莱特，现在呢？”

“现在嘛，”她答道，“现在，蒂博，说起来也许奇怪，可尽管外面关于你传了那么多吓人的话，我其实并没真害怕过；我笃定你不会对我起坏心，所以我才一个人大胆地穿过森林——直到你救我脱险的那头可怖畜生，猛地朝我扑了过来。”

===Original===
“But how is it that you are near your old home? do you not live with your husband?”

“We lived together for a while at Vez, but there was no room there for the grandmother; and so I said to my husband, ‘the grandmother must be thought of first; I must go back to her; when you wish to see me you will come.’”

“And he consented to that arrangement?”

“Not at first, but I pointed out to him that the grandmother is seventy years of age; that if she were only to live another two or three years, God grant it may be more! it would only be two or three years of some extra trouble for us, whereas, in all probability we had long years of life before us. Then he understood that it was right to give to those that had least.”

But all the while that Agnelette was giving this explanation, Thibault could think of nothing but that the love she once had for him was not yet dead.

“So,” said Thibault, “you loved me? and so, Agnelette you could love me again…?”

“That is impossible now because I belong to another.”

“Agnelette, Agnelette! only say that you love me!”

“No, Thibault, if I loved you, I should do everything in the world to hide it from you.”

===Chinese===
“可你怎么会在老屋附近？你不跟你的丈夫一起过吗？”

“我们俩在韦兹（Vez）同住过一阵子，可那里没有祖母住的份儿；于是我对丈夫说：‘祖母得先顾着；我得回到她那儿去；你想见我的时候，就来吧。’”

“他答应了这样的安排？”

“起初不肯，可我对他点明：祖母已经七十岁了；就算她只能再活两三年——上帝保佑但愿还能活更长！——那也不过是叫我们多操两三年心罢了，而我们多半还有长长的日子在后头呢。他这才明白：该把关爱先给那最缺少依靠的人。”

可是阿涅莱特这一番陈说的当儿，蒂博心里只想着一件事——她从前对他的那份情，至今未死。

“这么说，”蒂博道，“你爱过我？那么，阿涅莱特，你还能再爱我一次……？”

“这如今是不可能的了，因为我已属于另一个人。”

“阿涅莱特，阿涅莱特！只要你一句——说你爱我！”

“不，蒂博，万一我真爱你，我便会千方百计地瞒着你。”

===Original===
“And why?” cried Thibault. “Why? you do not know my power. I know that I have only a wish or two left, but with your help, by combining these wishes together, I could make you as rich as a queen.… We could leave the country, leave France, Europe; there are large countries, of which you do not even know the names, Agnelette, called America and India. They are paradises, with blue skies, tall trees and birds of every kind. Agnelette, say that you will come with me; nobody will know that we have gone off together, nobody will know where we are, nobody will know that we love one another, nobody will know even that we are alive.”

“Fly with you, Thibault!” said Agnelette, looking at the wolf-leader as if she had but half understood what he said, “do you forget that I no longer belong to myself? do you not know that I am married?”

“What does that matter,” said Thibault, “if it is I whom you love, and if we can live happily together!”

“Oh! Thibault! Thibault! what are you saying!”

===Chinese===
“为什么？”蒂博叫道，“为什么？你可不知道我的能耐。我知道我剩下的愿望（wish）已不过一两个而已，但有你在，把这些愿合在一处使出去，我就能叫你富比王后……我们可以远走他乡，离开法国，离开欧洲；世上还有好些大国，阿涅莱特，你连名字都没听说过——什么美洲（America）啊、印度（India）啊。那些都是人间天堂，有蓝蓝的天，高高的树，各种各样的鸟儿。阿涅莱特，说你愿意跟我走；谁也不会知道咱们私奔了，谁也不会知道咱们在哪里，谁也不会知道咱们彼此相爱，谁也不会知道咱们还活在人世。”

“跟你私奔，蒂博！”阿涅莱特说道，望着这位狼之领主，仿佛只听懂了他一半的话，“你忘了，我已经不再属于我自己了？你不知道我已经嫁了人吗？”

“那又有什么打紧，”蒂博说，“只要你爱的是我，只要咱们能在一起快快活活地过日子！”

“噢！蒂博！蒂博！你说的是什么呀！”

===Original===
“Listen,” went on Thibault, “I am going to speak to you in the name of this world and the next. Do you wish to save me, Agnelette, body and soul? If so, do not resist my pleading, have pity on me, come with me; let us go somewhere together, where we shall no longer hear these howlings, or breathe this atmosphere of reeking flesh; and, if it scares you to think of being a rich, grand lady, somewhere then where I can again be Thibault the workman, Thibault, poor but beloved, and, therefore, Thibault happy in his hard work, some place where Agnelette will have no other husband but me.”

“Ah! Thibault! I was ready to become your wife, and you scorned me!”

“Do not remember my sins, Agnelette, which have been so cruelly punished.”

“Thibault, another has done what you were not willing to do. He took the poor young girl; he burdened himself with the poor old blind woman; he gave a name to the one and bread to the other; he had no ambition beyond that of gaining my love; he desired no dowry beyond my marriage vow; can you think of asking me to return evil for good? Do you dare to suggest that I should leave the one who has given me such proof of his love for the one who has given me proof only of his indifference?”

===Chinese===
“你听我说，”蒂博接着道，“我要以这一世和下一世的名义对你说话。你愿不愿意救我，阿涅莱特，连同肉身带灵魂一起救？若是愿意，就别再驳回我的恳求，可怜可怜我，跟我走吧；让咱们一道去个地方——在那里咱们再也听不见这些嚎叫，再也吸不着这股血腥恶臭的气息；倘若你一想做大富大贵的阔太太就害怕，那咱们就去个我能重新做起匠人蒂博的地方——一个贫苦却被人爱着、因而虽苦也乐、埋头干活的蒂博所在的地方；一个阿涅莱特除我之外再没别的丈夫的地方。”

“啊！蒂博！我本是愿意做你妻子的，是你嫌弃了我！”

“阿涅莱特，别记着我那些罪过吧——它们已经被狠狠地惩罚过了。”

“蒂博，另一个人做了你不肯做的事。他收留了这可怜的少女；他甘愿背上这又穷又瞎的老太婆这个包袱；他给了那少女一个名分，又给了那老太婆一口饭吃；他除了想赢得我的爱之外再无别的奢望；除了我的婚誓之外，他不图我半点嫁妆；如今你竟想叫我对恩人恩将仇报吗？你竟敢撺掇我，叫我把那位以如此行动证明他爱我的丈夫甩了，去跟那个只拿冷淡来证明他薄情的人？”

===Original===
“But what matter still, Agnelette, since you do not love him and since you do love me?”

“Thibault, do not turn and twist my words to make them appear to say what they do not. I said that I still preserved my friendship for you, I never said that I did not love my husband. I should like to see you happy, my friend; above all I should like to see you abjure your evil ways and repent of your sins; and last of all, I wish that God may have mercy upon you, and that you may be delivered from that spirit of evil, of which you spoke just now. For this I pray night and morning on my knees; but even that I may be able to pray for you, I must keep myself pure; if the voice that supplicates for mercy is to rise to God’s throne, it must be an innocent one; above all, I must scrupulously keep the oath which I swore at His altar.”

===Chinese===
“可这又有什么打紧呢，阿涅莱特，既然你不爱他，既然你爱的是我？”

“蒂博，别曲解我的话，把它们绕成它们本没有的意思。我说的是，我对你仍存着一份友情，我从未说过我不爱我的丈夫。我愿你幸福，我的朋友；我尤其愿你摒弃恶道、痛悔前愆；最后，我祈求上帝怜悯你，愿你从你方才所说的那恶灵之手得解救。为这事，我早晚跪着求告；可要让我还能为你祷告，我自己便必须保持身心洁净；那求怜悯的声音若要上达上帝的宝座，那必得是一颗无辜之心所发；首要的是，我必须一丝不苟地守着我在他祭坛前所立下的誓言。”

===Original===
On hearing these decisive words from Agnelette, Thibault again became fierce and morose.

“Do you not know, Agnelette, that it is very imprudent of you to speak to me here like that?”

“And why, Thibault?” asked the young woman.

“We are alone here together; it is dark, and not a man of the open would dare to come into the forest at this hour; and know, the King is not more master in his kingdom than I am here?”

“I do not understand you, Thibault?”

“I mean that having prayed, implored, and conjured, I can now threaten.”

“You, threaten?”

“What I mean is,” continued Thibault, paying no heed to Agnelette’s words, “that every word you speak does not excite my love for you more than it rouses my hatred towards him; in short, I mean that it is imprudent of the lamb to irritate the wolf when the lamb is in the power of the wolf.”

===Chinese===
一听阿涅莱特这些决绝的话，蒂博又变得凶狠乖戾起来。

“阿涅莱特，你不知道，你在这儿这样跟我说话，是极不明智的吗？”

“为什么呢，蒂博？”少妇问。

“此刻这儿只有你我两个；天又这么黑，没有一个开化的汉子敢在这种时辰进森林里来；你要知道，国王在他自己的国土上，也不见得比我在这里更是主子。”

“我不懂你的话，蒂博？”

“我的意思是——既已求过、恳过、哀告过了，如今我便可以威吓了。”

“你，威吓？”

“我的意思是，”蒂博接着说，根本不接阿涅莱特的话茬，“你每吐一个字，与其说是在撩拨我对你的爱，不如说更在激起我对他那股恨；总之，我的意思是——羊羔落入狼爪之下时，还去撩拨那狼，便是大大的不智。”

===Original===
“I told you, Thibault, before, that I started to walk through the forest without any feeling of fear at meeting you. As I was coming to, I felt a momentary terror, remembering involuntarily what I had heard said about you; but at this moment, Thibault, you will try in vain to make me turn pale.”

Thibault flung both hands up to his head.

“Do not talk like that,” he said, “you cannot think what the devil is whispering to me, and what an effort I have to make to resist his voice.”

“You may kill me if you like,” replied Agnelette, “but I will not be guilty of the cowardice which you ask of me; you may kill me, but I shall remain faithful to my husband; you may kill me, but I shall pray to God to help him as I die.”

“Do not speak his name, Agnelette; do not make me think about that man.”

“You can threaten me as much as you like, Thibault, for I am in your hands; but, happily, he is far from you, and you have no power over him.”

===Chinese===
“蒂博，我早告诉过你，我起初穿过这片森林时，对会撞见你一点儿都不害怕。我苏醒过来那一瞬，有过一阵的惶恐，是下意识地记起了人家说你的那些话；可此一刻，蒂博，你想把我吓得脸色发白，是白费心机。”

蒂博双手猛地抱住头。

“你别这么说，”他道，“你想象不到魔鬼这会儿在我耳边嘀咕些什么，我要费多大的力气才能顶住他的话。”

“你愿意的话，可以杀了我，”阿涅莱特答道，“可你叫我做的那种卑怯事，我断断不做；你可以杀我，但我对我的丈夫绝不变心；你可以杀我，但我断气之际仍要祷告上帝，求他护佑我的丈夫。”

“别提他的名字，阿涅莱特；别叫我去想那个人。”

“你要怎么威吓我都可以，蒂博，反正我在你手里；可幸而他离你远远的，你奈何他不得。”

===Original===
“And who told you that, Agnelette? do you not know that, thanks to the diabolical power I possess and which I can hardly fight against, I am able to strike as well far as near?”

“And if I should become a widow, Thibault, do you imagine that I should be vile enough to accept your hand when it was stained with the blood of the one whose name I bear?”

“Agnelette,” said Thibault falling on his knees, “Agnelette, save me from committing a further crime.”

“It is you, not I, who will be responsible for the crime. I can give you my life, Thibault, but not my honour.”

===Chinese===
“这是谁告诉你的，阿涅莱特？你不知道，多亏了我身上这股我几乎抵挡不住的邪魔之力，我能远隔千里取人性命，与近在咫尺一样地准？”

“倘若我真成了寡妇，蒂博，你以为我会下贱到去接那只沾满了我丈夫——我冠着他的姓——鲜血的手吗？”

“阿涅莱特，”蒂博双膝跪地说道，“阿涅莱特，救救我，别叫我再犯一桩罪。”

“那罪是你犯的，不是我犯的。我可以把命给你，蒂博，但不能把名节给你。”

===Original===
“Oh,” roared Thibault, “love flies from the heart when hatred enters; take care, Agnelette! take heed to your husband! The devil is in me, and he will soon speak through my mouth. Instead of the consolation which I had hoped from your love, and which your love refuses, I will have vengeance. Stay my hand, Agnelette, there is yet time, stay it from cursing, from destroying; if not, understand that it is not I, but you, who strike him dead! Agnelette, you know now.… Agnelette, you do not stop me from speaking? Let it be so then, and let the curse fall on all three of us, you and him and me! Agnelette, I wish your husband to die, and he will die!”

Agnelette uttered a terrible cry; then, as if her reason reasserted itself, protesting against this murder at a distance which seemed impossible to her, she exclaimed:

“No, no; you only say that to terrify me, but my prayers will prevail against your maledictions.”

“Go then, and learn how heaven answers your prayers. Only, if you wish to see your husband again alive, Agnelette, you had better make haste, or you will but stumble against his dead body.”

===Chinese===
“噢，”蒂博怒吼道，“仇恨一进心，爱情便要飞走；当心，阿涅莱特！留神你的丈夫！魔鬼就在我身上，他马上就要借我的口说话了。你的爱不肯给我所盼望的那份慰藉，那我便要复仇。拦住我的手吧，阿涅莱特，还来得及，拦住它别叫它去诅咒、去毁灭；不然，你就明白——杀他的不是我，而是你！阿涅莱特，你如今知道了……阿涅莱特，你不拦着我往下说吗？那就这样吧，让这诅咒落在我们三个头上——你、他、我！阿涅莱特，我愿你的丈夫死，他便会死！”

阿涅莱特发出一声凄厉的尖叫；接着，仿佛理智重又占了上风，抗拒着这桩在她看来绝无可能的隔空杀人，她喊道：

“不，不；你这么说不过是想吓唬我罢了，可我的祷告定能压过你的诅咒。”

“那你就去吧，去看看上天怎样应答你的祷告。只是，阿涅莱特，你若还想再见你丈夫一面、活的——那你最好赶紧些，不然你只会被他的尸首绊一跤。”

===Original===
Overcome by the tone of conviction with which these last words were pronounced, and yielding to an irresistible feeling of terror, Agnelette, without responding to Thibault, who stood on the further side of the lane with his hand held out and pointing towards Préciamont, set off running in the direction which it seemed to indicate, and soon disappeared into the night as she turned out of sight at the corner of the road. As she passed from his view, Thibault uttered a howl, which might have been taken for the howling of a whole pack of wolves, and plunging into the thicket, “Ah! now,” he cried aloud to himself, “I am indeed a lost and accursed soul!”

===Chinese===
这些最后的话说出口时那斩钉截铁的语气，叫阿涅莱特彻底垮了下来；她在一种不可抵御的恐惧驱使下，也不理会蒂博——他正站在小路的另一边，伸手指着普雷西亚蒙（Préciamont）的方向——便顺着那所指的方向撒腿飞奔，转眼在道路转角处一拐，便消失在了夜色里，再看不见了。她从他的视线里消失的那一刻，蒂博发出一声嚎叫——那声音简直可以被当作整整一群狼的齐嚎；他一头钻进了树丛，高声自语道：“啊！这一下，我可真成了一个万劫不复、永受天谴的孤魂了！”

## I The Grand Master of His Highness’ Wolf Hounds / I 殿下狼犬总管

===Original===
The Seigneur Jean, Baron of Vez, was a hardy and indefatigable sportsman.

If you follow the beautiful valley which runs between Berval and Longpré, you will see, on your left hand, an old tower, which by reason of its isolated position will appear doubly high and formidable to you.

At the present moment it belongs to an old friend of the writer of this tale, and everyone is now so accustomed to its forbidding aspect, that the peasant passing that way in summer has no more fear of seeking shelter from the heat beneath its walls than the martins with their long black wings and shrill cries, and the swallows with their soft chirrupings, have of building their nests under its eaves.

===Chinese===
让领主（the Seigneur Jean），韦兹（Vez）的男爵，是个彪悍刚健、不知疲倦的猎人。

你若沿着贝尔瓦尔（Berval）与龙普雷（Longpré）之间那条秀美的山谷走去，便会在左手边望见一座古塔；只因它孤零零地立在那儿，便显得格外高耸、格外森然可怖。

如今这座塔已归本书作者的一位老友所有；人们也早已看惯了它那拒人千里的模样，以至于夏日里路过的庄稼汉，全不怕在塔墙的阴影下躲避暑热——正如那些拖着长长黑翼、叫声尖锐的岩燕，和那些啁啾呢喃的家燕，全不怕在它的檐下衔泥筑巢一般。

===Original===
But at the time we are now speaking of, somewhere about 1780, this lordly dwelling of Vez was looked upon with different eyes, and, it must be confessed, it did not then offer so safe a place of retreat. It was a building of the twelfth or thirteenth century, rugged and gloomy, its terrifying exterior having assumed no kindlier aspect as the years rolled by. True, the sentinel with his measured tread and flashing steel-cap no longer paced its ramparts, the archer with his shrill-sounding horn no longer kept watch and ward on the battlements; true the postern was no longer guarded by true men at arms, ready at the least signal of danger to lower the portcullis and draw up the bridge; but the solitude alone which surrounded this grim giant of granite was sufficient to inspire the feeling of awe-inspiring majesty awakened by all mute and motionless things.

===Chinese===
然而在我们所讲的那个年月，大约 1780 年光景，人们看这座韦兹的领主宅邸，眼光却大不相同；也不得不承认，那时它绝非一个安稳可靠的避难之所。那是十二、十三世纪遗下来的建筑，粗粝阴沉，岁月流转，它那骇人的外貌不曾变得和善半分。诚然，那踏着匀步、钢盔闪亮的哨兵已不再在城头巡行；那吹着尖厉号角的弓弩手也不再在雉堞间守望；诚然，那角门已不再由忠诚的兵士把守，一有警报便降下铁闸、拉起吊桥；然而单是环绕着这位花岗岩巨人的那一片荒寂，便足以唤起一切沉默不动之物所唤起的那种令人敬畏的庄严之感。

===Original===
The lord of this old fortress, however, was by no means so much to be dreaded; those who were more intimately acquainted with him than were the peasants, and could do him more justice, asserted that his bark was worse than his bite, and that he caused more fear than harm—that is, among his fellow Christians. With the animals of the forest it was different, for he was avowedly their mortal and implacable enemy.

He was chief wolf-hunter to his Royal Highness Louis Philippe of Orleans, the fourth of that name—a post which allowed him to gratify the inordinate passion he had for the chase. Although it was not easy, it was yet possible to bring the Baron to listen to reason in other matters; but as regards the chase, if once he had got a fixed idea in his head, nothing would satisfy him until he had carried it out and had achieved his purpose.

===Chinese===
然而这古堡的主人，倒也并不怎么叫人害怕；那些比乡下庄稼汉更与他相熟、也更肯公道待他的人都说，他是雷声大雨点小，叫人怕得厉害，害人却有限——至少对他的基督徒同类是如此。至于林中的走兽，情形便不同了，因为他摆明就是它们不共戴天、死不休解的仇敌。

他是奥尔良公爵路易·菲利普（Louis Philippe of Orleans）殿下——同名的第四位——的首席猎狼官；这个职位正好遂了他那股子难以遏制的狩猎狂热。在别的事情上，虽说不易，到底还能叫这位男爵（the Baron）听进几句道理；唯独论到狩猎，他脑子里一旦钻进一个主意，便非得照办、非得遂愿不可，不然什么事都堵不住他的心。

===Original===
His wife, according to report, was the natural daughter of the Prince, which, in conjunction with his title of chief wolf-hunter, gave him almost absolute power throughout the domains of his illustrious father-in-law, a power which no one dared to contest with him, especially after the remarriage of his Royal Highness with Madame de Montesson. This had taken place in 1773, since which date he had almost abandoned his castle at Villers-Cotterets for his delightful residence at Bagnolet, where he entertained all the first wits of the day and amused himself with playacting.

And so, whether the sun was shining to rejoice the earth, or the rain was saddening it, whether the winter fields lay hidden beneath a shroud of snow, or the spring had spread her fresh green carpet over the meadows, it was rare, on any day of the year, not to see the great gates of the Castle thrown wide open between eight and nine o’clock in the morning, and first the Baron come forth, and immediately after him his chief pricker, Marcotte, followed by the other prickers. Then appeared the dogs, coupled and held in leash by the keepers of the hounds, under the superintendence of Engoulevent, who aspired to become a pricker. Even as the German executioner walks alone, behind the nobles and in front of the citizens, to show that he is the least of the former and the first of the latter, so he walked immediately after the prickers and ahead of the keepers of the hounds, as being the chief of the whippers-in and least of the prickers.

===Chinese===
据说，他的妻子是亲王的私生女；这重身份，再加上他那首席猎狼官的头衔，便使他在那位显赫岳丈的领地之内，几乎握有绝对的权柄——这权柄无人敢与他相争，尤其是在殿下与蒙特森夫人（Madame de Montesson）再婚之后。这桩婚事成于 1773 年；自那以后，他几乎把维莱-科特雷（Villers-Cotterets）的城堡弃置一旁，搬进了巴尼奥莱（Bagnolet）那座宜人的别业，在那里招待当时第一等的才子名士，又以登台串戏自娱。

因此，无论日光欢腾着照临大地，还是雨水使它黯然神伤，无论冬日的田野在白雪的尸衣下隐没，还是春神在草甸上铺开她那嫩绿的新毯，一年之中鲜有哪一天，你不见城堡的大门在八点到九点之间豁然大开——先是男爵本人骑马而出，紧跟在他身后的，是他的首席猎手马尔科特（Marcotte），其余猎手再鱼贯跟上。随后出现的是猎犬，由犬奴们两两拴着、牵住皮带，归昂古勒旺（Engoulevent）统领——此人正巴望着有朝一日也能当上一名猎手。正如日耳曼的刽子手独自走在贵族之后、百姓之前，以表明他既是前者的末流、又是后者的首位；他也这般走在猎手们之后、犬奴们之前，因为他是犬奴的头儿，又是猎手里的末流。

===Original===
The whole procession filed out of the castle court in full hunting array, with the English horses and the French hounds; twelve horses, and forty dogs.

Before we go any farther, let me say that with these twelve horses and forty dogs the Baron hunted every sort of quarry, but more especially the wolf, in order no doubt to do honour to his title.

===Chinese===
整队人马这般全副狩猎行头，从城堡的庭院里鱼贯而出——英国的马，法国的犬；十二匹马，四十条狗。

在往下细讲之前，且容我说一句：男爵就凭着这十二匹马、四十条狗，猎取各种各样的猎物，而尤以猎狼为甚——这无疑是为了不辱他那猎狼官的头衔。

===Original===
No further proof will be needed by the genuine sportsman of the fine faith he had in the general quality of his hounds, and in their keenness of scent, than the fact that next to the wolf he gave preference to the boar, then to the red deer, then to the fallow-deer, and lastly to the roebuck; finally, if the keepers of the pack failed to sight the animal they had tracked, he uncoupled at random, and went after the first hare that crossed his path. For, as we have already stated, the worthy Baron went out hunting every day, and he would sooner have gone for four-and-twenty hours without food or drink, although he was often thirsty, than have spent that time without seeing his hounds run.

===Chinese===
真正的猎人，只消知道下面这一层，便不必再要什么旁的证据，来印证这位男爵对自己犬群的整体品性、对它们嗅觉之敏锐怀着怎样坚定的信心：那就是——除了狼之外，他猎物的偏好次序，先是野猪，再是赤鹿，其次是扁角鹿，最后才是狍子；到最后，倘若管犬的人没能在追踪中瞧见那畜生，他便随手放开几条犬，去追第一只撞进他路上的野兔。因为，如前所述，这位好男爵天天都要出猎，他宁可整整二十四小时不吃不喝——他本是常常口渴的——也不愿这么长一段时光里，看不见他那群猎犬奔跑。

===Original===
But, as everybody knows, however swift the horses, and however keen the dogs, hunting has its bad times as well as its good.

One day, Marcotte came up to where the Baron was awaiting him, with a crestfallen expression of countenance.

“How now, Marcotte,” asked the Baron frowning, “what is the matter this time? I see by your face we are to expect bad sport today.”

Marcotte shook his head.

“Speak up, man,” continued the Baron with a gesture of impatience.

===Chinese===
然而众所周知，无论马跑得多快，无论犬嗅觉多灵，打猎这桩事，也总有走运和不走运的日子。

有一天，马尔科特走到男爵候着他的地方，一脸垂头丧气。

“怎么啦，马尔科特，”男爵皱着眉头问，“这回又出了什么岔子？我看你这副脸色，今天怕是要扫兴了。”

马尔科特摇了摇头。

“你倒是说话呀，伙计，”男爵带着不耐烦的手势，接着道。

===Original===
“The matter is, my Lord, that the black wolf is about.”

“Ah! ah!” exclaimed the Baron, his eyes sparkling; for you must know that this made the fifth or sixth time that the worthy Baron had started the animal in question, but never once had he been able to get within gunshot of him or to run him down.

“Yes,” Marcotte went on, “but the damned beast has employed himself so well all night crossing his track and doubling, that after having traced him over half the forest, I found myself at the place from which I started.”

“You think then, Marcotte, that there is no chance of getting near him.”

“I am afraid not.”

===Chinese===
“回大人，是这么回事：那头黑狼出动了。”

“啊！啊！”男爵叫出声来，两眼直放光；要知道，这已是这位好男爵第五、第六回惊起这头野兽了，可一次也不曾能够逼近到枪程之内，更不曾把它追得力竭就擒。

“是，”马尔科特接着说，“可这该死的畜生，一整夜把心思都用在了来回穿插、兜圈子折返上头，结果我循着它的踪迹追了大半个林子，到头来又回到了动身的地方。”

“那么照你想，马尔科特，这一回是没指望挨近它了。”

“怕是没指望了。”

===Original===
“By all the devils in hell!” exclaimed the Lord of Vez, who had not had his equal in swearing since the mighty Nimrod, “however, I am not feeling well today, and I must have a burst of some kind, to get rid of these bad humours. What do you think we can hunt, Marcotte, in place of this damned black wolf?”

“Well, having been so taken up with the wolf,” answered Marcotte, “I have not traced any other animal. Will my Lord uncouple at random and hunt the first animal that we come across?”

===Chinese===
“凭地狱里所有的魔鬼发誓！”韦兹领主（the Lord of Vez）嚷道——自有伟大的猎户宁录（Nimrod）以来，论起赌咒发誓，再没人比得上他——“不过，我今天身子不爽，总得痛痛快快地奔上一场，把这些邪火发散出去才好。你说咱们能猎点什么，马尔科特，来顶替这头该死的黑狼？”

“这个么，既然一心都扑在这头狼身上了，”马尔科特答道，“旁的畜生我一概不曾循迹追过。大人可愿随意放几条犬，撞见什么就猎什么？”

===Original===
The Baron was about to express his willingness to agree to this proposal when he caught sight of little Engoulevent coming towards them cap in hand.

“Wait a moment,” he said, “here comes Engoulevent, who, I fancy, has some advice to give us.”

“I have no advice to give to a noble Lord like yourself,” replied Engoulevent, assuming an expression of humility on his sly and crafty face; “it is, however, my duty to inform you that there is a splendid buck in the neighbourhood.”

“Let us see your buck, Engoulevent,” replied the chief wolf-hunter, “and if you are not mistaken about it, there will be a new crown for you.”

“Where is this buck of yours?” asked Marcotte, “but look to your skin, if you make us uncouple to no purpose.”

===Chinese===
男爵正要满口答应这个主意，却一眼瞧见小个子昂古勒旺摘着帽子，朝他们走来。

“且等一等，”他说，“昂古勒旺来了，我瞧他像是有话要向我们进言。”

“像大人这般高贵的领主，小人哪敢有什么进言，”昂古勒旺答道，在那张刁滑狡黠的脸上堆出一副谦卑的神情，“不过，小人有责任禀报大人：邻近有一头极出色的雄鹿。”

“领我们去瞧瞧你那头雄鹿吧，昂古勒旺，”这位首席猎狼官答道，“若是你没看走眼，这儿有一枚崭新的埃居（crown）赏你。”

“你这头雄鹿在哪儿？”马尔科特问，“不过你可得当心你的皮，要是害我们白放了一回犬。”

===Original===
“Let me have Matador and Jupiter, and then we shall see.” Matador and Jupiter were the finest among the hounds belonging to the Lord of Vez. And indeed, Engoulevent had not gone a hundred paces with them through the thicket, before, by the lashing of their tails, and their repeated yelping, he knew that they were on the right scent. In another minute or two a magnificent ten-tined stag came into view. Marcotte cried Tally-ho, sounded his horn, and the hunt began, to the great satisfaction of the Lord of Vez, who, although regretting the black wolf, was willing to make the best of a fine buck in its stead. The hunt had lasted two hours, and the quarry still held on. It had first led its pursuers from the little wood of Haramont to the Chemin du Pendu, and thence straight to the back of Oigny, and it still showed no sign of fatigue; for it was not one of those poor animals of the flat country who get their tails pulled by every wretched terrier.

===Chinese===
“把斗牛士（Matador）和朱庇特（Jupiter）给我，咱们便见分晓。”斗牛士和朱庇特，是韦兹领主那一群猎犬里最出色的两条。果然，昂古勒旺牵着它们在矮树林里还没走上百步，凭它们那不住甩动的尾巴和一声紧似一声的吠叫，他便知道嗅迹已上了正路。不出一两分钟，一头壮美的十杈雄鹿便现出身来。马尔科特一声“发现猎物”的吆喝，又吹响号角，围猎就此开场——韦兹领主好不称心，他虽惋惜那头黑狼，却也乐得拿一头顶刮的雄鹿来将就。这场围猎持续了整整两个钟头，猎物仍在苦苦支撑。它先把追兵从阿拉蒙（Haramont）那片小树林引到“吊死人路”（Chemin du Pendu），又从那里径直奔到瓦尼（Oigny）的背后，却依然不见半点疲乏的迹象；因为它可不是平原上那种任什么蹩脚小犬都能扯住尾巴的可怜畜生。

===Original===
As it neared the low grounds of Bourg-fontaine, however, it evidently decided that it was being run rather hard, for it gave up the bolder measures which had hitherto enabled it to keep ahead, and began to double.

Its first manoeuvre was to go down to the brook which joins the ponds of Baisemont and Bourg, then to walk against stream with the water up to its haunches, for nearly half a mile; it then sprang on to the right bank, back again into the bed of the stream, made another leap to the left, and with a succession of bounds, as vigorous as its failing strength allowed, continued to outdistance its pursuers. But the dogs of my lord Baron were not animals to be put out by such trifles as these. Being both sagacious and well-bred, they, of their own accord, divided the task between themselves, half going up stream, and half down, these hunting on the right those on the left, and so effectually that they ere long put the animal off its changes, for they soon recovered the scent, rallying at the first cry given by one of the pack, and starting afresh on the chase, as ready and eager as if the deer had been only twenty paces in front of them.

===Chinese===
然而它一挨近布尔丰泉（Bourg-fontaine）那片洼地，显然便觉出自己被追得紧了，于是放弃了先前那套使它一直领先的凌厉路数，开始兜起圈子来。

它的头一个花招，是下到那条连通贝斯蒙（Baisemont）池塘与布尔（Bourg）池塘的小溪里，逆流涉水而行，水没到大腿根，走了将近半英里；接着它猛地纵上右岸，又折回河床，再一跃跳到左岸，趁着尚存的气力，一连几纵，竭力甩开追兵。然而男爵大人的犬可不是这般区区小计便能难倒的。它们既聪明又有良种，自行分了工：一半逆流而上，一半顺流而下，两岸彼此呼应搜寻，如此奏效，不多时便搅乱了那畜生的连环诡计——因为它们很快重新嗅到了气味，一听犬群里哪一条开了腔便齐声呼应，重又追了上去，那般精神抖擞、急不可耐，活像那头鹿不过只跑在它们前面二十步开外似的。

===Original===
And so with galloping of horses, with cry of hounds and blare of horn, the Baron and his huntsmen reached the ponds of Saint Antoine, a hundred paces or so from the Confines of Oigny. Between these and the Osier-beds stood the hut of Thibault, the sabot-maker.

We must pause to give some description of this Thibault, the shoemaker, the real hero of the tale.

You will ask why I, who have summoned kings to appear upon the stage, who have obliged princes, dukes, and barons to play secondary parts in my romances, should take a simple shoemaker for the hero of this tale.

===Chinese===
就这样，马蹄翻飞，犬声鼎沸，号角嘹亮，男爵带着他那一众猎手，奔到了圣安托万（Saint Antoine）的几口池塘边——离瓦尼的边界不过百步之遥。在池塘与柳条丛之间，立着木屐匠蒂博（Thibault）的小屋。

这里我们得停一停，把这蒂博——这个鞋匠、本书真正的主角——略作一番描摹。

诸位定要发问：我这个人，曾把一国之君召上舞台，曾逼着亲王、公爵、男爵在我的小说里充任配角，何以这一回，却挑了个区区鞋匠来当主角？

===Original===
First, I will reply by saying that, in my dear home country of Villers-Cotterets, there are more sabot-makers than barons, dukes and princes, and that, as soon as I decided to make the forest the scene of the events I am about to record, I was obliged to choose one of the actual inhabitants of this forest as hero, unless I had wished to represent such fantastic persons as the *Incas* of Marmontel or the *Abencerrages* of M. de Florian.

More than that, it is not the author who decides on the subject, but the subject which takes possession of the author, and, good or bad, this particular subject has taken possession of me. I will therefore endeavour to draw Thibault’s portrait for you, plain shoemaker as he was, as exactly as the artist paints the portrait which a prince desires to send to his ladylove.

===Chinese===
头一句，我要这样回答：在我那可爱的故乡维莱-科特雷，木屐匠比男爵、公爵、亲王加起来还多；而既然我已打定主意，要把即将记述的种种事件安排在林子里发生，我便不得不从这片森林真正的居民里挑出一个来当主角——除非我情愿去描摹马尔蒙泰尔笔下那种《印加人》（*Incas*）、或是弗洛里安先生笔下那种《阿本塞拉赫人》（*Abencerrages*）那样子虚乌有的人物。

更有甚者——并不是作者来择定题材，倒是题材攫住了作者；而这一回的题材，无论好坏，已把我也攫住了。所以我将尽力为诸位描一幅蒂博的肖像：纵使他不过是个寻常鞋匠，我也要像那位画师为亲王绘下要送给意中人的肖像一般，把他画得分毫不差。

===Original===
Thibault was a man between twenty-five and twenty-seven years of age, tall, well made, physically robust, but by nature melancholy and sad of heart. This depression of spirits arose from a little grain of envy, which, in spite of himself, perhaps unconsciously to himself, he harboured towards all such of his neighbours as had been more favoured by fortune than himself.

His father had committed a fault, a serious one at all times, but more especially in those days of absolutism, when a man was not able to rise above his station as nowadays, when with sufficient capacity he may attain to any rank. Thibault had been educated above his position; he had been at school under the Abbé Fortier, at Villers-Cotterets, and had learnt to read, write, and cipher; moreover he knew a little Latin, which made him inordinately proud of himself. Thibault had spent a great part of his time in reading, and his books had been chiefly those which were in vogue at the close of the preceding century. But he had not been a sufficiently clever analyst to know how to separate the good from the bad, or rather he *had* separated what was bad, and swallowed it in large doses, leaving the good to precipitate itself at the bottom of the glass.

===Chinese===
蒂博年约二十五到二十七岁之间，身量高挑，体格匀称，筋骨强健，天性却忧郁、心头沉郁。这股子郁郁寡欢，源自他心底里那一星妒意：他对所有命途比自己顺遂的邻里，都不由自主地——或许连他自己也不曾察觉地——怀着一丝嫉恨。

他的父亲犯下过一桩过错——一桩无论何时都称得上严重的过错，而在那个专制的年代尤为致命；那时一个人不能像今日这般逾越自己的本分往上爬，今日只要有足够的才干，便可平步青云，位列任何品级。蒂博受的教育高过了他的出身：他曾在维莱-科特雷的福尔蒂埃神父（the Abbé Fortier）门下求学，学会了读、写、算；此外还懂一点拉丁文，这点学问叫他对自己无比自负。蒂博把大把的光阴都花在了读书上，他所读的，又多半是上世纪末风行一时的那一类书。然而他算不得什么高明的鉴赏家，分不清书中精华与糟粕；不如说，他恰恰是把糟粕挑拣了出来，大口大口吞下肚去，倒让精华沉淀在了杯底。

===Original===
At twenty years of age Thibault had certainly had dreams of being something other than a sabot-maker. He had, for instance, for a very little while, cast his eyes towards the army. But his comrades who had worn the double livery of king and country, had left the service as they entered it, mere soldiers of the ranks, having failed during five or six years of slavery to obtain promotion, even to the not very exalted grade of corporal.

Thibault had also thought of becoming a sailor. But a career in the navy was as much forbidden to the plebeian as one in the army. Possibly after enduring danger, and storm and battle for fifteen or twenty years, he might be made a boatswain’s mate, that was all, and then! besides, it was by no means Thibault’s ambition to wear a short vest and sailcloth trousers, but the blue uniform of the king with red vest and gold epaulettes. He had moreover known of no single case in which the son of a mere shoemaker had become Master of a Frigate, or even Lieutenant. So he was forced to give up all idea of joining the King’s Navy.

===Chinese===
二十岁那年，蒂博无疑做过几场梦，梦见自己总该不至于一辈子做个木屐匠。比方说，曾有那么短短一阵子，他把目光投向过军旅。然而他那些穿过“国王与祖国”那身双重号衣的同伴，进营时是大头兵，出来时依旧是大头兵：五六年苦熬下来，连个算不上多么显赫的班长都没捞上。

蒂博也曾想过当个水手。可是海军这条路，和陆军一样，对平民紧闭着大门。或许熬过十五二十年的风涛险阻、枪林弹雨，他能挣上个水手长副手，也就到头了——再说！况且，蒂博心心念念想穿的，可不是短马甲和帆布裤，而是镶红马甲金肩章的国王蓝军服。况且他也从未听说过，哪位区区鞋匠的儿子当上了巡防舰的舰长，哪怕只是个海军上尉。于是他只得打消了投奔王家海军的念头。

===Original===
Thibault would not have minded being a Notary, and at one time thought of apprenticing himself to the Royal Scrivener, Maître Niquet, as a stepping-stone, and of making his way up on the strength of his own legs and with the help of his pen. But supposing him to have risen to the position of head clerk with a salary of a hundred crowns, where was he to find the thirty thousand francs which would be required for the purchase of the smallest village practice.

There was, therefore, no better chance of his becoming a scrivener than of becoming an officer on sea or land. Meanwhile, Thibault’s father died, leaving very little ready money. There was about enough to bury him, so he was buried, and this done, there remained some thirty or forty francs over for Thibault.

===Chinese===
当个公证人，蒂博倒也不嫌弃；有一阵子，他想过去给王家文书尼凯师傅（Maître Niquet）当学徒，以此为敲门砖，再凭自己的双腿和手中那管笔，一步步往上爬。可就算他当上了首席书记，拿着一百埃居的年俸，他又上哪儿去凑那三万法郎——那可是买下哪怕最小的乡村公证处所必不可少的数目。

可见他当上文书的指望，并不比当上海陆两军的军官更大。就在这当口，蒂博的父亲撒手人寰，留下的现钱少得可怜。钱刚够殡殓他，于是便把他殡殓了；办完这桩事，蒂博手里还剩下三四十个法郎。

===Original===
Thibault knew his trade well; indeed, he was a first-rate workman; but he had no inclination to handle either auger or parer. It ended, therefore, by his leaving all his father’s tools in the care of a friend, a remnant of prudence still remaining to him, and selling every vestige of furniture; having thus realised a sum of five hundred and forty livres, he determined to make what was then called the tour of France.

Thibault spent three years in travelling; he did not make his fortune during that time, but he learnt a great many things in the course of his journey of which he was previously ignorant, and acquired certain accomplishments which he had previously been without.

He learned amongst other things that, although it was as well to keep one’s word on matters of business with a man, it was no use whatever keeping love vows made to a woman.

===Chinese===
蒂博手艺精熟，实打实是一等一的匠人；可他全无心思再去摸那钻子或刨刀。于是乎，他把父亲的全副工具都寄存在一个朋友处——这是他仅存的一点谨慎——又把每一件家具都变卖净尽；这样凑足了一笔五百四十里弗（livres）的款子，便决意去作当时所谓的“环法之行”（the tour of France）。

蒂博在外游历了三年；三年里他并未发财，却在旅途中学会了许许多多从前一窍不通的事，也添了几样本事，是先前所没有的。

比方说，他从中学到：和男人在生意上打交道，说话固然要算话；可对女人许下的情誓，守着却全无用处。

===Original===
So much for his character and habits of mind. As to his external accomplishments, he could dance a jig beautifully, could hold his own at quarterstaff against four men, and could handle the boar-spear as cleverly as the best huntsman going. All these things had not a little served to increase Thibault’s natural self-esteem, and, seeing himself handsomer, stronger, and cleverer than many of the nobles, he would exclaim against Providence, crying, “Why was I not nobly born? why was not that nobleman yonder born a peasant?”

===Chinese===
以上便是他的性情与心思。至于他外在的本事：他能把号笛舞跳得极漂亮，抡起短棍（quarterstaff）能独敌四人，执起野猪矛，其身手不让当世最好的猎手。这些种种，都大大助长了蒂博与生俱来的自命不凡；他见自己比许多贵族都更俊美、更强壮、更机巧，便不禁怨起老天来，嚷道：“我何以没生在高门？那边那位老爷，又何以没生在农家？”

===Original===
But as Providence took care not to make any answer to these *apostrophés*, and as Thibault found that dancing, playing at quarterstaff, and throwing the boar-spear only fatigued the body, without procuring him any material advantage, he began to turn his thoughts towards his ancient trade, humble though it was, saying to himself, if it enabled the father to live, it would also enable the son. So Thibault went and fetched away his tools; and then, tools in hand, he went to ask permission of the Steward of his Royal Highness Louis Philippe of Orleans, to build a hut in the forest, in which to carry on his trade. He had no difficulty in obtaining this, for the steward knew by experience that his master was a very kindhearted man, expending as much as two hundred and forty thousand francs a year on the poor; he felt sure, therefore, that one who gave away a sum like this, would be willing to let an honest workman who wished to ply his trade, have thirty or forty feet of ground.

As he had leave to establish himself in whatever part of the forest he liked best, Thibault chose the spot near the osier-beds, where the roads crossed, one of the most beautiful parts of the woods, less than a mile from Oigny and about three times that distance from Villers-Cotterets. The shoemaker put up his workshop, built partly of old planks given him by M. Panisis, who had been having a sale in the neighbourhood, and partly of the branches which the steward gave him leave to cut in the forest.

===Chinese===
然而老天爷对这些 *apostrophés*（呼告之词）一向不肯作答；蒂博也渐渐发觉，跳舞、抡短棍、掷野猪矛，只会徒然累乏了筋骨，却带不来半点实在的好处。于是他重新把心思转向了那门古老的行当——纵然卑微，心想：这门手艺既养活了父亲，也就能养活儿子。蒂博便去取回了工具；随后工具在手，便去求奥尔良公爵路易·菲利普殿下的管家恩准，许他在林子里搭一间小屋，好操持他的营生。这事他办得毫不费力，因为那位管家凭经验深知自己的主人为人心善，每年施与穷人的款项多达二十四万法郎；他因而笃信，一个肯如此慷慨解囊的人，断不会不肯让一个诚实的匠人——一个只想安安分分做活的人——占用三四十英尺见方的一块地皮。

既然准他在林子里任择一处最中意的地方安身，蒂博便挑中了柳条丛附近、几条路交汇的那块地——那是整片林子里最美的一隅，离瓦尼不到一英里，离维莱-科特雷大约是这个距离的三倍。鞋匠搭起了他的工棚，材料一部分是帕尼齐先生（M. Panisis）给他的旧木板——那位先生恰在邻近办一场拍卖——另一部分则是管家准他从林子里砍来的树枝。

===Original===
When the building of the hut, which consisted of a bedroom, cosily shut in, where he could work during the winter, and of a lean-to, open to the air, where he could work in the summer, was completed, Thibault began to think of making himself a bed. At first, a layer of fern had to serve for this purpose; but after he had made a hundred pairs of wooden shoes and had sold these to Bedeau, who kept a general shop at Villers-Cotterets, he was able to pay a sufficient deposit to get a mattress, to be paid for in full by the end of three months. The framework of the bed was not difficult to make; Thibault was not the shoemaker he was without being a bit of a carpenter into the bargain, and when this was finished he plaited osiers to take the place of sacking, laid the mattress upon them, and found himself at last with a bed to lie upon.

===Chinese===
小屋落了成：里头是一间卧房，严严实实地封拢，可供他冬天在里面做活；外头搭一个披屋，四面通风，可供他夏天作业。屋成之后，蒂博便开始琢磨给自己置一张床。起初，他只好铺一层蕨草将就。可等他打出一百双木鞋、卖给了在维莱-科特雷开杂货铺的贝多（Bedeau）之后，他便能付下一笔定金，赊来一张床垫，言明三个月内付清全款。床的框架倒不难做：蒂博既是这般手艺的鞋匠，自然也兼带几分木匠的本事；框架一成，他便编了柳条来替代网垫，把床垫铺在上头，临了总算有一张床可躺了。

===Original===
Little by little came the sheets, and then in their turn the coverlids; the next purchase was a chafing-dish, and earthenware pots to cook in, and finally some plates and dishes. Before the year was out Thibault had also made additions to his furniture of a fine oak chest and a fine walnut-wood cupboard, both, like the bed, his own handiwork. All the while he was driving a brisk trade, for none could beat Thibault in turning a block of beech into a pair of shoes, and in converting the odd chips into spoons, saltcellars and natty little bowls.

He had now been settled in his workshop for three years, that is, ever since his return after the completion of his tour round France, and there was nothing for which anyone could have reproached him during this interval except the failing we have already mentioned—that he was rather more envious of the good fortune of his neighbour than was altogether conducive to the welfare of his soul. But this feeling was as yet so inoffensive, that his confessor had no need to do more than awaken in him a sense of shame for harbouring thoughts which had, so far, not resulted in any active crime.

===Chinese===
床单一点一点添置齐了，接着便是床罩；再置办的是一只炭炉和几只煮饭的陶罐，最后才是若干盘碟。不出一年，蒂博又给家具添了两件：一只上好的橡木箱子，一只上好的胡桃木橱柜——两件都和那张床一样，是他亲手打的。这一向，他的营生红红火火：把一块山毛榉旋成一双木鞋，再把刨下的碎料做成汤匙、盐罐和精巧的小碗，论手艺谁也比不过蒂博。

他在工棚里安顿下来，至今已是第三个年头——也就是说，自他环法之行结束后回来便住到了这里。这段日子里，旁人无可指责他什么，除了我们已提到过的那一桩短处：他对邻人的好福气，嫉妒得未免重了些，重到了于他灵魂的安宁颇有妨碍的地步。好在这一点心思至今仍近乎无害，他的听告解神父也只需在他心里唤起一丝羞愧——为那些迄今尚未酿成任何实际罪行的念头而羞愧——便已足够。

## XV The Lord of Vauparfond / XV 沃帕丰领主

===Original===
Thibault, on arriving at the Dauphin d’Or, ordered himself as fine a dinner as he could think of. It would have been quite easy for him to have engaged a private room, but he would not then have enjoyed the personal sense of superiority. He wished the company of ordinary diners to see him eat his pullet, and his eel in its delicate sauce. He wished the other drinkers to envy him his three different wines, drunk out of three different shaped glasses. He wished everybody to hear him give his orders in a haughty voice, to hear the ring of his money.

===Chinese===
蒂博一进了金王太子客栈（the Dauphin d’Or），便照着自个儿所能想到的最讲究的样式，给自己点了一桌好饭。他要个雅座单间本是再容易不过的事，可那样一来，他便享受不到那种高人一等的快活了。他要让大堂里那些寻常食客瞧着他吃童子鸡、吃那浇着考究酱汁的鳗鱼；他要让旁的酒客眼红他能喝上三种不同的好酒，用的是三种不同形状的酒杯；他要叫满堂的人都听见他拿腔拿调地吩咐堂倌，听见他银钱落地的脆响。

===Original===
As he gave his first order, a man in a grey coat, seated in the darkest corner of the room with a half bottle of wine before him, turned round, as if recognising a voice he knew. And, as it turned out, this was one of Thibault’s acquaintances﻿—it is scarcely necessary to add, a tavern acquaintance.

Thibault, since he had given up making shoes by day and, instead, had his wolves about at night, had made many such acquaintances. On seeing that it was Thibault, the other man turned his face away quickly, but not so quickly but that Thibault had time to recognise Auguste François Levasseur, valet to Raoul the Lord of Vauparfond.

===Chinese===
头一道菜方才点下，便有一个穿灰大衣的汉子转过身来——这人原本坐在大堂最暗的角落里，面前搁着半瓶酒——那神气，像是听出了一个熟人的嗓音。果不其然，这竟是蒂博的一个相识——几乎不必多说，是酒馆里结识的那种相识。

自从蒂博白日里撂下了鞋匠的营生、夜里倒与那群狼厮混起来，这类相识他结下了不少。那人一瞧见是蒂博，赶紧把脸扭了过去，只是这扭头的工夫毕竟不够快，蒂博已来得及认出他来——此人正是奥古斯特·弗朗索瓦·勒瓦瑟（Auguste François Levasseur），沃帕丰领主拉乌尔（Raoul the Lord of Vauparfond）贴身伺候的跟班。

===Original===
“Halloa! François!” Thibault called out, “what are you doing sitting there in the corner, and sulking like a Monk in Lent, instead of taking your dinner openly and cheerfully as I am doing, in full view of everybody?”

François made no reply to this interrogation, but signed to Thibault to hold his tongue.

===Chinese===
“喂！弗朗索瓦（François）！”蒂博高声招呼道，“你缩在那角落里做什么？跟四旬斋里生闷气的修士似的，干嘛不像我这般光明正大、快快活活地当着众人的面吃你的饭？”

弗朗索瓦对这一通盘问并不答腔，只冲蒂博使了个眼色，叫他住嘴。

===Original===
“I am not to speak? not to speak?” said Thibault, “and supposing it does not suit me to hold my tongue, supposing I wish to talk, and that I am bored at having to dine alone? and that it pleases me to say; ‘Friend François, come here; I invite you to dine with me?’ You will not? no? very well, then I shall come and fetch you.” And Thibault rose from his seat, and followed by all eyes, went up to his friend and gave him a slap on the shoulder vigorous enough to dislocate it.

“Pretend that you have made a mistake, Thibault, or you will lose me my place; do you not see that I am not in livery, but am only wearing my drab greatcoat! I am here as proxy in a love affair for my master, and I am waiting for a letter from a lady to carry back to him.”

===Chinese===
“不叫我说话？不叫我说话？”蒂博道，“可要是闭上嘴不合我的意呢？要是我有话要说、嫌一个人吃饭闷得慌呢？要是我高兴这么说：‘弗朗索瓦老兄，过来；我请你一道吃饭！’你又待如何？你不肯？不肯？好极了，那我就过来请你。”说罢蒂博便离了座，在满堂人目光的追随下，走到他那位朋友跟前，照他肩上就是一巴掌，那劲头险些把人家的肩胛骨拍散了架。

“蒂博，你装作认错了人，不然你要砸了我的饭碗；你没瞧见我没穿号衣，只披着这件灰褐大衣吗！我是替我家老爷来办一桩风流勾当的，正等着一位太太的信，好替他捎回去。”

===Original===
“That’s another matter altogether, and I understand now and am sorry for my indiscretion. I should like, however, to have dined in your company.”

“Well, nothing is easier; order your dinner to be served in a separate room, and I will give word to our host, that if another man dressed in grey like me comes in, he is to show him upstairs; he and I are old cronies, and understand one another.”

“Good,” said Thibault; and he therewith ordered his dinner to be taken up to a room on the first floor, which looked out upon the street.

===Chinese===
“那可就完全是另一回事了，我如今明白了，为我这莽撞赔个不是。不过，我倒真想跟你一块儿吃这顿饭。”

“嗐，这有什么难的；你叫他们把饭开到一间单间里去，我再交代咱们这位店主一声：要是再进来一个跟我一样穿灰衣的汉子，就领他上楼；那是我多年的老伙计了，我俩彼此肚里明白。”

“好。”蒂博说，当即吩咐把他的饭菜端到二楼一间临街的屋子里去。

===Original===
François seated himself so as to be able to see the person he was expecting, while some distance off, as he came down the hill of Ferté-Milon. The dinner which Thibault had ordered was quite sufficient for the two; all that he did was to send for another bottle or so of wine. Thibault had only taken two lessons from Maître Magloire, but he had been an apt pupil, and they had done their work; moreover Thibault had something which he wished to forget, and he counted on the wine to accomplish this for him. It was good fortune, he felt, to have met a friend with whom he could talk, for, in the state of mind and heart in which he was, talking was as good a help towards oblivion as drinking. Accordingly, he was no sooner seated, and the door shut, and his hat stuck well down on to his head so that François might not notice the change in the colour of his hair, than he burst at once into conversation, boldly taking the bull by the horns.

===Chinese===
弗朗索瓦拣了个位子坐下，从这个角度，远远地便可望见他等候的那个人——那人从费尔泰-米隆（Ferté-Milon）那边的山坡上下来时，他便瞧得见。蒂博方才点的菜两个人吃绰绰有余，他只又叫人添了大约一瓶光景的酒。蒂博统共只在马格卢瓦师傅（Maître Magloire）那儿上过两课，可他是个伶俐的学生，那两课已尽够他受用了；况且蒂博心头有一桩事想要忘掉，他便指望靠这酒来替他办成。他觉得这会儿撞见一个能聊天的朋友，实是运气——以他眼下这般心境，说话之于忘忧，与喝酒一样地顶用。于是他一落座、门一关，先把帽子使劲往下扣了扣，免得弗朗索瓦瞧出他头发变了颜色，便立时打开了话匣子，单刀直入地开了腔。

===Original===
“And now, friend François,” he said, “you are going to explain to me some of your words which I did not quite understand.”

“I am not surprised at that,” replied François, leaning back in his chair with an air of conceited impertinence, “we attendants on fashionable lords learn to speak court language, which everyone of course does not understand.”

“Perhaps not, but if you explain it to your friends, they may possibly understand.”

“Quite so! ask what you like and I will answer.”

===Chinese===
“这会儿，弗朗索瓦老兄，”他说，“你可得给我解释解释，你嘴里有几句话我没怎么听明白。”

“这我倒不奇怪，”弗朗索瓦答道，往椅背上一靠，带着一股自负而放肆的神气，“我们这些伺候时兴老爷的人，学的是一口宫廷话，自然不是人人都能听懂的。”

“或许吧，可你若给朋友们解说解说，他们说不定也就听懂了。”

“正是！你尽管问，我有问必答。”

===Original===
“I look to your doing so the more, that I will undertake to supply you with what will help to loosen your tongue. First, let me ask, why do you call yourself a *grey-coat*? I thought *grey-coat* another name for a jackass.”

“Jackass yourself, friend Thibault,” said François, laughing at the shoemaker’s ignorance. “No, a *grey-coat* is a liveried servant, who puts on a grey overall to hide his livery, while he stands sentinel behind a pillar, or mounts guard inside a doorway.”

===Chinese===
“我更有把握指望你答得痛快了，因为我自会拿些东西来给你润润嗓子、松松舌头。头一个我就问：你为何自称‘灰衣（grey-coat）’？我还当‘灰衣’是蠢驴的另一个叫法呢。”

“你才是蠢驴呢，蒂博老兄。”弗朗索瓦说着，笑话这鞋匠的孤陋寡闻。“不对，‘灰衣’是指穿号衣的仆人，他在号衣外头套一件灰色罩褂，好藏在柱子后头放哨，或是在门道里头把守。”

===Original===
“So you mean that at this moment then, my good François, you are on sentry go? And who is coming to relieve you?”

“Champagne, who is in the Comtesse de Mont-Gobert’s service.”

“I see; I understand exactly. Your master, the Lord of Vauparfond, is in love with the Comtesse de Mont-Gobert, and you are now awaiting a letter which Champagne is to bring from the lady.”

“*Optimé!* as the tutor to Monsieur Raoul’s young brother says.”

===Chinese===
“那么你的意思是，这会儿我亲爱的弗朗索瓦，你是在站岗放哨了？又是谁来替你的班呢？”

“香槟（Champagne），他在蒙-戈贝尔伯爵夫人（the Comtesse de Mont-Gobert）那儿当差。”

“我明白了；我完全明白了。你家老爷沃帕丰领主，爱上了蒙-戈贝尔伯爵夫人，这会儿你正等着香槟替你从那位太太那儿捎一封信来。”

“*Optimé*（好极了）！”这正是拉乌尔先生幼弟的那位家庭教师常说的口头禅。

===Original===
“My Lord Raoul is a lucky fellow!”

“Yes indeed,” said François, drawing himself up.

“And what a beautiful creature the Countess is!”

“You know her then?”

“I have seen her out hunting with his Highness the Duke of Orleans and Madame de Montesson.”

===Chinese===
“拉乌尔老爷真是个有福之人！”

“可不是嘛。”弗朗索瓦说着，把腰杆一挺。

“伯爵夫人又是何等标致的一位美人！”

“那么你认得她？”

“我见过她跟着奥尔良公爵殿下（his Highness the Duke of Orleans）和蒙特森夫人（Madame de Montesson）一道出外打猎。”

===Original===
Thibault in speaking had said *out hunting*.

“My friend, let me tell you that in society we do not say *hunting* and *shooting*, but *huntin’* and *shootin’*.”

“Oh!” said Thibault, “I am not so particular to a letter as all that. To the health of my Lord Raoul!”

As François put down his glass on the table, he uttered an exclamation; he had that moment caught sight of Champagne.

===Chinese===
蒂博方才说的是“出外打猎（out hunting）”。

“我的朋友，我且告诉你，在上流社会里，我们不说‘打猎（hunting）’‘打靶（shooting）’，要说‘打猎儿（huntin’）’‘打靶儿（shootin’）’。”

“噢！”蒂博说，“我倒不曾在这一字半句上头这般斤斤计较。为拉乌尔老爷的健康干杯！”

弗朗索瓦把酒杯往桌上一搁，惊呼了一声——他这当儿已瞧见了香槟。

===Original===
They threw open the window and called to this third comer, and Champagne, with all the ready intuition of the well-bred servant, understood at once, and went upstairs. He was dressed, like François, in a long grey coat, and had brought a letter with him.

“Well,” asked François, as he caught sight of the letter in his hand, “and is there to be a meeting tonight?”

“Yes,” answered Champagne, with evident delight.

“That’s all right,” said François cheerfully.

===Chinese===
两人推开窗子，招呼这位第三位来客；香槟凭着上过台面的仆人那点子眼到心到的机灵劲儿，立时会意，便上楼来了。他也跟弗朗索瓦一样，披着一件长长的灰大衣，手里还捎来了一封信。

“怎么样，”弗朗索瓦一瞧见他手里的信便问，“今儿晚上可有约会？”

“有。”香槟答道，脸上喜色分明。

“这就齐了。”弗朗索瓦欢快地说。

===Original===
Thibault was surprised at these expressions of apparent sympathy on the part of the servants with their master’s happiness.

“Is it your master’s good luck that you are so pleased about?” he asked of François.

“Oh, dear me no!” replied the latter, “but when my master is engaged, I am at liberty!”

“And do you make use of your liberty?”

“One may be a valet, and yet have one’s own share of good luck, and also know how to spend the time more or less profitably,” answered François, bridling as he spoke.

===Chinese===
这两个下人对他家老爷的这桩喜事竟流露出这般同情的快活，蒂博看在眼里，倒有些诧异。

“你这么欢喜，是为你家老爷的好福气么？”他问弗朗索瓦。

“哎呀，才不是呢！”后者答道，“只是我家老爷一有了约会，我便落得自由了！”

“那你又如何使唤你这份自由呢？”

“人纵然是个跟班，也自有他的一份好福气，也懂得怎样把光阴打发得多少有点儿进账。”弗朗索瓦答道，说着把头一昂。

===Original===
“And you, Champagne?”

“Oh, I,” replied the last comer, holding his wine up to the light, “yes, I too hope to make good use of it.”

“Well, then, here’s to all your love affairs! since everybody seems to have one or more on hand,” said Thibault.

“The same to yours!” replied the two other men in chorus.

===Chinese===
“你呢，香槟？”

“噢，我么，”这位后到的把酒杯举到灯下，答道，“是了，我也盼着能把这份自由使唤得妥帖。”

“既如此，那就为你们各位的风流勾当干杯！瞧这架势，人人手里都攥着一桩、甚至不止一桩呢。”蒂博说。

“也为你那一桩干杯！”那两人齐声回道。

===Original===
“As to myself,” said the shoemaker, a look of hatred to his fellow creatures passing over his face, “I am the only person who loves nobody, and whom nobody loves.”

His companion looked at him with a certain surprised curiosity.

“Ah! ah!” said François, “is the report that is whispered abroad about you in the countryside a true tale then?”

“Report about me?”

“Yes, about you,” put in Champagne.

===Chinese===
“至于我嘛，”鞋匠说道，一脸痛恨同类的阴霾掠过脸上，“我是这世上唯一一个既不爱人、也没人爱的人。”

他的同伴以一种带着诧异的好奇眼光打量他。

“啊哈！”弗朗索瓦说，“那么乡间私下里传你的那些风言风语，竟是真的了？”

“传我的风言风语？”

“是的，传你的。”香槟插嘴道。

===Original===
“Oh, then they say the same thing about me at Mont-Gobert as they do at Vauparfond?”

Champagne nodded his head.

“Well, and what is it they do say?”

“That you are a werewolf,” said François.

Thibault laughed aloud. “Tell me, now, have I a tail?” he said, “have I a wolf’s claws, have I a wolf’s snout?”

“We only repeat what other people say,” rejoined Champagne, “we do not say that it is so.”

===Chinese===
“噢，那么他们在蒙-戈贝尔那儿说我些什么，跟在沃帕丰说的是一样的了？”

香槟点了点头。

“好，那他们到底怎么说？”

“说你是狼人（werewolf）。”弗朗索瓦说。

蒂博放声大笑。“那么请问，我有尾巴么？”他说，“我有狼爪子么？我有狼嘴筒子么？”

“我们不过是人云亦云罢了，”香槟答道，“我们并没说真是这么回事。”

===Original===
“Well, anyhow, you must acknowledge,” said Thibault, “that werewolves have excellent wine.”

“By my faith, yes!” exclaimed both the valets.

“To the health of the devil who provides it, gentlemen.”

The two men who were holding their glasses in the hand, put both glasses down on the table.

“What is that for?” asked Thibault.

“You must find someone else to drink that health with you,” said François, “I won’t, that’s flat!”

“Nor I,” added Champagne.

“Well and good then! I will drink all three glasses myself,” and he immediately proceeded to do so.

===Chinese===
“也罢，无论如何，你们总得承认，”蒂博说，“狼人喝的酒可是上等好酒。”

“凭我的良心起誓，不错！”两个跟班齐声叫道。

“那就为供这酒的好魔鬼干一杯，诸位。”

那两人手里正擎着酒杯，一听这话，双双把杯子搁回了桌上。

“这是干什么？”蒂博问。

“你这杯酒，还是另找旁人陪你喝吧，”弗朗索瓦说，“我不干，就这么定了！”

“我也不干。”香槟补上一句。

“那好得很！这三杯我一个人全喝了。”说罢他果真一气把三杯酒饮尽。

===Original===
“Friend Thibault,” said the Baron’s valet, “it is time we separated.”

“So soon?” said Thibault.

“My master is awaiting me, and no doubt with some impatience﻿ ﻿… the letter, Champagne?”

“Here it is.”

“Let us take farewell then of your friend Thibault, and be off to our business and our pleasures, and leave him to his pleasures and business.” And so saying, François winked at his friend, who responded with a similar sign of understanding between them.

===Chinese===
“蒂博老兄，”男爵的跟班说道，“咱们该散了。”

“这就走？”蒂博说。

“我家老爷正等着我，多半还等得有些着急了……信呢，香槟？”

“在这儿。”

“那么，跟你这位蒂博老兄道个别吧，我们办我们的事、寻我们的乐去，留他自己寻他的乐、办他的事。”弗朗索瓦说着，冲他的朋友使了个眼色，那一位也心照不宣地回了一个同样的眼色。

===Original===
“We must not separate,” said Thibault, “without drinking a stirrup-cup together.”

“But not in those glasses,” said François, pointing to the three from which Thibault had drunk to the enemy of mankind.

“You are very particular, gentlemen; better call the sacristan and have them washed in holy water.”

“Not quite that, but rather than refuse the polite invitation of a friend, we will call for the waiter, and have fresh glasses brought.”

===Chinese===
“咱们总不能就这么散了，”蒂博说，“不一道喝一杯上马酒（stirrup-cup）是不成的。”

“可不能用那几只杯子。”弗朗索瓦说，指了指蒂博方才用来替人类之敌祝寿的那三只。

“诸位可真挑剔；不如把教堂司事叫来，拿圣水把那几只杯子洗一洗。”

“倒也不至于，不过与其拂了一位朋友的盛情，不如叫堂倌换几只新杯子来。”

===Original===
“These three, then,” said Thibault, who was beginning to feel the effects of the wine he had drunk, “are fit for nothing more than to be thrown out of window? To the devil with you!” he exclaimed as he took up one of them and sent it flying. As the glass went through the air it left a track of light behind it, which blazed and went out like a flash of lightning. Thibault took up the two remaining glasses and threw them in turn, and each time the same thing happened, but the third flash was followed by a loud peal of thunder.

Thibault shut the window, and was thinking, as he turned to his seat again, how he should explain this strange occurrence to his companions; but his two companions had disappeared.

“Cowards!” he muttered. Then he looked for a glass, but found none left.

“Hum! that’s awkward,” he said. “I must drink out of the bottle, that’s all!”

And suiting the action to the word, Thibault finished up his dinner by draining the bottle, which did not help to steady his brain, already somewhat shaky.

===Chinese===
“那么这三只杯子，”蒂博说——酒劲儿已在他身上发作起来——“除了扔出窗外，再没别的用场了？见鬼去吧！”他嚷着，抄起其中一只，便甩了出去。那玻璃杯半空中划过，身后留下一道光的尾迹，一亮即灭，恰似一道闪电。蒂博又抄起剩下的两只杯子，挨个儿甩了出去，每一回都是同样的光景，只是到了第三道闪光之后，紧跟着便是一声响亮的炸雷。

蒂博关上窗子，一边转回座位，一边心里盘算着该怎样向两位同伴解释这一桩怪事；可他那两位同伴，竟已不知去向。

“胆小鬼！”他咕哝道。随后他想找一只杯子，却一只也找不着了。

“哼！这可尴尬了，”他说，“只好对着瓶口喝了，就这么着！”

蒂博说到做到，对着瓶口把这瓶酒一饮而尽，算是给这顿饭作了收尾——这一来，他那本已有些发晕的脑袋，便愈发地不稳当了。

===Original===
At nine o’clock, Thibault called the innkeeper, paid his account, and departed.

He was in an angry disposition of enmity against all the world; the thoughts from which he had hoped to escape possessed him more and more. Agnelette was being taken farther and farther from him as the time went by; everyone, wife or mistress, had someone to love them. This day which had been one of hatred and despair to him, had been one full of the promise of joy and happiness for everybody else; the lord of Vauparfond, the two wretched valets, François and Champagne, each of them had a bright star of hope to follow; while he, he alone, went stumbling along in the darkness. Decidedly there was a curse upon him. “But,” he went on thinking to himself, “if so, the pleasures of the damned belong to me, and I have a right to claim them.”

===Chinese===
到了九点钟，蒂博叫来店主，结了账，便动身离去。

他满怀着一种与天下人为敌的怨毒心境；他原想躲开的那一桩桩心事，反倒越发紧紧地缠住了他。光阴一寸寸过去，阿涅莱特（Agnelette）正被带得离他越来越远；普天之下，无论是为人妻的、还是为人外室的，个个都有人来疼爱。这一天，于他是仇恨与绝望的一日；于旁人，却处处透着欢喜与幸福的兆头：那沃帕丰领主，那两个腌臢的跟班弗朗索瓦和香槟，一个个都有那么一颗明亮的希望之星可追；唯独他，唯独他一个，在黑暗里跌跌撞撞地摸索。分明是有一道诅咒压在他身上。“可是，”他一边走，一边在心里接着想，“既是这样，那受诅咒之人的种种乐趣便属我所有，我理直气壮地该来讨这一份。”

===Original===
As these thoughts went surging through his brain, as he walked along cursing aloud, shaking his fist at the sky, he was on the way to his hut and had nearly reached it, when he heard a horse coming up behind him at a gallop.

“Ah!” said Thibault, “here comes the Lord of Vauparfond, hastening to the meeting with his love. I should laugh, my fine Sir Raoul, if my Lord of Mont-Gobert managed just to catch you! You would not get off quite so easily as if it were Maître Magloire; there would be swords out, and blows given and received!”

Thus engaged in thinking what would happen if the Comte de Mont-Gobert were to surprise his rival, Thibault, who was walking in the road, evidently did not get out of the way quickly enough, for the horseman, seeing a peasant of some kind barring his passage, brought his whip down upon him in a violent blow, calling out at the same time: “Get out of the way, you beggar, if you don’t wish to be trampled under the horse’s feet!”

===Chinese===
这一团念头在他脑子里翻涌，他一路走一路破口咒骂，时不时朝天空挥一挥拳。他正走在回自己那间茅屋的路上，已快到了，忽然听得身后一匹马疾驰而来。

“啊哈！”蒂博道，“沃帕丰领主来啦，急着赶去会他的心上人呢。我好心的拉乌尔爵士，这回要是蒙-戈贝尔老爷偏偏撞见了你，那可真要叫我笑出声来了！你这一回，可不能像撞见马格卢瓦师傅那么轻易就脱身了；这一回该是拔剑相向、刀光剑影地你来我往了！”

蒂博正一门心思地盘算着，倘若蒙-戈贝尔伯爵（the Comte de Mont-Gobert）当真撞破了他的情敌，那会是一番什么光景——他这么想着，走在路中央，显然是让路让得不够快；那骑马的见一个不知什么来路的庄稼汉挡了他的道，便一鞭子狠狠抽了下来，嘴里同时喝道：“滚开，你这叫花子，再不躲开，叫马蹄子踩成肉泥！”

===Original===
Thibault, still half drunk, was conscious of a crowd of mingled sensations, of the lashing of the whip, the collision with the horse, and the rolling through cold water and mud, while the horseman passed on.

He rose to his knees, furious with anger, and shaking his fist at the retreating figure:

“Would the devil,” he exclaimed, “I might just for once have my turn at being one of you great lords, might just for twenty-four hours take your place, Monsieur Raoul de Vauparfond, instead of being only Thibault, the shoemaker, so that I might know what it was to have a fine horse to ride, instead of tramping on foot; might be able to whip the peasants I met on the road, and have the opportunity of paying court to these beautiful women, who deceive their husbands, as the Comtesse de Mont-Gobert does!”

The words were hardly out of his mouth, when the Baron’s horse shied, throwing the rider over its head.

===Chinese===
蒂博这时还半醉着，只觉一团五味杂陈的滋味涌上来：那一鞭子抽在身上的火辣，那撞上马身的力道，那在冰水烂泥里打着滚的寒凉——那骑马的却已扬长而去了。

他撑着跪起身来，怒火中烧，朝着那远去的背影挥拳：

“魔鬼啊，”他嚷道，“但愿我也能轮上一回，做一回你们这帮大老爷中的一个！但愿我能顶替你二十四小时，拉乌尔·德·沃帕丰先生（Monsieur Raoul de Vauparfond），不再光是鞋匠蒂博一个！好叫我也尝尝骑一匹骏马的滋味，不再用两条腿在这道上颠；好叫我也拿鞭子抽一抽路上碰见的庄稼汉；也好叫我有机会向那些背着丈夫偷汉子的标致娘儿们献献殷勤——就像蒙-戈贝尔伯爵夫人那样！”

话音未落，男爵的马一个急闪，把骑马的人从马头上方甩了出去。

## V The Pact with Satan / V 与撒旦的契约

===Original===
Thibault stopped short on the threshold, overcome with astonishment at this re-apparition. “I was saying,” began the wolf, as if nothing had happened to interrupt the conversation, “that it is out of my power to grant you the accomplishment of all the wishes you may have in future for your own comfort and advancement.”

“Then I am to expect nothing from you?”

“Not so, for the ill you wish your neighbour can be carried out with my help.”

“And, pray, what good would that do me personally?”

“You fool! has not a moralist said, ‘There is always something sweet to us in the misfortune of our friends﻿—even the dearest.’ ”

“Was it a wolf said that? I did not know wolves could boast of moralists among their number.”

“No, it was not a wolf, it was a man.”

“And was the man hanged?”

“On the contrary, he was made Governor of part of Poitou; there are, to be sure, a good many wolves in that province﻿—well then, if there is something pleasant in the misfortune of our best friend, cannot you understand what a subject of rejoicing the misfortune of our worst enemy must be!”

“There is some truth in that, certainly,” said Thibault.

“Without taking into consideration that there is always an opportunity of profiting by our neighbour’s calamity, whether he be friend or foe.”

===Chinese===
蒂博在门槛上猛地收住脚步，被这再度出现的一幕惊得呆住了。“我方才正说到，”狼开口道，仿佛什么都不曾打断过他们的谈话似的，“我无力成全你往后为自身安逸与飞黄腾达而生的种种愿望。”

“这么说，我指望从你这儿得不到什么了？”

“那倒不然，因为你盼着降到你邻人身上的祸事，倒是可以借我的力量来促成。”

“可这于我个人又有什么好处呢，请问？”

“你这蠢材！不是有位道德家说过么：‘朋友——哪怕是最要好的朋友——遭了不幸，于我们总有一点甘甜的滋味。’”

“这是一头狼说的吗？我倒不知狼群里也拿得出道德家来夸口。”

“不，这不是狼说的，这是人说的。”

“那人给吊死了没有？”

“正相反，他倒被派去做了一任普瓦图（Poitou）的地方总督；说实在的，那一省的狼着实不少——那么，好朋友遭了不幸尚且有几分快意，最恨的仇人遭了不幸，岂不是一桩值得额手称庆的大喜事！”

“这话倒也有些道理。”蒂博道。

“更不必说，无论那遭灾的邻人是友是敌，我们总能从中找到个把渔利的良机。”

===Original===
Thibault paused for a minute or two to consider before he answered:

“By my faith, you are right there, friend wolf, and suppose, then, you do me this service, what shall you expect in exchange? I suppose it will have to be a case of give and take, eh?”

“Certainly. Every time that you express a wish that is not to your own immediate advantage, you will have to repay me with a small portion of your person.”

Thibault drew back with an exclamation of fear.

“Oh! do not be alarmed! I shall not demand a pound of flesh, as a certain Jew of my acquaintance did from his debtor.”

“What is it then you ask of me?”

“For the fulfilment of your first wish, one of your hairs; two hairs for the second wish, four for the third, and so on, doubling the number each time.”

Thibault broke into a laugh: “If that is all you require, Master Wolf, I accept on the spot; and I shall try to start with such a comprehensive wish, that I shall never need to wear a wig. So let it be agreed between us!” and Thibault held out his hand. The black wolf lifted his paw, but he kept it raised.

===Chinese===
蒂博沉吟了一两分钟，方才答道：

“凭我的信仰说，狼朋友，这话你算说对了。那么，就请你替我办这件事，你想要什么作回报呢？我琢磨着，这总得有来有往才成，是吧？”

“自然。每当你发一个并非为你自身直接利益的愿望，你便得用你身上的一小部分来偿还我。”

蒂博倒退一步，失声惊呼起来。

“噢！不必惊慌！我可不会像我所认识的一位犹太人那样，向他的债主讨一磅肉。”

“那你向我要的是什么呢？”

“成全你第一个愿望，要你一根头发；第二个愿望两根；第三个四根；依此类推，每回数目翻倍。”

蒂博哈哈大笑起来：“狼老爷，若是你所要的不过如此，我当场应承下来便是；我且要力争头一个愿望许得面面俱到，好叫我往后一辈子都不必戴假发。咱们就这么说定了！”说罢蒂博伸出手去。那黑狼也抬起一只前爪，却悬着不落下来。

===Original===
“Well?” said Thibault.

“I was only thinking,” replied the wolf, “that I have rather sharp claws, and, without wishing to do so, I might hurt you badly; but I see a way whereby to clinch the bargain without any damage done to you. You have a silver ring, I have a gold one; let us exchange; the barter will be to your advantage, as you see.” And the wolf held out its paw, Thibault saw a ring of the purest gold shining under the fur of what corresponded to the ring finger, and accepted the bargain without hesitation; the respective rings then changed ownership.

“Good!” said the wolf, “now we two are married.”

“You mean betrothed, Master Wolf,” put in Thibault. “Plague upon you! you go too fast.”

“We shall see about that, Master Thibault. And now you go back to your work, and I’ll go back to mine.”

“Goodbye, my lord Wolf.”

“Till we meet again, Master Thibault.”

The wolf had hardly uttered these last words, on which it had laid an unmistakeable emphasis, ere it disappeared like a pinch of lighted gunpowder, and like the gunpowder, left behind a strong smell of sulphur.

Thibault again stood for a moment dumbfounded. He had not yet grown accustomed to this manner of making one’s exit, to use a theatrical expression; he looked round him on every side, but the wolf was not there.

===Chinese===
“怎么啦？”蒂博问。

“我不过在想，”狼答道，“我的爪子尖得很，怕一个不当心，便把你伤得不轻；可我想出一个法子来，既敲定了这笔买卖，又不叫你受半点损伤。你有一枚银戒指，我有一枚金的；咱们交换；这交易于你是有利的，你也看得出来。”说罢狼伸出了前爪，蒂博只见在相当于无名指的那处皮毛底下，熠熠生辉地闪着一枚成色最纯的金戒指，便毫不犹豫地应承了这交易；于是各自的戒指便易了主。

“好！”狼说道，“这下咱俩可算是成了亲了。”

“狼老爷，您该说是订了亲才是。”蒂博插嘴道。“呸！你也太性急了。”

“这事咱们走着瞧，蒂博先生。这会儿你回去做你的活，我也回去做我的。”

“再会了，狼老爷。”

“后会有期，蒂博先生。”

狼末后这句话说得再明白不过地加重了语气，话音未落，它便像一撮点着的火药似的“噗”地一下消失了，又跟火药一样，留下了一股刺鼻的硫磺味。

蒂博又呆立了片刻。他还没习惯这种——套一句戏班子的话——退场的手法；他四下里张望了一遍，那狼却已无影无踪。

===Original===
At first he thought the whole thing must have been a dream, but, looking down, he saw the devil’s ring on the third finger of his right hand; he drew it off and examined it. He saw a monogram engraved on the inner side, and looking more closely, perceived that it was formed of two letters, T. and S.

“Ah!” he exclaimed, in a cold sweat, “Thibault and Satan, the family names of the two contracting parties. So much the worse for me! but when one gives oneself to the devil, one has to do it without reserve.”

And Thibault began humming a song, trying to drown his thoughts, but his voice filled him with fear, for there was a new and curious sound in it, even to his own ears. So he fell silent, and went back to his work as a distraction.

He had only just begun, however, to shape his wooden shoe, when, some distance off, from the direction of Baisemont, he again heard the baying of the hounds, and the notes of the Baron’s horn. Thibault left off working to listen to these various sounds.

===Chinese===
起先他还道这整件事不过是场梦，可一低头，却见右手中指上套着那枚魔鬼的戒指；他把它褪下来，细细端详。只见戒指内侧刻着一个花押，凑近了细看，认出是由两个字母拼成的——T. 与 S.

“啊！”他冷汗直冒地叫道，“蒂博（Thibault）与撒旦（Satan），立约双方的姓氏缩写。这下我可倒了大霉啦！不过，人既已把自己交给了魔鬼，便不得不交得彻彻底底、毫无保留。”

蒂博便哼起一支小调来，想把心头的念头压下去，可他自己的嗓音倒叫他害怕起来——因为那声音里头添了一种古怪的新音色，连他自己听着都觉着异样。于是他不作声了，回去做活计分心。

然而他刚开始给一只木鞋定形，便听得远处贝斯蒙（Baisemont）那边又传来猎犬的吠声和男爵（the Baron）的号角声。蒂博停下手来，侧耳细听这一片声响。

===Original===
“Ah, my fine Lord, you may chase your wolf as long as you like; but I can tell you, you won’t get this one’s paw to nail up over the door of your castle. What a lucky beggar I am! here am I, almost as good as a magician, and while you ride on, suspecting nothing, my brave dispenser of blows, I have but to say the word, and a spell will be cast over you whereby I shall be amply avenged.” And in thinking thus, Thibault suddenly paused.

“And, after all,” he went on, “why shouldn’t I revenge myself on this damned Baron and Master Marcotte? Pshaw! with only a hair at stake I may well gratify myself on this score.” And so saying Thibault passed his hand through the thick, silky hair which covered his head like a lion’s mane.

“I shall have plenty of hairs left to lose,” he continued. “Why bother about one! And, besides, it will be an opportunity for seeing whether my friend the devil has been playing false with me or not. Very well then, I wish a serious accident to befall the Baron, and as for that good-for-nothing of a Marcotte, who laid on to me so roughly yesterday, it is only fair that something as bad again should happen to him.”

===Chinese===
“哈，我的好老爷，你爱追你的狼追多久便追多久吧；可我告诉你，这一只的爪子，你休想钉到你城堡门楣上去当战利品。我这人可真是走了大运！我眼下几乎跟个魔法师差不多了，你只管策马往前赶，一点儿风声也没察觉，我这位好挥鞭子的好汉——我只消一句话，一道咒语便落到你头上，叫你吃不了兜着走，好生替我出了这口恶气。”正想到这里，蒂博忽然顿了一顿。

“再说，”他接着想道，“我怎就不该向这该死的男爵和马尔科特（Marcotte）师傅报仇雪恨呢？呸！不过押上一根头发罢了，这一口恶气我尽可以痛痛快快地出了。”蒂博一边说，一边把手指插进那一头浓密、丝一般柔滑、如狮鬃般披满头颅的头发里。

“我掉的头发还多着呢，”他接着说道，“何必吝惜这一根！况且，这也正好试一试我那位魔鬼朋友到底有没有拿假话诳我。很好，那么，我愿男爵遭一桩大祸；至于昨天那般狠狠整治我的那个无赖马尔科特，也理当叫他遭一桩同样倒霉的事才公平。”

===Original===
While expressing this double wish, Thibault felt anxious and agitated to the last degree; for in spite of what he had already seen of the wolf’s power, he still feared the Devil might only have been playing on his credulity. After uttering his wish, he tried in vain to return to his work, he took hold of his parer, wrong side up, and took the skin off his fingers, and still going on with his paring he spoilt a pair of shoes worth a good twelve sous. As he was lamenting over this misfortune, and wiping the blood off his hand, he heard a great commotion in the direction of the valley; he ran into the Chrétiennelle road and saw a number of men walking slowly two and two in his direction. These men were the prickers and kennelmen of the Lord of Vez. The road they were traversing was about two miles long, so that it was some time before Thibault could distinguish what the men were doing, who were walking as slowly and solemnly as if forming part of a funeral procession. When, however, they got to within five hundred paces of him, he saw that they were carrying two rough litters, on which were stretched two lifeless bodies, those of the Baron and of Marcotte. A cold sweat broke out over Thibault’s forehead. “Ah!” he exclaimed, “What do I see here?”

===Chinese===
发这双料的愿望时，蒂博心里又焦灼又激动，简直到了无以复加的地步；因为尽管他已亲眼见过那狼的神通，他仍旧怕魔鬼不过是借他的轻信来耍弄他一番。愿望既出，他想接着做活却怎么也做不下去——他把削刀拿反了面，反倒把指头上的皮削去了一层；他仍旧一刀一刀削下去，又糟蹋了一双足值十二个苏（sous）的鞋。他正对着这桩损失长吁短叹、一面擦手上的血，忽听得山谷那边传来一阵大骚动；他奔到克雷蒂埃内尔（Chrétiennelle）的大路上，只见一伙人两个两个地排着，正缓缓朝他这边走来。这些人正是韦兹领主（the Lord of Vez）手下的猎犬长和管犬役夫们。他们走的那条路约莫有两英里长，因此好一会儿之后，蒂博才辨得清这些人到底在做什么——他们走得又慢又肃穆，活像是一支送殡的队伍。然而，待他们走到离他不出五百步的地方，他便瞧见他们抬着两副粗陋的担架，架上直挺挺躺着两具没了气息的躯体——正是男爵和马尔科特。蒂博额上登时沁出一层冷汗。“啊！”他失声叫道，“我瞧见的是什么！”

===Original===
What had happened was this:

Thibault’s expedient for putting the dogs on the wrong scent had succeeded, and all had gone well as long as the buck remained in covert; but it doubled, when near Marolle, and while crossing the heath passed within ten paces of the Baron. The latter thought at first that the animal had been startled by hearing the hounds, and was trying to hide itself.

But at that moment, not more than a hundred paces behind him, the whole pack of hounds appeared, forty dogs, running, yelping, yelling, crying, some in a deep bass like great cathedral bells, others with the full sound of a gong, and again others in a falsetto key, like clarinettes out of tune, all giving cry at the top of their voices, as eagerly and merrily as if they had never followed the scent of any other beast.

===Chinese===
事情的经过是这样的：

蒂博把猎犬引上歧路的那点子计谋居然奏了效，只要那头雄鹿还藏身在树丛里，一切便都顺顺当当；可它在马罗勒（Marolle）附近折了个弯，穿过荒原时，竟打男爵跟前不出十步的地方跑了过去。男爵起初还道那畜生是听得猎犬吠声受了惊，正想找个地方藏身。

可就在那一刻，他身后不出一百步的地方，整群猎犬冒了出来——四十条狗，跑着、吠着、嚷着、叫着，有的嗓音低沉如大教堂的钟鸣，有的鼓荡如铜锣轰响，又有的扯着假声高亢，像走了调的 clarinet（单簧管）——一条条都在拼命扯开嗓子叫，那股子又急又欢的劲头，仿佛这辈子还没追过别的畜生的气味似的。

===Original===
Then the Baron gave way to one of his wild fits of rage, fits only worthy of Polichinello tearing a passion to tatters in a puppet-show. He did not shout, he yelled; he did not swear, he cursed. Not satisfied with lashing his dogs, he rode them down, trampling them beneath his horse’s hoofs, flinging himself about in his saddle like a devil in a stoup of holy water.

All his maledictions were hurled at his chief pricker, whom he held responsible for the stupid blunder that had occurred. This time Marcotte had not a word to say either in explanation or excuse, and the poor man was terribly ashamed of the mistake his hounds had made, and mighty uneasy at the towering passion into which it had thrown my lord. He made up his mind therefore to do everything in the power of man, if possible more, to repair the one and calm the other, and so started off at full gallop, dashing among the trees and over the brushwood, crying out at the top of his voice, while he slashed right and left with such vigour, that every stroke of his whip cut into the flesh of the poor animals. “Back, dogs! back!” But in vain he rode, and whipped, and called aloud, the dogs only seemed to become more wildly anxious to follow up the new found scent, as if they recognised the buck of the day before, and were determined that their wounded self-esteem should have its revenge. Then Marcotte grew desperate, and determined on the only course that seemed left. The river Ourcq was close by, the dogs were already on the point of crossing the water, and the one chance of breaking up the pack was to get across himself and whip back the dogs as they began to climb the opposite bank. He spurred his horse in the direction of the river, and leaped with it into the very middle of the stream, both horse and rider arriving safely in the water; but, unfortunately, as we have already mentioned, the river just at this time was terribly swollen with the rains, the horse was unable to stand against the violence of the current, and after being swept round two or three times finally disappeared. Seeing that it was useless trying to save his horse, Marcotte endeavoured to disengage himself, but his feet were so firmly fixed in the stirrups that he could not draw them out, and three seconds after his horse had disappeared, Marcotte himself was no longer to be seen.

===Chinese===
于是男爵又发作起他那阵狂风骤雨般的暴怒来——这种种发作，只配得上木偶戏里那个把一腔子激情撕成碎片的丑角波利希内尔（Polichinello）。他不是喊，是吼；他不是骂娘，是诅咒。他鞭子抽狗还不解气，索性纵马践踏，把它们踩在马蹄底下，自己在马鞍上折腾得像个掉进圣水钵里的魔鬼。

他这一通诅咒全劈头盖脸地落到他那猎犬长头上，因为这一桩蠢事的干系，他认定要这位猎犬长来担。这回马尔科特要解释也罢、要分辩也罢，一个字都说不出来，可怜人手下的猎犬闹出这般差错，他自己也羞愧得无地自容，更兼眼见老爷发了这么一场雷霆之怒，心里惶恐得很。于是他打定主意，但凡人力之所及——若是做得到，还要再过分些——也要弥补这一桩、平息那一桩。他便纵马全速飞驰，在树木间、在灌木丛中横冲直撞，一边扯开嗓子高喊，一边左右开弓地挥鞭，下手之狠，每一鞭子都抽进了那些可怜畜生的皮肉里。“回来，狗！回来！”可任凭他策马、抽鞭、高声呼唤，统统无济于事，那些狗反倒越发撒了欢地要追这条新嗅上的气味，仿佛认出了这就是昨天那头鹿，铁了心要为它们受了伤的自尊心雪耻复仇。马尔科特便急红了眼，决心走那似乎是仅剩的一条路。乌尔克河（the Ourcq）就在近旁，猎犬们眼看便要渡水，拆散这一群狗的唯一指望，便是抢在它们之前先渡过河去，等它们往对岸爬时再挥鞭把它们截回来。他一夹马刺朝河边冲去，连人带马一纵身跃进河心，人与马都平安落到了水里；可不巧——我们前头已经说过——这阵子连日大雨，河水暴涨，水流凶猛，那马抵不住激流的冲撞，被水流打着旋儿卷了两三圈，终于没顶不见了。马尔科特见救马无望，便想挣脱身子，可他的两脚死死卡在马镫里抽不出来，那马没顶之后不过三秒，马尔科特自己也跟着不见了踪影。

===Original===
