Thibault, hearing of the hopeless condition in which Agnelette was, had not been able to resist his longing to see her once again, and at the risk of what might happen to him, he had passed through the village, trusting to the rapidity of his movements, had opened the door of the hut and rushed in to see the dying woman.

===Chinese===
从维莱-科特雷（Villers-Cotterets）和费尔泰-米隆（Ferté-Milon）请来的医生都无济于事，阿涅莱特的病势越来越沉重；她的气力正飞速地衰竭；过了最初几天之后，她的嗓音愈发微弱，呼吸愈发短促，可那谵狂之态却与先前一般猛烈，种种情形——连医生们那沉默不语的态度也算在内——都叫人相信可怜的阿涅莱特不久便要随她丈夫同赴黄泉了。唯有那瞎眼老太太的嗓音，似乎还有几分退烧定神的效力。她一听见祖母说话，便安静下来，那双睁得大大的、失神枯槁的眼睛也柔和了几分，潸然泪下；她会举手抚一抚额头，仿佛要赶走某个挥之不去的念头，一抹哀伤的、游移不定的微笑便掠过她的唇边。

一天傍晚，将近入夜时分，她的睡态似乎比平日更加烦躁痛苦。小屋里只一盏小铜灯发出微弱的光，半明半暗；祖母坐在炉边，面容纹丝不动——庄户人与野蛮人便是用这副神情掩饰心头最强烈的情感的。阿涅莱特躺在一张床上，消瘦惨白得教人看着，若不是她胸口随着那急促的呼吸一起一伏尚有规律，你简直要当她已是一具死尸了。床脚边跪着一个女人——那是男爵（the Baron）出钱雇来伺候他那年轻猎手遗孀的两个女人中的一个——正一粒一粒地数着念珠祷告；另一个则默不作声地拿着纺锤纺线。蓦地，那病人——几分钟来一阵一阵地打着寒噤——似乎在与什么可怖的梦魇搏斗，发出一声撕心裂肺的惨叫。就在这一刹那，门砰地被撞开了，一个周身仿佛裹在火焰里的汉子冲进屋来，几步蹿到阿涅莱特的床前，将那濒死的女人紧紧搂在怀里，把嘴唇贴上她的额头，发出一阵悲恸的号叫，随即又奔向另一扇通向原野的门，推门而出，一闪便不见了。这一来一去快得几乎像是一场幻觉，而阿涅莱特仿佛还在推开某个看不见的东西，口中喊着：“把他带走！把他带走！”可那两个守夜的女人已瞧见那汉子，认出正是蒂博，屋外也正喧嚷成一片，那一片嘈杂之中，可以辨出“蒂博”这个名字。不多时那喧嚷声越来越近，直逼到阿涅莱特的小屋跟前，那嚷叫的人们不多一会儿便出现在门槛上——他们正是追捕狼之领主而来的。先前有人瞧见蒂博在小屋附近出没，庄户人得了哨兵的报信，便纷纷拿起干草叉和棍棒，预备着追捕他。蒂博听说阿涅莱特已病入膏肓、回天乏术，再也按捺不住再见她一面的渴望，便将自身安危置之度外，仗着脚程飞快，穿村而过，推开了小屋的门，冲进屋来见那濒死的女人。

===Original===
The two women showed the peasants the door by which Thibault had escaped, and like a pack that has recovered the scent they started afresh on his track with renewed cries and threats. Thibault, it need hardly be said, escaped from them and disappeared in the forest.

Agnelette's condition, after the terrible shock given her by Thibault's presence and embrace, became so alarming that before the night was over the priest was sent for; she had evidently now but a few hours longer to live and suffer. Towards midnight the priest arrived, followed by the sacristan carrying the cross, and the choirboys bearing the holy water. These went and knelt at the foot of the bed, while the priest took his place at the head beside Agnelette. And now, some mysterious power seemed to reanimate the dying woman. For a long time she spoke in a low voice with the priest, and as the poor child had no need of long prayers for herself, it was certain that she must be praying for another. And who was that other? God, the priest, and Agnelette alone knew.

===Chinese===
那两个女人指给庄户人看蒂博逃走的那扇门，众人便如一群重新嗅到气味的猎犬，重又吆喝着、威吓着，循着他的踪迹追了下去。蒂博呢，自不待言，早已脱身而去，消失在森林之中了。

阿涅莱特经蒂博现身、又是那一搂所给予的这可怕一击之后，病势变得愈发凶险，还没到天亮，便已派人去请神父了；她眼下显然只剩下几个钟头的活头和苦头了。将近半夜时分，神父到了，后头跟着擎着十字架的教堂司事和捧着圣水的唱诗班男童。这些人进得屋来，跪在床脚边，神父则在床头、阿涅莱特身旁落了座。这时，冥冥之中似有一股神秘的力量，使这濒死的女人重新振奋起来。她低声与神父交谈了很久很久，而这苦命的孩子既然无须为自己念那么多冗长的祷文，那她必定是在为另一个人祈祷了。那另一个人是谁呢？这唯有上帝、神父，以及阿涅莱特自己知道。

## VIII Thibault’s Wishes / VIII 蒂博的愿望

===Original===
The widow, on perceiving the effect which the sight of the soldiers advancing towards the mill had upon Landry, was almost as frightened as the lad himself.

“Ah! dear God!” she cried, “what is the matter, my poor Landry?”

“Say, what is the matter?” asked Thibault in his turn.

“Alas!” replied Landry, “last Thursday, in a moment of despair, meeting the recruiting-sergeant at the Dauphin Inn, I enlisted.”

“In a moment of despair!” exclaimed the mistress of the mill, “and why were you in despair?”

“I was in despair,” said Landry, with a mighty effort, “I was in despair because I love you.”

“And it is because you loved me, unhappy boy! that you enlisted?”

“Did you not say that you would turn me away from the mill?”

“And have I turned you away?” asked Madame Polet, with an expression which it was impossible to misinterpret.

“Ah! God! then you would not really have sent me away?” asked Landry.

“Poor boy!” said the mistress of the mill, with a smile and a pitying movement of the shoulders, which, at any other time, would have made Landry almost die of joy, but, as it was, only doubled his distress.

===Chinese===
那寡妇一瞧见士兵朝磨坊走来在朗德里（Landry）身上引起的那般反应，险些和这小伙子本人一样吓坏了。

“哎呀！好老天！”她叫道，“出什么事了，我可怜的朗德里？”

“是啊，出什么事了？”蒂博也跟着问。

“唉！”朗德里答道，“上礼拜四，我一时间想不开，在多菲内客栈（the Dauphin Inn）碰上那个招兵的中士，便应了募。”

“一时间想不开！”磨坊女主人失声叫道，“你有什么好想不开的？”

“我想不开，”朗德里拼了老大劲才说出口，“我想不开，是因为我爱您。”

“就因为你爱我，你这傻孩子！才去投的军？”

“您不是说过，要把我从磨坊赶走吗？”

“我可真把你赶走了？”波莱太太（Madame Polet）问道，那一副神色，谁也不会看错。

“啊！老天！这么说，您并不是真要赶我走？”朗德里问。

“可怜的孩子！”磨坊女主人说着，微微一笑，又怜惜地耸了耸肩——这一笑一耸肩，搁在别的时候，足叫朗德里欢喜得死过去，可眼下却只叫他的苦楚加倍。

===Original===
“Perhaps even now I might have time to hide,” he said.

“Hide!” said Thibault, “that will be of no use, I can tell you.”

“And why not?” said Madame Polet, “I am going to try, anyhow. Come, dear Landry.”

And she led the young man away, with every mark of the most loving sympathy.

Thibault followed them with his eyes: “It’s going badly for you, Thibault, my friend,” he said; “fortunately, let her hide him as cleverly as she may, they have a good scent, and will find him out.”

In saying this, Thibault was unconscious that he was giving utterance to a fresh wish.

===Chinese===
“说不定这会儿还来得及躲起来，”他说。

“躲！”蒂博说，“我告诉你，那可没用。”

“怎么没用？”波莱太太说，“好歹我要试一试。来，亲爱的朗德里。”

她带着那年轻人走了，一举一动都透着最体贴入微的怜爱。

蒂博目送他们离去：“蒂博啊蒂博，我的朋友，这回你可不妙，”他自言自语道，“好在——随她藏得多么巧妙，他们鼻子灵着呢，定能把他搜出来。”

蒂博说这话时，自己也没察觉，他又发了一个新的愿望。

===Original===
The widow had evidently not hidden Landry very far away, for she returned after a few seconds of absence; the hiding-place was probably all the safer for being near. She had scarcely had time to take breath when the recruiting-sergeant and his companions appeared at the door. Two remained outside, no doubt to catch Landry if he should attempt to escape, the sergeant and the other soldier walked in with the confidence of men who are conscious of acting under authority. The Sergeant cast a searching glance round the room, brought back his right foot into the third position and lifted his hand to the peak of his cap. The mistress of the Mill did not wait for the Sergeant to address her, but with one of her most fascinating smiles, asked him if he would like some refreshment, an offer which no recruiting-sergeant is ever known to refuse. Then, thinking it a favourable moment to put the question, she asked them while they were drinking their wine, what had brought them to Croyolles Mill. The Sergeant replied that he had come in search of a lad, belonging to the Mill, who, after drinking with him to his Majesty’s health and signed his engagement, had not reappeared. The lad in question, interrogated as to his name and dwelling-place, had declared himself to be one Landry, living with Madame Polet, a widow, owner of the Mill at Croyolles. On the strength of this declaration, he had now come to Madame Polet, widow, of Croyolles Mill, to reclaim the defaulter.

The widow, quite convinced that it was permissible to lie for a good cause, assured the Sergeant that she knew nothing of Landry, nor had anyone of that name ever been at the Mill.

===Chinese===
那寡妇显然没把朗德里藏到多远的地方去，因为她离开不过几秒钟便回来了；这藏身之处想必正因为近，反倒更稳妥。她还没来得及喘口气，那招兵中士和他手下的弟兄便出现在门口了。两个留在外头，想必是防备朗德里企图逃脱，中士和另一个士兵则大摇大摆地走了进来，那副神气，是分明仗着公事在身的人才有的笃定。中士把屋里扫视了一遍，把右脚收到第三姿势上，举手到帽檐边行了个礼。磨坊女主人并不等中士开口，便堆起她那最迷人的笑脸，问他要不要进点茶点提提神——这种好意，从无一个招兵中士肯推辞。随后，她趁他们喝酒的当儿，觉得是个问话的好时机，便问他们到克鲁瓦约尔磨坊（Croyolles Mill）来有何贵干。中士答道，他是来找磨坊里的一个小伙子的，那人跟他一同为国王陛下的健康干过杯、又签了志愿书，过后便不见了踪影。那小伙子被盘问姓名住址时，自称朗德里，与克鲁瓦约尔磨坊的东家——寡妇波莱太太同住。既有这一番陈词，他如今便来找克鲁瓦约尔磨坊的寡妇波莱太太，索回那个临阵脱逃的新兵。

那寡妇素来信奉为善事撒谎不为过的道理，便一口咬定她对朗德里其人一无所知，磨坊里也从来没有这么一号人物。

===Original===
The Sergeant in reply said that Madame had the finest eyes and the most charming mouth in the world, but that was no reason why he should implicitly believe the glances of the one or the words of the other. He was bound, therefore, he continued, “to ask the fair widow to allow him to search the Mill.”

The search was begun, in about five minutes the Sergeant came back into the room and asked Madam Polet for the key of her room. The widow appeared very much surprised and shocked at such a request, but the Sergeant was so persistent and determined that at last she was forced to give up the key. A minute or two later, and the Sergeant walked in again, dragging Landry in after him by the collar of his coat. When the widow saw them both enter, she turned deadly pale. As for Thibault, his heart beat so violently, that he thought it would burst, for without the black wolf’s assistance, he was sure the Sergeant would never have gone to look for Landry where he had found him.

===Chinese===
中士回话道，太太生着一双天下最美的眼睛、一张天下最迷人的嘴，可这并不等于他就该全信那秋波一转，或那樱唇一启所说的话。因此之故，他接着说道，“不得不请这位俏寡妇准许他搜一搜磨坊。”

搜查开始了，约莫五分钟工夫，中士回到屋里，向波莱太太讨她卧房的钥匙。寡妇听了这要求，一脸的又惊又气，可中士死缠硬磨、志在必得，她到底拗不过，只好交出了钥匙。过了一两分钟，中士又走了进来，一手拽着朗德里的衣领，把他拖在身后。寡妇一见两人一齐进来，脸刷地白得吓人。至于蒂博，他的心猛烈地撞着胸口，几乎要炸开——因为他心知肚明：若没有那黑狼相助，中士万万不会到方才找见朗德里的那个地方去寻他。

===Original===
“Ah! ah! my good fellow!” cried the Sergeant in a mocking voice, “so we prefer the service of beauty to the King’s service? That is easy to understand; but when one has the good fortune to be born in his Majesty’s domains and to have drunk his health, one has to give him a share of service, when his turn comes. So you must come along with us, my fine fellow, and after a few years in the King’s uniform, you can come back and serve under your old flag. So, now then, march!”

“But,” cried the widow, “Landry is not yet twenty, and you have not the right to take him under twenty.”

“She is right,” added Landry, “I am not twenty yet.”

“And when will you be twenty?”

“Not until tomorrow.”

“Good,” said the Sergeant, “we will put you tonight on a bed of straw, like a medlar, and by tomorrow, at daybreak, when we wake you up, you will be ripe.”

===Chinese===
“哈！哈！我的好伙计！”中士拿嘲弄的口气叫道，“这么说，咱们宁可在美人麾下效劳，也不愿给国王当差了？这也容易明白；可一个人既有福生在国王陛下的疆土之内，又饮过为陛下健康而祝的酒，那么轮到陛下要人效力的时候，便得为陛下出一分力。所以你只好跟我们走一遭了，我的好老兄，穿上几年国王的号衣，回头你再回来在你那面旧旗帜下当差就是。那么，这就开步走吧！”

“可是，”寡妇叫道，“朗德里还不满二十岁，他没满二十你们无权带走。”

“她说得对，”朗德里也说，“我还没满二十呢。”

“那你什么时候满二十？”

“得等到明天。”

“好，”中士说，“今儿夜里我们便把你搁在一床稻草上，活像一只楂子，等到明天天一亮，我们把你叫醒，你也就熟透啦。”

===Original===
Landry wept. The widow prayed, pleaded, implored, allowed herself to be kissed by the soldiers, patiently endured the coarse pleasantry excited by her sorrow, and at last offered a hundred crowns to buy him off. But all was of no avail. Landry’s wrists were bound, and then one of the soldiers taking hold of the end of the cord, the party started off, but not before the lad of the mill had found time to assure his dear mistress, that far or near, he would always love her, and that, if he died, her name would be the last upon his lips. The beautiful widow, on her side, had lost all thought of the world’s opinion in face of this great catastrophe, and before he was led away, she clasped Landry to her heart in a tender embrace.

When the little party had disappeared behind the willows, and she lost sight of them, the widow’s distress became so overpowering that she became insensible, and had to be carried and laid on her bed. Thibault lavished upon her the most devoted attention. He was somewhat taken aback at the strong feeling of affection which the widow evinced for his cousin; however, as this only made him applaud himself the more for having cut at the root of the evil, he still cherished the most sanguine hopes.

===Chinese===
朗德里哭了。寡妇苦苦哀求、百般恳请、低声下气地求情，任凭那些士兵亲她，耐着性子受下他们因她悲伤而起的粗俗调侃，末了又拿出一百埃居要把他赎出来。可这一切全然无用。朗德里的手腕被捆上了，一个士兵牵着绳头，一行人便要动身，好在临走之前，磨坊的这个小伙子还来得及向他亲爱的女主人保证：无论天涯海角，他永远爱她，万一死在外头，她名字也将是他临终前最后念叨的。那美丽的寡妇呢，在这场大祸面前早已顾不得旁人会说长道短，在朗德里被带走之前，她把他紧紧搂在怀里，情意绵绵地抱了又抱。

待那一小队人马消失在柳树丛后，再也望不见了，寡妇的悲痛排山倒海般压来，她当下便昏死过去，只得被人抱起来抬到床上躺着。蒂博对她百般殷勤、悉心照应。寡妇对他表弟所流露出的那一片深情，倒叫他有些始料未及；不过，他转念一想，这反倒愈发叫他庆幸自己已把这祸患连根铲除了，于是仍抱着最乐观的指望。

===Original===
On coming to herself, the first name the widow uttered was that of Landry, to which Thibault replied with a hypocritical gesture of commiseration. Then the mistress of the mill began to sob. “Poor lad!” she cried, while the hot tears flowed down her cheeks, “what will become of him, so weak and delicate as he is? The mere weight of his gun and knapsack will kill him!”

Then turning to her guest, she continued:

“Ah! Monsieur Thibault, this is a terrible trouble to me, for you no doubt have perceived that I love him? He was gentle, he was kind, he had no faults; he was not a gambler, nor a drinker; he would never have opposed my wishes, would never have tyrannised over his wife, and that would have seemed very sweet to me after the two cruel years that I lived with the late M. Polet. Ah! Monsieur Thibault, Monsieur Thibault! It is a sad grief indeed for a poor miserable woman to see all her anticipations of future happiness and peace thus suddenly swallowed up!”

===Chinese===
寡妇一醒过来，头一个念出的名字便是朗德里，蒂博便装出一副假惺惺的怜悯模样来应她。随后磨坊女主人便抽抽搭搭地哭了起来。“可怜的孩子！”她叫道，滚烫的泪珠顺着脸颊往下淌，“他那么瘦弱、那么娇嫩，往后的日子可怎么熬啊？光那杆枪、那个背囊的分量，就要了他的命了！”

接着她转过脸来，对她的客人接着说道：

“哎呀！蒂博先生，这桩事可真是叫我痛心极了，您想必也看出来我是爱他的了？他性子温存，心地又好，没一点毛病；他不爱赌钱，也不贪杯；他从不违拗我的心意，将来也绝不会欺压他的妻子——我跟着已故的波莱先生（M. Polet）过了那两年苦日子，如今想想，这对我该是何等甜蜜啊。哎呀！蒂博先生，蒂博先生！一个可怜巴巴的女人，眼睁睁瞧着自己满心指望的今后的幸福安宁，就这么一下子全泡了汤，这着实是天大的悲痛啊！”

===Original===
Thibault thought this would be a good moment to declare himself; whenever he saw a woman crying, he immediately thought, most erroneously, that she only cried because she wished to be consoled.

He decided, however, that he would not be able to attain his object without a certain circumlocution.

“Indeed,” he answered, “I quite understand your sorrow, nay, more than that, I share it with you, for you cannot doubt the affection I bear my cousin. But we must resign ourselves, and without wishing to deny Landry’s good qualities, I would still ask you, Madame, to find someone else who is his equal.”

“His equal!” exclaimed the widow, “there is no such person. Where shall I find so nice and so good a youth? It was a pleasure to me to look at his smooth young face, and with it all, he was so self-composed, so steady in his habits! He was working night and day, and yet I could with a glance make him shrink away and hide. No, no, Monsieur Thibault, I tell you frankly, the remembrance of him will prevent me ever wishing to look at another man, and I know that I must resign myself to remaining a widow for the rest of my life.”

===Chinese===
蒂博心想这倒是个表白的好时机；每逢他瞧见一个女人哭泣，便想当然地——其实大错特错——以为她哭，不过是盼着有人来宽慰她罢了。

不过他转念又想，要达到目的，总得拐弯抹角地绕上几个圈子才成。

“可不，”他答道，“您的伤心我太明白了，何止明白，我还与您一同伤心呢，因为我对我那表弟的心意，您总不会怀疑的。可咱们到底得认命，虽说我无意贬低朗德里的好处，可我还是要劝您一句，太太，另寻一个与他不相上下的人罢。”

“与他不相上下！”寡妇叫道，“哪有这样的人。我上哪儿再找这么个好性儿、这么个好品行的少年郎去？我瞧着他那张光溜溜的娃娃脸就满心欢喜，况且他又是那么稳得住神、那么守规矩！他起早贪黑地做活，可我只消一个眼色，便叫他讪讪地缩回去躲起来。不，不，蒂博先生，我跟您掏心窝子说吧，单是记着他这个人，就够叫我再不愿瞧别的男人一眼了，我也认了命，下半辈子就这么当寡妇了。”

===Original===
“Phew!” said Thibault; “but Landry was very young!”

“There is no disadvantage in that,” replied the widow.

“But who knows if he would always have retained his good qualities. Take my advice, Madame, do not grieve any more, but, as I say, look out for someone who will make you forget him. What you really need is not a baby-face like that, but a grown man, possessing all the qualities that you admire and regret in Landry, but, at the same time sufficiently mature to prevent the chance of finding one fine day that all your illusions are dispersed, and that you are left face to face with a libertine and a bully.”

The mistress of the Mill shook her head; but Thibault went on:

“In short, what you need, is a man who while earning your respect, will, at the same time make the Mill work profitably. You have but to say the word, and you would not have to wait long before you found yourself well provided for, my fair Madame, a good bit better than you were just now.”

“And where am I to find this miracle of a man?” asked the widow, as she rose to her feet, looking defiantly at the shoemaker, as if throwing down a challenge. The latter, mistaking the tone in which these last words were said, thought it an excellent occasion to make known his own proposals, and accordingly hastened to profit by it.

===Chinese===
“嗤！”蒂博说，“可朗德里年纪还小着呢！”

“这算不得什么短处，”寡妇答道。

“可谁保得准他这些好处能留得住一辈子呢。听我一句劝，太太，别再伤心了，依我说，不如找个能叫您忘了他的人。您真正需要的，可不是那张娃娃脸，而是一个成年的汉子，他既要有您在朗德里身上所爱所惜的一切品性，同时又足够老成，省得有朝一日您忽然发觉美梦全成空，面前只剩下一个浪荡子、一个恶霸王。”

磨坊女主人摇了摇头；蒂博却接着说下去：

“总而言之，您所要的，是一个既能赢得您敬重、又能把磨坊经营得红红火火的汉子。您只需一句话，您这俊俏的太太，保管不用等多久，便能给自己寻下一个好依靠，比您眼下这光景可要强上百倍。”

“那我上哪儿去找这样一位奇男子呢？”寡妇问道，一面站起身来，挑衅似的瞪着鞋匠，像是摔下一只手套。后者却会错了这末了几句话的语气，只当这是个亮出自己心思的绝佳时机，便赶忙抓住机会。

===Original===
“Well, I confess,” he answered, “that when I said that a handsome widow like you would not have to go far before finding the man who would be just the very husband for her, I was thinking of myself, for I should reckon myself fortunate, and should feel proud, to call myself your husband. Ah! I assure you,” he went on, while the mistress of the Mill stood looking at him with ever-increasing displeasure in her eyes, “I assure you that with me you would have no occasion to fear any opposition to your wishes: I am a perfect lamb in the way of gentleness, and I should have but one law and one desire, my law would be to obey you, my desire to please you! and as to your fortune, I have means of adding to it which I will make known to you later on.﻿﻿ ﻿…”

But the end of Thibault’s sentence remained unspoken.

“What!” cried the widow, whose fury was the greater for having been kept in check until then, “What! you, whom I thought my friend, you dare to speak of replacing him in my heart! you try to dissuade me from keeping my faith to your cousin. Get out of the place, you worthless scoundrel! out of the place, I say! or I will not answer for the consequences; I have a good mind to get four of my men to collar you and throw you under the Mill-wheel.”

===Chinese===
“这个嘛，我招认，”他答道，“方才我说像您这般标致的寡妇，要找一个正配做您丈夫的人，不必往远处寻——那当口我心里想的正是我自己，因为能当上您的丈夫，我会觉得自己是天底下最有福气的人，是天底下最得意的人。啊！我向您保证，”他接着说，磨坊女主人却站着不动，眼里那厌恶之色越来越浓，“我向您保证，跟了我，您想怎样便怎样，绝无半分掣肘：论性情，我是再温顺不过的一只羔羊，我只有一条规矩、一个心愿，我的规矩便是听您的话，我的心愿便是讨您欢心！至于您的家产嘛，我自有法子叫它添上一笔，这法子我日后再同您细说。……”

可蒂博这下半截话，到底没能说出口。

“什么！”寡妇叫道，她那一腔怒火只因强压到此刻才发作，便愈发不可收拾，“什么！我原当你是我朋友，你竟敢妄想取他而代之，占我心中的位置！你竟敢挑唆我背叛你那表弟！给我滚，你这个下作无赖！滚出去，我说！不然我可保不住会做出什么事来；我真恨不得叫上我四个伙计，揪住你的脖子，把你扔到磨坊的车轮底下去。”

===Original===
Thibault was anxious to make some sort of response, but, although ready with an answer on ordinary occasions, he could not for the moment think of a single word whereby to justify himself. True, Madame Polet, gave him no time to think, but seizing hold of a beautiful new jug that stood near her, she flung it at Thibault’s head. Luckily for him, Thibault dodged to the left and escaped the missile, which flew past him, crashing to pieces against the chimneypiece. Then the mistress of the house took up a stool, and aimed it at him with equal violence; this time Thibault dodged to the right, and the stool went against the window, smashing two or three panes of glass. At the sound of the falling glass, all the youths and maids of the Mill came running up. They found their mistress flinging bottles, water-jugs, saltcellars, plates, everything in short that came to hand, with all her might at Thibault’s head. Fortunately for him widow Polet was too much incensed to be able to speak; if she had been able to do so, she would have called out; “Kill him! Strangle him! Kill the rascal! the scoundrel! the villain!”

===Chinese===
蒂博急着要分辩几句，可他平日里虽能言善辩，这会儿竟一时想不出半句话来替自己开脱。好在波莱太太也不容他多想，她一把抄起手边一只崭新漂亮的瓦罐，便朝蒂博的脑袋砸了过来。蒂博侥幸往左边一闪，躲开了这件暗器，瓦罐擦着他飞了过去，在壁炉架上摔了个粉碎。紧接着这家女主又抄起一张板凳，使着同样的狠劲儿朝他砸来；这一回蒂博往右边一闪，板凳直奔窗户而去，砸碎了两三块玻璃。听得玻璃落地的声响，磨坊里那些长工小伙子和使女丫头全都跑了过来。他们只见自家女主人正把手边够得着的家什——酒瓶、水罐、盐罐、盘子，总之是抓到什么算什么——一股脑儿朝蒂博的头上砸去，用尽了吃奶的力气。也算蒂博走运，波莱太太气得连话都说不出来了；她若是还说得出口，必定要喊：“打死他！掐死他！打死这个泼皮！这恶棍！这无赖！”

===Original===
On seeing the reinforcements arriving to help the widow, Thibault endeavoured to escape by the door that had been left open by the recruiting party, but just as he was running out, the good pig, that we saw taking its siesta in the sun, being roused out of its first sleep by all this hullabaloo, and thinking the farm people were after it, made a dash for its stye, and in so doing charged right against Thibault’s legs. The latter lost his balance, and went rolling over and over for a good ten paces in the dirt and slush. “Devil take you, you beast!” cried the shoemaker, bruised by his fall, but even more furious at seeing his new clothes covered with mud. The wish was hardly out of his mouth, when the pig was suddenly taken with a fit of frenzy, and began rushing about the farmyard like a mad animal, breaking, shattering, and turning over everything that came in its way. The farm hands, who had run to their mistress on hearing her cries, thought the pig’s behaviour was the cause of them﻿—and started off in pursuit of the animal. But it eluded all their attempts to seize hold of it, knocking over boys and girls, as it had knocked Thibault over, until, at last, coming to where the mill was separated from the sluice by a wooden partition, it crashed through the latter as easily as if it were made of paper, threw itself under the mill wheel﻿ ﻿… and disappeared as if sucked down by a whirlpool. The mistress of the mill had by this time recovered her speech. “Lay hold of Thibault!” she cried, for she had heard Thibault’s curse, and had been amazed and horrified at the instantaneous way in which it had worked. “Lay hold of him! knock him down! he is a wizard, a sorcerer! a werewolf!”﻿—applying to Thibault with this last word, one of the most terrible epithets that can be given to a man in our forest lands. Thibault, who scarcely knew where he was, seeing the momentary stupefaction which took possession of the farm people on hearing their mistress’s final invective, made use of the opportunity to dash past them, and while one went to get a pitchfork and another a spade, he darted through the farmyard gate, and began running up an almost perpendicular hillside at full speed, with an ease which only confirmed Madame Polet’s suspicions, for the hill had always hitherto been looked upon as absolutely inaccessible, at any rate by the way Thibault had chosen to climb it.

“What!” she cried, “what! you give in like that! you should make after him, and seize hold of him, and knock him down!” But the farm servants shook their heads.

“Ah! Madame!” they said, “what is the use, what can we do against a werewolf?”

===Chinese===
蒂博一见寡妇那边来了帮手，便想从招兵那一队人马走时留下的那扇敞着的门溜之大吉，可他正往外冲的当儿，那头好猪——就是咱们瞧见它在日头底下打盹的那一头——被这一阵闹腾从第一觉里惊醒，只当是磨坊的人要来捉它，撒腿便往猪圈里奔，这一奔不打紧，正撞在蒂博两条腿上。蒂博一个失脚，在泥泞里骨碌碌地直滚出去足有十来步远。“魔鬼带你走吧，你这畜生！”鞋匠叫道，他摔得浑身生疼，可更叫他火冒三丈的，是瞧见自己那身新衣裳上糊满了烂泥。这话才出口，那头猪便忽然像疯了一般，在磨坊的院子里横冲直撞起来，活像一头疯兽，凡是挡在它路上的，无不撞的撞、碎的碎、掀的掀。那些长工原本是听见女主人的叫声才跑来的，还以为她的叫声正是这猪闹的——便纷纷追起这畜生来。可那猪东躲西闪，他们连它的边都沾不上，它把那些男男女女撞翻在地，就像方才撞翻蒂博一样，直撞到最后，到了磨坊与水闸之间隔着一道木板壁的地方，它一头撞了上去，那木板壁被它撞穿，轻而易举得仿佛那是纸糊的一般，它纵身一跃，钻到了磨坊的车轮底下……便如同被一个漩涡吸了进去，再无踪影。这时磨坊女主人总算又能开口说话了。“抓住蒂博！”她喊道，原来她听见了蒂博那句诅咒，也为这句咒语立竿见影地应验而既惊且骇。“抓住他！把他打翻在地！他是个巫师，是个妖人！是个狼人！”——她这末了一个词，在我们这林子地带，加诸一个人身上可算是最骇人的恶名了。蒂博自己都不知身在何处，一见那些磨坊的长工听了女主人这最后一句詈骂，一时之间都愣住了，便趁这工夫从他们中间一冲而过，趁着这一个去拿叉、那一个去拿锹的空当，他一头窜出磨坊院子的栅门，撒腿便往一座几乎笔直的山坡上狂奔上去，那身轻脚健，反倒坐实了波莱太太的疑心——因为那座山向来被视为万万攀不上去的，至少从蒂博所选的那条路是断断爬不上的。

“什么！”她叫道，“什么！你们就这么算了！你们该追上去，抓住他，把他打翻在地！”可那些长工们只是摇头。

“哎呀！太太！”他们说，“追又有什么用，我们拿什么对付一个狼人呢？”

## XX True to Tryst / XX 践约

===Original===
On quitting the Countess’s room, Thibault had left the castle by the way which he had described to her, and soon found himself safe beyond its walls and outside the park. And now, for the first time in his life, Thibault had really nowhere to go. His hut was burnt, he was without a friend, and like Cain, he was a wanderer on the face of the earth. He turned to the unfailing shelter of the forest, and there made his way to the lower end of Chavigny; as the day was breaking he came across a solitary house, and asked if he could buy some bread. The woman belonging to it, her husband being away, gave him some, but refused to receive payment for it; his appearance frightened her. Having now food sufficient for the day, Thibault returned to the forest, with the intention of spending his time till evening in a part which he knew between Fleury and Longpont, where the trees were especially thick and tall. As he was looking for a resting place behind a rock, his eye was attracted by a shining object lying at the bottom of a slope, and his curiosity led him to climb down and see what it was. The shining object was the silver badge belonging to a huntsman’s shoulder-belt; the shoulder-belt was slung round the neck of a dead body, or rather of a skeleton, for the flesh had been entirely eaten off the bones, which were as clean as if prepared for an anatomist’s study or a painter’s studio. The skeleton looked as if it had only lain there since the preceding night.

===Chinese===
离开伯爵夫人（the Countess）的房间之后，蒂博便依着他向她描述的那条路出了城堡，不多时便已安然身在城堡围墙与园子之外了。事到如今，蒂博这辈子头一回，当真连个去处都没有了。他那小屋早已烧成灰烬，他又举目无亲，恰似那该隐（Cain）一般，成了在地上流离飘泊的流浪汉。他转向那从不曾辜负他的森林这片避难之所，一路走到沙维尼（Chavigny）那一头的尽头；天将破晓时分，他撞见一所孤零零的人家，便上前问能不能买些面包。那家的妇人——丈夫不在家——给了他一些，却怎么也不肯收钱；只因他这一副模样把她吓着了。有了这一日够吃的口粮，蒂博便折回林子里，打算在弗勒里（Fleury）与龙蓬（Longpont）之间一片他熟稔的地方捱到傍晚——那儿树木格外浓密高大。他正要在一片岩石背后寻个歇脚之处，目光却被一样亮闪闪的物件引了去，那物件卧在一处坡底，他便受好奇心驱使，攀下去要看个究竟。那亮闪闪的物件，原是一枚猎户肩带上挂的银质徽章；那肩带却套在一具尸首——不如说一具骷髅——的颈上，因那上头的皮肉已被啃啮得干干净净，骨骼剔得如同送给解剖家的标本室或画家的画室里去备用一般洁净。瞧那骷髅的样子，仿佛昨夜才刚刚倒在这里。

===Original===
“Ah! ah!” said Thibault, “this is probably the work of my friends, the wolves; they evidently profited by the permission which I gave them.”

Curious to know if possible who the victim was, he examined it more closely; his curiosity was soon satisfied, for the badge, which the wolves had no doubt rejected as less easily digestible than the rest, was lying on the chest of the skeleton, like a ticket on a bale of goods.

> **J. B. Lestocq**,

> Head Keeper to the Comte de Mont-Gobert.

===Chinese===
“啊！啊！”蒂博说，“这大概是我那群狼朋友的杰作罢；它们分明是得了我那一句准许，趁势捞了一票。”

他心生好奇，想知道这遇难者究竟是谁，便凑近前去细看；这好奇心没多久便得了满足，因为那枚徽章——狼群想必嫌它不如其余部分好消化、便丢下了——正躺在骷髅的胸膛上，恰似一包货物上系着的货签一般。

> **J. B. 勒斯托克（Lestocq）**，

> 蒙-戈贝尔伯爵（the Comte de Mont-Gobert）之猎场总管。

===Original===
“Well done!” laughed Thibault, “here is one at least who did not live long to enjoy the result of his murderous act.”

Then, contracting his brow, he muttered to himself, in a low voice, and this time without laughing:
“Is there perhaps, after all, what people call a Providence?”

Lestocq’s death was not difficult to account for. He had probably been executing some order for his master that night, and on the road between Mont-Gobert and Longpont, had been attacked by wolves. He had defended himself with the same knife with which he had wounded the Baron, for Thibault found the knife a few paces off, at a spot where the ground showed traces of a severe struggle; at last, being disarmed, the ferocious beasts had dragged him into the hollow, and there devoured him.

===Chinese===
“干得好！”蒂博笑道，“这一位，至少没活多久去享他那一桩凶行的成果。”

说罢，他却皱起眉头，压低嗓门自言自语起来，这一回却不再带着笑了：
“莫非，到底真有人们所说的那种天意不成？”

勒斯托克的死，倒也不难交代。他那夜想必是奉主人之命在外办事，在蒙-戈贝尔与龙蓬之间的路上，遭到了狼群的袭击。他拿的正是那把伤了男爵的刀来自卫——因为蒂博在几步开外寻见了那把刀，落在一处地皮上显出有过一番剧烈挣扎痕迹的地方；末了，他兵刃脱手，那一群凶兽便将他拖进洼地，在那里把他吞吃了个干净。

===Original===
Thibault was becoming so indifferent to everything that he felt neither pleasure nor regret, neither satisfaction nor remorse, at Lestocq’s death; all he thought was, that it simplified matters for the Countess, as she would now only have her husband upon whom she need revenge herself. Then he went and found a place where the rocks afforded him the best shelter from the wind, and prepared to spend his day there in peace. Towards midday, he heard the horn of the Lord of Vez, and the cry of his hounds; the mighty huntsman was after game, but the chase did not pass near enough to Thibault to disturb him.

===Chinese===
蒂博对万事万物都已麻木到这般地步，对勒斯托克之死，他既不觉得痛快，也不觉得惋惜，既无得意，也无懊悔；他心里想的只是：这样一来，伯爵夫人那边的事倒省了手续，因为往后她要报仇，便只剩丈夫一个对头了。于是他另寻了一处岩石最能挡风的所在，打算在那儿安安静静地度过这一日。将近正午时分，他听见了韦兹领主（the Lord of Vez）的号角声，和他那群猎犬的吠声；那位声势赫赫的猎人正在追逐猎物，可那一场围猎并没近得足以搅扰到蒂博。

===Original===
At last the night came. At nine o’clock Thibault rose and set out for the Castle of Mont-Gobert. He found the breach, followed the path he knew, and came to the little hut where Lisette had been awaiting him on the night when he had come in the guise of Raoul. The poor girl was there this evening, but alarmed and trembling. Thibault wished to carry out the old traditions and tried to kiss her, but she sprang back with visible signs of fear.

“Do not touch me,” she said, “or I shall call out.”

“Oh, indeed! my pretty one,” said Thibault, “you were not so sour-tempered the other day with the Baron Raoul.”

“May be not,” said the girl, “but a great many things have happened since the other day.”

“And many more to happen still,” said Thibault in a lively tone.

“I think,” said the waiting-maid in a mournful voice, “that the climax is already reached.”

===Chinese===
夜终于来了。九点钟，蒂博起身，朝蒙-戈贝尔城堡进发。他寻着了那处围墙缺口，循着他认得的小径走去，到了那间小屋——上一回他乔装成拉乌尔（Raoul）赴约那夜，莉赛特（Lisette）便是在这屋里等他。今儿晚上这可怜的丫头也在，却惊惶万分，浑身发抖。蒂博有心照着老规矩行事，便想凑上去吻她，可她一脸掩饰不住的惧色，猛地往后缩了开去。

“别碰我，”她说，“不然我就喊人了。”

“噢，当真么！我的小美人，”蒂博说，“前儿跟拉乌尔男爵在一块儿的时候，你的脾气可没这么大呀。”

“兴许是没有，”那丫头说，“可自打前儿到现在，出了好多好多事啦。”

“往后还要出更多的事呢。”蒂博用一种轻快的口吻说。

“依我想，”那侍女用一种凄苦的嗓音答道，“顶到头的那一桩，已经出过了。”

===Original===
Then, as she went on in front.
“If you wish to come,” she added, “follow me.”

Thibault followed her; Lisette, without the slightest effort at concealment, walked straight across the open space that lay between the trees and the castle.

“You are courageous today,” said Thibault, “and supposing someone were to see us.…”

“There is no fear now,” she answered, “the eyes that could have seen us are all closed.”

Although he did not understand what the young girl meant by these words, the tone in which they were spoken made Thibault shiver.

===Chinese===
说罢，她便走到前头去。
“您要是愿意来，”她又补了一句，“跟着我走便是。”

蒂博跟着她走去；莉赛特毫无遮掩之意，径直穿过树木与城堡之间那片开阔的空地。

“你今儿倒胆大得很，”蒂博说，“万一有人瞧见咱们呢……”

“这会儿没什么好怕的了，”她答道，“那能瞧见咱们的眼睛，都闭上了。”

蒂博虽不明白这少女这番话是何意，可她说这话时那一种腔调，却叫他打了个寒噤。

===Original===
He continued to follow her in silence as they went up the winding-stairs to the first floor. As Lisette laid her hand on the key of the door, Thibault suddenly stopped her. Something in the silence and solitude of the castle filled him with fear; it seemed as if a curse might have fallen on the place.

“Where are we going?” said Thibault, scarcely knowing himself what he said.

“You know well enough, surely.”

“Into the Countess’s room?”

“Into the Countess’s room.”

“She is waiting for me?”

“She is waiting for you.”

And Lisette opened the door. “Go in,” she said.

Thibault went in, and Lisette shut the door behind him and waited outside.

===Chinese===
他默不作声地跟着她，一路登上那道螺旋楼梯，到了二楼。莉赛特正要把手搁到门钥匙上，蒂博猛地拦住了她。这城堡里那一派死寂与空旷，有某种说不清的东西叫他心底发怵；这地方仿佛已遭了什么诅咒似的。

“咱们这是上哪儿？”蒂博问道，连他自己也几乎不知说的是什么。

“您心里再清楚不过了，这还用问。”

“上伯爵夫人的房里去？”

“上伯爵夫人的房里去。”

“她在等我？”

“她在等您。”

莉赛特便推开了门。“进去罢。”她说。

蒂博走了进去，莉赛特在他身后把门带上，在外头守着。

===Original===
It was the same exquisite room, lighted in the same manner, filled with the same sweet scent. Thibault looked round for the Countess, he expected to see her appear at the dressing-room door, but the door remained closed. Not a sound was to be heard in the room, except the ticking of the Sèvres clock, and the beating of Thibault’s heart. He began to look about him with a feeling of shuddering fear for which he could not account; then his eyes fell on the bed; the Countess was lying asleep upon it. In her hair were the same diamond pins, round her neck the same pearls; she was dressed in the same pink silk dressing-gown, and had on the same little slippers of cloth of silver which she had worn to receive the Baron Raoul.

===Chinese===
还是那一间精雅绝伦的屋子，照着同一副样子点着灯，弥漫着同一股幽香。蒂博四下张望，寻找伯爵夫人，指望着瞧见她从梳妆室的门口现身，可那扇门却始终紧闭着。屋里一点声息也听不见，只除了塞夫勒（Sèvres）瓷钟的滴答声，和蒂博自己心头那扑通扑通的跳。他开始带着一种自己也说不明白的战栗恐惧，四下打量起来；继而他的目光落到了床上；伯爵夫人正躺在床上睡着。她发间插着同样几枚钻石别针，颈上挂着同一挂珍珠；她身上披着那一回接待拉乌尔男爵时所穿的那一袭粉红绸晨衣，脚上趿着同一双银料小拖鞋。

===Original===
Thibault went up to her; the Countess did not stir.

“You are sleeping, fair Countess?” he said, leaning over to look at her.

But all at once, he started upright, staring before him, his hair standing on end, the sweat breaking out on his forehead. The terrible truth was beginning to dawn upon him; was the Countess sleeping the sleep of this world or of eternity?

He fetched a light from the mantelpiece, and with trembling hand, held it to the face of the mysterious sleeper. It was pale as ivory, with the delicate veins traced over the temples, and the lips still red. A drop of pink burning wax fell on this still face of sleep; it did not awake the Countess.

“Ah!” cried Thibault, “what is this?” and he put down the candle, which his shaking hand could no longer hold, on the night-table.

===Chinese===
蒂博走上前去；伯爵夫人一动不动。

“您睡着了吗，美丽的伯爵夫人？”他说着，俯下身去端详她。

可猛然间，他直起身子，两眼直瞪瞪地望着前方，头发根根倒竖，额上沁出一层冷汗。那可怖的真相开始在他心头浮起；伯爵夫人这一觉，睡的是今世的觉，还是永恒的长眠？

他从壁炉台上取过一支烛火，用颤抖的手把它凑近这位神秘睡客的面庞。那张脸白得象象牙，两鬓隐隐透出纤细的青筋，嘴唇却仍旧红润。一滴灼热的粉色烛泪落在这张沉睡不动着的脸上；它没能唤醒伯爵夫人。

“啊！”蒂博叫道，“这是怎么回事？”他那只抖得拿不住的手，便把蜡烛搁到了床头小几上。

===Original===
The Countess lay with her arms stretched out close to her sides; she appeared to be clasping something in either hand. With some effort, Thibault was able to open the left one; within it he found the little bottle, which she had taken from her dressing case the night before. He opened the other hand; within it lay a piece of paper on which were written these few words: “True to tryst,”﻿—yes, true and faithful unto death, for the Countess was dead!

All Thibault’s illusions were fading one after the other, like the dreams of the night which gradually fade away, as the sleeper becomes more and more thoroughly awake. There was a difference, however, for other men find their dead alive again in their dreams; but with Thibault, his dead did not arise and walk, but remained lying forever in their last sleep.

===Chinese===
伯爵夫人直挺挺地躺着，两臂贴着身侧伸得笔直；看那样子，她两只手里仿佛各攥着一件什么东西。蒂博费了好一番气力，才掰开了她的左手；里头赫然是那只小瓶子——那是她头天夜里从化妆匣里取出来的。他又去掰另一只手；里头是一张折着的纸片，上头写着这么寥寥几个字：“践约而来（True to tryst）”——不错，践约而来，至死不渝，因为伯爵夫人已经死了！

蒂博心中种种幻象，正如夜里一场接一场的梦，随着做梦的人越醒越透，也便一个接一个地消散淡去。只是这里头到底有一层不同：别人的梦，能让死去的人在梦里复活如初；可蒂博的死者却不会起身行走，只是永远地躺着，长眠不起。

===Original===
He wiped his forehead, went to the door leading into the corridor, and opened it, to find Lisette on her knees, praying.

“Is the Countess dead then?” asked Thibault.

“The Countess is dead, and the Count is dead.”

“From the effect of the wounds given him by the Baron Raoul?”

“No, from the blow with the dagger given him by the Countess.”

“Ah!” said Thibault, grimacing hideously, in his effort to force a laugh in the midst of this grim drama, “all this tale you hint at is new to me.”

Then Lisette told him the tale in full. It was a plain tale, but a terrible one.

===Chinese===
他抹了一把额头上的汗，走到通往走廊的那扇门前，推开来，只见莉赛特正跪在地上祷告。

“这么说，伯爵夫人死了？”蒂博问。

“伯爵夫人死了，伯爵也死了。”

“是叫拉乌尔男爵所刺的那几处伤要了命的？”

“不是，是伯爵夫人亲手给了他一匕首。”

“啊！”蒂博说道，在这出阴森的惨剧当中，他极力想挤出一个笑来，面孔却扭得狰狞可怖，“你这话里话外所影射的这桩事，我可是一无所知。”

于是莉赛特便把这桩事的始末原原本本告诉了他。这是一桩平平常常的事，却又是一桩骇人的事。

===Original===
The Countess had remained in bed part of the day, listening to the village bells of Puiseux, which were tolling as the Baron’s body was being borne from thence to Vauparfond, where he was to be laid in the family grave. Towards four o’clock the bells ceased; then the Countess rose, took the dagger from under her pillow, hid it in her breast, and went towards her husband’s room. She found the valet in attendance in good spirits; the doctor had just left, having examined the wound, and declared the Count’s life out of danger.

“Madame will agree that it is a thing to rejoice at!” said the valet.

“Yes, to rejoice at indeed.”

And the Countess went on into her husband’s room. Five minutes later she left it again.

“The Count is sleeping,” she said, “do not go in until he calls.”

The valet bowed and sat down in the anteroom to be in readiness at the first call from his master. The Countess went back to her room.

===Chinese===
那一日大半天，伯爵夫人都卧在床上，听着皮瑟（Puiseux）村教堂的钟声——那钟正一声一声地敲着丧钟，因为男爵的遗体正从那个村子运往沃帕丰（Vauparfond），要在那里安葬进家族的坟茔。将近四点钟，钟声停了；伯爵夫人便起了身，从枕下摸出那把匕首，藏进怀里，朝她丈夫的房间走去。她看见侍候的贴身男仆正满面喜色；医生方才来过，验了伤，已断言伯爵的性命脱离了险境。

“夫人想必也承认，这实在是一桩可喜的事！”那男仆说。

“不错，实在可喜。”

伯爵夫人便径直走进她丈夫房里去了。五分钟之后，她又从里头出来。

“伯爵睡着了，”她说，“他不叫，你们莫进去。”

那男仆一躬到地，便在外间坐下，好随时听候主人第一声呼唤。伯爵夫人回到了自己房里。

===Original===
“Undress me, Lisette,” she said to her waiting maid, “and give me the clothes that I had on the last time *he* came.”

The maid obeyed; we have already seen how every detail of toilet was arranged exactly as it had been on that fatal night. Then the Countess wrote a few words on a piece of paper, which she folded and kept in her right hand. After that, she lay down on her bed.

“Will Madame not take anything,” asked the maid.

The Countess opened her left hand, and showed her a little bottle she was holding inside it.

“Yes, Lisette,” she said, “I am going to take what is in this bottle.”

“What, nothing but that!” said Lisette.

“It will be enough, Lisette; for after I have taken it, I shall have need of nothing more.”

And as she spoke, she put the bottle to her mouth and drank the contents at a draught. Then she said:
“You saw that man, Lisette, who waited for us in the road; I have a meeting with him this evening, here in my room, at half past nine. You know where to go and wait for him, and you will bring him here. I do not wish that anyone should be able to say that I was not true to my word, ever after I am dead.”

===Chinese===
“替我宽衣罢，莉赛特，”她对她的贴身侍女说，“把我那一回他来时所穿的衣裳给我拿出来。”

那侍女依言照办了；我们已经见过，她梳妆打扮的每一处细节，都照着那个致命之夜的样子安排得分毫不差。随后，伯爵夫人在一张纸片上写下几个字，折起来，攥在右手里。做完这些，她便在床上躺下了。

“夫人不再用点什么了吗？”侍女问。

伯爵夫人张开左手，把藏在手里的一只小瓶子亮给她瞧。

“是啦，莉赛特，”她说，“我就要把这瓶子里的东西喝下去。”

“怎么，就只喝这个！”

“这就够啦，莉赛特；因为我把它喝下去之后，便再也不需要什么了。”

她说着，便把那小瓶凑到嘴边，一口将里头的药水饮尽。然后她又说：
“你在路上瞧见那个等着咱们的男人了，莉赛特；今儿晚上九点半，我与他有约，就在我这间屋里。你知道该到哪儿去等他，你也得把他领到这儿来。我不愿意有人能说我一句不守约——哪怕是我死了之后。”

===Original===
Thibault had nothing to say; the agreement made between them had been kept. Only the Countess had accomplished her revenge herself, single-handed, as everyone understood, when the valet feeling uneasy about his master, and going softly into his room to look at him, found him lying on his back with a dagger in his heart; and then hurrying to tell Madame what had happened, found the Countess dead also.

The news of this double death soon spread through the Castle, and all the servants had fled, saying that the exterminating Angel was in the Castle; the waiting-maid alone remained to carry out her dead mistress’s wishes.

===Chinese===
蒂博无话可说；他与她之间所定的那桩约，到底践了。只不过伯爵夫人这一桩复仇，是她亲手了结的，独力完成的——这一点人人都明白了：那贴身男仆放心不下主人，蹑手蹑脚进房去看他，却见他仰面朝天躺着，心口里插着一把匕首；男仆慌忙赶去禀报夫人出了什么事，却发觉伯爵夫人也已断了气。

这双双丧命的消息不多时便传遍了整座城堡，仆人们都逃了个干净，只说那灭命的天使已进了城堡；唯独那贴身侍女一个留下来，为的是替她那已死的女主人了却心愿。

===Original===
Thibault had nothing more to do at the castle, so he left the Countess on her bed, with Lisette near her, and went downstairs. As Lisette had said, there was no fear now of meeting either master or servants; the servants had run away, the master and mistress were dead. Thibault once more made for the breach in the wall. The sky was dark, and if it had not been January, you might have imagined a thunderstorm was brewing; there was barely light enough to see the footpath, as he went along. Once or twice Thibault paused; he fancied he had detected the sound of the dry branches cracking under someone’s footsteps keeping pace with his, both to right and left.

Having come to the breach, Thibault distinctly heard a voice say: “that’s the man!” and at the same moment, two gendarmes, concealed on the farther side of the wall, seized Thibault by the collar, while two others came up behind.

===Chinese===
蒂博在城堡里再无事情可做，于是他把伯爵夫人留在床上，由莉赛特守在旁边，自己下了楼。果如莉赛特所言，这会儿不论是撞见主子还是撞见仆人，都再没什么好怕的了；仆人们已作鸟兽散，主子与主母俱已亡故。蒂博重又朝那围墙缺口走去。天色阴沉，倘若不是正月里，你简直会以为一场雷暴雨正在酝酿；他一路走着，那点亮光勉强够他辨清脚下的路径。蒂博有一两回停下脚来；他像是听见了干树枝在什么人的脚下被踩断的声响，那脚步忽而在左、忽而在右，与他亦步亦趋。

到了缺口处，蒂博分明听见一个声音说道：“就是这个汉子！”话音未落，埋伏在墙那边的两名宪兵便一把揪住了蒂博的衣领，与此同时，另外两名又从背后扑了上来。

===Original===
It appeared that Cramoisi, jealous with regard to Lisette, had been prowling about at nights on the watch, and had, only the evening before, noticed a strange man come in and go out of the park along the more secluded paths, and he had reported the fact to the head of the police. When the recent serious events that had taken place at the Castle became generally known, orders were given to send four men and take up any suspicious looking person seen prowling about. Two of the men, with Cramoisi for guide, had ambushed on the farther side of the breach, and the two others had dogged Thibault through the park. Then as we have seen, at the signal given by Cramoisi, they had all four fallen upon him as he issued from the breach.

===Chinese===
原来，克拉莫西（Cramoisi）为着莉赛特的缘故，心怀妒意，夜里常在这一带逡巡窥伺，就在头一天傍晚，他撞见一个陌生汉子顺着那些僻静的小径进出园子，便将这事报告了警官。待到城堡里新近发生的几桩重大变故传扬开来，上头便下了命令，拨四个人去，凡见有形迹可疑、在那儿鬼鬼祟祟的人，一概拿下。其中两名由克拉莫西领道，埋伏在缺口的那一边，另外两名则一路尾随蒂博穿过园子。于是正如我们所见，克拉莫西一发暗号，四个人便趁蒂博从缺口钻出来的一瞬，齐刷刷地扑了上去。

===Original===
There was a long and obstinate struggle; Thibault was not a man that even four others could overcome without difficulty; but he had no weapon by him, and his resistance was therefore useless. The gendarmes had been more bent on securing him, on account of having recognised that it was Thibault, and Thibault was beginning to earn a very bad name, so many misfortunes having become associated with it; so Thibault was knocked down, and finally bound and led off between two mounted men. The other two gendarmes walked one in front, and one behind. Thibault had merely struggled out of a natural feeling of self-defence and pride, for his power to inflict evil was, as we know, unlimited, and he had but to wish his assailants dead, and they would have fallen lifeless at his feet. But he thought there was time enough for that; as long as there still remained a wish to him, he could escape from man’s justice, even though he were at the foot of the scaffold.

===Chinese===
一场持久而胶着的搏斗随之展开；蒂博这种人，便是一齐上四个汉子也轻易拿他不下；只是他手头寸铁皆无，这番反抗到底无济于事。宪兵们之所以越发要死死拿下他，全因认出了此人正是蒂博——蒂博这名字如今已开始背着极坏的名声，许多祸事都与它牵连在一处了；于是蒂博被打翻在地，终至被捆缚起来，夹在两名骑马的宪兵中间押走。另外两名宪兵，则一前一后地步行随行。蒂博这一番挣扎，不过是出于自卫与自尊的天性罢了，因为正如我们所知，他降祸于人的能耐原是无穷无尽的，他只需发一愿要这几个袭击他的人死，他们便会立时断气，倒在他脚下。可他心想着这事儿来日方长；只要他手里还有一愿可许，便是站在断头台的脚下，他也能逃脱人间的刑罚。

===Original===
So, Thibault, securely bound, his hands tied, and fetters upon his feet, walked along between his four gendarmes, apparently in a state of resignation. One of the gendarmes held the end of the rope with which he was bound, and the four men made jokes and laughed at him, asking the wizard Thibault, why, being possessed of such power, he had allowed himself to be taken. And Thibault replied to their scoffings with the well-known Proverb: “He laughs best who laughs last,” and the gendarmes expressed a wish that they might be the ones to do so.

===Chinese===
于是，蒂博被捆得结结实实，两手反绑，脚上加了镣铐，便在那四名宪兵中间走着，一副逆来顺受的模样。其中一名宪兵牵着捆他的绳头，四个人一路拿他打趣取笑，盘问那巫师蒂博：既有这般神通，又怎会让人家轻而易举地拿了去。蒂博对他们这一番奚落，只用一句尽人皆知的谚语作答：“谁笑到最后，谁笑得最好。”那几个宪兵则回敬了一句，但愿这最后笑的正是他们自己。

===Original===
On leaving Puiseux behind, they came to the forest. The weather was growing more and more threatening; the dark clouds hung so low that the trees looked as if they were holding up a huge black veil, and it was impossible to see four steps ahead. But he, Thibault saw; saw lights swiftly passing, and crossing one another, in the darkness on either side. Closer and closer drew the lights, and pattering footfalls were heard among the dry leaves. The horses became restive, shied and snorted, sniffing the air and trembling beneath their riders, while the coarse laughter of the men themselves died down. It was Thibault’s turn to laugh now.

“What are you laughing at?” asked one of the gendarmes. “I am laughing at your having left off laughing,” said Thibault.

The lights drew nearer, and the footfalls became more distinct, at the sound of Thibault’s voice. Then a more ominous sound was heard, a sound of teeth striking together, as jaws opened and shut.

===Chinese===
把皮瑟抛在身后，他们便走进了森林。天色越发险恶，一片欲雨未来之势；乌云压得极低，低得那些树木仿佛正擎着一幅巨大的黑幕，往前四步之外便什么也瞧不见了。可他蒂博却瞧得见；他看见一点一点亮光在两旁的黑暗里飞快地掠过、穿梭交错。那亮光越逼越近，干枯的落叶间也响起了噼噼啪啪的脚步声。胯下的马都焦躁起来，又惊又嗅，打着响鼻，在骑手的胯下颤栗不止，而那几个汉子粗鲁的笑声也渐渐收了场。这回轮到蒂博笑了。

“你笑什么？”一名宪兵问。“我笑你们怎么不笑啦。”蒂博说。

听得蒂博的声音，那亮光更近了，脚步声也更分明。继而，一种更要命的声响传了过来——那是牙齿开合、一张一合磕碰出来的声响。

===Original===
“Yes, yes, my friends,” said Thibault, “you have tasted human flesh, and you found it good.”

He was answered by a low growl of approbation, half like a dog’s, and half like a hyena’s.

“Quite so,” said Thibault, “I understand; after having made a meal of a keeper, you would not mind tasting a gendarme.”

The gendarmes themselves were beginning to shudder with fear. “To whom are you talking?” they asked him.

“To those who can answer me,” said Thibault; and he gave a howl. Twenty or more howls responded, some from close at hand, some from farther off.

“H’m!” said one of the gendarmes, “what are these beasts that are following us? this good-for-nothing seems to understand their language?”

“What!” said the shoemaker, “you take Thibault the wolf-master prisoner, you carry him through the forest at night, and then you ask what are the lights and the howls that follow him!… Do you hear, friends?” cried Thibault, “these gentlemen are asking who you are. Answer them, all of you together, that they may have no further doubt on the matter.”

===Chinese===
“是啦，是啦，我的朋友们，”蒂博说，“你们尝过人肉的滋味，觉得那是好东西罢。”

回答他的，是一阵低低的附和咆哮，一半像狗吠，一半像鬣狗叫。

“正是如此，”蒂博说，“我明白；你们既已拿一个看林人当了顿饭，再尝尝宪兵的滋味，想必也不在话下。”

那几个宪兵自己也开始吓得打起哆嗦来。“你在跟谁说话？”他们问他。

“跟那些能答得上我话的。”蒂博说；说罢便嚎了一声。二十几声嚎叫应声而起，有的就在近旁，有的发自远处。

“哼！”其中一名宪兵说，“跟着咱们的这群是什么畜生？这无赖倒像是懂它们的语言？”

“什么！”那鞋匠说，“你们拿住了蒂博这狼之领主（the wolf-master），趁黑里把他押过这片林子，倒又来问那一路追着他的亮光和嚎叫是什么东西！……听见没有，朋友们？”蒂博高声道，“这几位老爷在打听你们是谁呢。大伙儿一齐答他们一句，好叫他们再无半点疑心。”

===Original===
The wolves, obedient to their master’s voice, gave one prolonged, unanimous howl. The horses panted and shivered, and one or two of them reared. The gendarmes endeavoured to calm their animals, patting and gentling them.

“That is nothing,” said Thibault, “wait till you see each horse with two wolves hanging on to its hindquarters and another at its throat.”

The wolves now came in between the horses’ legs, and began caressing Thibault; one of them stood up, and put its front paws on Thibault’s chest, as if asking for orders.

“Presently, presently,” said Thibault, “there is plenty of time; do not be selfish, give your comrades time to come up.”

The men could no longer control their horses, which were rearing and shying, and although going at a foot’s pace, were streaming with sweat.

===Chinese===
那些狼听得主人一声令，齐齐发出一声悠长而同声相应嚎叫。那几匹马喘息着、战栗着，其中有一两匹直立起来。宪兵们极力安抚坐骑，又是拍抚，又是柔声哄劝。

“这算不得什么，”蒂博说，“等你们瞧见每匹马上挂着两匹狼——一匹吊在马臀上，另一匹扑在马喉咙上——那才叫有热闹看呢。”

此时狼群已钻进了马腿之间，开始对蒂博亲热起来；其中一匹竟立起身子，把两只前爪搭在蒂博胸口，仿佛在讨一道命令。

“且慢，且慢，”蒂博说，“日子还长着呢；莫要只顾自个儿，给你们那些同伴留点工夫赶上来罢。”

那些汉子已再也勒不住自己的马了；马匹尥着蹶子、惊跳着，虽说还只一步步地走着，却已是一身淋漓的大汗。

===Original===
“Do you not think,” said Thibault, “you would do best now to come to terms with me? That is, if you were to let me free on condition that you all sleep in your beds tonight.”

“Go at a walking pace,” said one of the gendarmes, “as long as we do that, we have nothing to fear.”

Another one drew his sword. A second or two later there was a howl of pain; one of the wolves had seized hold of this gendarme’s boot, and the latter had pierced him through with his weapon.

“I call that a very imprudent thing to do,” said Thibault; “the wolves eat each other, whatever the proverb may say, and once having tasted blood, I do not know that even I shall have the power to hold them back.”

The wolves threw themselves in a body on their wounded comrade, and in five minutes there was nothing left of its carcase but the bare bones. The gendarmes had profited by this respite to get on ahead, but without releasing Thibault, whom they obliged to run alongside of them; what he had foreseen, however, happened. There was a sudden sound as of an approaching hurricane﻿—the whole pack was in pursuit, following them up at full gallop. The horses, having once started trotting, refused to go at a walking pace again, and frightened by the stamping, the smell, and the howls, now set off galloping, in spite of their riders’ efforts to hold them in. The man who had hold of the rope, now requiring both hands to master his horse, let go of Thibault; and the wolves leaped on to the horses, clinging desperately to the cruppers and withers and throats of the terrified animals. No sooner had the latter felt the sharp teeth of their assailants, than they scattered, rushing in every direction.

===Chinese===
“你们不想想看，”蒂博说，“这会儿跟我讲和才是上策么？也就是说，你们放了我，作为交换，今儿夜里大伙儿都能安安稳稳地睡在自家的床上。”

“照常步子走，”一名宪兵说，“只要咱们照这个步子走，便没什么好怕的。”

另一名却拔出了剑。一两秒之后便响起一声痛嚎；原来一匹狼已咬住了这名宪兵的靴子，这宪兵便一剑把它捅了个对穿。

“我倒要说，这事儿办得未免太鲁莽了，”蒂博说，“狼是要吃狼的，且不管那句谚语是怎么说的；它们一旦尝了血味，便连我，只怕也没有把握勒得住它们了。”

众狼便一齐扑到那匹受伤的同伴身上，不出五分钟，那具尸首便只剩下一副白骨。宪兵们趁着这一刻喘息之机往前赶路，却仍不肯松开蒂博，逼着他跟在他们身边跑；然而他所预料的，到底应验了。猛然间响起一阵如飓风迫近般的声息——整群狼穷追不舍，以全速奔驰而来。那几匹马一旦开了小跑，便再不肯回到原先那一步步走的步子，又被那一片踩踏声、那一股膻腥气和嚎叫声吓得发了狂，这会儿竟不顾骑手勒马的气力，撒开蹄子狂奔起来。那牵着绳头的汉子，到这会儿两只手都要用来制住自己的马，便撒了蒂博；狼群便一跃扑到马背上，拼了命地吊在那些吓破了胆的畜生的臀上、鬐甲上、喉头上。那些马才一觉出袭击者那利齿的锋利，便四散奔逃，朝四面八方冲了出去。

===Original===
“Hurrah, wolves! hurrah!” cried Thibault. But the fierce animals had no need of encouragement, and soon each horse had six or seven more wolves in pursuit of him.

Horses and wolves disappeared, some one way some the other, and the men’s cries of distress, the agonised neighings of the horses, and the furious howls of the wolves became gradually fainter and fainter as they travelled farther away.

Thibault was left free once more, and alone. His hands however were still bound, and his feet fettered. First he tried to undo the cord with his teeth, but this he found impossible. Then he tried to wrench his bonds apart by the power of his muscles, but that too was unavailing; the only result of his efforts was to make the cord cut into his flesh. It was his turn to bellow with pain and anger. At last, tired of trying to wrest his hands free, he lifted them, bound as they were, to heaven, and cried:

“Oh! black wolf! friend, let these cords that bind me be loosened; thou knowest well that it is only to do evil that I wish for my hands to be free.”

And at the same moment his fetters were broken and fell to the ground, and Thibault beat his hands together with another roar, this time of joy.

===Chinese===
“好哇，狼群！好哇！”蒂博高声叫道。可那一群凶兽哪里还用得着人给它们打气，不多时每匹马身后便又多了六七匹狼紧追不舍。

马与狼都不见了踪影，有的朝这一边，有的朝那一边，那些汉子绝望的呼号、马匹哀痛的嘶鸣、与狼群狂怒的嚎叫，都随着他们越去越远，渐渐地、渐渐地微弱下去。

蒂博又得了自由，这一回却是孤身一人。只是他的双手仍被反绑着，脚上还戴着镣铐。他先试着用牙齿去咬断绳索，却发现根本办不到。继而又想凭一身筋骨的蛮力把绳索挣开，却也徒然；他这一番挣扎唯一的收获，不过是那绳索勒进了他的皮肉。这回轮到他痛得、气得直声嚎叫起来。末了，他挣不开这双手，索性把那双绑着的手举向苍天，喊道：

“噢！黑狼！朋友，松开绑着我的绳索罢；你心里清楚，我之所以要这双手自由，无非是为了作恶。”

话音方落，他的镣铐便齐齐断裂，铛啷一声落在地上，蒂博把两只手拍到一处，又是一声吼——这一回，却是欢喜的吼。

## XIII Where It Is Demonstrated That a Woman Never Speaks More Eloquently Than When She Holds Her Tongue / XIII 试论女人沉默时反能说出最雄辩的话语

===Original===
As Thibault was talking to himself he did not catch the few hurried words which Suzanne whispered to the Baron; and all he saw was that she appeared to totter, and then fell back into her lover’s arms, as if in a dead faint.

The Bailiff stopped short as he caught sight of this curious group, lit up by his candle. He was facing Thibault, and the latter endeavoured to read in Monsieur Magloire’s face what was passing in his mind.

But the Bailiff’s jovial physiognomy was not made by nature to express any strong emotion, and Thibault could detect nothing in it but a benevolent astonishment on the part of the amiable husband.

The Baron, also, evidently detected nothing more, for with a coolness and ease of manner, which produced on Thibault a surprise beyond expression, he turned to the Bailiff, and asked:

“Well, friend Magloire, and how do you carry your wine this evening?”

===Chinese===
蒂博正自顾自地嘀咕，没听清苏珊娜（Suzanne）匆匆向男爵耳边低语的那么几句；他只瞧见她身子一晃，便像死过去一般，倒进了她那情郎的怀里。

执达吏（the Bailiff）举着蜡烛一照，瞥见这古怪的一伙人，顿时站住了脚。他正与蒂博面对面，蒂博便竭力想从马格卢瓦先生（Monsieur Magloire）的脸上读出他心里正在打什么主意。

可这位执达吏那副乐天派的长相，天生就不是用来流露什么强烈情感的；蒂博在他脸上什么也瞧不出来，只瞧出这位和蔼可亲的丈夫那一脸善意的惊讶。

男爵显然也没看出什么别的来，只见他带着一种沉着自在的神气——这份沉着自在叫蒂博惊愕得难以言表——转过身去朝执达吏搭话道：

“嗳，马格卢瓦朋友，您今晚这酒量扛得怎么样啊？”

===Original===
“Why, is it you, my lord?” replied the Bailiff opening his fat little eyes. “Ah! pray excuse me, and believe me, had I known I was to have the honour of seeing you here, I should not have allowed myself to appear in such an unsuitable costume.”

“Pooh-pooh! nonsense!”

“Yes, indeed, my lord; you must permit me to go and make a little toilette.”

“No ceremony, I pray!” rejoined the Baron. “After curfew, one is at least free to receive one’s friends in what costume one likes. Besides, my dear friend, there is something which requires more immediate attention.”

“What is that, my lord?”

“To restore Madame Magloire to her senses, who, you see, has fainted in my arms.”

“Fainted! Suzanne fainted! Ah! my God!” cried the little man, putting down his candle on the chimneypiece, “how ever did such a misfortune happen?”

===Chinese===
“哟，是您吗，老爷？”执达吏睁开他那双胖乎乎的小眼睛答道，“啊！千万恕罪，请相信我，要是我早知道在此地能有幸见到您，我绝不至于穿这么一身不合适的衣裳出来见您。”

“呸呸！胡扯！”

“真的呢，老爷；您总得容我进去稍微整一整容。”

“不必客气，我求您啦！”男爵接话道，“敲过了熄灯钟，一个人爱穿什么衣裳接待朋友，总归是自由的了。况且，我亲爱的朋友，眼下还有一桩更要紧的事得办呢。”

“什么事，老爷？”

“想法子把马格卢瓦太太（Madame Magloire）救醒过来呀，您瞧，她方才在我怀里晕过去了。”

“晕过去了！苏珊娜晕过去了！啊！我的天！”这小个子男人叫道，一面把手里的蜡烛放到壁炉台上，“这样的祸事到底是怎么闹出来的？”

===Original===
“Wait, wait, Monsieur Magloire!” said my lord, “we must first get your wife into a more comfortable position in an armchair; nothing annoys women so much as not to be at their ease when they are unfortunate enough to faint.”

“You are right, my lord; let us first put her in the armchair.… Oh Suzanne! poor Suzanne! How can such a thing as this have happened?”

“I pray you at least, my dear fellow, not to think any ill of me at finding me in your house at such a time of night!”

“Far from it, my lord,” replied the Bailiff, “the friendship with which you honour us, and the virtue of Madame Magloire are sufficient guarantees for me to be glad at any hour to have my house honoured by your presence.”

“Triple dyed idiot!” murmured the shoemaker, “unless I ought rather to call him a doubly clever dissembler.… No matter which, however! we have yet to see how my lord is going to get out of it.”

===Chinese===
“等等，等等，马格卢瓦先生！”老爷说道，“咱们得先把尊夫人安顿到一张扶手椅里，让她躺得舒坦些；女人家最懊恼的，莫过于不幸晕过去的时候没能舒舒服服地躺着。”

“您说得对，老爷；咱们先把她扶到扶手椅里。……噢，苏珊娜！可怜的苏珊娜！这种事到底是怎么发生的呢？”

“我亲爱的朋友，我至少求您一件事，大半夜的在您府上撞见我，可千万别往坏处想我！”

“哪里哪里，老爷，”执达吏答道，“您对我们厚赐的友谊，再加上马格卢瓦太太的贞操，对我来说便是足够的担保了，无论什么时候我的寒舍能蒙您大驾光临，我都是高兴的。”

“三重的彩头大笨蛋！”鞋匠咕哝道，“要不，我倒该叫他一个双料的装假老手。……管他哪个呢！咱们且瞧老爷这一回怎么脱身。”

===Original===
“Nevertheless,” continued Maître Magloire, dipping a handkerchief into some aromatic water, and bathing his wife’s temples with it, “nevertheless, I am curious to know how my poor wife can have received such a shock.”

“It’s a simple affair enough, as I will explain, my dear fellow. I was returning from dining with my friend, de Vivières, and passing through Erneville on my way to Vez, I caught sight of an open window, and a woman inside making signals of distress.”

“Ah! my God!”

“That is what I exclaimed, when I realised that the window belonged to your house; and can it be my friend the Bailiff’s wife, I thought, who is in danger and in need of help?”

“You are good indeed, my lord,” said the Bailiff quite overcome. “I trust it was nothing of the sort.”

“On the contrary, my dear man.”

“How! on the contrary?”

“Yes, as you will see.”

“You make me shudder, my lord! And do you mean that my wife was in need of help and did not call me?”

“It had been her first thought to call you, but she abstained from doing so, for, and here you see her delicacy of feeling, she was afraid that if you came, your precious life might be endangered.”

The Bailiff turned pale and gave an exclamation.

“My precious life, as you are good enough to call it, is in danger?”

“Not now, since I am here.”

“But tell me, I pray, my lord, what had happened? I would question my wife, but as you see she is not yet able to answer.”

“And am I not here to answer in her stead?”

“Answer then, my lord, as you are kind enough to offer to do so; I am listening.”

The Baron made a gesture of assent, and went on:

===Chinese===
“话虽如此，”马格卢瓦师傅（Maître Magloire）接着说，一面把手帕浸了点芳香水，替妻子擦拭太阳穴，“话虽如此，我倒好奇，我可怜的内人怎么会受这一场惊吓。”

“这事说起来倒也简单，我讲给您听就是了，亲爱的朋友。我刚从我朋友德维维埃尔（de Vivières）那儿吃了晚饭回来，打埃纳维尔（Erneville）经过往韦兹（Vez）去，一眼瞧见一扇开着的窗子，里头有个女人在打求救的信号。”

“啊！我的天！”

“我那时也是这么叫了一声，待我看清楚那扇窗子正是您府上的；我就想，莫非有危险的、求救的，是我这位执达吏朋友的太太不成？”

“您可真是太好心啦，老爷，”执达吏感动得不知如何是好，“但愿并不是那么回事。”

“恰恰相反，我亲爱的人。”

“怎么！恰恰相反？”

“不错，您这就明白了。”

“您叫我直打寒颤啦，老爷！您的意思是，我太太需要人搭救，却没有唤我？”

“她头一个念头原是要唤您的，可她没有唤，因为——您这就可以看出她的体贴来——她生怕您一来，您那条宝贵的性命便要担风险。”

执达吏脸色煞白，惊叫了一声。

“您好心称作宝贵的那条性命，正担着风险？”

“这会儿不了啦，有我在这里呢。”

“可您倒是告诉我呀，老爷，到底出了什么事？我原想问我太太，可您也瞧见了，她还答不上话呢。”

“有我在这里替她代答，不就行了吗？”

“那么就请答吧，老爷，您既然好心肯答；我洗耳恭听。”

男爵点了点头表示应允，接着讲道：

===Original===
“So I ran to her, and seeing her all trembling and alarmed, I asked, ‘What is the matter, Madame Magloire, and what is causing you so much alarm?’ ‘Ah! my lord,’ she replied, ‘just think what I feel, when I tell you that yesterday and today, my husband has been entertaining a man about whom I have the worst suspicions. Ugh! A man who has introduced himself under the pretence of friendship to my dear Magloire, and actually makes love to me, to me.…’”

“She told you that?”

“Word for word, my dear fellow! She cannot hear what we are saying, I hope?”

“How can she, when she is insensible?”

“Well, ask her yourself when she comes to, and if she does not tell you exactly to the letter what I have been telling you, call me a Turk, an infidel and a heretic.”

“Ah! these men! these men!” murmured the Bailiff.

“Yes, race of vipers!” continued my lord of Vez, “do you wish me to go on?”

“Yes, indeed!” said the little man, forgetting the scantiness of his attire in the interest excited in him by the Baron’s tale.

===Chinese===
“于是我朝她奔过去，见她浑身发抖、惊慌失措，便问：‘怎么啦，马格卢瓦太太？什么事叫您这样惊慌？’‘啊！老爷，’她答道，‘您替我想想我这心里是什么滋味吧，我告诉您，昨天和今天这两天，我丈夫款待着一个让我疑心得最厉害的男人。呸！一个打着交朋友的幌子混到我亲爱的马格卢瓦身边来的男人，竟然对我调起情来，对我……’”

“她跟您说这话？”

“一字不差，亲爱的朋友！她这会儿听不见我们说的话吧，我想？”

“她这副不省人事的样子，哪里听得见？”

“那好，等她缓过气来您自个儿问她，她要是不把我方才告诉您的话一字不差地照样说给您听，您就管我叫土耳其人、叫异教徒、叫异端好了。”

“啊！这些男人！这些男人！”执达吏嘀咕道。

“可不，一窝毒蛇！”韦兹领主接着说，“您要我往下讲吗？”

“要的，要的！”这小个子男人说道，已被男爵这段故事勾起了兴致，竟忘了自己身上衣衫的单薄。

===Original===
“‘But Madame,’ I said to my friend Madame Magloire, ‘How could you tell that he had the audacity to love you?’”

“Yes,” put in the Bailiff, “how did she find it out? I never noticed anything myself.”

“You would have been aware of it, my dear friend, if only you had looked under the table; but, fond of your dinner as you are, you were not likely to be looking at the dishes on the table and underneath it at the same time.”

“The truth is, my lord, we had the most perfect little supper! just you think now—cutlets of young wild-boar.…”

“Very well,” said the Baron, “now you are going to tell me about your supper, instead of listening to the end of my tale, a tale which concerns the life and honour of your wife!”

“True, true, my poor Suzanne! My lord, help me to open her hands, that I may slap them on the palms.”

The Lord of Vez gave all the assistance in his power to Monsieur Magloire, and by dint of their united efforts they forced open Madame Magloire’s hands.

The good man, now easier in his mind, began slapping his wife’s palms with his chubby little hands, all the while giving his attention to the remainder of the Baron’s interesting and veracious story.

===Chinese===
“‘可是太太，’我对这位马格卢瓦太太说，‘您怎么会看出他竟有这等胆量，敢对您起这种心思？’”

“对呀，”执达吏插嘴道，“她又是怎么看出来的呢？我自己可什么也没察觉。”

“您早该察觉了，亲爱的朋友，您只要往桌子底下瞧一眼便成；可您既是个贪嘴的人，自然不可能一边盯着桌上那些菜，一边又同时盯着桌底下。”

“实情是，老爷，我们那顿夜宵可真是没得说！您想想看——那可是小野猪的排骨……”

“好得很，”男爵说，“这会儿您倒要跟我讲起您的夜宵来了，我这段故事还没听完呢，这故事可关系到您太太的性命和名节啊！”

“对，对，我可怜的苏珊娜！老爷，帮我掰开她的手，我好拍她的手心。”

韦兹领主尽力相助，与马格卢瓦先生一道；两人合力，总算掰开了马格卢瓦太太紧攥的双手。

这位老实人心里松快了些，便开始用他那双胖乎乎的小手拍打起太太的手心来，一边拍，一边仍把耳朵竖起来听男爵那段有趣而又真实的故事的下半段。

===Original===
“Where had I got to?” he asked.

“You had got just to where my poor Suzanne, whom one may indeed call ‘the chaste Suzanna.…’”

“Yes, you may well say that!” interrupted the lord of Vez.

“Indeed, I do! You had just got to where my poor Suzanne began to be aware.…”

“Ah, yes—that your guest like Paris of old was wishing to make another Menelaus of you; well, then she rose from table.… You remember that she did so?”

“No.… I was perhaps a little—just a little—overcome.”

“Quite so! Well then she rose from table, and said it was time to retire.”

“The truth is, that the last hour I heard strike was eleven,” said the jovial Bailiff.

“Then the party broke up.”

“I don’t think I left the table,” said the Bailiff.

“No, but Madame Magloire and your guest did. She told him which was his room, and Perrine showed him to it; after which, kind and faithful wife as she is, Madame Magloire tucked you into bed, and went into her own room.”

“Dear little Suzanne!” said the bailiff in a voice of emotion.

===Chinese===
“我讲到哪儿了？”他问。

“您正讲到我这可怜的苏珊娜——真真可以叫她一声‘贞洁的苏珊娜……’”

“可不，您尽可以这样叫她！”韦兹领主插嘴道。

“我就是这么叫的！您正讲到我这可怜的苏珊娜开始察觉……”

“啊，对——察觉您那位客人，就像当年的帕里斯（Paris）一样，正想把您再做成一个墨涅拉俄斯（Menelaus）；于是她便离席起身……您可还记得她离席的事吧？”

“不记得了……我那会儿怕是有那么一点——就那么一点——喝高了。”

“正是如此！她离席起身，说是该歇着了。”

“实情是，我听到的最后报时是十一点，”这位快活的执达吏说。

“随后这一席便散了。”

“我怕是没离过桌呢，”执达吏说。

“不错，可马格卢瓦太太和您那位客人离了席。她把他的房间的所在告诉了他，佩琳（Perrine）引他过去；随后，马格卢瓦太太这个体贴忠实的妻子，便把您安顿上床睡下，自己进了她那间房。”

“亲爱的小苏珊娜！”执达吏用动情的声调说。

===Original===
“And it was then, when she found herself in her room, and all alone, that she got frightened; she went to the window and opened it; the wind, blowing into the room, put out her candle. You know what it is to have a sudden panic come over you, do you not?”

“Oh! yes,” replied the Bailiff naively, “I am very timid myself.”

“After that she was seized with panic, and not daring to wake you, for fear any harm should come to you, she called to the first horseman she saw go by—and luckily, that horseman was myself.”

“It was indeed fortunate, my lord.”

“Was it not?… I ran, I made myself known.”

“‘Come up, my lord, come up,’ she cried. ‘Come up quickly—I am sure there is a man in my room.’”

“Dear! dear!…” said the bailiff, “you must indeed have felt terribly frightened.”

“Not at all! I thought it was only losing time to stop and ring; I gave my horse to l’Eveillé, I stood up on the saddle, climbed from that to the balcony, and, so that the man who was in the room might not escape, I shut the window. It was just at that moment that Madame Magloire, hearing the sound of your door opening, and overcome by such a succession of painful feelings, fell fainting into my arms.”

“Ah! my lord!” said the Bailiff, “how frightful all this is that you tell me.”

===Chinese===
“正是在那会儿，她一个人进了自己的房间，独自一个，这才发起怕来；她走到窗前，把窗子推开；一阵风灌进屋里，把她的蜡烛吹灭了。您知道那种突如其来的惊惶是怎么回事吧？”

“噢！知道，”执达吏憨憨地答道，“我自己就是个胆子极小的人。”

“打那以后她便慌了神，又不敢叫醒您，生怕您有什么好歹，便冲她瞧见的头一个骑马经过的人呼救——万幸，那个骑马的人正是我。”

“那可真是万幸啊，老爷。”

“可不是么？……我奔过去，报了姓名。”

“‘上来呀，老爷，上来，’她叫道，‘快上来——我敢肯定我这房里有个男人。’”

“老天！老天！……”执达吏说，“您那会儿想必是吓坏了。”

“一点儿也不！我心想停下来敲门是白费工夫；我便把马交给勒韦耶（l’Eveillé），踩着马鞍子站起身，从那儿攀上了阳台，又为了叫屋里那个男人逃不了，把窗子关上了。恰恰就在那一刻，马格卢瓦太太听得您那扇房门开门的声响，一连串的痛楚交加，受不住了，便晕倒在我怀里。”

“啊！老爷！”执达吏说，“您对我讲的这些事，可真叫人毛骨悚然。”

===Original===
“And be sure, my dear friend, that I have rather softened than added to its terror; anyhow, you will hear what Madame Magloire has to tell you when she comes to.…”

“See, my lord, she is beginning to move.”

“That’s right! burn a feather under her nose.”

“A feather?”

“Yes, it is a sovereign antispasmodic; burn a feather under her nose, and she will revive instantly.”

“But where shall I find a feather?” asked the bailiff.

“Here! take this, the feather round my hat.” And the lord of Vez broke off a bit of the ostrich feather which ornamented his hat, gave it to Monsieur Magloire, who lighted it at the candle and held it smoking under his wife’s nose.

The remedy was a sovereign one, as the Baron had said; the effect of it was instantaneous; Madame Magloire sneezed.

“Ah!” cried the bailiff delightedly, “now she is coming to! my wife! my dear wife! my dear little wife!”

Madame Magloire gave a sigh.

“My lord! my lord!” cried the bailiff, “she is saved! saved!”

===Chinese===
“您放心吧，亲爱的朋友，我只把这事往轻里说，绝没有添油加醋地加重它的可怖；无论如何，等马格卢瓦太太缓过气来，您自会听她说个分明……”

“瞧，老爷，她动了。”

“好极啦！拿根羽毛在她鼻子底下烧一烧。”

“羽毛？”

“不错，这是顶灵验的镇痉药；在她鼻子底下烧根羽毛，她立时三刻就能醒过来。”

“可我上哪儿去找一根羽毛呢？”执达吏问。

“这儿！拿这个去，我帽子上这一圈毛。”韦兹领主说罢，从自己帽檐上那圈作装饰的鸵鸟毛里折下一截，递给马格卢瓦先生；后者就着蜡烛把它点着了，把那冒着烟的羽毛凑到妻子鼻子底下。

这法子果然如男爵所说，是再灵不过的；立竿见影，马格卢瓦太太打了个喷嚏。

“啊！”执达吏欢天喜地地叫道，“她醒过来啦！我的太太！我亲爱的太太！我亲爱的小太太！”

马格卢瓦太太叹了一口气。

“老爷！老爷！”执达吏叫道，“她得救了！得救了！”

===Original===
Madame Magloire opened her eyes, looked first at the Bailiff and then at the Baron, with a bewildered gaze, and then finally fixing them on the Bailiff:

“Magloire! dear Magloire!” she said, “is it really you? Oh! how glad I am to see you again after the bad dream I have had!”

“Well!” muttered Thibault, “she *is* a brazen-faced hussy, if you like! if I do not get all that I want from the ladies I run after, they, at least, afford me some valuable object lessons by the way!”

“Alas! my beautiful Suzanne,” said the Bailiff, “it is no bad dream you have had, but, as it seems, a hideous reality.”

“Ah! I remember now,” responded Madame Magloire. Then, as if noticing for the first time that the lord of Vez was there:

“Ah! my lord,” she continued, “I hope you have repeated nothing to my husband of all those foolish things I told you?”

“And why not, dear lady?” asked the Baron.

“Because an honest woman knows how to protect herself, and has no need to keep on telling her husband a lot of nonsense like that.”

“On the contrary, Madame,” replied the Baron, “I have told my friend everything.”

===Chinese===
马格卢瓦太太睁开眼睛，先瞧了瞧执达吏，又瞧了瞧男爵，目光里满是迷惘，末了把眼睛定在执达吏身上：

“马格卢瓦！亲爱的马格卢瓦！”她说，“当真是你吗？噢！我做了一场噩梦，这会儿又能见到你，我多高兴啊！”

“好哇！”蒂博嘀咕道，“这可真是个不要脸的小贱人，可不是么！我追起女人来，纵然得不到我所想要的一切，她们至少也能给我上一路上几堂顶有教益的课呢！”

“唉！我美丽的苏珊娜，”执达吏说，“您方才做的可不是一场噩梦，倒像是——一桩骇人的真事。”

“啊！我这会儿想起来了，”马格卢瓦太太应道。接着，她像是头一回察觉韦兹领主也在场：

“啊！老爷，”她接着说，“我方才跟您说的那一堆糊涂话，您总没拿去跟我丈夫学舌吧，我希望？”

“怎么就不呢，亲爱的夫人？”男爵问。

“因为一个正经女人懂得怎样护住自己，犯不着一个劲儿地跟她丈夫絮叨那一套胡话。”

“恰恰相反，太太，”男爵答道，“我已经把一切都告诉了我的朋友。”

===Original===
“Do you mean that you have told him that during the whole of supper time that man was fondling my knee under the table?”

“I told him that, certainly.”

“Oh! the wretch!” exclaimed the Bailiff.

“And that when I stooped to pick up my table napkin, it was not that, but his hand, that I came across.”

“I have hidden nothing from my friend Magloire.”

“Oh! the ruffian!” cried the Bailiff.

“And that Monsieur Magloire having a passing giddiness which made him shut his eyes while at table, his guest took the opportunity to kiss me against my will?”

“I thought it was right for a husband to know everything.”

“Oh! the knave!” cried the Bailiff.

“And did you even go so far as to tell him that having come into my room, and the wind having blown out the candle, I fancied I saw the window curtains move, which made me call to you for help, believing that he was hidden behind them?”

“No, I did not tell him that! I was going to when you sneezed.”

===Chinese===
“您的意思是，您竟告诉他，整个夜宵的时候，那男人在桌子底下一直抚摸我的膝盖？”

“那个我自然告诉了他。”

“噢！这恶棍！”执达吏叫道。

“还告诉他，我弯腰去捡餐巾的时候，摸到的不是餐巾，而是他的手。”

“我对朋友马格卢瓦绝无隐瞒。”

“噢！这流氓！”执达吏叫道。

“还告诉他，马格卢瓦先生席间一时头晕、闭上了眼睛，他那客人便趁这工夫违背我的心愿强吻了我？”

“我以为做丈夫的理当知道这一切。”

“噢！这无赖！”执达吏叫道。

“您甚至还告诉他，我进了自己的房间，一阵风把蜡烛吹灭了，我恍惚看见窗帘动了一下，这才向您呼救，以为他就藏在窗帘后头？”

“这一桩我倒没告诉他！我刚要讲，您就打了个喷嚏。”

===Original===
“Oh! the vile rascal!” roared the Bailiff, taking hold of the Baron’s sword which the latter had laid on a chair, and drawing it out of the scabbard, then, running toward the window which his wife had indicated, “He had better not be behind these curtains, or I will spit him like a woodcock,” and with this he gave one or two lunges with the sword against the window hangings.

But all at once the Bailiff stayed his hand, and stood as if arrested like a schoolboy caught trespassing out of bounds; his hair rose on end beneath his cotton nightcap, and this conjugal headdress became agitated as by some convulsive movement. The sword dropped from his trembling hand, and fell with a clatter on the floor. He had caught sight of Thibault behind the curtains, and as Hamlet kills Polonius, thinking to slay his father’s murderer, so he, believing that he was thrusting at nothing, had nearly killed his crony of the night before, who had already had time enough to prove himself a false friend. Moreover as he had lifted the curtain with the point of the sword, the Bailiff was not the only one who had seen Thibault. His wife and the Lord of Vez had both been participators in the unexpected vision, and both uttered a cry of surprise. In telling their tale so well, they had had no idea that they were so near the truth. The Baron, too, had not only seen that there was a man, but had also recognised that the man was Thibault.

===Chinese===
“噢！这卑鄙的小人！”执达吏怒吼一声，一把抓起男爵搁在椅上的那把剑，从鞘中抽了出来，随即朝妻子所指的那扇窗子奔过去，“他最好别藏在这窗帘后头，不然我把他像串田鹬一样串起来，”说着便朝那窗帘帷子一剑两剑地捅了过去。

可执达吏的手突然停住了，他僵在那里，活像一个被当场拿住的越界学童；他棉睡帽底下的头发一根根竖了起来，这顶做丈夫的睡帽也跟着痉挛似地颤动起来。剑从他发抖的手中滑落，“当啷”一声掉在地上。原来他从窗帘后头瞧见了蒂博——正如哈姆雷特（Hamlet）一剑刺死波洛涅斯（Polonius）、以为是杀了杀父仇人一样，他呢，本以为是对着空处一刺，险些就把昨夜的酒友——那个早有工夫证明自己是假朋友的——给结果了。况且他用剑尖挑开窗帘的当儿，瞧见蒂博的不止执达吏一个。他太太和韦兹领主也都撞见了这意想不到的一幕，两人齐声惊叫起来。他们把那套谎话编得如此天花乱坠，却万没想到自己竟离真相这么近。男爵呢，不但看清了窗帘后头有个男人，更认出了那男人就是蒂博。

===Original===
“Damn me!” he exclaimed, as he went nearer to him, “if I mistake not, this is my old acquaintance, the man with the boar-spear!”

“How! how! man with the boar-spear?” asked the Bailiff, his teeth chattering as he spoke. “Anyway I trust he has not his boar-spear with him now!” And he ran behind his wife for protection.

“No, no, do not be alarmed,” said the Lord of Vez, “even if he has got it with him, I promise you it shall not stay long in his hands. So, master poacher,” he went on, addressing himself to Thibault, “you are not content to hunt the game belonging to his Highness the Duke of Orleans, in the forest of Villers-Cotterets, but you must come and make excursions in the open and poach on the territory of my friend Maître Magloire?”

“A poacher! do you say?” exclaimed the Bailiff. “Is not Monsieur Thibault a landowner, the proprietor of farms, living in his country house on the income from his estate of a hundred acres?”

“What, he?” said the Baron, bursting into a loud guffaw, “so he made you believe all that stuff, did he? the rascal has got a clever tongue. He! a landowner! that poor starveling! why, the only property he possesses is what my stable-boys wear on their feet—the wooden shoes he gets his living by making.”

===Chinese===
“见鬼！”他一边朝蒂博走去一边叫道，“要是我没认错，这可是我的老相识，那个玩野猪矛的朋友！”

“怎么！怎么！玩野猪矛的朋友？”执达吏问，牙齿捉对儿厮打，“无论如何，但愿他这会儿没把那野猪矛带在身上！”说着便躲到妻子身后去寻庇护。

“别怕，别怕，不用慌，”韦兹领主说，“就算他把它带在身上，我保管它在他手里待不长。那么，偷猎先生，”他转向蒂博接着说，“您在维莱-科特雷（Villers-Cotterets）的林子里偷猎奥尔良公爵（the Duke of Orleans）殿下的野味还不满足，又得到我这朋友马格卢瓦师傅的地盘上来明火执仗地打 excursion、上这儿偷猎来了？”

“偷猎的！您说？”执达吏叫道，“蒂博先生（Monsieur Thibault）不是个地主，不是好些田产的业主，不是靠着他那一百亩地的出息、住着自家的乡间别墅过活的人吗？”

“什么，他？”男爵说着哈哈大笑起来，“敢情那一套鬼话都叫您信了，是吗？这泼皮倒有副伶牙俐齿。他！一个地主！那个饿死鬼！呸，他名下全部的家当，不过是我马厩里小厮们穿在脚上的——他赖以为生地做出来的那种木鞋罢了。”

===Original===
Madame Suzanne, on hearing Thibault thus classified, made a gesture of scorn and contempt, while Maître Magloire drew back a step, while the colour mounted to his face. Not that the good little man was proud, but he hated all kinds of deceit; it was not because he had clinked glasses with a shoemaker that he turned red, but because he had drunk in company with a liar and a traitor.

During this avalanche of abuse Thibault had stood immovable with his arms folded and a smile on his lips. He had no fear but that when his turn came to speak, he would be able to take an easy revenge. And the moment to speak seemed now to have come. In a light, bantering tone of voice—which showed that he was gradually accustoming himself to conversing with people of a superior rank to his own—he then exclaimed; “By the Devil and his horns! as you yourself remarked a little while ago, you can tell tales of other people, my lord, without much compunction, and I fancy if everyone followed your example, I should not be at such a loss what to say, as I choose to appear!”

The lord of Vez, perfectly aware, as was the bailiff’s wife, of the menace conveyed in these words, answered by looking Thibault up and down with eyes that were starting with anger.

“Oh!” said Madame Magloire, somewhat imprudently, “you will see, he is going to invent some scandalous tale about me.”

“Have no fear, Madame,” replied Thibault, who had quite recovered his self-possession, “you have left me nothing to invent on that score.”

===Chinese===
苏珊娜太太一听蒂博被这般定了位分，便做了一个鄙夷轻蔑的手势；马格卢瓦师傅则往后退了一步，脸上红一阵白一阵。倒不是这位好心的小个子人有多么自矜身价，而是他生平最恨形形色色的欺诈；他脸红，并非因为自己竟与一个鞋匠碰过杯，而是因为他竟与一个说谎的骗子、一个背信弃义的小人同饮过。

在这一通劈头盖脸的臭骂之下，蒂博始终抱臂而立，纹丝不动，嘴角还挂着一丝笑。他毫不担心：轮到他开口的时候，他轻而易举便能报这一箭之仇。而眼下，开口的时候似乎到了。他用一种轻佻戏谑的腔调——这腔调表明他正在渐渐习惯与比自己身份高的人攀谈——叫道：“凭魔鬼和他的犄角起誓！这话还是您方才自己说的呢，老爷，您编排起别人来，心里可没多少过意不去；我琢磨着，要是人人都学您的样，我要想找出几句该说的话来，可就不像我爱装出来的那样词穷啦！”

韦兹领主——同执达吏太太一样——心知肚明这话里所裹挟的威胁，他只是把蒂博从头到脚打量了一番，一双眼睛气得都快瞪出眼眶来了，权作回答。

“噢！”马格卢瓦太太未免冒失地说，“您瞧着吧，他这就要编排我一桩什么下流的丑事啦。”

“您放心，太太，”蒂博答道，这会儿他早已完全镇定下来，“那一头您可什么也没给我留下发明的余地。”

===Original===
“Oh! the vile wretch!” she cried, “you see, I was right; he has got some malicious slander to report about me; he is determined to revenge himself because I would not return his sheep’s-eyes, to punish me because I was not willing to warn my husband that he was paying court to me.” During this speech of Madame Suzanne’s the lord of Vez had picked up his sword and advanced threateningly towards Thibault. But the Bailiff threw himself between them, and held back the Baron’s arm. It was fortunate for Thibault that he did so, for the latter did not move an inch to avoid the blow, evidently prepared at the last moment to utter some terrible wish which would avert the danger from him; but the Bailiff interposing, Thibault had no need to resort to this means of help.

“Gently, my lord!” said the Maître Magloire, “this man is not worthy our anger. I am but a plain citizen myself, but you see, I have only contempt for what he says, and I readily forgive him the way in which he has endeavoured to abuse my hospitality.”

Madame Magloire now thought that her moment had come for moistening the situation of affairs with her tears, and burst into loud sobs.

===Chinese===
“噢！这卑鄙的坏蛋！”她叫道，“您瞧，我说得没错吧；他这是要编些恶毒的中伤来造我的谣；他这是铁了心要报复，因为我没肯回报他抛过来的媚眼，要惩罚我，因为我不肯提醒我丈夫他在向我献殷勤。”苏珊娜太太这番话讲着讲着，韦兹领主已拾起了自己的剑，气势汹汹地朝蒂博逼过去。可执达吏却扑到两人中间，一把按住了男爵的手臂。蒂博能逃过这一劫，多亏他这一按；原来蒂博一寸也没挪动身子去避这一剑，显然是预备在最后关头发下一个可怕的愿望，好叫这危险从自己身上移开；不过既有执达吏从中斡旋，蒂博也就不必动用这一桩救命的法门了。

“悠着点，老爷！”马格卢瓦师傅说，“这个人犯不上叫咱们动这么大的肝火。我不过是个平平常常的百姓，可您瞧见了，他说的话我只当是放屁，他这样辜负我的款待、反咬一口，我也乐于宽恕他。”

马格卢瓦太太这时觉得轮到她拿眼泪来润一润这一摊局面了，便放声痛哭起来。

===Original===
“Do not weep, dear wife!” said the Bailiff, with his usual kind and simple good-nature. “Of what could this man accuse you, even suppose he had something to bring against you! Of having deceived me? Well, I can only say, that made as I am, I feel I still have favours to grant you and thanks to render you, for all the happy days which I owe to you. Do not fear for a moment that this apprehension of an imaginary evil will alter my behaviour towards you. I shall always be kind and indulgent to you, Suzanne, and as I shall never shut my heart against you, so will I never shut my door against my friends. When one is small and of little account it is best to submit quietly and to trust; one need have no fear then but of cowards and evildoers, and I am convinced, I am happy to say, that they are not so plentiful as they are thought to be. And, after all, by my faith! if the bird of misfortune should fly in, by the door or by the window, by Saint Gregory, the patron of drinkers, there shall be such a noise of singing, such a clinking of glasses, that he will soon be obliged to fly out again by the way he came in!”

===Chinese===
“别哭，亲爱的太太！”执达吏说，照旧带着他那副和善淳厚的好脾气，“就算他真有什么凭据要说您的不是，这男人又能告您什么呢！告您骗了我？好吧，我只能说，凭我生就的这副性子，我仍觉得我还有好处可以赏您，还有谢意要对您表示，为了您带给我这许多快活的日子。您一刻也不用担心，这一桩凭空臆想出来的祸事会改变我对您的态度。我永远都会待您和蔼宽厚，苏珊娜；我既永远不会对您关上我的心扉，也就永远不会对我的朋友们关上我的家门。一个人既生得渺小、又无足轻重，最好的法子便是俯首顺从、信靠旁人；这样便什么都不用怕，只怕那些懦夫和作恶之徒罢了——而我可以欣慰地说，我深信这些人并不像人们所想的那么多。况且，凭我的信仰发誓！纵然那不祥之鸟当真从门里、或者从窗口飞进来，凭酒徒们的守护神圣格列高利（Saint Gregory）起誓，我们也必得把歌唱得那么响亮，把杯碰得那么叮当响，叫它没多久就不得不从进来的原路飞出去！”

===Original===
Before he had ended, Madame Suzanne had thrown herself at his feet, and was kissing his hands. His speech, with its mingling of sadness and philosophy, had made more impression upon her than would have a sermon from the most eloquent of preachers. Even the lord of Vez did not remain unmoved; a tear gathered in the corner of his eye, and he lifted his finger to wipe it away, before holding out his hand to the Bailiff, saying as he did so:

“By the horn of Beelzebub! my dear friend, you have an upright mind and a kind heart, and it would be a sin indeed to bring trouble upon you; and if I have ever had a thought of doing you wrong, may God forgive me for it! I can safely swear, whatever happens, that I shall never have such another again.”

While this reconciliation was taking place between the three secondary actors in this tale, the situation of the fourth, that is of the principal character in it, was becoming more and more embarrassing.

===Chinese===
他话还没讲完，苏珊娜太太已经扑倒在他脚下，亲吻起他的双手来。他这一番话，悲凉与达观交织，比最能言善辩的布道者的一篇讲道，还更重重地打动了她。便是韦兹领主，也不能无动于衷；一滴泪在他眼角聚起，他抬手抹去了它，这才把手伸给执达吏，一边说道：

“凭别西卜（Beelzebub）的犄角起誓！我亲爱的朋友，您有一副正直的心肠、一颗善良的心，给您招灾惹祸可真是作孽啊；倘若我曾经动过加害于您的念头，愿上帝饶恕我！我可以发一个稳当的誓：无论日后如何，我绝不会再起这样的念头了。”

正当这一段故事里头三个次要角色之间达成这番和解之际，第四位——也就是本故事的主角——处境却越来越尴尬了。

===Original===
Thibault’s heart was swelling with rage and hatred; himself unaware of the rapid growth of evil within him, he was fast growing, from a selfish and covetous man, into a wicked one. Suddenly, his eyes flashing, he cried aloud: “I do not know what holds me back from putting a terrible end to all this!”

On hearing this exclamation, which had all the character of a menace in it, the Baron and Suzanne understood it to mean that some great and unknown and unexpected danger was hanging over everybody’s heads. But the Baron was not easily intimidated, and he drew his sword for the second time and made a movement towards Thibault. Again the Bailiff interposed.

“My lord Baron! my lord Baron!” said Thibault in a low voice, “this is the second time that you have, in wish at least, passed your sword through my body; twice therefore you have been a murderer in thought! Take care! one can sin in other ways besides sinning in deed.”

“Thousand devils!” cried the Baron, beside himself with anger, “the rascal is actually reading me a moral lesson! My friend, you were wanting a little while ago to spit him like a woodcock, allow me to give him one light touch, such as the matador gives the bull, and I will answer for it, that he won’t get up again in a hurry.”

“I beseech you on my knees, as a favour to your humble servant, my lord,” replied the Bailiff, “to let him go in peace; and deign to remember, that, being my guest, there should no hurt nor harm be done to him in this poor house of mine.”

===Chinese===
蒂博的心因狂怒与仇恨而鼓胀欲裂；他自己也未察觉心头那恶念滋长之速，他正飞快地从一个自私贪婪的人，变成一个作恶的人。蓦地，他双目闪光，高声叫道：“我也不知是什么拦着我，没叫我把这一切收个可怕的场！”

一听这句喝喊——其中十足的威胁意味——男爵和苏珊娜便明白，这是说有一桩巨大、莫测、突如其来的危险，正悬在每个人的头顶上。可男爵不是轻易就被吓倒的，他再度拔出剑来，朝蒂博逼近一步。执达吏又一次居间挡住了。

“男爵老爷！男爵老爷！”蒂博压低了嗓子说，“这已是第二回了，您至少在心里，已经把您的剑戳过了我的身子；这么着，您在念头上已经做了两回杀人凶手啦！小心着！人除了在行为上犯罪，也还可以在别的法子上犯罪呢。”

“一千个小鬼！”男爵怒不可遏地叫道，“这泼皮竟敢给我上起道德课来！我的朋友，方才您想把他像田鹬一样串起来，这会儿就请准我给他轻轻来一下——就像斗牛士给公牛那一刺——我保管他一时半会儿爬不起来。”

“我跪下求您啦，权当赏您这卑微的仆人一个面子，老爷，”执达吏答道，“放他平平安安地走吧；并请您开恩记着，他既是我这儿的客人，在我这寒舍里就不该让他受什么伤、遭什么祸。”

===Original===
“So be it!” answered the Baron, “I shall meet him again. All kinds of bad reports are about concerning him, and poaching is not the only harm reported of him; he has been seen and recognised running the forest along with a pack of wolves—and astonishingly tame wolves at that. It’s my opinion that the scoundrel does not always spend his midnights at home, but sits astride a broomstick oftener than becomes a good Catholic; the owner of the mill at Croyolles has made complaint of his wizardries. However, we will not talk of it any more now; I shall have his hut searched, and if everything there is not as it should be, the wizard’s hole shall be destroyed, for I will not allow it to remain on his Highness’s territory. And now, take yourself off, and that quickly!”

The shoemaker’s exasperation had come to a pitch during this menacing tirade from the Baron; but, nevertheless, he profited by the passage that was cleared for him, and went out of the room. Thanks to his faculty of being able to see in the dark, he walked straight to the door, opened it, and passed over the threshold of the house, where he had left behind so many fond hopes, now lost forever, slamming the door after him with such violence that the whole house shook.
